## 1. ViRA: Toward Ultra-High-Resolution Visual Agents via Cross-Input Relative Policy Optimization

- **作者**：Huawei Lin, Jinting Liu, Tongyan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：74 pages, 11 figures, 26 tables. Code: https://github.com/huawei-lin/ViRA

Visual agents answer questions about ultra-high-resolution photos by zooming into the relevant regions. However, existing agents often answer from common sense instead of reading the evidence. Reinforcement learning (RL) on the final answer cannot correct this, because a guess earns the same reward as reading. We propose Cross-Input Relative Policy Optimization (CrossRPO), which trains agents on contrast sets. A contrast set asks one question about several versions of an image that differ only in a small region with the evidence and have different answers, so only reading the evidence solves the whole set. CrossRPO rewards solving the whole set, and we prove that it does not reinforce a set answered from common sense. We build contrast sets for training (ViRA-Train) and evaluation (ViRA-Bench) by editing one small object in images of 21 to 120 megapixels, and we photograph further sets with real changes (ViRA-Bench-Real) to exclude editing traces. We train two Visual Reasoning Agents, ViRA-9B and ViRA-27B, with CrossRPO on only about 3,500 items of ViRA-Train each. CrossRPO achieves 63.3% set accuracy on ViRA-Bench at the maximum resolution, against 59.3% for GRPO. ViRA-9B is the most accurate open model of its size on all benchmarks, and ViRA-27B outperforms Claude Haiku 5.5 and GPT-6-Luna, both at their highest thinking level, on most benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21605) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21605)

## 2. FaceJev: Direct Visual Decisions for Face Forgery Detection

- **作者**：Siran Peng, Tianshuo Zhang, Yibo Xue et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Face forgery detection methods often perform well on seen manipulations but generalize poorly to unseen ones. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) can recognize facial parts and assess their visual plausibility. Existing MLLM-based methods typically express these judgments through textual explanations, but seldom treat individual part decisions as explicit learning targets. We present FaceJev, an MLLM-based method for face forgery detection through direct visual decisions. Following the Jev formulation, FaceJev answers eight independent questions about facial parts using a shared image encoding and reads anomaly probabilities from the native answer logits. A face is classified as forged if any part is predicted to be abnormal, directly linking the detection result to the part decisions. We construct PartForensics, a dataset of approximately 160k images with binary anomaly targets for the eight facial parts, to supervise these decisions. We further propose FS-GRPO, which optimizes each part decision through an exact reward comparison with its alternative, using both detection and part rewards. FaceJev outperforms the strongest baseline by 7.25 percentage points on unseen manipulations. Extensive ablation studies validate our design choices and the effectiveness of the main components.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21604) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21604)

## 3. How VLMs Plan What's Next

- **作者**：Rohit Gandikota, Arnab Sen Sharma, Adam Belfki et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.LG

When a vision-language model describes an image, it names one object at a time and it rarely repeats itself, yet how the model plans this order is far from obvious. We characterize the mechanism a VLM uses to decide where to look before it looks. The mechanism has four main parts. First, binding heads match the relevant text tokens against the image and resolve them into pointers to the matched image region. Second, the same binding heads bind each written answer to the region it came from, and the model turns it into a progress signal: a cursor, the position in the image up to which the list has been written. Third, a select step chooses the nearest unwritten region after the cursor, or stops. Finally, gaze heads execute the chosen pointer and return a concept of what is drawn there, which language heads and the late MLPs turn into a word in the language of the question. We validate each part of this mechanism through causal patching in Qwen3-VL models on a synthetic visual retrieval task, and test our mechanisms against alternative hypotheses. We further show that the mechanism holds across input formats, natural photographs, and languages, and that its binding, relay, and gaze heads appear at every model size from 2B to 32B. This work allows us to investigate how a VLM turns a question and an image into a sequence of answers, layer by layer and token by token. More broadly, it opens a way to study how models plan over complex multi-step tasks with long rollouts. Our project is available at https://plan.baulab.info/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21600) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21600)

## 4. When Do Histology Foundation Models Need Geometric Test-Time Augmentation?

- **作者**：Lars Chen, Valentino F. Foit, Dorry Segev et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：17 pages, 5 figures

Test-time augmentation (TTA) aggregates model predictions across multiple transformed views of an input during inference. Because histology images lack a canonical orientation, $D_4$ TTA---pooling across the 8 rotations and reflections of a square---is a standard default in computational pathology. However, the rise of self-supervised histology foundation models (FMs) with inherent rotational invariance calls its continued utility into question. We benchmark 9 histology FMs and 6 general-purpose models across four patch-level and two whole-slide image (WSI) datasets. We find that $D_4$ TTA remains a reliable post hoc improvement, but benefits diminish from $+1.98$ percentage points (pp) for general-purpose backbones to $+0.50$ pp for histology FMs on TCGA-UT, and shrink further for the largest models. To eliminate computational overhead, our experiments demonstrate that entropy routing---applying TTA selectively to uncertain predictions---recovers most of the gain while augmenting only ${\sim}13\%$ of samples. Finally, we show that patch-level TTA improvements do not transfer to slide-level multiple instance learning (MIL). Code: https://github.com/lars-chen/histology-tta

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21565) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21565)

## 5. Wildfire-SSR: Geolocation-Aware Multimodal Fusion for Structure-Level Wildfire Risk Assessment

- **作者**：Yu-Hsuan Ho, Yiming Xiao, Hao-En Lai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Structure-level wildfire vulnerability assessment is critical for reducing losses in wildland-urban interface communities, yet existing methods often fail to combine parcel-scale ignition pathways with community-scale geographic coverage. This study introduces Wildfire-SSR (Satellite and Street-view for wildfire Risk), a geolocation-aware multimodal framework that integrates macro-scale satellite context with micro-scale street-view evidence. A Vision-Language Model extracts wildfire-relevant building and site attributes from pre-event street-view panoramas and converts them into structured semantic text. These features are spatially aligned with satellite imagery through a decoupled cross-attention fusion decoder, while a frozen satellite-pretrained DINOv3 encoder captures overhead visual context. Alternative geolocation embedding strategies are evaluated to anchor text-derived features within each satellite patch. The framework is tested on approximately 37,000 structures affected by the 2025 Eaton and Palisades fires in Los Angeles, using wildfire risk labels derived from relative post-event damage patterns to better represent inherent structural vulnerability. Rotary Positional Embedding provides the strongest spatial grounding, achieving a building localization F1 score of 0.6735 and a harmonic mean F1 score of 0.2476 for five-class pixel-level risk classification, outperforming unimodal baselines and an unanchored multimodal configuration. The model also shows a conservative bias in higher-risk classes, reducing severe-risk underestimation, and completes end-to-end assessment of the study area in 37 minutes on a single GPU. Wildfire-SSR reframes cross-view fusion as localized semantic alignment and provides a scalable tool for mitigation prioritization, resilience planning, and insurance-oriented wildfire risk assessment.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21563) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21563)

## 6. Reference Forcing: Long Video Generation with Region-Guided Drift Correction

- **作者**：Sheng Zhao, Haitian Zheng, Weikai Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages, 12 figures, 3 tables. Project page: https://zhaosheng-thu.github.io/reference-force/

Long video generation should keep the subject and background consistent while supporting rich, natural motion. However, autoregressive models are often distilled with distribution matching distillation (DMD) over short windows, which does not train them to keep this consistency over long ranges. As a result, the subject and background can drift over time. Methods that manage the key-value (KV) cache retain or retrieve history to reduce drift. Yet this history may itself have drifted. We propose Reference Forcing, which uses the initial frames as an appearance reference. We first build a teacher that uses attention masks to force each region of a new frame to attend to the same region in the initial frames. The teacher finds these regions with off-the-shelf segmentation models. We then distill this behavior into a separate attention path of the student, which needs no external models at inference. We further introduce value correction, which corrects drifted values in the KV cache toward the matching values of drift-free initial frames. Experiments show that Reference Forcing achieves state-of-the-art subject and background consistency among long-video methods on different base models, and its advantage grows with video length. Ablations show both components are needed to gain consistency without losing motion. More visualizations and code are available at https://zhaosheng-thu.github.io/reference-force/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21559) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21559)

## 7. BLIP-Live: Balancing Timeliness & Firing Precision in Proactive Streaming Video Agents

- **作者**：Kanchana Ranasinghe, Honglu Zhou, Dominick Reilly et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Streaming video understanding is becoming increasingly important for real-world interactive systems. Such systems must be proactive for natural and useful human interaction: they should respond as soon as sufficient evidence becomes available while avoiding premature, redundant, or incorrect responses. We present BLIP-Live, a framework unifying data, metrics, harness, and training around a shared formulation of proactivity as effective causal response decisions balancing the two competing objectives of responding vs silence. First, we construct BLIP-Live-780K dataset containing human annotations of proactive response content and timing, which defines this desired behavior. We then propose PAUC-F, a metric to effectively measure our desired proactive behavior. PAUC-F combines existing response timeliness metrics with firing precision to reward early useful responses without rewarding excessive firing. With our desired behavior defined and measurable, we finally investigate model-agnostic inference harnesses that draw out proactive streaming capabilities of offline vision language models (VLMs), together with training pipelines that learn to further improve their proactive responsiveness. Across both settings, we find that combinations of sliding visual windows, online interaction memory, and structured response outputs consistently yield strongest proactive performance. Our experiments result in two models, with our BLIP-Live-Qwen model achieving state-of-the-art performance across four proactive streaming benchmarks, with gains up to 44% over baselines and outperforming multiple closed API models. Our code and data will be released publicly.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21553) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21553)

## 8. Seen but Unsaid: Locating and Repairing Cross-Image Comparison in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Zhaoning Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.LG
- **备注**：18 pages, 3 figures, 19 tables

Vision-language models (VLMs) often fail to match a point or an object across two images, even when the answer can be decoded from their own hidden states. We trace this failure from the visual tokens to the answer letter, in Qwen3-VL (2B-32B), InternVL3.5-8B, and Gemma-3-12B, on synthetic shape matching, same-instance point correspondence (HPatches), and semantic correspondence (SPair-71k), and find two distinct failure points, a small set of attention heads that carries the comparison, and a repair that touches 0.02% of the parameters. Two failure points: exact copies are compared but not read out. An answer-position probe exceeds the calibrated output by 15 to 25 points in every family, for nameable and unnameable shapes alike, and steering along the probe's direction moves no more answers than random directions. Semantic correspondences are present in the visual tokens but never reach the answer. One pathway: redistributing the cross-image attention of 16-32 heads, with its mass preserved, removes 17-22 points of synthetic and 15-32 points of HPatches accuracy, against at most 7 for random or attention-matched heads. A lever that training finds on its own: training only these heads' query and key projections raises SPair-71k accuracy by 28.3 points (Qwen3-VL-8B) and 25.1 points (InternVL3.5-8B), beats both layer-matched and attention-matched heads, and transfers to HPatches and BLINK. An unconstrained all-layer adapter routes 21-51% of its gain through the attention of the same heads, against at most 7% for random heads, and does so by changing what they read (83-86% of the effect) rather than their weights. Every decision rule was frozen before its run, and every reported number is recomputed by independent verification scripts.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21539) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21539)

## 9. How Much Does Saliency Accuracy Matter for Saliency-Guided 360-degree Image Compression?

- **作者**：Sakib Ahmed, Sidi-Mohammed Senouci, Philippe Brunet et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 7 figures, 1 table. Accepted at the 2027 IEEE/SICE International Symposium on System Integration (SII 2027)

Saliency-guided coding allocates bits to the regions viewers are most likely to attend, but accurate 360-degree saliency prediction is computationally expensive, an obstacle for low-latency immersive streaming applications such as robot teleoperation. We quantify how much saliency-prediction accuracy actually translates into compression gain. Building on a saliency-guided rate-distortion-optimized (RDO) learned codec, modified so that the training-time latent mask is driven by ground-truth saliency, which yields a single, predictor-agnostic codec, we evaluate saliency predictors spanning two orders of magnitude in compute on both the learned codec and H.265/HEVC. The results show that higher saliency-prediction accuracy yields diminishing compression gains: a lightweight predictor fast enough for live use (52 frames per second) captures 65% of the ground-truth oracle's bit-rate saving, a 3.3x heavier one reaches 74%, and a further 38x increase in compute adds no measurable benefit, on either codec. Therefore, lightweight predictors might also be a practical choice for latency-constrained 360-degree streaming.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21532) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21532)

## 10. DuoFlow: Towards Synchronized Speech and Motion Generation with Fine-Grained Control

- **作者**：Jun Dong, Ronghui Li, Zhongyuan Hu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

An embodied speaker's expressiveness depends not only on what is said, but also on how the voice sounds and how the body moves. However, existing joint speech-motion generation models are largely driven implicitly by transcript semantics or intermediate speech representations, offering little explicit control over motion hierarchy, body parts, or vocal style. To bridge this gap, we construct MotionSpeech, a structured benchmark for controllable joint speech-motion generation that includes transcripts, voice-style descriptions, and motion conditions at two hierarchical levels. Building on this benchmark, we propose DuoFlow, a dual-stream latent flow transformer that jointly denoises speech and motion under a shared noise level and a unified second-based temporal coordinate. Modality-specific residual connectors inject structured conditions into the corresponding streams, layer-wise bidirectional cross-modal attention coordinates their generation, and a monotonic phoneme pathway combines normalized progress anchors with CTC supervision to strengthen speech temporal modeling. DuoFlow provides a unified interface for fine-grained motion and vocal-style control and demonstrates the ability to synthesize high-quality, synchronized, and instruction-consistent speech-motion sequences.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21530) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21530)

## 11. GHySSeg: Real-Time Geometric Ground and Road Segmentation for Hybrid Solid-State LiDAR

- **作者**：Martín Palos, Fernando García
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 8 figures, 3 tables. Submitted to IEEE Transactions on Intelligent Transportation Systems. This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible. HySoLiD-Drive dataset: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21705196

Hybrid solid-state LiDAR sensors are making their way into production vehicles, yet the data they produce differs structurally from that of the established spinning mechanical sensors behind today's autonomous-driving datasets: it is ring-free, dense, cluttered, and concentrated in a frontal field of view. The classical geometric methods that autonomous vehicles rely on for drivable space computation and navigation assume the ordered ring-and-column structure of a 360-degree spinning LiDAR and degrade or fail on this data, while learned alternatives are computationally heavy, hard to certify, and trained almost exclusively on spinning-LiDAR data. We demonstrate that the same dense, per-curve geometry is instead an opportunity to close the divide between the industry's preferred sensors and autonomous-driving research. We present GHySSeg, a purely geometric method that segments ground and drivable road from a single hybrid solid-state LiDAR frame by tracing each scan curve and reading two geometric signatures, a step in height and a fold-back in range, with no training and no graphics processing unit. To evaluate it, we release HySoLiD-Drive, the first public benchmark dedicated to ground and road segmentation on an automotive hybrid solid-state LiDAR, comprising 100 manually labeled and 7000 unlabeled frames recorded by two vehicles in real driving. On this benchmark, GHySSeg surpasses both geometric and learned state-of-the-art baselines across ground and road segmentation, reaching an intersection-over-union of 0.907 for road and 0.896 for ground at over 0.96 precision, while running in real time at about 22 milliseconds per frame on a single core.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21507) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21507)

## 12. Lost in the Hallway: Benchmarking Persistent Spatial Memory Across Revisits

- **作者**：Henry Zheng, Weibo Gong, Yang Yue et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Persistent spatial understanding is essential for embodied agents to operate reliably in environments they repeatedly encounter. It requires recognizing revisited places despite viewpoint changes, distinguishing them from similar-looking rooms, and updating memories as environments change. Yet existing evaluations often leave physical place identity across separated visits implicit. We introduce ReSpaceBench, a benchmark comprising 3,244 questions across 590 synthetic and real-home scenes, organized into 9 tasks spanning room association, persistent state tracking, and downstream reasoning. Controlled walkthroughs define visits by physical room entry and deliberately include same-type room distractors and cross-session object changes. Our evaluation of 32 models and 3 agentic video-understanding frameworks reveals substantial limitations: the strongest evaluated zero-shot model achieves 50.5% overall accuracy and 52.2% on a 450-question subset, compared with 90.1% for human annotators. The evaluated agentic frameworks offer no overall improvement over direct inference with the same base model. Analysis of agent execution traces reveals evidence omissions and incorrect cross-visit room associations as major failure modes, suggesting that iterative evidence seeking alone does not ensure consistent spatial memory. Further analyses expose a gap between recognizing familiar rooms and identifying the correct visited room, while annotation-guided sampling highlights the importance of preserving intervening visits and transitions. We also provide RS-30K, a separate training dataset from synthetic sources with no template, scene, route, or video overlap with the benchmark. Fine-tuning Qwen3-VL-8B raises overall accuracy from 35.5% to 60.4%, with the largest gains in association and improvements extending to real-life scenes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21485) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21485)

## 13. LynnReal-Hand: Unlocking World Interaction with Hand Control

- **作者**：Shaohao Rui, Chaoyang Zhang, Xiaofeng Mao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Interactive video world models enable exploration through text and camera controls, yet direct object interaction requires finer control. Hands provide a natural interface, but translating continuous hand movements into coherent visual responses remains challenging because of complex hand-object dynamics and demanding real-time computation. To address these challenges, we introduce LynnReal-Hand, a hand-controlled video world model that unifies precise interaction modeling with efficient streaming generation. We curate 100K interaction clips spanning diverse real and synthetic environments and develop hand-aware supervision to improve fine-grained hand motions and object interactions. Building on this foundation, we learn a causal flow map for few-step generation and introduce region-aware score distillation that combines complementary video and image priors to refine interaction dynamics and visual appearance. To further reduce inference latency, we propose Ultra-Fast-VAE, a compact video decoder that preserves high reconstruction fidelity with substantially lower computational cost. Extensive evaluations on HandBench demonstrate superior hand-following accuracy and visual quality compared with existing approaches. Our full-sequence model achieves an FVD of 181.67 and a MANO Dice score of 93.02, while the three-step causal model maintains a MANO Dice score of 92.49. For real-time interaction, 540p streaming on a single H200 reaches 18.03 FPS with 741ms latency and an optimized peak GPU memory footprint of only 34.89GB. We will release the datasets, model weights, and the full training and inference code to support research on hand-controlled video world models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21479) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21479)

## 14. Event-Triggered Visual Perception for Real-Time Harvest Outcome Estimation in Mechanical Blueberry Harvesting

- **作者**：Nicolas Miroslav Husár, Jelle Driessen, Petra Bosilj et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Mechanical blueberry harvesting requires a balance between removing ripe fruit and avoiding premature removal of unripe berries that could remain on the bush for a later harvest. However, operators generally lack real-time information about the maturity composition of the fruit being collected. This study develops an event-triggered machine-vision system that measures the maturity of harvested blueberries directly on the harvester. When a filled crate is moved by the machine, an RGB image is automatically captured and analysed using a three-class detector that identifies ripe, semi-ripe, and unripe berries. The system operates locally on the existing CPU-only industrial computer and therefore requires neither continuous image processing nor dedicated GPU hardware. During field testing, 79 crate images containing 48,634 visible blueberries in manually verified reference annotations were evaluated. The vision system estimated an overall ripe-fruit fraction of 90.22%, compared with 90.97% determined manually. Per-image ripe-fruit estimates showed a mean absolute error of 0.935 percentage points and a Pearson correlation of r=0.967. Maturity estimates remained similar between the two sides of the harvester for which the light conditions varied despite poorer absolute counting performance under direct sunlight. All field events were processed within the available 14 s per-side crate interval. The proposed approach provides a practical means of quantifying one component of harvest selectivity while the machine is operating. Such information could support operator decision-making and, when combined with measurements of fruit recovery and losses, contribute to future feedback-based control of mechanical harvesting.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21472) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21472)

## 15. Balanced Twin Weight Factorization for Efficient MLLMs

- **作者**：Bryan A. Plummer, Shan Lu, Keval Doshi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL

Weight Factorization (WF) methods aim to reduce a model's latency, memory, or both. These typically decompose a pretrained model into two or more factors that approximate the original model weights with fewer parameters and floating point operations (FLOPs). However, translating fewer FLOPs into reduced latency is challenging for WF methods in practice due to overhead introduced by replacing a single large weight matrix with multiple smaller ones. To address this, we propose Balanced Twin Weight Factorization (BTWF), which uses the unique structure of factorized models to simultaneously lower latency and increase learning capacity. Specifically, BTWF operates in the shared basis WF setting, where two or more layers are decomposed into a shared basis matrix and a layer-specific mixing matrix. If these shared layers have the same input (i.e., are twins), then we need only perform this shared forward pass once, reducing inference time. However, twin layers are often of varying sizes, and methods from prior work tend to favor the larger layer. In contrast, our approach boosts performance by combining Factor Component Balancing (FCB), which ensures every shared member is well represented in the selected factors, with Block Contribution Balancing (BCB), which considers downstream model effects. Further, we also show that an overparameterized BTWF, where some layer's factors result in higher FLOPs than the original during training, can boost task performance without additional resources at test time. Experiments on six models over nine benchmarks show BTWF obtains a 2-6% gain over the state-of-the-art without expensive finetuning (1-3.5% gain with it) at comparable computational costs.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21459) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21459)

## 16. Out of Sight, Into Memory: Compact Visual Latents Retain Local Evidence in Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing

- **作者**：Meng Tang, Siyuan Du, Yuxin Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Question answering over ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing scenes poses a tension for multimodal large language models: global overviews obscure small objects and local attributes, whereas detailed crops consume substantial context. Compressing inspected crops into short sequences of continuous visual latents offers a compact memory once crop tokens are discarded, yet diagnostic studies on natural images find that models often ignore latent content, leaving unclear whether compact latents can retain fine-grained targets lost in downsampled UHR remote sensing scenes. To isolate evidence retention from region selection, we encode high-resolution crops into latent memory tokens for frozen backbones and evaluate both their predictive utility and content dependence. Across two generations of Qwen-VL on LRS-GRO, a 64-token memory achieves 7.6- to 9.9-fold compression of retained crop tokens and improves accuracy by 2.5 to 4.8 percentage points over the downsampled global image alone, recovering most of the high-resolution crop gain with consistent behavior on XLRS-Bench. Substituting memory from another image or another region of the same scene reduces accuracy by 5.8 to 7.4 percentage points and yields lower accuracy than removing memory altogether, demonstrating that predictions depend on the encoded regional evidence. Restoring the source crop within the same model reduces the accuracy loss caused by zeroing or removing memory. These findings establish that compact visual latents retain useful local evidence in UHR remote sensing and identify evidence availability as a key factor governing their contribution.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21457) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21457)

## 17. [CLS] and [EOS] Tokens are not Enough for CLIP-based Class-Incremental Learning

- **作者**：Hao Sun, Tao Hu, Shixiong Kai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to TMM. Code is available at: https://github.com/LAMDA-CL/PACE

Class-Incremental Learning (CIL) aims to empower models to continuously acquire new knowledge while mitigating catastrophic forgetting. With the advent of vision-language models like CLIP, leveraging their cross-modal alignment capabilities to address CIL challenges has emerged as a dominant paradigm. Existing methods leverage CLIP's global representations (i.e., the visual [CLS] token and the textual [EOS] token) for cross-modal alignment. While achieving effective classification, they overlook the rich patch-level visual features and token-level textual semantics inherent in CLIP. For example, the [CLS] token aggregates local information to capture the general concept of "cat", while the patch-level details retain specific localized features essential for recognition, such as the tail, fur, and face. We propose PACE (PAtch-token Cross-modal Enhancement) to uncover the long-neglected local representations within CLIP. First, based on the global representations like [CLS] token and [EOS] token, we identify the top-k most crucial visual patches and textual tokens. Subsequently, to better utilize the extracted key local features, we design a cross-modal fusion module. This module leverages local textual semantics to refine visual representations, while augmenting textual semantics with local visual features. As the fusion module is shared across all tasks, directly fine-tuning it on new tasks would interfere with the knowledge learned from previous tasks, leading to catastrophic forgetting. To alleviate this, we employ a projection-based update strategy to effectively preserve the critical parameter space of previous tasks during the adaptation to new tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PACE significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods across multiple CIL benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21440) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21440)

## 18. Power Line Detection in Point Clouds with Point-SAM and RANSAC

- **作者**：Christoph Dalitz, Oskar Waedt, Steffen Goebbels et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 10 figures, accepted for VISAPP 2027

This article deals with the problem of detecting the position of power lines in airborne laser scanning point clouds. We address both the problem of segmenting power lines from background like ground and vegetation and of identifying individual lines. For segmentation we compare a rule-based approach with one based on transfer learning (Point-SAM). For individual line detection, we derive the mathematical formulas for RANSAC applied to 3D catenaries and compare it with a non-parametric method (TriplClust). All methods are evaluated on a newly created dataset "PowLiDFR", which we make publicly available. The samples have been taken from the French project LiDAR HD and cover a variety of terrain layouts and point cloud resolutions. On this dataset, an unsupervised transfer learning outperformed a mere rule-based segmentation, and for individual power line detection, RANSAC was better in following the shape of catenaries than the non-parametric algorithm.

## 19. Quality and Similarity Indicators for Video-Based Heart Rate and Respiratory Rate Fusion: A Controlled Evaluation

- **作者**：Nhi Nguyen, Le Nguyen, Manuel Lage Cañellas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 7 figures, 4 tables; with 14 pages supplementary material included

Camera-based heart rate (HR) and respiratory rate (RR) estimates vary in reliability from window to window, and a common remedy is to fuse several extraction methods with weights from reference-free signal quality indicators. However, few studies test whether these indicators identify the most accurate method in each window or outperform simpler fusion. We evaluate 11 quality indicators and 8 inter-method similarity measures for fusing 7 HR and 10 RR extractors on four public datasets (75 test subjects). Filters, indicator directions, weighting parameters and a learned ridge baseline are selected on a separate five-subject set before testing. Fusion is compared with equal averaging, a constant typical-rate predictor, the best single method selected in advance and in hindsight, and a per-window oracle with a permutation-based null, using subject-level bootstrap confidence intervals. For HR, quality-weighted fusion reduced mean absolute error by 10.9% [5.2, 18.4] relative to averaging and by 15.6% [8.1, 23.1] relative to the method selected in advance, and was statistically indistinguishable from the best method selected in hindsight. Weighting methods by their agreement did not improve on averaging. For RR, the indicators could not identify the more accurate estimates: sharpened quality weighting increased error (-2.6% [-4.8, -0.8]), fused estimates were uncorrelated with the reference (r <= 0.22), and averaging was less accurate than the typical-rate predictor (-3.1% [-6.1, -0.4]). With a second respiratory reference, apparent RR gains reflected estimates moving toward its typical rate. Quality-guided fusion is worthwhile for video HR; for RR, 30-s windows improved estimates but not fusion.

## 20. Flexible Multi-Stack Super-Resolution of Clinical Brain MRI Using Hetero-Orientation Variational Encoding: Expert Reader Evaluation in a Nigerian Cohort

- **作者**：Kabir Hamzah Muhammad, Lorena Garcia-Foncillas Macias, Soumya Snigdha Kundu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages, 14 figures

High-resolution brain MRI is important for neuroradiological assessment, yet routine clinical protocols often rely on fast, anisotropic 2D acquisitions, particularly in resource-constrained settings. These acquisitions may differ in orientation, alignment and anatomical coverage, resulting in partially overlapping image stacks. Existing learning-based super-resolution methods typically rely on single, under-constrained inputs or fixed multi-stack configurations with idealised acquisition geometry. We introduce MS-HVED, a sequence-agnostic framework for flexible multi-stack brain MRI super-resolution. Available axial, coronal and sagittal stacks are independently encoded into multi-scale Gaussian posteriors and fused using a field-of-view-aware Product-of-Gaussians formulation that limits contributions from unsupported regions. Physics-informed domain randomisation simulates heterogeneous clinical acquisition conditions, including obliqueness, partial coverage and imaging artefacts. MS-HVED achieved competitive or superior reconstruction fidelity across synthetic benchmarks and improved downstream tissue segmentation relative to low-resolution inputs. To assess generalisation beyond the training cohorts, we performed external clinical validation on 30 routine brain MRI examinations from a Nigerian cohort, representing an under-represented population in medical imaging research. These examinations formed the largest component of a blinded reader study involving 44 real cases and seven expert neuroradiologists. In the Nigerian cohort, MS-HVED was preferred over the supervised baseline for overall image quality in 187 of 210 paired assessments. Across all clinical cohorts, MS-HVED achieved higher overall-quality scores with all available inputs (5.93 vs. 4.49, p<.001), with the preference persisting when axial or coronal inputs were omitted (p<.001). Code at github.com/cai4cai/ms-hved

## 21. Portrait Fitness as a Pretext: Probing Implicit Demographic Bias in VLMs

- **作者**：Alexandre Fournier-Montgieux, Akram Elbouanani, Aboubacar Tuo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CY
- **备注**：Accepted at WACV 2027

We audit face-related bias in VLMs using BALPORTRAITS, a new balanced dataset of 9,000 fictional portraits across ethnicity, gender, and age segments. Sixteen VLMs estimate portrait fitness scores for occupations, brands, activities, and banking or insurance products within an advertising-casting framework designed to avoid direct inference of protected attributes. Item-level estimations are aggregated with scenario-specific prestige scores to compare rankings across demographic groups. While the magnitude and direction of these biases vary across scenarios, model families, and demographic dimensions, with age, ethnicity, and gender contributing differently depending on the task, we find demographic dispersion across all evaluated models, with a majority of pairwise segment differences reaching statistical significance. This study introduces a controlled protocol to assess implicit face-related associations and demonstrates that single-scenario or single-attribute audits are insufficient.

## 22. VideoNexus: Video Perception Beyond the Reasoning Context

- **作者**：Yancheng Long, Xuanyu Zheng, Wei Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Understanding long videos requires locating evidence, resolving visual details, and integrating observations into an answer. Even when relevant evidence is present, performing these operations within long, dense visual sequences burdens both perception and reasoning. Video agents enable selective inspection, but returning frames to the planner still accumulates visual information in its reasoning context. Delegating perception reduces this burden, yet sparse sampling can miss decisive details, and per-frame descriptions can preserve context growth. We introduce VideoNexus, a training-free framework that separates detailed video perception from evidence-driven planning and reasoning. The planner controls where to inspect, what to resolve, and how densely to sample. Perception sub-agents process native audio-video in independent contexts and return compact, temporally grounded evidence for iterative search and verification. Using Gemini 3.8 Flash for both planning and perception, VideoNexus achieves 88.6% accuracy on full LVBench, exceeding same-backbone static ingestion and built-in agentic processing by 3.6 and 1.1 points, respectively, at 44% of the latter's measured API cost. A paired LVBench subset ablation shows lower planner input and higher accuracy. With Kimi K3 as the shared backbone, VideoNexus improves over the official Video-GAIA ReAct harness by 8.5 points in our evaluation. These results support compact evidence exchange for combining detailed observation with efficient planning and reasoning.

## 23. The Mask Is Not the Object: Volumetric Supervision for 3D Gaussian Segmentation

- **作者**：Jeonghwan Cho, Minsu Kim, Junyoung Hong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://jhxcho.github.io/voda/

3D Gaussian Splatting enables object-level operations on Gaussian primitives, yet existing segmentation methods are largely trained and evaluated through rendered views. This mismatch can produce accurate rendered masks from incomplete 3D selections since alpha-blended feature supervision concentrates learning on dominant contributors while leaving occluded or low-contribution Gaussians weakly supervised. We propose VODA, a plug-and-play supervision objective that exploits local depth structure to group per-pixel contributors and directly supervises all Gaussians in selected depth clusters, bypassing contribution-weighted gradient delivery. To evaluate volumetric completeness, we introduce Removal-3D, a synthetic object-removal benchmark, and the Removal Depth Error (RDE), a metric comparing predicted and ground-truth depth changes. Across four rendering-based baselines, VODA consistently improves volumetric segmentation on Removal-3D while maintaining or improving performance on LERF and ScanNet. Further analysis shows that VODA recovers lower-contribution Gaussians, while simple gradient reweighting and spatial propagation fail to reproduce comparable gains.

## 24. WeVisForensics: Self-Evolving Agent for Omni AI-Generated Video Detection

- **作者**：Yipeng Du, Yuxi Li, Ying Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recent advances in video generation call for reliable detection grounded in explicit forensic evidence. However, current methods primarily depend on single-pass chain-of-thought reasoning that overlooks brief or localized artifacts, or on predefined multi-agent workflows that offer limited flexibility in selecting relevant inspections. Even with adaptive investigation, optimizing the investigation policy alone does not directly improve the forensic capabilities of fixed experts. We introduce WeVisForensics, an agentic framework in which a main investigator agent coordinates a self-evolving library of forensic inspector sub-agents spanning visual and audio cues. The investigator learns which artifact type to examine, over which window, and when to conclude, and these interactions provide shared feedback for both levels of agents. Our dual-timescale policy optimization strategy, DTPO, closes this loop through fast reinforcement learning of the investigator and slower refinement of the inspectors, guided by annotated artifact intervals, to report subtle artifacts they once dismissed. Furthermore, we curate ForenCue-30K, combining role-specific forensic annotations and interaction trajectories to supervise individual inspections and their coordination, with an accompanying reinforcement-learning pool. Experiments show that WeVisForensics averages 95.2% detection accuracy across in-domain, out-of-domain and agentic benchmarks, 9.0% above the strongest prior detector, while grounding its verdicts in localized evidence.

## 25. ALIVE: Making Streaming Video Models Interactive

- **作者**：Jinzhao Li, Yuan Xu, Jinxuan Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Streaming video LLMs can continuously observe the world, yet often handle user requests in isolation. We introduce ALIVE, an interactive streaming video model supporting proactive monitoring, streaming task coordination, and feedback-seeking interaction. We construct ALIVE-SFT-288K and ALIVE-RL-3K for two-stage training. Contrastive supervised fine-tuning teaches when and how to respond, while reinforcement learning with Behavior-Aligned Credit Assignment (BACA) combines trajectory-level and local advantages to refine decisions, responses, and silence across multi-turn interactions. We also introduce FSI-Bench to evaluate feedback seeking and subsequent task completion. ALIVE achieves the highest overall scores among the compared models on four public benchmarks and FSI-Bench, demonstrating strong streaming understanding and interactive capabilities.

## 26. Exploring the link between predictive uncertainty and the sim-to-real gap in deep learning models utilizing synthetic data

- **作者**：Alexander Moriz, Lukas Krebs, Amon Göppert et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：A Preprint

Today, state-of-the-art methods for quality control (QC) are based on Machine Vision, combining deep learning (DL) models with camera-based inspection. Yet, the integration of such solutions in industrial applications is often impeded by data sparsity and trust in model reliability. While synthetic data offers a promising approach to generate the necessary data with perfect annotations, it poses risks related to performance degradation due to the possible occurrence of the sim-to-real gap. This study conducts experiments using the ensemble method to estimate the predictive uncertainty of DL models trained on synthetic data. The results show that predictive uncertainty increases under the sim-to-real domain shift. However, the observed performance drop is dominated by a systematic sim-to-real bias rather than by ensemble disagreement.These findings contribute to the effective utilization of synthetic data in industrial settings while highlighting the importance of risk assessment for the deployment of DL solutions for QC tasks.

## 27. Symmetry Detection with Patch Embedding Models by Means of a Scalar Product Transform

- **作者**：Christoph Dalitz, Jan Beeker
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 7 figures, accepted for VISAPP 2027

We present a transformation of vector embeddings representing image patches into scores for point reflection symmetry at the crossing points of patches. The transformation is heavily inspired by the Gradient Product Transform, but it uses the vector embeddings instead of the grayscale gradients. We therefore call it the "Scalar Product Transform" (SPT). Unlike deep learning models for symmetry detection, the method does not require a specific training step tailored for symmetry detection, but it works with arbitrary pre-trained patch embedding models. We have tested the method with DINOv3 and Franca. In our tests, the method outperformed the Gradient Product Transform and was even better than EquiSym, a convolutional neural network specifically designed and trained for symmetry detection. Although the runtime of the SPT is greater than that of EquiSym, the SPT has the advantage that it directly yields a bounding box for the symmetric region.

## 28. Specificity-Guided Knowledge Diversion for Decomposable Generative Models

- **作者**：Yucheng Xie, Fu Feng, Jing Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV

Generative model pre-training typically optimizes a model as a whole without explicitly organizing its parameters for selective reuse. Decomposable pre-training instead learns reusable components for downstream adaptation while retaining strong generative performance. However, existing knowledge diversion methods incur substantial parameter overhead through weight factorization and rely on fixed component assignments to induce specialization, potentially compromising in-distribution generation quality. We therefore propose Specificity-Guided Knowledge Diversion (SGKDiv), which learns how generative knowledge is shared and specialized for effective generation and selective downstream reuse. SGKDiv constructs Knowledge Units from paired feed-forward channels, preserving native computational structure without SVD-based reparameterization. Calibrated condition-wise routing combines a shared usage prior with conditional adjustments, while complementary regularization shapes their allocation, diversity, and magnitude. Together, these designs replace predefined component assignments with adaptively learned and explicitly regulated knowledge specialization. Experiments across diverse model and training settings demonstrate better in-distribution generation than dense and MoE baselines, alongside stronger downstream adaptation than representative PEFT methods across eight datasets.

## 29. MovieRoll: A Systematic Framework for Rolling Out Long-Form Videos

- **作者**：Yunyang Ge, Weijie Kong, Ryan Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Streaming video generation must sustain quality beyond the training horizon while handling self-generated history and growing cache costs. To address these challenges, we present MovieRoll, a base-to-distilled framework that connects streaming fine-tuning, long-rollout distillation, and long-horizon inference. We first establish a robust multi-step base through Window-wise Teacher Forcing (WinTF), which jointly denoises chunks at progressive noise levels with bidirectional attention within each window and causal access to preceding history. To retain historical context efficiently, hierarchical KV-cache compression (HieraKV) exploits layer-wise attention patterns, retaining full history in shallow layers and sink--recent history in deeper layers. This reduces cache memory by up to 78% while improving generation quality. Building on this base, cumulative long tuning with distribution matching distillation (CuLT-DMD) holds parameters fixed throughout each long self-rollout and averages local gradients before updating. Each update therefore combines supervision across temporal positions while retaining only one local computation graph, enabling efficient long-rollout training of a few-step generator. To extend generation beyond the training horizon, KV recomputation bounds context for both models, while guidance annealing (AnnealCFG) reduces guidance toward the clean endpoint to stabilize MovieRoll-Base. Complementing these modeling advances, FBCA attention and PS-VAE decoding support efficient training and streaming output. On HeliosBench, MovieRoll-Base remains competitive at 30s after training on videos of up to 15s, and MovieRoll-Distilled achieves the highest total scores among the compared methods across single- and multi-event evaluations up to 60s, combining sustained naturalness with active motion.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21339) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21339)

## 30. A2A-UAV: A Multimodal Air-to-Air Dataset for UAV Detection and Identification

- **作者**：Hongtao Liang, Yiyao Wan, Kezhi Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages, 9 figures

The rapid growth of unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) in low-altitude airspace increases the risk of unauthorized intrusions, making reliable detection and identification of non-cooperative UAVs essential for low-altitude airspace safety. However, the existing public UAV datasets are mostly acquired from fixed ground-based viewpoints and are limited to imaging or radio-frequency (RF) observations, which restricts the sensing coverage and deployment flexibility. To address this gap, we present A2A-UAV, a multimodal air-to-air dataset for UAV detection and identification. Specifically, 15 target UAVs of 13 commercial models were recorded at three outdoor sites under daytime and nighttime conditions by using a UAV-mounted platform equipped with an RGB-thermal gimbal camera, a panoramic camera, an RF spectrum analyzer, and a millimeter-wave radar. The dataset contains 105,294 time-aligned samples of RGB, panoramic, thermal, and RF time-frequency images and radar point clouds. Moreover, all 209,539 bounding boxes of the predominantly small UAV targets were manually annotated and cross-checked. Therefore, A2A-UAV provides a valuable resource for air-to-air UAV detection, identification, and multimodal fusion in low-altitude airspace.

## 31. Rotation-Invariant Machine Learning for Highly Symmetric Molecular Configurations

- **作者**：Logan Bolton, Tomas Karella, Nicholas Lubbers et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：physics.chem-ph, cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurReps 2026 Extended Abstract Track (NeurIPS 2026 Workshop on Symmetry and Geometry in Neural Representations)

Atomistic machine learning can approximate expensive quantum mechanical calculations at a fraction of their computational cost, but accuracy depends strongly on how the three-dimensional atomic environments are represented. Invariant representations enable fast computation but can fail to distinguish geometrically distinct configurations, especially in highly symmetric environments. We introduce a lightweight benchmark to evaluate expressivity on $n$-fold symmetric configurations, and use it to expose degeneracies in both invariant and equivariant architectures. Guided by these results, we construct a new invariant basis for HIP-HOP which resolves most of the identified failures. On a methane simulation dataset, the extended basis achieves the lowest error among the HIP-HOP variants and has similar accuracy to EquiformerV3 while running $3.2\times$ faster at inference. These results show that carefully constructed invariant representations can mitigate key expressivity limitations while balancing accuracy and computational efficiency.

## 32. Fix, Don't Retake: Physics-Simulated Training for Correcting Patient Positioning Errors in Dental Panoramic Radiographs

- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Patient-positioning errors can distort panoramic radiographs and lead to repeat examinations. This study investigated artificial intelligence for correcting these errors in existing images. A physics-based simulator generated paired correctly positioned and mispositioned panoramics from cone-beam computed tomography volumes, with controlled individual and combined positioning errors. Five approaches were compared: U-Net, pix2pix, conditional diffusion, an error-conditioned model, and a mixture of error-specific specialists. Evaluation used image similarity and geometric metrics across three test sets. All approaches improved mean image similarity and reduced geometric errors, with U-Net and the two error-aware approaches outperforming pix2pix and conditional diffusion.

## 33. Déjà-Events: Multi-view 3D Geometry with Event Cameras via Sparsity-Aware Adaptive Gating

- **作者**：Arda Cinar Demirtas, Pablo Varela Bernal, Marc Pollefeys et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：8 pages, 6 figures

Event cameras keep capturing structure where conventional cameras blur or saturate, which makes them attractive for 3D perception in fast or poorly lit scenes. Event datasets with dense 3D ground truth, however, are far smaller and less diverse than the image collections behind feed-forward 3D reconstruction models. We present Déjà-Events, an event-only model that predicts depth, camera pose and pointmaps from multiple views in a single forward pass. Rather than relying on scarce 3D labels, we distil it from Déjà View, a feed-forward RGB model that refines its tokens by repeatedly applying one shared transformer block. On paired RGB and event recordings, the event student learns to reproduce the frozen teacher's final tokens, which requires no depth or pose ground truth and trains only about 1% of its parameters. Because events are dense at edges and moving texture but nearly absent in textureless regions, we further introduce sparsity-aware adaptive gating, which adapts the shared block's gates to events and adjusts them per patch by event density, so that accuracy holds as refinement steps are added. Trained only on simulated data, Déjà-Events achieves the lowest pointmap error among the event-based methods we evaluate, on EventScape and zero-shot on the real MVSEC and DSEC datasets. On DSEC it halves the error of the strongest baseline (0.15 vs. 0.30 AbsRel) with about a tenth of its parameters. It also has the lowest depth error on EventScape and DSEC and the lowest trajectory error on EventScape.

## 34. AnachroBench: Evaluating Historical Consistency in Large Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Keunhyeung Park, Byeonggeuk Lim, Kahyeon Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：18 pages, 14 figures

Existing Large Vision-Language Model benchmarks primarily evaluate visual understanding in general everyday scenarios, leaving historical consistency with a specified era and country largely underexplored. To address this gap, we propose AnachroBench, a controlled discriminative benchmark for evaluating historical consistency in visual scenes. It contains 1,600 image pairs from eight countries spanning the late 19th to the early 20th century, each pairing an original historical image with an anachronistic version containing a seamlessly inserted modern object. AnachroBench evaluates three aspects: Inserted Object Perception, which tests whether models detect the inserted modern object; Era-Country Consistency, which assesses whether the scene is consistent with the specified era and country; and Conditional Era-Country Consistency, which measures consistency judgments only when the inserted object is correctly perceived. A false consistency judgment occurs when a model perceives the modern object but still judges the scene as historically consistent. Overall, AnachroBench reveals a gap between inserted object perception and era-country consistency judgments, providing a targeted evaluation of this behavior.

## 35. FM-Recon: Exploring Foundation Model Features for Accelerated Cardiac Cine MRI Reconstruction

- **作者**：Donghang Lyu, Marius Staring, Ruochen Gao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Foundation models have demonstrated strong feature extraction capabilities across various medical imaging tasks, yet their potential for MRI reconstruction remains largely unexplored, particularly within unrolled reconstruction networks and under artifact-corrupted inputs. In this work, we explore whether and how features from pretrained foundation models can benefit accelerated cardiac cine MRI reconstruction. To this end, we propose FM-Recon, a framework that refines and integrates extracted features from frozen image encoder of foundation models into an unrolled reconstruction network. Through extensive experiments on the CMRxRecon2023 dataset, we systematically evaluate foundation model features across different reconstruction settings. Our results show that foundation models can extract informative representations from artifact-corrupted inputs and improve reconstruction performance, particularly at moderate acceleration factors. These findings demonstrate the potential of foundation model features for accelerated MRI reconstruction and provide insights into their effective integration into unrolled reconstruction networks.

## 36. PLECTA: Overlap-aware instance segmentation of dense strand networks from binary masks

- **作者**：Oday Allan, Igor Ostanin
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cond-mat.soft

Many materials and tissues, including carbon-nanotube sheets, collagen and paper, comprise overlapping strands whose arrangement influences their effective properties. Measuring this geometry from micrographs requires individual strands to be traced through crossings and signal gaps, which merge distinct strands or split one strand into disconnected fragments. We present PLECTA (from Latin plecta, a braid), a deterministic method that segments individual strands using only a binary image whose foreground pixels mark strand centrelines. At each junction, PLECTA selects which strand segments continue into one another while allowing segments to terminate. It formulates this decision as an exact maximum-weight partial-matching problem, with an explicit penalty for unpaired segments and no restriction on the number of segments entering a junction. The output permits crossing pixels to belong to multiple strands. On synthetic scenes, PLECTA resolved strand identities more accurately than three released alternatives at every density tested. The same ranking held on an independently published generator run at its own settings. A separate stage uses the corresponding greyscale micrograph to estimate which strand lies on top at each crossing. The complete workflow is demonstrated on scanning electron micrographs of carbon nanotube sheets, from raw images to individual strand instances with estimated crossing order, without manual annotations for training or as pipeline inputs.

## 37. QuantileGS: Quantile-Based Conformal Bounds on RGB Rendering Errors for Novel View Synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Haiyang Xu, Ziye Zhang, Bin Pan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Uncertainty quantification for novel view synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) assesses rendering reliability at unseen viewpoints. This reliability depends on the discrepancy between rendered and true colors, which cannot be measured without ground-truth images. We therefore quantify uncertainty in this unknown discrepancy through upper bounds on per-pixel RGB mean absolute error. However, learned bounds may underestimate these errors and overstate rendering reliability, motivating calibration to a prescribed coverage level. In this paper, we propose QuantileGS, a post-hoc framework that combines learned rendering-error quantiles with scale-normalized conformal calibration to produce spatially adaptive error bounds. The framework comprises three phases. Phase 1, Rendering-Error Computation, measures RGB mean absolute error between observed images and predictions from a frozen 3DGS model. Phase 2, Ordered Gaussian Quantile Fields, learns two scalar attributes per Gaussian to predict lower and upper rendering-error quantiles. Phase 3, Scale-Normalized Conformal Calibration, calibrates the upper prediction on held-out images, using the quantile gap to scale the correction. Under the stated assumptions, the resulting bounds provide finite-sample marginal coverage. At a nominal coverage level of 90%, calibration raises mean empirical test coverage from 69.6% to 89.6% on LLFF and from 80.1% to 92.1% on Mip-NeRF 360. On LF, coverage remains conservative at 96.3% before and 96.4% after calibration. Applications to active view selection, Gaussian pruning, and RGB-D simultaneous localization and mapping illustrate how these bounds inform downstream decisions.

## 38. A Study on Performance Enhancement of a Webcam-Based Real-Time Eye-Tracking System

- **作者**：Minuk Jung, Kyeonghun Kim, Sooyeon Yoon
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

This study proposes a real-time gaze-tracking system that uses a standard RGB webcam to estimate screen coordinates. The system combines ETH-XGaze-based gaze estimation with nine-point calibration and polynomial ridge regression. To reduce errors caused by changes in head position, it also applies a conditional residual correction derived from head and pose features extracted with RTMPose. Whether this correction is used depends on the reliability of the detected features and the characteristics of the calibration data. In a preliminary experiment, one participant completed trials at 12 screen targets. The resulting mean screen-coordinate error was 204.24 pixels, which was 35.83% lower than that of xgaze-js and 57.69% lower than that of gaze_tracker. The system produced valid gaze estimates for all 12 targets. These preliminary findings demonstrate the feasibility of combining user-specific calibration with conditional pose-based correction for webcam gaze tracking. Because the evaluation involved only one participant, further experiments with more participants and a wider range of postures are required.

## 39. Investigating the Benefit of Transfer Learning from the Locus Coeruleus for Substantia Nigra Pars Compacta Segmentation

- **作者**：Tayebeh Bahador, Max Dünnwald, Matthew J. Betts et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV

The substantia nigra pars compacta (SNpc) is a small midbrain nucleus that degenerates early in Parkinson's disease (PD). T1-weighted FLASH MRI enables its visualization and may support early detection of neurodegeneration. However, manual segmentation is difficult and time-consuming, and deep learning models often suffer from the limited availability of labeled data. Transfer learning (TL) may help by adapting pre-trained models to small datasets. We investigate whether TL from a model trained on the locus coeruleus (LC), a nucleus with a similar appearance in this sequence, improves SNpc segmentation. Several TL strategies were evaluated using a 3D U-Net. With as few as 5-14 labeled subjects, TL and training from scratch achieved similar performance, reaching a Dice similarity coefficient of up to 0.77, close to reported inter-rater agreement. No TL strategy differed significantly from training from scratch, suggesting that initialization from the LC offers no significant benefit for SNpc segmentation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21229) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21229)

## 40. Oriented Semi-DETR for Semi-Supervised Aerial Object Detection

- **作者**：Yuqiu Li, Jian Xue, Fan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM
- **备注**：Submitted to ICASSP 2027

Semi-supervised object detection has recently been extended to DETR, where oriented boxes introduce an ambiguity that does not arise for axis-aligned boxes: the map from parameters to rectangles is two-to-one, since $(c_x,c_y,w,h,θ)$ and $(c_x,c_y,h,w,θ+π/2)$ denote the same rotated rectangle. Losses and matching costs are computed from the parameters, so two boxes that coincide in space can be far apart in the metric that drives training. A correctly localized student box is then penalized by the regression loss, and the inflated matching cost leads the Gaussian mixture model (GMM) filter to discard it. We propose Oriented Semi-DETR, which removes this ambiguity wherever a pseudo box enters training. Swap-aligned Target Selection regresses towards the encoding closer to the student prediction. A Swap-invariant Reliability Cost minimizes over the two encodings inside the matching cost, so the filter decides on geometry alone. Swap-aware Pseudo-label Denoising starts denoising earlier, ramps its loss weight linearly, and weights each query by the teacher score and encoding margin. All three are parameter-free and require no additional forward pass. Across all label ratios of DOTA-v2.0, Oriented Semi-DETR outperforms DETR-based supervised and semi-supervised baselines, with the largest gains at the strict IoU threshold.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21223) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21223)

## 41. RAGE: Residual-Adaptive Gating for Flow-Based Image Editing

- **作者**：Ruofeng Mei, Xiangyu Xu
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Recent inversion-free, flow-based image editing methods evolve the source image toward the target via a differential velocity field. We observe that this velocity field does not explicitly account for which regions should be preserved, leading to unintended drift in non-editing regions. To address this, we formulate source fidelity control as a stochastic optimal control problem in which a selective fidelity term on the editing residual is enforced along the editing trajectory. Solving this problem and approximating its solution yields the Residual Gating Principle: each latent component is pulled back toward the source with a gain that decays exponentially with the local residual magnitude, so that small deviations are suppressed while large, semantically meaningful edits pass through unimpeded. The proposed method, RAGE, requires only one lightweight gated interpolation per sampling step, involves no mask estimation, attention maps, retraining, or architecture-specific modifications, and is applied on top of FlowEdit-based editors. On PIE-Bench, RAGE achieves the best overall rank among 17 methods and substantially improves background preservation on both Stable Diffusion 3 and FLUX backbones while maintaining competitive editing quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21177) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21177)

## 42. Beyond Token Stacking: Multi-scale Visual Modulation and Adaptive Fused-feature Calibration for MLLMs

- **作者**：Ye Xiao, Shesheng Song, Yi Ren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have advanced rapidly and follow two main paradigms: one involves concatenating visual and textual tokens for input into Large Language Model (LLM), while the other injects visual knowledge into Feed-Forward Networks (FFNs) of the LLM. However, the former introduces a large number of redundant visual tokens, substantially increasing computational overhead. The latter reduces sequence length but removes images from the input stage entirely, which may hinder the activation of image-related knowledge. Moreover, naively injecting visual knowledge into the FFNs could induce activation distribution shifts due to cross-modal semantic discrepancies. To address these challenges, we develop Beyond Token Stacking (BTS), a novel visual enhancement framework for MLLMs. Specifically, we propose a Multi-scale Visual Modulation (MVM) mechanism that injects multi-scale visual information at the input stage via modulation without increasing the token sequence length. Furthermore, we develop an Adaptive Fused-feature Calibration (AFC) module that performs per-channel affine calibration to decouple visual injection from distribution alignment in the FFN layers, improving multimodal fusion stability while preserving language priors. Experiments show that our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21168) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21168)

## 43. DRaGAN: Dynamic Graph Attention Network for Multi-Radar Point Cloud Encoding in Automotive Radar Perception

- **作者**：Christof Leitgeb, Thomas Puchleitner, Max Peter Ronecker et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to 2027 IEEE/SICE International Symposium on System Integration (SII)

Radar represents a key modality for weather-robust perception. Unlike optical sensors such as camera and LiDAR, radar can penetrate fog, rain and snow due to its longer wavelength, and recent hardware advances have steadily improved its spatial resolution, thus making it increasingly relevant for autonomous systems. While processing raw radar data requires substantial memory computation, most existing work has focused on preprocessed point-cloud data similar to LiDAR. However, the sparse radar points and sensor-relative anisotropy make LiDAR-oriented encoders suboptimal. To address this problem, we present DRaGAN, a dynamic graph attention network for multi-radar point clouds. DRaGAN introduces a sensor-relative Fourier encoding of angle and range to capture fixed-aperture radar characteristics, gated short- and long-range hub nodes along each sensor's line of sight for efficient local-global message passing, and attention-guided dynamic rewiring that prunes weak edges and adds a bounded number of latent connections restricted to two-hop neighborhoods. This reduces the number of processed edges in deeper layers while preserving representation quality. On TruckScenes, DRaGAN improves test NDS by 10.10 points over the evaluated RadarGNN baseline and reduces the reported model FLOP count by 63.7%. This trade-off includes an increase in model latency from 40 to 53.16 ms and in trainable parameters from 1.2M to 1.7M on the evaluated implementations. Code and pretrained models are available at https://github.com/chr-is-tof/DRaGAN.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21162) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21162)

## 44. Model-based neural network for global floodplains analysis

- **作者**：Pierre Audisio, Barbara Belletti, Nelly Pustelnik
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We address the super-resolution of multispectral remote sensing imagery for long-term floodplain monitoring, enhancing Landsat acquisitions towards Sentinel-2 resolution. Exploiting the period during which both missions observed the same areas, we build DLhasa-L2S , to our knowledge the first paired dataset dedicated to multispectral super-resolution for river analysis. It covers the Lhasa River (Tibet), whose strong anthropogenic changes and diverse hydromorphological profiles make it an ideal study area. We developed two model-based neural networks: a forward-backward (FB) plug-and-play scheme and an unfolded FB scheme. We compared them against learned neural networks baselines (SwinIR, RDN) on synthetic data, on real scenes, and through a statistical test over the whole real paired Landsat-Sentinel dataset. The unfolded network achieves the highest PSNR and SSIM on the real RGB components with fast inference, offering the best trade-off between accuracy and computational cost. For NDWI-based water segmentation, its performance shows no statistically significant difference from that of the best methods.
