## 45. Scribble Once, Segment the Sequence: Bidirectional Temporal Consistency for Weakly Supervised Segmentation in Echocardiography Sequences

- **作者**：Vincent Li Weixin, Chen Jingkun, Vicente Grau
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV

Temporal medical image segmentation relies on pixel-wise annotations, which are prohibitively expensive for long image sequences. Existing weakly-supervised methods reduce the annotation burden per frame but still require repeated annotations across the sequence. Furthermore, sparse supervision can result in temporally unstable representations. Addressing both challenges requires a model that can leverage strong spatial priors from large-scale pretraining while minimizing reliance on dense annotations. Frozen 2D vision foundation models provide a promising basis for this by offering transferable spatial representations with low trainable parameters. However, their lack of explicit temporal modeling limits their ability to propagate reliable information across anatomical sequences. To address this, we present a weakly-supervised framework for temporal segmentation from a single scribble-annotated frame, with all remaining frames left unlabeled. The framework combines parameter-efficient adaptation of DINOv3 encoders present in the mean-teacher architecture. Furthermore, a set of complementary losses, including a temporal loss helps generate reliable segmentation priors for the unlabeled frames. This formulation decouples annotation costs from sequence length while retaining the transferable spatial knowledge of the pretrained encoder. Extensive experiments on the publicly available CAMUS and CardiacNet datasets demonstrate that our approach outperforms other scribble-supervised methods. These results show that accurate and temporally consistent segmentation can be achieved from extremely sparse supervision, substantially reducing annotation requirements for temporal echocardiographic segmentation and providing a practical framework for scalable clinical deployment.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21137) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21137)

## 46. Region-VAD: Video Anomaly Localization and Detection via Region Selection

- **作者**：Giacomo D'Amicantonio
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：This paper has been accepted in the RWS workshop at WACV 2027

Anomalies are local events, yet weakly supervised video anomaly detection scores them globally. Most methods pool an entire frame into a single vector, rank frames by that vector, and derive a location afterwards as a saliency map of the score. Full-frame pooling mixes the anomaly with its background, so a detector can reach competitive benchmark scores from scene context without being able to show where in the scene the anomaly occurs. We introduce Region-VAD, which first selects a region and then detects the anomaly from that region alone. Region-VAD selects an explicit rectangle over the tokens of a frozen foundation model and scores anomalousness only from the tokens inside the box, through attention pooling followed by a standard temporal head. The rectangle is not learned, it is the box in which the frozen tokens change most between consecutive segments, in all four feature projections of the encoder, and it is found by exhaustive search over the rectangles of the token grid. Only the pooling and the temporal head are trained, from video-level labels without boxes, masks or frame-level annotations, and no trained component reads a token outside the selected region. Experiments on five datasets, with one localizer configuration shared by all datasets and backbones, show that Region-VAD achieves state-of-the-art anomaly localization on UCF-Crime and MSAD at competitive detection performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21132) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21132)

## 47. How to Explain your Unexplainable Video Anomaly Detector

- **作者**：Giacomom D'Amicantonio, Egor Bondarev
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：This paper has been accepted at the RWS workshop WACV 2027

Weakly-supervised Video Anomaly Detection (VAD) models assign anomaly scores from high-dimensional features, and their predictions remain unexplained. Saliency maps show where a model looks but not what it relies on, and the descriptions produced by Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are generated by branches that the anomaly score never reads. To address this faithfulness gap, we propose \textbf{Explain The Unexplainable (ETU)}, a classifier-agnostic framework that audits frozen VAD models through counterfactual interventions in their feature space. ETU maps video features to a discrete dependency graph of agents, actions and outcomes ($A_r \to A_a \to A_o$), regenerates the features by graph-conditioned diffusion with selected nodes of the graph masked, and measures the change in the score of a frozen detector; one set of interventions is applied unchanged to every detector. The generator is trained only to reconstruct the input features, so it cannot learn the contrast it is asked to measure, and we validate the audit with experiments that test every alternative explanation of its results, from the cost of regeneration to random deletions of matched size. On UCF-Crime features obtained from a CLIP encoder, we show that substituting the scene semantics results in the largest drop for most detectors, and that removing the outcome chain of the graph ($Δ_{rel}$) changes the anomaly score of all six audited detectors far more than removing the same number of random nodes, even though the random deletion perturbs the features about as much. We show that this targeted behavior comes from the divergence objective of the graph embedding. Without it, the two interventions become indistinguishable, and with a divergence objective in the generator as well, the score change of the detector follows the displacement of the reconstruction rather than the semantics that were removed.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21131) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21131)

## 48. IGRecon4D: Dynamic-Object-Aware Feed-Forward Reconstruction of Driving Scenes

- **作者**：Yifei Tong, Ziyang Huang, Yizhao Han et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Feed-forward 4D driving-scene reconstruction enables efficient modeling of dynamic autonomous-driving scenes, but current methods suffer from two complementary failure modes: semantic leakage of traffic participants into the static representation and geometric drift of Gaussian locations across viewpoints. We propose \textbf{IGRecon4D}, a feed-forward dynamic scene reconstruction framework that addresses these failures with training-time instance-prior distillation and LiDAR-anchored Gaussian positioning. Specifically, an instance-level semantic branch provides a potentially dynamic participant prior, which is distilled into dynamic-mask learning through the Instance-Dynamic Consistency Constraint to encourage high-recall localization of traffic-participant regions prone to dynamic-static leakage. In parallel, sparse LiDAR measurements guide dense depth estimation, and the resulting depths are back-projected to anchor Gaussian positions for improved multi-view geometric consistency. Experiments on the Waymo Open Dataset and zero-shot evaluation on nuScenes demonstrate improved full-image reconstruction on the main benchmark and stronger traffic-participant-region reconstruction under cross-dataset evaluation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21126) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21126)

## 49. VAE Rhythm: Temporal Decoding Traces for AI-Generated Video Detection

- **作者**：Ke Sun, Shuwei Li, Haiyang Mei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：24 pages, 7 figures. Project page: https://showlab.github.io/vae-rhythm

Visible artifacts are an unreliable cue for detecting generated videos, because newer generators learn to remove them. We instead detect a trace left by the generation pipeline itself. Most video generators decode latents with a 3D causal VAE that groups consecutive frames in time, so the fidelity of generated frames rises and falls periodically with their position in a group. We call this periodic trace VAE Rhythm. Real videos are captured frame by frame and do not have it. To reveal it without training, we propose a phase scan. The scan reconstructs a clip with an off-the-shelf VAE from successive start offsets and measures the periodicity of the reconstruction error across these offsets. We find VAE Rhythm in most of the 39 variants of 31 generators we test, including the closed-source Sora, Kling 3.0 and Seedance 2.0. We use the rhythm as a zero-shot score, as a plug-in for existing detectors and as a data engine that pairs real videos with their VAE reconstructions to train our detector CaDet. On DF26, the training-free rhythm score outperforms all 15 retrained baselines, and as a plug-in it improves all 19 tested detectors by 6.7% to 23.7% AUROC. CaDet achieves the best AUROC on AIGVDBench, DF26 and three 2026 closed-source models, and its variant trained without any generated video still exceeds 90% on AIGVDBench.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21114) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21114)

## 50. Source-Free Domain Adaptation for Multi-Label Image Classification: A Framework, Baselines, and Benchmark

- **作者**：Salih Numan Büyükbaş, Erchan Aptoula, Mamatha Thota et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 5 figures, 6 tables

Source-free domain adaptation (SFDA) adapts a model trained on a labelled source domain to an unlabelled target domain without access to the source data. Almost the entire SFDA literature assumes mutually exclusive classes, yet many deployed settings are multi-label. This paper formulates SFDA for multi-label classification and establishes the first benchmark for it: five adaptation tasks over six datasets spanning remote sensing, natural, clipart-style and driving imagery, with four single-label SFDA methods extended to provide the first baselines and a published multi-label method run alongside them, over three seeds. The benchmark shows that extending a single-label method is unreliable rather than merely suboptimal: half of the extended baselines fail to improve on the unadapted source model at all, and on two of the five tasks none of them does. The failures are not incidental. Nearest-centroid pseudo-labelling, softmax-based confidence, the diversity term of information maximization and confidence-weighted prototype construction each rely on mutual exclusivity, and each fails differently without it, so replacing the softmax with per-class sigmoids is a valid but insufficient extension. Multi-label Prototype Alignment with Contrastive Teaching (MPACT) is proposed in response, a mean-teacher framework whose objective, prototype construction and alignment term are each reconsidered for overlapping label sets. It attains the best average on every reported metric and is the only method that improves on the unadapted source model on all five tasks. The analysis further introduces a prototype-agreement diagnostic separating feature-space alignment from self-reinforcing agreement with the classifier, and reports a negative result for confidence-based pseudo-label filtering.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21107) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21107)

## 51. CivilBench: Benchmarking Vision-Language Models for Multimodal Information Integration in Civil Engineering

- **作者**：Dan Yuan, Yue Wen, Zhili He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages, 8 figures

Artificial intelligence (AI), particularly large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs), is increasingly applied in civil and structural engineering for disaster assessment, structural analysis, and engineering decision support. However, the extent to which current VLMs possess the reasoning capabilities required for reliable engineering judgments remains unclear. In this work, we introduce CivilBench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating VLMs in civil engineering and disaster-related scenarios. CivilBench covers three major task families: Sequential Reasoning, Image-based Structural Understanding, and Open-world Understanding, encompassing numerical reasoning, structural damage assessment, risk inference, causal analysis, and repair planning. The benchmark is constructed through a systematic pipeline of domain-specific data collection, engineering-oriented captioning, expert annotation, question design, and quality verification. Evaluation of representative state-of-the-art VLMs reveals substantial limitations in domain-specific reasoning, with Sequential Reasoning emerging as a major bottleneck, particularly when models must infer numerical relationships from sparse structured observations. Further experiments show that numerical priors and external knowledge can alleviate these limitations, although their effectiveness depends on the model's ability to reliably interpret and use the available evidence. CivilBench provides a capability-oriented testbed for identifying reasoning limitations and evaluating progress toward reliable AI-assisted engineering decision-making. The benchmark will be publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/traceuryuan/CivilBench.

## 52. GRAZE: Text-Free Grasp Synthesis for Unseen Objects from Human Gaze

- **作者**：Jeongho Lee, Boeun Kim, Hyung Jin Chang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, 12 figures

Text-conditioned hand-object interaction (HOI) generation provides intuitive semantic control, but language is an indirect way to specify where an interaction should occur on an object. Naming the part to grasp requires object-specific words, and naming the approach side requires a reference frame that a prompt lacks. We propose GRAZE (GRAsp from gaZE), which generates hand-object grasp motion from recorded gaze instead of object and part names. Its Geometry-Aware Gaze Encoding (GeoGaze) maps gaze rays to cues on object geometry, and cross-attention conditions a diffusion Transformer on these cues. We also introduce HOT3D-Grasp, a grasp-centered benchmark with object-disjoint folds built on HOT3D recordings. On unseen objects, GRAZE grasps the target part more often than the strongest text-conditioned baseline (49.3% vs. 43.6%), achieves the highest grasp success (66.3% vs. 46.6%), and transfers without fine-tuning to GazeHOI (45.3% vs. 33.8%). Gaze conveys the approach side: when gaze is rotated around the object, the approach turns with it in 59.1% of cases, against 20.5% for a text model retrained with clock words. On the same backbone, a text prompt names parts better, but gaze adds the approach side. Given both, GRAZE keeps the prompt's part accuracy and follows gaze.

## 53. Beyond Family Labels: A Taxonomic Ornstein-Uhlenbeck Prior for Avian 3D Shape Recovery

- **作者**：Yunchan Jeon, SooGon Kim, Seungwook Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026 (Oral)

Recovering 3D pose and shape of birds from single images is bottlenecked by the long tail of avian diversity: out of ~10,000 species, only a few dozen have 3D-labeled training data, and in-the-wild test sets routinely contain species never seen at training. Generalizing across clades is therefore a central problem. The current state of the art, AniMer+, mitigates it with a family-level contrastive loss that conveys only a binary same-family indicator per pair and discards the continuous structure of the species tree. We propose a taxonomic Ornstein-Uhlenbeck prior on the shape code of the AVES parametric bird mesh model: a multivariate Gaussian whose covariance decays exponentially with Linnaean rank distance, with a learnable selection strength and noise scale. The prior generalizes family contrastive into a continuous, generative form, exposing the graded cross- and within-family similarity that a binary indicator cannot, and brings continuous comparative phylogenetics in as a biology-grounded inductive bias for the long tail. Our method improves over AniMer+ on both metrics of the unseen-species CowBird benchmark and on CtrlAVES3D, while remaining comparable on seen-species CUB. A permutation ablation isolates taxonomic structure from generic Gaussian smoothing, and a rank-granularity ablation identifies cross-order distinctions (the structure beyond a family-vs-not indicator) as the dominant source of the unseen-species gain.

## 54. MedicalDPO: Multi-Objective Direct Preference Optimization for CT to PET Translation

- **作者**：Huiting Zhang, Yingxin Cai, Siyuan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

CT-to-PET synthesis estimates functional PET images from anatomical CT scans for auxiliary analysis, representation pretraining, and missing-modality completion. Brownian Bridge diffusion models can produce multiple PET candidates of different quality under the same CT condition, exposing an intra-condition quality hierarchy. Candidate quality spans complementary spatial, structural, and intensity dimensions, while DPO-only fine-tuning may compromise absolute synthesis fidelity. We propose MedicalDPO which converts this hierarchy into preference supervision automatically constructed from paired CT-PET training data. Multiple candidates are ranked across the three fidelity dimensions, and strict cross-metric verification retains preferred-rejected pairs with consistent quality separation. Relative denoising-error preference optimization is jointly trained with the original Brownian Bridge objective to anchor the pretrained CT-to-PET mapping, while condition-balanced multi-pair rotation distributes preference supervision across CT conditions. Experiments on BBDM and CPDM show improvements across LPIPS, MAE, SSIM, and PSNR. Further analyses demonstrate metric complementarity and clear pair separation, while ablations show that the original training objective prevents the substantial degradation caused by DPO-only fine-tuning.

## 55. State-Message Interaction Adapter for Cross-Domain Few-Shot Segmentation

- **作者**：Hang Xing, Yan Xia, Yongjiang Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables, including the appendix

Cross-domain few-shot segmentation adapts natural-image pretrained encoders to medical or industrial domains from a few pixel-annotated support masks. Within frozen DINOv3, intermediate per-head attention messages already carry target-background distinctions that baseline final-layer similarities and predictions do not consistently reflect. We propose the State-Message Interaction Adapter (SMIA) to learn how per-head messages should update each receiving token representation while keeping pretrained attention and FFN parameters frozen. Inserted right after per-head aggregation, SMIA conditions the transformation of each head's aggregated output (message) on each patch's pre-attention feature (state), allowing similar contextual messages to produce distinct or opposing updates depending on the receiving position. Because scarce support masks cover only limited state-message combinations, a shared low-rank bilinear map confines the learned interactions to low-dimensional state, message, and output subspaces. Added before each adapted block's frozen FFN, these updates yield adapted features that are refined by frozen FFNs and deeper blocks for linear readout. With only 0.51M trainable parameters and single-pass, support-free inference, SMIA achieves six-dataset mean mIoUs of 77.40% at 5 shots and 80.99% at 10 shots across 20 medical and industrial tasks, surpassing the strongest prior methods by +3.61 and +3.93 percentage points. In controlled 10-shot comparisons across three benchmarks on the same DINOv3 backbone, SMIA also outperforms parameter-matched AdaptFormer, LoRA, and ParaX with a better precision-recall balance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21085) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21085)

## 56. MORE: Merge Once, Route Early for Training-Free Multi-Subject Composition from Large LoRA Pools

- **作者**：Tian Xia
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Public hubs offer many personalized LoRAs per text-to-image backbone, yet composing two of them in one image remains fragile. In FLUX, keeping a pool of $K=10$ or $20$ subject LoRAs loaded makes their deltas interfere, and even loading only the two requested LoRAs keeps both subjects in fewer than 4% of images: one subject vanishes, the two fuse, or the image splits into a collage. We present MORE (Merge Once, Route Early), a training-free two-stage pipeline for FLUX. Merge once: the whole pool is folded into a single fixed-rank adapter by the subspace-regression router of SSR-Merge, made reliable by ridge, rank and calibration choices, and factored exactly into a shared part and per-subject parts. Route early: during the first 30% of the denoising steps each subject's part is gated to its layout region; a decaying soft attention bias ties every region to its own subject tokens, and a cyclic swap guidance extrapolates away from the prediction with the subjects' regions permuted, which counteracts region homogenization. On held-out seeds, MORE keeps both subjects in 94.2% ($K=10$) and 78.3% ($K=20$) of images, against 50.8% / 35.8% for FreeFuse and 25.0% / 24.2% for LoRAShop, and reaches an ImageReward of 1.32 (best baseline 0.07), while all $K$ subjects stay in one adapter. With layouts inferred automatically it still keeps both subjects in 70.0% / 55.0% of images.

## 57. What Makes a Good Move? Learning to Act from Experience in Visual Worlds

- **作者**：Cehao Yang, Ziyi He, Jing Yi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Learning visual policies requires turning interaction experience into guidance for individual decisions. Trajectory outcomes obscure action quality, while unguided exploration can revisit familiar situations. We present \textsc{LightAct}, an experience-learning framework that connects skill-guided exploration with policy internalization. Interaction segments are summarized into experience entries and consolidated into a game-specific skill that guides root collection toward broader state coverage. At each root, candidate actions are compared through short rollouts under a shared frozen policy. Environment-reported score gains are averaged across continuations and normalized into relative action rewards, producing reusable supervision for reinforcement learning. Policy training uses a compact dataset of just 1,024 decision roots collected in a single source game and paired with their action rewards. Across 170 GameWorld tasks spanning 34 games, \textsc{LightAct} improves overall progress by 23.6, 29.2, and 13.0 percentage points at the 2B, 4B, and 9B scales, respectively, with our strongest model competitive with reported proprietary-model results. Ablations show broader root-state coverage with skill guidance and consistent gains across three policy optimizers. Case studies show how rollouts distinguish actions with identical immediate gains.

## 58. MetricHand: Geometry-Aware Flow Matching for 4D Hand Reconstruction in Metric World Space

- **作者**：Yibo Bai, Likui Zhang, Jinglei Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Egocentric hand motion provides physical motion supervision for dexterous manipulation and vision-language-action pretraining. Reliable use of this supervision requires accurate and temporally coherent 4D hand reconstruction in metric world space. Existing methods typically lift framewise estimates produced under weak perspective into world coordinates. By ignoring joint-dependent depth variation at close range, this approximation couples errors in translation, orientation, and articulation that persist after world lifting and worsen under occlusion. We present MetricHand, a geometry-aware flow matching framework for 4D hand reconstruction in metric world space. MetricHand encodes depth priors from hand-object interaction regions into joint-associated representations that preserve local spatial structure and metric scale. Fused with visual features, this geometry guides coarse metric estimation and conditions every step of temporal generation. The model jointly recovers world-space translation, global orientation, MANO articulation, and shape, while structural priors and temporal context support estimation under partial occlusion. MetricHand reduces camera-space MPJPE by 49.3% and 52.9%, absolute world-space MPJPE by 28.7% and 21.7%, and wrist localization error by 48.5% and 37.9% relative to HaWoR, respectively. World-space acceleration error decreases by 44.2% and 64.3%, demonstrating improved temporal fidelity alongside more accurate absolute localization.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21060) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21060)

## 59. Dataset Distillation for Handwritten Text Recognition: An Empirical Study

- **作者**：Félix Fuentes-Hurtado, Aric Ayllón, Juan C. Alfaro
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Dataset distillation (DD) replaces large training datasets with smaller synthetic ones to reduce computational costs. While traditionally used for classification, we present, to our knowledge, the first DD study for line-level handwritten text recognition (HTR). Unlike classification tasks, which use discrete labels, HTR employs unaligned sequences from a virtually infinite output space as labels. To adapt classification-based DD methods to this scenario, we define text pseudo-classes by clustering transcriptions based on character n-grams. We adapt gradient matching (GM), distribution matching (DM) and trajectory matching (MTT) to a Convolutional Recurrent Neural Network (CRNN) trained with Connectionist Temporal Classification (CTC). In this process, synthetic line images are generated by optimising only the pixels of real starting lines (either randomly chosen or selected as text medoids), while keeping their original transcriptions fixed. Across four datasets (IAM, George Washington, READ\,2016, LAM) and three budgets (1--10\,\% of the training lines), no distilled set outperforms its real initialisation lines. Random lines prove better than medoids, and further optimisation only degrades recognition. Methodologically, DM lowers its objective without benefit, while GM and MTT barely move theirs. We turn these findings into guidelines for evaluating data compression in HTR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21054) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21054)

## 60. Body of Evidence: Triangulating 3D Human Pose with Geometry-Conditioned Depth

- **作者**：Erik Brorsson, Kilian Freitag, Josef Bengtson et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Even in multi-camera setups, occlusions can leave individual body joints with only a single usable 2D observation. Such an observation defines a viewing ray but leaves the joint's depth undetermined. We introduce Body of Evidence (BoE), a multi-view 3D human pose estimator that predicts per-camera joint depths alongside 2D joint locations. To reduce monocular depth ambiguity, we propose geometry-conditioned depth prediction, which combines image features with previously reconstructed 3D body geometry as contextual depth cues. We further introduce learnable depth-aware triangulation (LDAT), which augments differentiable algebraic triangulation with predicted depth constraints, enabling reconstruction even from a single contributing camera while preserving the benefits of geometric triangulation when multiple views are available. The reconstructed 3D poses subsequently guide depth prediction in the next iteration, allowing depth estimates and 3D geometry to progressively refine each other. Experiments on CMU Panoptic demonstrate state-of-the-art in-domain performance and superior average performance across evaluated camera subsets and unseen camera arrangements. BoE is particularly effective under sparse camera coverage, outperforming the previous state of the art by 10.6 AP25 points in the challenging three-camera setting.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21044) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21044)

## 61. ScoutCache: Efficient Few-Step Autoregressive Video Diffusion via Conditional Readout and Selective Caching

- **作者**：Hao Tan, Jinghui Qin, Xuming Hu
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Few-step autoregressive video diffusion enables efficient long video generation, but inference still incurs substantial computational costs from repeated denoising and KV cache operations. Existing acceleration methods reduce parts of this overhead, while historical KV reading and cache construction remain costly across video chunks. To address these challenges, we propose ScoutCache, a training-free framework that jointly optimizes denoising, historical KV reading, and cache construction. We observe that shallow feature changes provide useful information for estimating clean latent updates, while historical KV dependence varies across Transformer layers and attention heads but remains relatively stable across video chunks. Based on these observations, we develop Conditional Denoising Readout (CDR) to estimate intermediate denoising updates from shallow feature changes and known noise variations. We further introduce Output Risk Guided Historical KV Reading (ORHR) to allocate historical reading budgets based on attention output perturbations, and Content and Coverage Guided Sparse Cache Construction (CCSC) to reduce cache construction costs by selecting informative spatial tokens while maintaining spatial coverage. Extensive experiments on Self-Forcing and LongLive using VBench and MovieGen demonstrate that ScoutCache achieves up to 2.28$\times$ and 1.86$\times$ speedups, respectively, while consistently improving generation quality without modifying pretrained model parameters.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21038) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21038)

## 62. POKE: Probe Offline, Keep Efficiently for Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Jaehyuk Lee, Yeajin Lee, Suhyun Bae et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Visual token pruning reduces the inference cost of Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models, but attention-based scores do not directly measure a token's effect on the predicted action. We define action sensitivity as the magnitude of the first-order action change when a token is replaced by its camera's mean token and estimate it for all tokens with a single backward pass. Computing it online is costly: scoring on the full (unpruned) graph needs an extra forward pass at every control step. Scoring on the pruned forward pass is cheaper but causes scoring-graph lock-in: removed tokens lose their direct paths through later layers and tend to receive lower scores, making those locations less likely to return. On replayed observations, even with a random conditioning mask, pruned-graph scoring re-selects 59% of the mask's tokens, versus 6% expected by chance; attention histories show a similar bias. POKE (Probe Offline, Keep Efficiently) computes full-graph action sensitivities offline and distills them into a student with under 0.3M parameters. At inference, the student scores the current observation without backpropagation or previous masks, and score-guided diversity selection retains the tokens. Keeping 32 of 512 visual tokens on LIBERO, POKE achieves 95.2% success on OpenVLA-OFT and 90.8% on $π_{0.5}$ at $1.85\times$ and $1.26\times$ speedups, outperforming attention-, diversity-, and distillation-based pruners, with larger margins on LIBERO-Plus.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21033) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21033)

## 63. Sparse Attention for Visual Generation: Toward Algorithm-System Co-Design

- **作者**：Majun Zhang, Yuan Guo, Decheng Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Rapid advances in image and video generation have enabled high-fidelity synthesis at higher resolutions and longer durations, making the quadratic cost of dense self-attention a major bottleneck as visual token sequences grow. Sparse attention seeks to preserve generation quality at lower computational cost by retaining informative query-key interactions and omitting less consequential ones, with end-to-end gains depending on the cost of constructing sparse support and the efficiency with which the system maps the resulting workload to hardware. We survey sparse attention for visual generation through a 4+1+3 analytical framework that links algorithmic design to system implementation. The framework examines how static and dynamic methods use source information and the evidence derived from it to decide which interactions to retain and at what granularity, while accounting for the roles of reuse in reducing repeated computation and correction in compensating for omitted contributions. We relate these algorithmic choices to their implementation on hardware through the framework's three system dimensions of data organization, execution, and dataflow. We summarize common evaluation metrics and discuss open challenges and research directions, providing researchers and practitioners with a reference for the joint design of sparse attention algorithms and systems.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21029) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.21029)

## 64. Digits in Notation Are Not Numbers: A Scoring Pitfall in PDF-to-Markdown Conversion of Japanese Technical Documents (Preliminary Evaluation)

- **作者**：Mitsumasa Wada
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Preliminary evaluation. 26 pages

Converting PDFs to Markdown is now routine preprocessing for search and language models, yet scoring the numbers in the output is easy to get wrong. We evaluate MinerU, a plain-text OCR baseline (NDLOCR-Lite), and a deterministic post-processing pipeline (anchor insertion, footnote reordering, fixes of look-alike characters in fixed contexts, gated formula repair using two independent OCR partners, and heading reconstruction) on 48 public Japanese pages (three documents), text-layer and rasterized. Our pre-specified number-and-unit F1 favored the OCR baseline (0.575 vs 0.397), and a disagreement check between the two engines seemed to flag about half of the converter's number errors (54.6%). Both were artifacts. The scorer counted digits in the converter's notation (image-link paths, HTML attributes), which plain text lacks. With the notation removed (a post-hoc check), the F1 scores tie (0.575 vs 0.594), and the check flags only 13% of number errors, because most errors are misses that both engines share. What remains: the OCR baseline has a lower body character error rate (0.012 vs 0.053 rasterized). The pipeline raises extra-number recall at page level (+0.145; the document-level interval touches 0), mostly by restoring page numbers, but lowers heading-level agreement (0.133 to 0.061) and leaves spurious formulas unchanged; its formula repair never fires. A pre-specified precision gate for its flags (0.80, human-judged) is met at 0.929, yet the post-processing hypothesis fails on its improvement items. We recommend scoring numbers after removing notation and checking critical figures against the source. By the pre-specified criteria, the study stays a technical report.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20993) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20993)

## 65. MedJev: A System One Vision-Language Decision Model for Fast and Effective Medical Image Understanding

- **作者**：Fan Zhang, Hao Wu, Jinyang Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Medical image understanding with vision-language models (VLMs) typically relies on autoregressively generating reasoning and answer tokens. These unnecessary tokens make the inference cost scale with the response length and force the answer to be parsed from free-form text, incurring avoidable latency and decision errors. To this end, we introduce \textsc{MedJev}, a \emph{system one} vision-language decision model that maps medical images, questions, and candidate answers directly to decisions in a single multimodal forward pass, without any autoregressive generation process. For fast inference, \textsc{MedJev} removes the upper language layers and the generative output pathway, while keeping the full vision pathway and all visual tokens. On this shortened backbone, a decision head scores each candidate by pretrained query--key matching and refines the scores with a lightweight multilayer perceptron (MLP) residual, coupling the pretrained matching structure with task-specific nonlinear discrimination. Built on Qwen3.5-4B, \textsc{MedJev}-4B improves both accuracy and speed over the original model on each of four medical image classification and visual question answering benchmarks, with an absolute accuracy gain of \textbf{7.93\%} and a \textbf{6.04$\times$} end-to-end speedup. Further comparison with 20 cutting-edge models shows that \textsc{MedJev}-4B achieves state-of-the-art performance and inference speed among 4B--9B general and medical VLMs, indicating that direct multimodal decision making is an effective route to fast and effective medical image understanding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20992) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20992)

## 66. FAVR: Flow-Aware Visual Residual Alignment for Vision-Language-Action Policies

- **作者**：Qixuan Wu, Xiaole Yang, Yuguang Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures. Equal contribution: Qixuan Wu and Xiaole Yang. Corresponding authors: Yuguang Yang and Baochang Zhang

Pretrained video representations provide rich temporal information for robotic manipulation, yet effectively integrating them into existing vision-language-action (VLA) policies remains challenging. We present Flow-Aware Visual Residual Alignment (FAVR), a lightweight framework for flow-aware visual conditioning of frozen VLA policies. FAVR maintains a compact temporal visual state through linear Test-Time Training (TTT) and retrieves action-conditioned visual features throughout flow-based action generation. By exploiting the affine structure of the frozen action head, we explicitly characterize how injected visual features influence predicted action velocities and formulate flow-aware channel selection as a box-constrained optimization problem. We develop a FISTA-based solver for joint gate optimization and derive an efficient closed-form approximation through diagonalization and rescaling. With only 1.74-2.19M trainable parameters and 5,000 adaptation steps, FAVR improves pretrained VLA policies across benchmarks. On RoboTwin 2.0, FAVR improves LingBot-VLA from 87.12% to 90.04% under clean conditions and from 86.88% to 89.38% under randomized conditions. A controlled ablation on five RoboTwin tasks further shows that flow-aware gating increases average success from 72.0% with a gate shared across flow steps to 82.0%, demonstrating the benefit of adapting visual conditioning throughout action generation.

## 67. vividRGB: Various visual cue detection in RGB photographs with forced focused attention

- **作者**：Masahiro Ryo
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：32 pages including supplementary material; 6 numbered figures and a graphical summary

Natural photographs contain dense, overlapping structures that can be weakly expressed in feature maps from whole-image vision encoders. We introduce forced focused attention (FFA) and its Python implementation, vividRGB. FFA is an encoder-agnostic inference-time procedure that applies a frozen encoder to enlarged, overlapping local crops, averages inverse-aligned reflections, and reconstructs a feature field in source coordinates. We evaluate 50 high-resolution photographs from grassland vegetation, fungal networks, airborne forests, coral reefs, and phytoplankton microscopy, using DINOv3 ViT-B/16. Across the five domains, FFA at 5% or 10% focus produces higher mean RGB-edge boundary F1 than direct DINOv3 encoding and automatic SAM3 masks. Domain means range from 0.244 to 0.596 for FFA, versus 0.028-0.076 for direct DINOv3 and 0.020-0.090 for SAM3. Qualitative feature maps also show finer appearance structure. These results concern RGB-edge preservation rather than verified species, traits, or biological segmentation. Narrower focus generally improves boundary preservation, while extraction time and GPU energy use increase sharply and gains diminish at finer settings. FFA may be useful for relatively small, high-resolution ecological image collections where dense visual cues matter. The alpha release vividRGB 0.1.0 and versioned research resources support reproducible cue inspection.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20942) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20942)

## 68. Learning What the True Labels Would Teach: Posterior Analytic Class-Incremental Learning with Unlabeled Data

- **作者**：Wei Tang, Tong Wei, Jiahao Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV

In class-incremental learning with unlabeled data, each task brings only class names and unlabeled images, so CLIP's zero-shot predictions replace labels. Existing methods train on hard pseudo-labels, but a wrong one gives the true class zero probability, which self-training often confirms. We argue that each image should carry its label posterior, as an analytic classifier trained on posteriors equals the true-label one in expectation. The zero-shot prediction is not this posterior, as its class proportions confound name bias with true imbalance and it ignores other images. We propose Posterior Analytic Class-Incremental Learning (PACIL), which corrects the class prior with a prior selection rule and refines each distribution by a fixed-prior von Mises-Fisher mixture on whitened features. These soft labels train the classifier and replace labels to select its fusion weight with zero-shot CLIP by closed-form leave-one-out cross-validation. We prove that posterior targets minimize the mean-square gap to the true-label prediction and that their argmax widens this gap only through ambiguous images. The fusion weight selected with soft labels loses at most their distance to the posteriors in expected correct decisions. PACIL has the best average incremental accuracy among methods trained without labels on all 18 settings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20937) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20937)

## 69. ReAccess: Look Once, Stay Focused with Efficient Test Time Memory

- **作者**：Tianhao Chen, Yilong Fan, Yuheng Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Project Page: https://ryu1ion.github.io/reaccess/

Despite recent advances, vision-language models (VLMs) still struggle with fine-grained visual understanding, often failing to effectively utilize critical evidence already encoded in their visual representations. Existing approaches address this limitation through repeated visual processing or explicit reasoning, introducing substantial computational overhead. In this paper, we propose ReAccess, a lightweight framework that preserves and reuses question-relevant visual evidence through compact associative memory. Constructed during a single visual prefill, the memory supports progressive evidence retrieval that steers both attention queries and hidden representations, enabling effective visual grounding without repeated image encoding or explicit reasoning traces. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent improvements in both general visual understanding and fine-grained perception, while achieving substantially faster inference than existing reasoning-intensive approaches, demonstrating a promising path to efficient multimodal reasoning.

## 70. EviBridge: Encoding Predictive Visual Evidence for Proactive Interaction

- **作者**：Zhichao Huang, Changheng Cai, Song Ren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Tool-handover interaction calls for preparation before a response is warranted. The model waits until interaction cues appear, then prepares while assessing whether the accumulating evidence supports a response or calls for cancellation. We introduce EviBridge, a framework that integrates predictive visual representations into a vision-language interaction model for joint state and tool prediction. A trainable evidence interface maps features from a frozen V-JEPA 2 encoder into the language decoder alongside native visual inputs, preserving their temporal context and using only observations available at each decision time. The framework has two instantiations. EviBridge-E distinguishes recent from historical evidence and encodes the timing and spatial location of each observation. EviBridge-I preserves the temporal order of pooled V-JEPA 2 features and interaction turns and generates dedicated interaction tokens. We evaluate on FuGIBench, a streaming-video benchmark for preparation, response, and cancellation in tool-handover assistance. The best-performing variants of EviBridge-E and EviBridge-I achieve Overall scores of 0.6448 and 0.5892, compared with 0.2178 and 0.5327 for their respective task-adapted native baselines. These gains span target handovers, long waits, and cancelled requests, demonstrating the value of predictive visual evidence for interaction decisions.

## 71. Palmprint Decomposer: Interpretable and Editable Factorization via Synthetic Learning and Instance-Wise Refinement

- **作者**：Licheng Yan, Yuqi Wang, Jingtao Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Palmprint recognition and synthesis methods focus on extracting discriminative textures or generating images with varied textures and appearances. However, they do not generally provide an explicit image-level representation that separates palmprint texture from its underlying base appearance for independent analysis or controlled editing. We introduce Palmprint Decomposer, an interpretable framework that represents a palmprint through independently editable factors and reconstructs the image through a multiplicative composition model. A color map and a light map characterize the base appearance, while a structure map and a global texture strength parameter describe texture modulation. However, accurate reconstruction alone does not guarantee separation of palmprint texture from base appearance, as fine palmprint textures may leak into the estimated base appearance. To mitigate this ambiguity, we train the decomposer exclusively on synthetic data with direct factor supervision, using asymmetric decoding paths and decomposition constraints to suppress texture leakage. Instance-Wise Refinement further improves real-image reconstruction by directly optimizing the predicted factors for each input without relying on the trained network. Experiments across seven datasets demonstrate high reconstruction fidelity, with the extracted structure maps generally achieving verification performance comparable to the original images. Compared with existing methods, our approach achieves better factor separation while preserving more discriminative palmprint texture information.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20913) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20913)

## 72. Can Image Generators Hold a Scene Together When Space Is Broken Apart?

- **作者**：Jhen-Ke Lin, Chung-Chun Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages

Image generators are good at drawing scenes in which things that belong together appear next to each other. In almost every image they generate, and in almost every benchmark used to evaluate these images, "belonging to the same whole" and "adjacent to each other" go together. Therefore, we cannot know whether the model truly grasps the scene as a whole, or simply makes each part connect with its adjacent parts. We propose GJBench to disentangle the two. It requires the model to draw a scene as a scattered jigsaw puzzle: an image is cut into interlocking pieces, then moved apart and rotated. Thus, the spatial plausibility of the scene exists only in how these pieces collectively point to a whole. The model may have a reference image, a target layout, both, or neither. We separately evaluate three requirements: whether the scene is broken down into a specified number of separate pieces, whether the pieces interlock, and whether the pieces can reconstruct the reference scene. While every open and closed model we evaluated could draw these scenes well as a whole, none could keep them together as scattered puzzle pieces, and the three requirements categorized failures into different patterns. The models draw separate pieces but miscount them in different ways, and unless their outlines are provided, the pieces rarely interlock, and their content does not reconstruct the reference image. We also found that the target layout helps the puzzle pieces fit together, while the reference image improves neither their count nor their fit. These findings show that keeping a scene continuous across space that the canvas no longer shows remains an open problem for image generators.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20906) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20906)

## 73. SciWorldBench: Do Video World Models Really Understand Scientific Experiments?

- **作者**：Xue Yang, Haonan Cai, Xinyu Hang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Preprint. Benchmark paper with paired scientific experiments for evaluating video world models. 8 pages of main text, 16 pages including figures and references

While the ability of video generation models to accurately depict scientific phenomena has received growing attention, determining whether these models genuinely understand the underlying principles remains a critical challenge. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate video samples in isolation, making it difficult to expose models that simply fit superficial visual priors. In scientific experiments, a small change in one condition produces a markedly different outcome even when the rest of the setup remains fixed. Capitalizing on this sensitivity, we introduce SciWorldBench, a benchmark comprising paired scientific experiments. Each pair varies a single condition in an otherwise fixed setup, yielding two mutually exclusive expected outcomes. Each generation prompt specifies the condition but omits the expected outcome. We evaluate ten text-and-image-to-video (TI2V) models using an independent vision-language model (VLM) that assesses each video without access to the condition text or the label identifying the correct outcome. A pair is scored as correct only when both videos depict their respective scientifically correct outcomes. Across all ten models, we find that the two videos in an experimental pair frequently depict the same outcome even though the conditions require different results. This recurring failure suggests that current video world models struggle to apply the underlying scientific principles reliably during generation. We will publicly release the benchmark dataset and open-source the evaluation code.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20899) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20899)

## 74. Certified, Not Explained: Exact Token Erasure in Gated Vision State Space Models

- **作者**：Sadia Parveen, Tehseen Zia
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：20 pages, 4 figures

Vision models built on Mamba are often explained through their selective step size Delta, on the assumption that a token the scan does not admit cannot influence the prediction. We test that in a concept bottleneck model for lung-nodule malignancy built on a single gated, bidirectional selective state-space block. The answer becomes an interpretability framework: a guarantee, a procedure that checks it, and a pre-registered audit of what it explains. For a fixed gate pattern, masking the input of the causal convolution of the block and giving closed tokens zero weight at readout makes the concept and task logits exactly invariant to the closed tokens. Gating Delta is neither necessary nor sufficient. On LIDC-IDRI, a path-by-path knockout over 15 cross-validated models agrees with this in every run: the largest logit change is exactly 0 whenever the conditions hold and between 4.1 and 6.7 when they do not. The gated model reaches a pooled out-of-fold AUROC of 0.8425 +/- 0.0085, level with an ungated Mamba bottleneck (0.8428). The guarantee belongs to the masked model, not to the gate as an explanation. Post-hoc rankers, which inherit the guarantee on this model, order tokens better than the gate. Gate maps from a model trained on permuted labels are as close to the real maps as two real-label seeds are to each other. Under retraining, the tokens of the gate carry more task information than random tokens and, on shifted crops, more than central ones, but no more than the tokens chosen by hidden attention. We therefore read the gate as a certified support set produced by mask supervision. Splitting the Oracle Impurity Score into own-concept and cross-concept parts reveals differences the aggregate score misses. Partitioning the readout reduced leakage slightly under an earlier recipe, and the final model sits at, rather than below, the level implied by correlations among the true concepts.

## 75. ProSE: Probing Reconstruction Error for Semantic-guided Expert Skipping in MoE-MLLMs

- **作者**：Jiameng Li, Minye Wu, Matthew B. Blaschko
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages

Mixture-of-Experts Multimodal Large Language Models (MoE-MLLMs) offer scalable inference through sparse expert activation, yet substantial computational redundancy remains among the activated experts. Existing expert skipping methods typically calibrate thresholds based on target skipping ratios or final-output divergence, overlooking the accumulation of skipping-induced error across layers and the varying semantic importance of individual tokens. To address that, we propose ProSE, a training-free, semantic-guided expert skipping framework that probes layer-wise reconstruction error to promote representation consistency compared with default activation. Specifically, we construct query-aware semantic masks to identify salient visual tokens and progressively calibrate modality-specific skipping thresholds for each MoE layer. By minimizing masked reconstruction error under a given budget, ProSE maintains semantic consistency after skipping and allows adaptive expert allocation under limited activation budgets. Extensive experiments on 9 benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements on average over existing expert skipping methods, particularly under aggressive skipping settings. ProSE maintains over 96\% performance on KimiVL-A3B-Inst with 83\% expert skipping and accelerates prefilling by $1.7\times$.

## 76. Multi-Rate Flow Matching for Point Cloud Upsampling

- **作者**：Jingkai Shang, Edgar Torres, Duy Nguyen
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Flow-matching point-cloud upsamplers refine sparse inputs into dense point clouds by integrating a learned velocity field with a fixed temporal discretization. However, local reconstruction requirements vary substantially across a shape, while a shared solver budget applies the same temporal resolution everywhere. We propose Multi-Rate Flow Matching (MRFM), a patch-adaptive framework that selects an integration budget for each local patch at inference time while keeping the learned velocity field frozen. MRFM relaxes the discrete budget into a continuous variable, optimizes it online through differentiable soft rollouts using an input-derived geometric fidelity objective, and projects the result back to a discrete budget for the final solver rollout. Experiments on PU1K and PUGAN show strong reconstruction quality, while matched-budget permutation and multi-factor analyses demonstrate that the benefit depends on patch-specific allocation rather than on the step distribution alone. MRFM is accuracy-oriented and intentionally uses additional test-time optimization to obtain more suitable local temporal resolutions.

## 77. MVSTF: Multi-View Spatio-Temporal Fusion for LiDAR-Based Moving Object Segmentation

- **作者**：Jiaoju Zhou, Chao Ye, Xuebo Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Autonomous mobile robots confront significant chal lenges in recognizing the motion properties of objects in dy namic environments. However, existing methods are hindered by limitations such as boundary ambiguity, occlusion, viewpoint constraints, and training complexity. In this paper, we propose an innovative end-to-end Multi-View Spatio-Temporal Fusion network, named MVSTF, which utilizes sequential point clouds to generate multi-view images and feature differences, and designs a multi-branch network to extract spatio-temporal and dimen sional features in parallel, and realizes high-precision moving object segmentation. First, sequential point clouds are utilized to generate multi-view images and feature differences. Then, an end-to-end multi-branch network including Dual Down-sampling Blocks (DDB), Spatial Attention Blocks (SAB), Multi-Kernel Convolutional Neural Network blocks (MKCNN), Dual Feature Fusion Blocks (DFFB), and the Point Fusion (PF) module, is designed to extract motion features from feature differences and geometric and semantic features from multi-view images. Finally, an auxiliary semantic head is introduced to further improve the algorithm's robustness and the model's interpretability. This work is validated on the SemanticKITTI and KITTI-Road bench marks, achieving state-of-the-art results on the SemanticKITTI MOS validation set and the KITTI-Road validation set, and competitive results on the hidden SemanticKITTI-MOS test set. The code is available at https://github.com/ZhouJJ111/MVSTF.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20872) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20872)

## 78. LiDSeg: Linear DehazeFormer Guidance for Efficient Semantic Segmentation in Adverse Weather Conditions

- **作者**：Yobsan Bayisa Leta, Christos Kyrkou, Maria K. Michael et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Includes supplementary material

Robust semantic segmentation in adverse weather is essential for safety-critical autonomous systems. Existing restore-then-segment pipelines introduce latency and prioritize pixel-level restoration over semantic discriminability, while domain adaptation methods often face representation bottlenecks and computational complexity. We introduce LiDSeg, a framework that uses reconstruction-informed guidance to improve segmentation under adverse weather. During training, a lightweight guidance encoder is jointly optimized with the segmentation branch using segmentation and auxiliary reconstruction objectives. At inference, the reconstruction decoder is discarded, while the guidance encoder is retained to extract weather-robust features. A lightweight gated fusion module integrates these features into the segmentation branch, preserving clear-weather accuracy while improving adverse-weather robustness. On Foggy Cityscapes, LiDSeg limits the clear-to-dense-fog mIoU degradation to 2.5-2.8 percentage points, reducing the baseline performance drop by more than fivefold. On the official ACDC test server, LiDSeg-PIDNet achieves 75.07% pooled all-condition mIoU, comparable to supervised HRNet-W48 at 75.0%, while remaining substantially smaller. The gains generalize zero-shot to Foggy Driving and Foggy Zurich without training on real-fog images. LiDSeg adds only 1.9-3.1M parameters across several models, with total model sizes of 5.2-39.5M parameters and no inference-time reconstruction overhead. It delivers real-time inference on the NVIDIA Jetson AGX Orin. Code: https://github.com/yobsanb/LiDSeg

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20864) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20864)

## 79. When Is the Expensive Method Worth It? A Content-Driven Analysis of Classical Bayer Demosaicing

- **作者**：Taranum Wasu, Harsh Kashyap, HS Pannu
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Preprint. 14 pages

Demosaicing reconstructs a full-colour image from a single-sensor colour-filter-array (CFA) mosaic, where only one colour is measured per pixel. Classical methods make different assumptions about images, from simple averaging to directional edge-aware interpolation, and the directional methods cost markedly more compute. We ask a practical question that a per-image quality ranking does not answer: for a given image, is the expensive directional method worth its cost? Using a controlled simulate-reconstruct pipeline on 80 benchmark images from four public datasets (Kodak, McMaster, BSD100, Urban100), six classical methods, and image-content descriptors fixed before any results were seen, we show that the quality gain of the edge-aware method (Menon 2007) over the best cheap linear method is not constant. It ranges from $-6.6$ to $+6.8$ dB, and on 31% of images it is zero or negative, meaning a cheap method matches or beats the expensive one. The gain is also predictable from content: it correlates with mean image saturation at $r=-0.86$ ($p<10^{-6}$). We then build an interpretable depth-2 decision tree that recommends the best cheap method from image descriptors, beating an always-pick-the-most-common baseline by 20.0 percentage points under leave-one-image-out validation. The analysis uses no deep learning, and every step can be inspected.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20854) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20854)

## 80. Vision-Language Model-Based Phase-OTDR Event Classification

- **作者**：Ahmet Bestami Kose, Mehmet Sergen Catal, Muhammet Cagri Yeke et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：20 pages, 7 Figures, 8 Tables

Phase-sensitive optical time-domain reflectometry (Phase-OTDR) enables distributed vibration sensing, but reliable event classification has traditionally relied on fully supervised models that require extensive annotated data. This study examines the adaptability of a Vision-Language Model (VLM) for Phase-OTDR event recognition through few-shot learning. Raw samples are converted into Short-Time Fourier Transform (STFT) dB-magnitude spectrogram mosaics, color-mapped and resized to RGB images, and processed by a Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) image encoder; few-shot adaptation optimizes only the Tip-Adapter-F cache keys, leaving both encoders unchanged. Under a leakage-controlled protocol (train-side support/validation pool of 512 images per class, fixed held-out test of 120 per class, no test-set access during selection, 20 distinct support-sampling trials), the selected configuration (the ViT-L-14 Vision Transformer encoder with OpenAI weights, 128 shots per class) reaches 96.81 +/- 0.42% accuracy and a macro-averaged F1 score of 96.79 +/- 0.42% on the held-out test set; a label-efficient 16-shot setting, that is, 96 labeled support images at adaptation time across the six classes, already attains 90.17 +/- 0.81%. Tip-Adapter-F clearly outperforms both zero-shot CLIP and the standard training-free Tip-Adapter, and prompt and backbone analyses show that performance is stable across Vision Transformer encoders and insensitive to prompt wording at the selected operating point. Overall, CLIP visual features carry a transferable structure that a lightweight support cache turns into a label-efficient Phase-OTDR event classifier. The code and the corresponding image dataset are made publicly available on GitHub to support further research: https://github.com/miralab-ai/vlm-phase-otdr.

## 81. Interpretable deep concepts of organoid morphology

- **作者**：Lion Jasper Gleiter, Tingying Peng
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Image-based classification of organoid morphologies can indicate drug response, viability, or successful differentiation. In recent work, learned representations outperform handcrafted features as the basis for downstream classifiers. However, their black-box nature hides the morphological cues a classifier relies on. Based on the foundation models DINOv3 (general-purpose) and ViTAMIn-O (organoid-specific), we train sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to learn interpretable concepts related to distinct organoid morphologies. We annotate concepts with spatial labels (organoid, organoid wall, background) and compute their dataset specificity and task relevance across five tasks. The spatial labels of DINOv3 concepts yield coarse organoid segmentations on out-of-domain datasets. Filtering concepts by spatial label in the DINOv3-SAE improves downstream AUROC for three of the five tasks. Qualitative analysis of the most relevant concepts further reveals a confounding factor introduced by our preprocessing in one task. Through such insights, interpretable concepts could help improve the reliability of classifiers and guide the future development of organoid representation models. The trained SAEs will be made available upon publication.

## 82. An Efficient Training Paradigm for Image Restoration via Improved Fréchet Loss

- **作者**：Shanyuan Jie, Wu Shi, Xinqi Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 14 figures, and 7 tables

Diffusion-based image restoration achieves strong perceptual quality but typically relies on iterative sampling, leading to slow inference. Existing single-step methods often use diffusion distillation or adversarial training, complicating optimization. To simplify training, we propose FDIR, a framework for training single-pass generative restoration models through regression-style supervised optimization using directly computable objectives. To realize this paradigm, FDIR employs the Fréchet distance to match the feature distributions of restored and real images in frozen representation spaces, providing explicit distribution-level supervision. To better accommodate image restoration, we incorporate local patch features to capture fine-grained restoration discrepancies. Moreover, we introduce instance-adaptive statistics by combining the feature moments of each SR-HQ pair with synchronized historical statistics, allowing distribution matching to incorporate input-specific correspondence while retaining distribution-level context. FDIR optimizes only the LoRA parameters of the restoration backbone. Experiments demonstrate strong perceptual restoration quality and efficient training, suggesting a simpler paradigm for generative image restoration.

## 83. Correspondence-Free Camera Pose Estimation by Matching Moments

- **作者**：Subhransu S. Bhattacharjee, Dylan Campbell, Rahul Shome
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO, eess.IV, math.OC
- **备注**：65 pages, including references and appendices; under review

The blind perspective-$n$-point (PnP) problem is to estimate the pose of a calibrated camera from 2D and 3D point sets with unknown correspondences. We show that, for complete, noiseless observations, fourteen permutation-invariant image moments up to order four identify a fixed camera pose for almost every 3D point set containing at least twenty points. Our learning-free method, MomentPnP, matches these moments between the observed 2D points and the projected 3D points to initialize a camera pose without correspondences and refines the pose and correspondence weights using expectation--maximization. Across the test sets of three benchmarks, our method reduces median rotation error by $5$--$170\times$ relative to the best learned baseline, both with and without outliers. We recover accurate poses even with as many outliers as inliers. On dense ETH3D scans, pose accuracy improves as the point sets grow, even as individual correspondences become harder to resolve.

## 84. TRR: A Thermal Interaction Engine for Robot Simulation and RGBT Data Generation

- **作者**：Biwen Yang, Zhe Cao, Jin Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

A robot's actions change the thermal conditions of its next interaction: heat retained after release, carried with objects or stored beneath the surface can change a later contact's outcome despite similar current temperatures. Training trajectories must capture how these effects connect earlier actions to later observations and task outcomes. We present TRR, a \emph{Thermal Robot Renderer} that attaches spatial and depth-resolved thermal state to both contact partners, updates local heat exchange through contact, transport and separation, and generates synchronized RGB--thermal (RGBT) observations and event-local task checks on one execution clock. Controlled interventions show why retained heat, spatial transport and internal storage matter for subsequent contact outcomes. From the same state, TRR produces a paired six-task benchmark of 2,224 static--dynamic pairs, that is, 4,448 trajectories with synchronized dual-view observations, states and actions. Visible thermal history raises safe contact-site selection from 75.0\% to 91.7\% on a dedicated learning corpus, and training on dynamic histories raises simulated transfer completion by 50 percentage points under a new heating procedure with shared feedback execution. We demonstrate RGBT observation adapters on MuJoCo and Isaac Sim, report an engineering inventory of ten prepared robot configurations and six demonstrated camera viewpoints, and describe a developer workflow that carries an existing robot scene to exported RGBT records. The benchmark, the workflow and the platform resources described in Section 6 form the first items of a planned public release. Demonstrations and resource updates are available on the project page.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20792) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20792)

## 85. VA-Assembly: View-Aligned Score Guidance for Training-Free 3D Part Assembly

- **作者**：Yeon-Jae Kim, Sangwon Kang, Byung-Kwon Min
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 9 figures

Image-to-3D foundation models have improved 3D reconstruction, but typically produce monolithic representations without explicit part-level structure. Part-aware generation and learning-based assembly methods often require part-level supervision, category-specific priors, or paired part-layout training data. We instead consider a practical but underexplored setting in which independently prepared or generated 3D parts have no predefined inter-part relationships, shared coordinate frames, or consistent scales, and must be assembled into a reference-consistent object from a single image. We present VA-Assembly, a training-free framework for 3D part assembly through part-level transformation estimation using view-aligned diffusion guidance. Given a reference image and fixed 3D part geometries, VA-Assembly optimizes each part's translation, rotation, and scale. We condition a frozen view-conditioned diffusion model on same-view renderings of a reference-derived monolithic 3D representation, producing score gradients geometrically aligned with the part configuration. This guidance provides multi-view structural cues under large initial misalignment. By repurposing pretrained visual-structural priors as transformation-level supervision, VA-Assembly requires no annotated part layouts, predefined part taxonomies, category-specific assembly training, or bounding-box-based layout initialization. Experiments compare VA-Assembly with geometric registration and compositional layout baselines using each baseline's supported transformation components, showing improved part-wise transformation estimation. Ablation studies show that view-aligned conditioning, rather than diffusion guidance alone, is critical for reliable transformation gradients. Updating only part transformations preserves explicit component separability and supports component-level editing, replacement, and reconfiguration.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20788) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20788)

## 86. PhaseCLIP: Phase-Aware Frequency-Domain Adaptation of Vision-Language Models for Few-Shot Remote-Sensing Classification

- **作者**：Mehmet Sergen Catal, Abdurrahman Gumus, Mehmet Zübeyir Ünlü
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, 7 figures, 5 tables. Code will be released at https://github.com/miralab-ai/PhaseCLIP upon publication

Remote-sensing (RS) image scene classification aims to assign a semantic category to an overhead image, and in practice must often do so from only a handful of labelled examples, since annotation requires expert interpretation and the resulting labels transfer poorly across sensors and regions. Recent progress has come from adapting vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP, through learned prompts or feature adapters. Although effective, these methods construct their representation entirely in the spatial domain. RS scenes are dominated by periodic texture and regular geometry, which the Fourier spectrum describes compactly through an amplitude component, carrying texture and contrast statistics, and a phase component, carrying spatial structure. Existing frequency-domain adaptation of VLMs either exploits amplitude alone or does not separate amplitude from phase, leaving the structural component unused. In this article, we propose PhaseCLIP, which inserts phase-capable spectral modules and a feature-gating module into a frozen CLIP visual encoder and trains only 1.30% of its parameters. Because the amplitude and phase pathways share an identical parameter layout, a single weight set is learned on phase and transferred exactly to amplitude, giving a two-stage scheme that adds no parameters. Experiments on five public RS datasets, EuroSAT, PatternNet, UC Merced, LSRS and RSSCN7, against eight baselines under a matched protocol, show that PhaseCLIP attains 84.28% mean accuracy and is the best method in 23 of 30 dataset-by-shot cells, with phase contributing 1.93 points overall and 4.36 points on the most difficult dataset. Because the modules act on the spectrum of image patches and transformer tokens and leave the text encoder untouched, the approach can be extended to other RS sensors and tasks, and to other domains in which class evidence lies in spatial structure.

## 87. ThermoSight: From Appearance to Internal Thermofluid Fields via Physics-Guided Neural Reconstruction

- **作者**：Yuhao Fang, Chenglin Lu, Hefei Ling et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, physics.app-ph

Most neural reconstruction methods focus on reproducing scene appearance, yet inferring physical states from visual observations is essential for machine understanding of the physical world. Reconstructing the internal temperature and soot fields of radiating thermofluids such as flames is challenging yet essential for improving energy efficiency and assessing heat exposure and fire spread. In flames, temperature and soot concentration jointly govern visible emission, and soot self-absorption alters the light that reaches each camera. With limited views, changes in the two fields can offset one another in the recorded RGB images, making temperature and soot difficult to disentangle. ThermoSight, a physics-guided neural framework for scientific imaging, jointly reconstructs continuous three-dimensional temperature and soot volume fraction fields from limited calibrated flame images without field-level ground truth during optimization. A differentiable spectral model links these fields to measured RGB through thermal emission, self-absorption, and calibrated camera responses. Temperature-dependent emission spectra and wavelength-dependent attenuation constrain shared fields across RGB channels, reducing temperature-soot ambiguity. Chromaticity consistency, observation-derived spatial constraints, and soot regularization complement intensity fitting. In a synthetic ablation, wavelength-resolved attenuation reduces temperature RMSE in the hot, sooty region from 234 to 112 K relative to a three-channel approximation, with nearly identical RGB PSNR (65.23 vs. 65.24 dB). Measured data yield coherent fields and competitive RGB reconstructions. These results demonstrate that physics-guided neural fields recover interpretable internal physical quantities beyond visually consistent appearance.

## 88. EEGProbe: Do Vision-Language Models Really Understand EEG? A Benchmark for Signal-Grounded EEG Signals Evaluation

- **作者**：Haozhe Zhu, Dan Yuan, Chenghao Gu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.HC
- **备注**：21 pages, 12 figures

Vision-language models (VLMs) have shown strong multimodal capabilities across scientific and biomedical domains, yet their ability to understand electroencephalography (EEG) signals remains unclear. We introduce EEGProbe, a signal-grounded benchmark for evaluating whether general-purpose VLMs can extract and reason from minimally processed, channel-wise EEG without task-specific feature engineering or EEG-specific adaptation. EEGProbe contains 620 EEG-centered questions across 13 subtasks and three capability categories: EEG Signal Classification, EEG Cross-modal Alignment, and EEG Neurophysiological Reasoning. To reduce shortcut-based answering, EEGProbe preserves the temporal structure of the recordings where possible, minimizes benchmark-induced processing, and applies signal-necessity testing followed by expert verification. We evaluate 11 representative open- and closed-source VLMs under a unified zero-shot protocol. The strongest model, GPT-5.6 Sol, achieves an overall score of only 41.63\%, with cross-modal EEG alignment emerging as a major bottleneck. We further examine model behavior from a neurophysiological perspective and analyze how temporal, spectral, spatial, and physiological EEG structure relates to model successes and failures. Inference-time neurophysiological guidance can reshape model predictions by helping models recognize the neurophysiological patterns present in the input and connect them with relevant neurological knowledge, narrowing the gap between general multimodal competence and EEG understanding. EEGProbe provides a testbed for current VLMs and a basis for developing future multimodal models that can interpret EEG evidence more reliably. The EEGProbe benchmark will be publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/2122ZHU/EEGProbe.

## 89. Deep Learning for Satellite-Based Mountain Hazard Screening: A Retrospective Failure Analysis on the 2021 Chamoli Rock-Ice Avalanche

- **作者**：Snigdhajyoti Rakshit
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages, 10 figures, 8 tables

The approach utilizes 10 bands from Sentinel-2 imagery, along with terrain information, in a deep learning pipeline to classify potential hazard sources in any given mountainous region. The performance of our model has been evaluated against the data collected during the rock-ice avalanche event in the Chamoli region in the Indian Himalayas on 7th February 2021. U-Net performs segmentation of the detected land surface in the satellite image, detects changes in the identify candidate hazard zones, and then the following steps are performed to calculate the potential energy using the Monte Carlo sampling for steepest descent (D8) flow paths, finding the exposed structures in the downstream locations, and ranking the zones depending on the risk score. In comparison to the simple spectral rule, our approach achieved the higher macro F1 score of 0.648 (as compared to 0.519). But the F1 score in case of rock and soil was low, being in the range of 0.3s. In case of optical change detection in winter, it's only that northeast-facing slope which couldn't be detected, as it was under 73% of shade prior to the incident. The parameters have been set for this particular scene alone and not independently; hence, this is a retrospective test, and this test locates a failure which has occurred earlier. The primary contribution of this paper is locating the pipeline leak.
