## 90. UNITE: Consolidating Embodied VLM Specialists via Shared-Anchor One-Bit Factorization

- **作者**：Tao Tao, Anke Tang, Enneng Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV

Embodied capabilities such as visual grounding, spatial reasoning, navigation, and driving understanding are often developed in separate vision-language specialists fine-tuned from a common pretrained model.Deploying the specialists separately multiplies storage, whereas merging them into one dense model forces conflicting task updates to share parameters and loses part of each specialist's expertise.We introduce UNITE, a framework that consolidates such specialists into a compact shared-backbone mixture of experts. UNITE keeps the feed-forward block of each specialist as a routable expert and represents the expert bank through a shared-anchor one-bit factorization: a shared floating-point anchor plus expert-specific, row-scaled binary deviations.The anchor is optimized jointly with the scales and signs through closed-form alternating updates, using only source weights and no calibration data.Router adaptation then adjusts token-level expert selection with the backbone and experts frozen, updating fewer than $0.6$M parameters ($<0.01\%$ of the $8.8$B active parameters). Built from four Qwen3-VL-8B specialists, the complete UNITE model occupies $20.3$ GB, $1.16\times$ the storage of one dense specialist and $59.6\%$ below the uncompressed MoE. Across $22$ benchmarks and R2R Val-Unseen, UNITE matches six static merging methods in mean retention of best-specialist performance, leads all of them on Scan2Cap, ScanQA, and all four navigation metrics, and surpasses every source specialist on all five 3D scene understanding benchmarks (up to $+19.1$ on Scan2Cap) and on R2R navigation. Relative to the uncompressed MoE, factorization is nearly lossless on $21$ of $22$ benchmarks; the exception is VSI-Bench, where frame-count experiments suggest impaired use of cross-frame evidence.UNITE thus consolidates complementary embodied capabilities near single-model storage.

## 91. A Seismic Foundation Model for Dense Feature Extraction

- **作者**：Zhixiang Guo, Xinming Wu, Yimin Dou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Deep-learning-based seismic interpretation is constrained by scarce high-quality annotations, and most existing models are trained separately for individual tasks. We present SeisDFE, a 7B-parameter seismic foundation model for dense feature extraction, pretrained on 5.34 million 2D images derived from more than 500 seismic volumes. A three-stage pretraining strategy, combining discriminative self-supervised learning, Gram feature anchoring, and multi-resolution adaptation, improves feature discriminability while preserving spatial coherence. With its encoder frozen, SeisDFE supports seismic facies classification, salt detection, fault detection, and relative geologic time estimation. It outperforms existing seismic foundation models on all four tasks and a natural-image-pretrained model on all but fault detection, with the largest gains under scarce supervision: using only two annotated sections of the F3 dataset, it improves whole-volume mIoU by 12-19 percentage points over the baselines. The learned features remain effective with task-specific decoders and extend to synthetic-to-field transfer and sparse 3D geobody interpretation. These results show that large-scale seismic pretraining organizes geological information into dense representations that are directly reusable across interpretation tasks.

## 92. Robotic Reward Models in the Foundation-Model Era

- **作者**：Guoqiang Liang, Yiqi Lin, Shangchen Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://ethanliang99.github.io/awesome-robot-reward-model

Robotic reward models (RRMs) use foundation models to replace per-task reward engineering with reusable, task-conditioned evaluation. Yet under one name, their outputs answer different questions, from whether a task succeeded to which execution is better. This survey organizes 114 methods into five semantic families by the question their native outputs answer: Outcome, Absolute Progress, Relative, Temporal Value, and Hybrid. Within this taxonomy, we review how model design determines what an evaluator can judge, how raw experience becomes supervision, and how existing evaluation practice tests the result. The review exposes a gap: agreement with demonstration order and downstream policy gains do not show whether a reward tracks task fulfillment. We therefore introduce RRM-Bench-1K, 1,117 real-robot videos of closed-loop executions with task-state and camera-evidence labels, and four contrast suites that separate the credited condition from its usual cues. A unified study of 27 evaluators shows that the strongest final scores rank whole executions well enough for selection and filtering. Dense credit, however, follows the progression of an execution more closely than its task state, and no evaluator signals when the decisive view is missing. How much fidelity a reward model needs therefore depends on the decision it drives. We outline directions from real-failure supervision to metrics that separate task state from chronology, and open-source RRM-Hub, a toolbox in which 14 existing RRMs can be readily applied and new evaluators tested on the same capabilities.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20741) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20741)

## 93. CLIFT: Contrastive LiDAR Instance Features for 3D Pedestrian Tracking

- **作者**：German Devchich, Timur Akhtyamov, Denis Fatykhoph et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A social robot has to keep track of the people around it over time to plan its motion, a task handled by 3D multi-object tracking. For the LiDAR modality, trackers usually sit on top of a detector and link its detections across frames with a separate motion model or re-identification network. We instead show how a 3D LiDAR detector itself can be turned into a tracker by adding an instance-level contrastive objective on its intermediate voxel features. Each embedding is read from the voxels inside a detected box, and boxes are matched across frames by cosine similarity and the Hungarian algorithm, without a motion model. The detector backbone is fine-tuned together with this objective, which learns identity-aware features while leaving detection accuracy essen- tially intact. Because the method uses only the voxel features that Li- DAR detectors already produce, it is backbone-agnostic. On the JRDB 3D tracking leaderboard our LiDAR-only tracker ranks first on HOTA (38.58) and IDF1 (37.99), 13.04 and 9.30 points above the next pub- lished method, and second on MOTA and OSPA. Code is available at https://hermak56.github.io/CLIFT/.

## 94. Selective Sensor Acquisition for Multimodal Place Recognition

- **作者**：Margaux Edwards, Tobias Fischer, Michael Milford
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 6 figures

Multimodal Place Recognition (PR) can improve robustness, but most systems rigidly read and process every sensor for every query, even when the primary sensor is sufficient, with implications for energy consumption, second sensor update rate, and domain-specific considerations such as passive stealth. We introduce selective, controllable sensor acquisition, where a user specifies a desired second sensor usage rate and the system acquires the additional sensor only when the primary sensor's estimated performance is sufficiently poor. An online threshold adjustment achieves the requested usage rate by adapting to recent primary sensor performance, directing additional sensing towards queries where fusion is most likely to help without calibration specific to sensor modality, PR method, or environment. The approach requires only a PR method that produces a match score and applies to both single-frame and sequence-based PR. Our system, across multiple datasets, PR descriptors, and sensor combinations, recovers 55% of the available PR performance gain while using the second sensor on only 25% of queries, and 81% while using it on 50%. We further analyse alternative acquisition decision mechanisms and usage-rate control methods, and examine performance when the second sensor is stronger than, comparable to, or weaker than the primary sensor.

## 95. QASK-Edit: Adaptive Sparse Attention Across Queries and Key Sources for Efficient Video Editing

- **作者**：Hengrui Liu, Wenbo Xu, Ziqi Sheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) have achieved strong performance in generation based video editing, but suffer from high inference latency due to the long token sequences formed by conditioning inputs and generated videos. Although training-free sparse attention reduces this cost, existing methods typically apply a fixed retention ratio or a single threshold shared across all tokens. In this work, we view video editing as conditional generation and identify a key insight: attention demand varies significantly both across different key sources (e.g., source videos, reference images, audio, text, and generated videos) and across queries within each source. Motivated by this, we propose QASK-Edit (Query-Adaptive Sparsity over Key Sources for Video Editing), a training-free sparse attention framework that allocates computation independently for each query and key source. Specifically, for each query, QASK-Edit considers both the attention mass and approximation risk of each block. It then evaluates candidate routing thresholds against each key source's risk budget, choosing exact computation or approximations to dynamically adjust sparsity for each query. Extensive experiments on three video editing models demonstrate that QASK-Edit achieves up to $1.46\times$ end-to-end speedup over dense attention pipeline while preserving both editing quality and unedited region fidelity. Furthermore, QASK-Edit generalizes directly to Reference-to-Video generation, delivering up to $1.36\times$ speedup while maintaining generation quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20704) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20704)

## 96. Efficient Multi-View Generation with Iterative Global-Local Proxy Attention

- **作者**：Wei Tan, Kunming Luo, Hongyu Yan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Supplementary material is available as an ancillary file

Generating consistent multi-view RGB images and normal maps from a single input image provides complementary appearance and geometric cues for 3D reconstruction. However, maintaining cross-view consistency requires information exchange across viewpoints, and dense cross-view attention becomes computationally expensive as the number of views and spatial resolution increase. In this paper, we propose Global-Local Proxy Attention (GLPA) to reduce cross-view communication costs while preserving fine-grained within-view processing. Specifically, GLPA retains full-resolution interactions within each view and uses compact global proxies for cross-view communication. To further leverage the exchanged information, GLPA incorporates iterative proxy refinement, which rebuilds the proxies from updated features and reuses the same attention parameters for subsequent interactions within a single denoising step. We evaluate GLPA on two structurally different diffusion backbones, FLUX and Stable Diffusion. Experiments on FLUX show that GLPA achieves a $2.65\times$ speedup in attention computation per denoising step over dense attention, while improving reconstruction IoU from 0.6849 to 0.7019 and reducing NVS LPIPS from 0.086 to 0.076. Results on Stable Diffusion further demonstrate the effectiveness of GLPA, with a $1.80\times$ attention speedup and improvements across all five quality metrics.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20703) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20703)

## 97. STARS: Stage-Adaptive Responsibility Separation for Shadow Removal in Remote Sensing Images

- **作者**：Gaoliang Song, Chengjie Li, Pengju Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 8 figures, 4 tables

Shadows are a ubiquitous radiative attenuation phenomenon in remote sensing images and can severely affect high-resolution remote sensing interpretation tasks. In shadow removal, although introducing a mask prior provides explicit positional information for the restoration network, it also causes non-shadow regions to be modulated by the shadow prior, producing a systematic brightness shift; notably, this phenomenon is independent of whether the mask is accurate. In remote sensing images, non-shadow regions usually occupy a larger proportion, so a small shift over these regions incurs a much higher cost on whole-image metrics than an equal gain in shadow regions. Adopting a task-decoupling perspective, we therefore decompose shadow removal into two tasks, shadow-region restoration and non-shadow-region identity, within a cascaded architecture, and handle them sequentially. Based on this insight, we propose STARS, a cascaded shadow removal framework that separates regional responsibilities across stages. The method retains the advantages of the shadow mask while resolving the degradation of non-shadow regions, without requiring a complex parallel dual-branch structure. Meanwhile, to introduce large-model prior knowledge into remote sensing tasks, we further design a Feature Selection and Fusion Adapter (FSFA). We conduct extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets, UAV-SC+, ISTD+ and WSRD+ 2026, demonstrating the superiority of STARS. The framework shows that decoupling regional responsibilities can resolve the intrinsic contradiction of mask priors, providing a new direction for image restoration with region heterogeneity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20698) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20698)

## 98. VIVID: A Visual Harness for Agentic Video Editing

- **作者**：Wenhan Yu, Zhaoxi Zhang, Zhilong Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.MM

Video editing requires agents to make editing decisions grounded in audiovisual observations and precise temporal localization, and to evaluate their effects in rendered outputs. General-purpose coding agents possess strong comprehension, reasoning, and code execution capabilities, while media interaction and evidence management mechanisms tailored to video tasks can further unlock their editing potential. We introduce VIVID, a visual harness that supports multiple native coding agents in video post-production without additional model training. VIVID provides tools for on-demand video inspection, audio analysis, and transcription, and preserves observations alongside their corresponding media versions and temporal coordinates. Agents can actively retrieve these observations as they reason, perform edits using their native coding and shell tools, and inspect rendered outputs to guide subsequent revisions. On AgenticVBench, comprising 100 tasks across Assembly, Repair, Sequencing, and Repurpose, Codex with VIVID achieves an average score of 58.6%, an improvement of 20.2 percentage points over native Codex. The same harness also integrates with Claude Code and OpenCode, achieving average scores of 55.1% and 37.1%, respectively, both substantially exceeding their native baselines. These results highlight VIVID's potential as a reusable visual harness for extending the capabilities of general-purpose coding agents to video editing.

## 99. Room for Supervision: Training-Free Dataset Distillation for Object Detection

- **作者**：Ziang Gan, Qi Zhu, Libao Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Dataset distillation (DD) reduces storage and training costs by compressing large datasets into small collections. Most existing methods focus on image classification, where each image typically has a single category label. In contrast, object detection requires accurate category and bounding box annotations for multiple objects, together with visual content consistent with those annotations. Existing detection distillation pipelines rely on learned models and can alter the spatial relationships between objects and their backgrounds. We introduce GRASP, which packs dense native supervision into limited pixels without network training or inference. GRASP jointly searches over image selections and spatial layouts using convex allocation subproblems that penalize insufficient object resolution, excessive image compression, and padding that source context cannot fill. During composition, each selected image is cropped around all annotated objects and expanded into surrounding context without reducing object scale. The resized regions are then combined into distilled images, preserving the spatial layout within each region and retaining all original class labels and bounding box annotations. We evaluate GRASP on MS COCO and PASCAL VOC with compression ratios ranging from 0.25\% to 2\%. GRASP constructs the 1\% MS COCO dataset in under two minutes. Under a common training protocol using hard labels, GRASP achieves the state of the art with both CNN and ViT backbones. Code is available at https://github.com/tade1s/GRASP

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20680) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20680)

## 100. RAMP: Rethinking Multimodal Evaluation through Answer Readout Protocols

- **作者**：Jianye Zeng, Qiao Yikang, Duan Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：10 pages, 9 figures, 1 table

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are often evaluated by converting model outputs into discrete answers. The conversion protocol determines which scores become predictions and can strongly influence the reported results. We introduce RAMP (Readout Aware Multimodal Protocol), a systematic framework that compares direct yes/no token logits, surface-form scores, and typed or reversed A/B interfaces under fixed model checkpoints and paired visual questions. On 500 POPE adversarial questions, typed versus bare readout changes accuracy by $-20.2$ percentage points for SmolVLM-256M, $-16.4$ for TinyLLaVA-1.1B, and $+1.8$ for Qwen2.5-VL-3B. Paired transitions reveal extensive prediction changes even when the net accuracy difference is small: 79 of 104 binary ChartQA items change correctness for SmolVLM-500M, despite a $+1.0$-point net difference. We further analyze prompt wording, candidate forms, option position, cross-task effects, and calibration. An independent offline replay reproduces 16,140 first-token decisions from saved raw scores with exact agreement. Our findings establish answer readout as a measurable component of multimodal evaluation and provide a reproducible protocol for analyzing model comparisons.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20653) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20653)

## 101. Competent Autonomous Driving Through Fine Tuning Vision Language Action Models with Symbolic Traffic Rules

- **作者**：Barkin Dagda, Vinal Asodia, Iman Sharifi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-language-action (VLA) models offer a unified approach to perception, reasoning, and control for autonomous driving, but accurate action prediction alone does not establish compliance with traffic regulations. This paper presents a rule-guided VLA framework that incorporates symbolic traffic-rule feedback into policy optimisation. The framework combines supervised fine-tuning on expert demonstrations with reinforcement learning (RL) refinement using a reward that encourages encoded-rule compliance while penalising deviations from expert actions. Selected provisions of the United Kingdom Highway Code are formalised as executable first-order logic rules using PyReason, covering speed adaptation, following distance, and road-user priority. A frozen vision-language model extracts scene predicates, which are combined with vehicle-state information to assess the projected consequences of candidate control actions. Offline evaluation on held-out data collected in the CARLA simulator compares three group-based RL methods. The results show improvements in both action prediction accuracy and encoded-rule compliance over the supervised baseline. The best-performing variant increases the overall rule pass rate from 92.9% to 97.6%, with zero observed violations of the encoded road-user-priority check in the evaluated samples. These findings support symbolic rule feedback as a means of improving VLA policy alignment with explicit traffic-rule criteria.

## 102. Defect-Aware Masked Autoencoders for Unsupervised Clustering of Steel Surface Defects

- **作者**：Shrey Patel
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at IVCNZ 2026

Reliable recognition of steel surface defects is essential for automated quality inspection, but supervised methods require manually annotated images that are costly to produce. This creates a need for methods that can learn useful defect representations directly from unlabelled surface images. This work presents Defect aware MAE, a label free masked autoencoder for clustering steel surface defects. Each image is divided into 196 patches, with 147 masked and 49 processed by a Vision Transformer encoder. Texture energy based masking preferentially hides structurally informative patches, while a shallow decoder reconstructs HOG descriptors that capture local texture and edge orientation. Strong augmentation and an intermediate layer readout are used to form the final image features. Pretraining uses no class labels, bounding boxes, detection branch, or contrastive loss. On the held out NEU DET split, UMAP followed by Ward clustering achieves 98.6% Hungarian matched accuracy, 0.968 NMI, 0.967 ARI, and 0.838 silhouette score. Under an identical encoder and training schedule, it exceeds a SimCLR contrastive baseline in accuracy, NMI, ARI and silhouette scores across 5 seeds.

## 103. Counterfactual Feature Steering for Adjacency-Preserving Identity Unlearning

- **作者**：FNU Shivam, Layth Sliman, Ramana Reddy et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Identity unlearning in text-to-image diffusion models is increasingly important for privacy, safety, and ethical deployment, as it aims to remove the model's ability to generate specific individuals. In this work, we study identity unlearning with \emph{adjacency preservation}, where the goal is to remove a target individual's identity while preserving related non-target identities. We propose Counterfactual Feature Steering (CFS), a method requiring one-time SAE training that performs bounded inference-time concept editing in the latent space of a sparse autoencoder attached to the text encoder. CFS derives a target direction by contrasting forget and retain distributions, then applies a per-token projection proportional to each token's current alignment with this direction, yielding counterfactual representations that suppress the target while limiting collateral damage. To evaluate adjacency preservation, we introduce the Adjacency-Weighted Unlearning Score (AWUS), which measures the tradeoff between target erasure and collateral damage, and extend it to face-specific identity similarity using ArcFace. Across 4,000 prompts derived from Celeb-100, spanning 100 celebrity identities, CFS achieves the strongest evaluated tradeoff between identity erasure of target identities and preservation of adjacent and generic concepts, while remaining stable across intervention strengths where other steering-based methods degrade. Independent ArcFace evaluation and a 10,000-caption COCO study further examine identity and general preservation. Quantitative nudity-suppression benchmarks and qualitative artistic-style examples provide evidence of transfer beyond identities on SD~1.4.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20637) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20637)

## 104. Instructions Before Actions: Prospective and Retrospective Language Supervision for Driving Vision-Language-Action Data

- **作者**：Angel Martinez-Sanchez, Ross Greer
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.RO

Driving vision-language-action (VLA) models can accept navigation instructions, yet predicting a driver's trajectory does not establish that a model follows the instruction. We study how driving-language supervision is collected and evaluated, distinguishing prospective instructions issued before an action from retrospective labels written after observing it. We develop ADVLAT-Engine to synchronize spoken instructions, road-scene video, and vehicle trajectories, and compare three navigation apps on 48 replayed routes. On 87 maneuvers announced by both Google Maps and a passenger, the passenger uses more scene-dependent language. We evaluate four open driving VLAs before and after fine-tuning on prospective navigation-app drives. Fine-tuning reduces passenger-drive trajectory error by 23-59%, but instruction effects remain inconsistent. At real intersections, counterfactual instructions reveal stronger responsiveness: fine-tuned Alpamayo~2 Super gains 31 and 18 accuracy points at 15 and 5~m, while Qwen-Drive follows alternative maneuvers more reliably with a discrete command than with a sentence. We additionally collected retrospective annotations from four people for the driving recordings and describe controlled comparisons of annotation content, timing, and downstream learning; those analyses are pending. Our findings separate maneuver prediction from instruction-following and establish a framework for comparing prospective and retrospective supervision.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20631) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20631)

## 105. Event-Guided Stereo Video Super-Resolution

- **作者**：Hanyu Xuan, Youtao Yao, Cheng Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Stereo Video Super-Resolution (SVSR) aims to reconstruct high-resolution stereo videos by exploiting temporal cues across consecutive frames and complementary spatial cues between stereo views. However, existing methods struggle to recover high-frequency texture details under rapid motion and complex illumination, compromising reconstruction quality and stereo consistency. Event cameras provide valuable motion and texture cues with high temporal resolution and wide dynamic range, yet their potential for SVSR remains underexplored. To bridge this gap, we present the first investigation of event-guided SVSR and propose a novel framework. Specifically, we develop an Event-Guided Texture Enhancement (EGTE) module that combines bidirectional temporal feature propagation with event-guided texture refinement to restore high-frequency details in complex motion regions. Subsequently, we introduce a Cross-View Texture Interaction (CVTI) module to model cross-view spatial correspondences and aggregate cross-view complementary texture information. With this design, our method effectively integrates event-driven motion and texture information, inter-frame temporal dependencies, and cross-view spatial relationships to recover motion-affected details and maintain stereo consistency. Extensive experiments on both real-world and synthetic datasets demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms existing super-resolution methods in reconstruction quality and stereo consistency. Project Page: https://wzlwlm13.github.io/EvSVSR

## 106. PixelBench: Evaluating Language Models for Pixel-Art Creation

- **作者**：Mutian Hong, Jianxuan Yao, Mingkun Yue et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：38 pages, 12 figures, 11 tables

Pixel art is widely used in games and digital content creation, requiring readable forms, coherent spatial relationships, and harmonious colors under limited resolution and palette constraints. Language models show growing promise in visual creation, yet their pixel art creation capabilities lack systematic evaluation. We introduce PixelBench, formalizing text-to-image generation and instruction-guided editing for pixel art and providing a benchmark for these tasks. We further present a creation environment in which language model agents invoke drawing tools through code to generate and edit pixel art. We assess the quality of generated artwork through human evaluation and employ a multimodal large language model as a judge, validating the agreement between its automated assessments and human preferences. We compare language models and configurations across diverse subjects and canvas sizes. In our experiments, GPT-6 Astra achieves both the highest average judge score and the best average human rank, highlighting the potential of general-purpose language models for pixel art creation. We release the tasks, all artworks, the human rankings, and the judge protocol.

## 107. ASCG: Adaptive Spatial Classifier Guidance for Surgical Concept Suppression in Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Younghwan Kil, Joonhyeong Park, Giung Nam et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, including references and supplementary material

While text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models achieve remarkable quality, they risk producing harmful content. Existing safety mechanisms often rely on indiscriminate, global interventions that induce unintended semantic drift and degrade image quality. To address this, we propose Adaptive Spatial Classifier Guidance (ASCG), a necessity-driven framework that employs probabilistic monitoring to activate guidance selectively upon harm detection. By leveraging Grad-CAM for localized suppression, ASCG effectively removes harmful concepts while preserving superior semantic fidelity compared to conventional methods. Furthermore, we overcome the limitations of traditional metrics like NudeNet---which fails to assess semantic integrity---by introducing a VLM-based evaluation framework to provide a fine-grained, robust analysis of both safety and context preservation. Our results demonstrate that ASCG not only balances safety and fidelity but also exhibits remarkable plug-and-play synergy with existing frameworks to establish state-of-the-art performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20625) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20625)

## 108. Example-Based Spatial Guidance for Training-Free Concept Erasure in Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Younghwan Kil, Giung Nam, Joonhyeong Park et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 (poster). 51 pages, including references, checklist, and appendix

Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models often synthesize policy-violating content, necessitating robust safeguards and rigorous evaluation frameworks. However, current approaches remain largely monolithic in both execution and evaluation, limited to coarse-grained categorization within broad unsafe concepts. Such approaches fail to account for the specific constituent factors of a violation, resulting in weak learning signals and allowing model collapse to bypass safety checks. To overcome these limitations, we propose Example-Based Spatial Guidance (EBSG), a training-free method that utilizes user-editable exemplar packs to provide granular spatial guidance for precise concept steering. Unlike existing methods that coarsely steer the entire image away from a broad concept, EBSG decomposes these categories into specific sub-concepts via exemplar text-image pairs, providing explicit, localized steering signals for more robust safety control. Furthermore, we introduce a vision language model (VLM)-based evaluation protocol that provides a fine-grained assessment, avoiding the pitfall of conventional binary evaluators that permit model collapse to bypass safety checks. Empirically, EBSG achieves state-of-the-art erasure performance across diverse safety datasets and concept categories, spanning four nudity benchmarks, seven I2P harmful-concept slices, and four MJA metaphor categories, while supporting multi-concept removal and transferring to a modern backbone, SD3.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20623) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20623)

## 109. From Joint Risk to Decision Invariance: Decision-Preserving Regularization for Exemplar-Free Class-Incremental Learning

- **作者**：Qi Zhu, Ziang Gan, Wanting Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Exemplar-free continual learning seeks to minimize joint risk over all seen tasks, yet historical data are unavailable for direct optimization. We observe that, for classification, preserving the model's decisions on previously learned classes helps retain old knowledge. This motivates Decision-Preserving Regularization (DPR). Penalizing changes in all pairwise old-class logit margins reduces algebraically to a centered relative-logit penalty. We further apply spectral whitening to remove the classifier's spectral scale bias. Because the global classifier supplies the decision constraints for the next stage, it must remain calibrated across all seen classes despite the drift of stored old-class statistics during representation updates. We therefore fit a closed-form affine map from paired current-task features to transport old-class Gaussian means and covariances into the updated feature space, then calibrate the global classifier using these distributions and current-task features. Across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-Subset, and ImageNet-1K under cold-start protocols, comparisons with state-of-the-art methods show that DPR achieves higher accuracy in most settings while remaining highly efficient. Code is available at https://github.com/bnufw/dpr.

## 110. SCoRe: Support-Calibrated Role Fusion for Training-Free Cross-Domain Few-Shot Segmentation

- **作者**：Abhishek Kathpal, Adarsh Jagan Sathyamoorthy, Utsav Patel
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present SCoRe (Support-Calibrated Role fusion), a framework to utilize two complementary vision foundation model-based (VFM) segmenters for training-free cross-domain few-shot segmentation (CD-FSS). Our approach is based on the observation that current VFMs for CD-FSS possess capabilities where one model locates the correct object, while another model computes the better contour. Our approach uses: 1. a method to identify a VFM segmenter to lead Few-Shot Segmentation (FSS) task for a given concept, 2. a novel deterministic boundary correction method using bands of near- and far-pixels at the lead segmenter's predicted boundary of an object, and 3. a region correction mechanism for the other segmenter to conservatively prune components, fill holes, and bridge short gaps on the lead segmenter's predicted masks to improve accuracy. We evaluate SCoRe using RSRM and FSS-SAM3 across 3,314 matched five-shot episodes from 10 datasets. SCoRe achieves 72.738% foreground mIoU averaged across datasets, a gain of 5.210 percentage points over RSRM (paired 95% CI [4.618, 5.972]), with per-dataset gains up to 8.461 points over the strongest standalone method and the best point estimate on 7 of 10 datasets.

## 111. PerturbJEPA: Joint-Embedding Predictive Learning of Cellular Perturbation Responses

- **作者**：Jiangshuan Pang, Youzhe He, Yiting Deng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, 6 figures, 14 tables. Code: https://github.com/BGI-Hangzhou-OmicsAI/PerturbJEPA

Identifying the drug and dose that produced a cellular response requires preserving treatment effects within each cellular background. A joint-embedding predictor can match its targets while ignoring the intervention if its encoder preserves differences across backgrounds but erases differences between treated and control populations within each one. We call this failure interventional contrast collapse. PerturbJEPA encodes the difference in mean gene expression between treated and control populations as an effect vector. It aligns the displacement between their latent centroids with this vector and regularizes the vectors to preserve variation across population pairs. We show that the measured displacements cannot all coincide within a batch when effect-vector variation exceeds the root-mean-square alignment error. Trained with separate drug and dose ranking objectives, the model predicts candidate responses from a common control population and matches them to the observed response without cell pairing or expression reconstruction. We evaluate 3,592 Tahoe-100M queries on held-out combinations of known interventions and cellular backgrounds, each with over 700 drug-dose candidates in a fixed, background-specific catalog. PerturbJEPA achieves 91.46% macro joint drug-dose Recall@1, exceeding STATE by 3.40 percentage points (95% drug-cluster bootstrap interval of 1.72 to 5.07 points). Controlled diagnostics show that treatment-control differences can shrink even when representations remain globally dispersed. With the drug ranking objective fixed, adding effect-vector regularization to alignment raises joint Recall@1 from 18.57% to 87.47%.

## 112. OrthoMark: Place Recognition with Point-Anchored Orthographic Height Mark

- **作者**：Haichuan Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

LiDAR place recognition provides the loop closures of Simultaneous Localization and Mapping(SLAM). Perspective from Z-axis, a gravity-aligned scan is an orthographic map of the scene, and a revisit from another position and heading moves this map rigidly in the plane, a motion that a polar Fourier spectrum ignores and phase correlation recovers. Along the vertical, the heights of the returns describe the structures, but which heights are observed depends on the range, because the sensor's vertical field of view cuts nearby structures low; height thus misleads a global key, yet is decisive once two scans are aligned and compared cell by cell where they overlap. We present \method, a training-free method built on one principle: two LiDAR scans can only be compared through features computed from the same geometric surfaces in both scans. \method retrieves candidates with a translation- and rotation-invariant polar spectrum of the bird's-eye view (BEV), 1.9,KB per keyframe, and verifies each candidate by estimating its planar pose through phase correlation and scoring it with \emph{point-anchored height columns}: every BEV cell keeps a 16-bit mask of the heights of its own returns; within a session, the score is weighted by the expected overlap of the two scans' sensing ranges, computed from their distance. We show that this vertical link pays off in verification but hurts in retrieval, where what a height slice contains depends on the range to the structure. For single robot, \method has the highest AP of the training-free methods without 3D registration on the KITTI development sequences; across robots, on all multi-robot sequences, it matches or exceeds RING++ at 1/350 of its descriptor size and closes more inter-robot loops than the learned BEVPlace++ on all tests.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20570) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20570)

## 113. Vision-Language Grounded Semantic Navigation for Autonomous Mobile Robots

- **作者**：Ravindran Vasoor Saravanan, Chittin Varunav Ravi, Madhumita Singh et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：7 pages, 4 figures

Language-guided navigation enables mobile robots to interpret natural language commands and navigate toward user-specified targets in unstructured environments. However, integrating open-vocabulary visual perception with classical robotics pipelines for mapping and navigation remains a challenging problem. In this work, we present a complete ROS2-based system that combines frontier-based exploration, open-vocabulary object detection using Grounding DINO, depth-based 3D localization, and semantic mapping to enable end-to-end vision-language grounded navigation in simulation. The system accepts natural language commands such as ``go to the yellow box,'' detects candidate objects from RGB images using text prompts, estimates their positions in the world frame, and generates navigation goals executed by the Nav2 stack. In a Gazebo-based indoor environment with a TurtleBot3 robot, the system autonomously explored the room, detected three of the five target objects, and successfully executed both natural language navigation commands tested, without task-specific training. The results indicate that open-vocabulary grounding models can be integrated into classical navigation pipelines, though performance is limited by detection uncertainty, depth-based localization error, and incomplete exploration coverage. These findings highlight both the feasibility and current limitations of deploying vision-language grounded navigation systems using lightweight, CPU-only architectures.

## 114. AsymEEF: Bimanual Manipulation with Asymmetric End Effectors

- **作者**：Xiaokang Sun, Ningyue Wang, Kunqi Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG

Teleoperated demonstrations are costly to collect and rarely reusable across end-effectors with different kinematics and action spaces. This burden grows in bimanual manipulation, where each end-effector pair may require new data. We present AsymEEF, a framework for executable cross-end-effector demonstration generation and unified bimanual policy learning. Given a small source dataset, AsymEEF transfers demonstrations to heterogeneous combinations of two-, three-, and five-fingered end-effectors without additional teleoperation. Kinematic teacher anchors provide geometric supervision, while sparse real-world X-Calib anchors correct discrepancies in grasp execution. A shared encoder maps wrist poses and actuator states to a morphology-agnostic latent space, and a morphology-conditioned decoder reconstructs target commands. Generated trajectories undergo replay filtering for task-level executability. The representation also supports a unified bimanual visuomotor policy trained across multiple pairs. Across four simulated and six real-world tasks, AsymEEF improves replay success over adapted transfer baselines, achieves a mean replay-valid expansion gain of 16.6 times in simulation, and is evaluated through downstream policy training on real robots. Project website: https://asymeef.vercel.app/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20557) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20557)

## 115. Learning a Task-Adaptive Low-Dimensional Semantic Space for Improved Visual Classification

- **作者**：Nikalal Kaldera, Wanqing Li, Chris Bunn et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 2 figures. Accepted at NeurIPS 2026

Semantic visual classification commonly aligns visual embeddings with semantic spaces induced by pretrained text encoders. However, such spaces are typically high-dimensional, weakly aligned with visual feature geometry, and insufficiently discriminative for downstream classification tasks. We propose TaSS, a framework for learning a task-adaptive low-dimensional semantic space that preserves semantic structure while explicitly optimizing class separability. TaSS is constructed through a parameterized geometry transformation of pretrained text embeddings that enlarges inter-class margins via distance reshaping and improves intra-class compactness through prototype-preserving semantic constraints. The resulting semantic space maintains meaningful semantic relationships while producing a discriminative geometry tailored for classification. Frozen visual encoder features are subsequently aligned to TaSS using a lightweight MLP, enabling improved discriminative learning without fine-tuning pretrained backbones. We further provide theoretical analysis showing that TaSS preserves semantic consistency while enforcing enhanced inter-class separation and controlled intra-class variance. Extensive experiments on skeleton-based human action recognition, video classification, and image classification benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over existing semantic alignment approaches, with gains exceeding 3 percentage points on average and up to 8.5 points on challenging datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20553) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20553)

## 116. A Gaussian Primitive Data Management Framework for Efficient 3DGS Trajectory Rendering

- **作者**：Haolun Ma, Ziyi Liu, Zizhuo Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.DB, cs.CV, cs.GR

Trajectory rendering, which renders novel views of a reconstructed 3D scene along a camera path, is a core operation in digital twins and autonomous inspection. Structured 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) renders a single view in real time by decoding sparse anchors into Gaussians on demand, but it renders a trajectory view by view: every view projects all anchors to select them, re-decodes anchors already requested by neighboring views, and runs all steps on the GPU while the CPU idles. We treat this as a spatial data management problem, where anchors are spatial objects, views are range queries, and decoded Gaussians are shared derived data, and propose GDM-GS, which removes redundancy at three levels. Within a view, an anchor index and an occluder index answer each view as a holed-frustum range query, so out-of-view and provably hidden anchors are never decoded. Across views, shared materialization decodes each requested anchor once per group of consecutive views while preserving each view's selection. Across processors, a hybrid pipeline balances selections between the CPU and the GPU before grouped decoding under bounded memory. Experiments on large-scale public scenes show that GDM-GS substantially reduces trajectory rendering latency while preserving image quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20546) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20546)

## 117. SmoothShift: Disrupting Visual Adversarial Attacks on LVLMs at Test Time

- **作者**：Xiaoqing Chen, Jiayi Ouyang, Dongrui Wu
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Visual adversarial perturbations can substantially impair large vision-language models (LVLMs) despite introducing only subtle input changes. To understand how these small changes cause substantial errors, we investigate their optimization and propagation through the model, revealing two key findings. First, stronger attacks rely on finer iterative optimization, while subtle input perturbations induce representation discrepancies that are amplified layer by layer through the vision encoder and persist in the language model. Second, disrupting the alignment between within-patch input patterns and the model's computation reduces clean-adversarial representation discrepancies and substantially weakens attacks. Together, these findings suggest that adversarial effectiveness depends on both fine-grained perturbation patterns and their coupling with the model's computation. Motivated by these findings, we propose SmoothShift, a lightweight, training-free defense that combines local smoothing and spatial transformations. Smoothing weakens fine-grained perturbation patterns, while spatial transformations disrupt their alignment with the encoder's patch grid. SmoothShift requires no model updates, auxiliary networks, or additional model inference. Experiments cover three LVLM families on four datasets and robustly fine-tuned Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) models on eight classification datasets. Evaluations across eight attack methods and multiple perturbation budgets demonstrate improved robustness while largely preserving clean performance. SmoothShift also provides robustness gains under defense-aware attacks using mixed-gradient BPDA with EOT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20537) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20537)

## 118. AFRNet: Adaptive Frequency Redistribution Network for Image Deraining

- **作者**：Dezhi Zhang, Shangrong Yang, Husheng Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 8 figures

Image deraining aims to recover rain-free images from rain-degraded observations. Recent frequency-aware deraining methods have demonstrated promising performance by removing rain artifacts in the spectral domain. However, since rain artifacts and background textures often appear in several correlated subbands, prior frequency-domain deraining models still enhance frequency subbands separately. Such band-wise processing limits information exchange across subbands. Without context from other subbands, rain and clean content that appear together in one subband cannot be separated. This leads to either residual rain streaks or over-smoothing of fine details. To address this limitation, we propose AFRNet, an Adaptive Frequency Redistribution Network for image deraining. First, we introduce an Adaptive Frequency Recombination Module (AFRM) that performs cross-band modulation among wavelet subbands, enabling information interaction and adaptive recombination of degraded frequency components. We then develop a Subband Compensation Block (SCB) that compensates for decoder subband degradation by leveraging encoder subband features through inter-subband attention in the wavelet domain, alleviating detail loss during reconstruction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AFRNet achieves competitive performance and consistent results across diverse deraining benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20522) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20522)

## 119. What the Averages Hide: Monocular Metric Depth Models Still Fail in Everyday Scenes, Mostly on Scale

- **作者**：Hualie Jiang, Yujie Luo, Ziyang Song et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：https://antigravity-tech.github.io/DIMED/

Zero-shot monocular metric depth models are evaluated by averaging metric and scale-aligned scores across datasets. Averages cannot show how many images still have severely wrong metric depth, whether depth is under- or overestimated, or whether different models fail on the same images. We propose an evaluation built on per-image results: the failure rate (metric $δ_1$ < 0.5); scale-aligned metrics for structure, and the bias, dispersion and absolute error of the alignment scale for scale; and a cross-model comparison that separates shared from model-specific failures. We apply it to 11 recent architectures with and without ground-truth intrinsics (18 model evaluations) on the 7 everyday-scene benchmarks (4,406 images) used by MoGe-2. Metric depth estimation is not solved in everyday scenes, and the remaining difficulty is mainly scale: the best of the 18 evaluations still has a failure rate of 7.7% (datasets weighted equally); pooled over all failures, 78.8% have intact structure, and after scale correction 98.3% no longer fail. Images failed by at least 6 architectures make up 7.3% of the data, concentrated on HAMMER and DIODE; even when selected only by architectures of other families, they hold 34% to 40% of the error of the top 6 models, against 14% to 18% for as many random images from the same datasets. Adding objects reduces the scale error of most models; ground-truth intrinsics and, in most families, larger models reduce the failures but do not remove them. We recommend reporting failure rates, structure and scale separately, and results stratified by how many models fail. We will release the code, the per-image results with shared-failure labels, and two diagnostic sets built from TUM RGB-D.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20518) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20518)

## 120. VoxSAOR: Semantic-Aware Occupancy Robustification via Aleatoric Uncertainty Modeling

- **作者**：Mengfei Duan, Yuheng Zhang, Guoqiang Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO, eess.IV
- **备注**：The source code will be made publicly available at https://github.com/MengfeiD/VoxSAOR

Vision-based 3D occupancy prediction provides a dense semantic representation of scenes for autonomous driving and embodied perception, yet remains vulnerable to occlusion, depth ambiguity, and projection errors that induce spatially varying reliability in voxel features. Existing methods typically map voxel representations to semantic predictions without explicitly modeling such observation-induced feature variations. To address this limitation, we propose VoxSAOR, a semantic-aware occupancy robustification framework that models aleatoric uncertainty through feature-space proxies. It first quantifies Local Semantic Consistency (LSC) to derive a voxel-wise structural stability prior and uses cross-view semantic consensus to filter unreliable semantic correspondences. Within each semantic category, spatially matched stable and ambiguous voxels form stable-to-ambiguous feature transitions, which are modeled by a class-conditional diagonal Gaussian distribution, capturing both feature displacement and dispersion. Guided by these proxy distributions, Aleatoric Regime Ensemble Learning (AREL) trains independently parameterized prediction heads using feature perturbations at progressively increasing scales, with perturbation strength modulated by structural reliability. At inference, proxy modeling and sampling are disabled, and all heads process the same observed features; their output probabilities are then averaged to produce a deterministic consensus prediction without requiring sampling. Experiments across multiple datasets and occupancy backbones demonstrate the effectiveness of VoxSAOR in improving semantic occupancy prediction, long-tail recognition, and predictive calibration. The source code will be made publicly available at https://github.com/MengfeiD/VoxSAOR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20507) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20507)

## 121. How Reliable Is Visual Token Selection? Rethinking Pruning in Multimodal LLMs

- **作者**：Chaehee Song, Seokil Ham, KiHyun Seol et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to Findings of the Association for Computational Linguistics: EACL 2027

Visual token pruning improves the efficiency of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by removing redundant visual tokens, yet the reliability of existing selection signals has not been systematically examined. We analyze token selection under semantics-preserving perturbations and across representative pruning criteria, revealing two limitations. First, attention-based importance shifts non-uniformly under perturbations, so magnitude alone cannot distinguish stable evidence from unstable saliency (local instability). Second, attention- and diversity-based methods select substantially different subsets despite comparable performance, showing that useful evidence is not confined to a unique subset (global inconsistency). These findings motivate reliable token-set construction. We instantiate this perspective in CoRePruner, a training-free framework combining instruction-aware diversity with reliability-aware attention. Across image benchmarks, multiple MLLM architectures, and token budgets, CoRePruner improves performance, especially under aggressive pruning, and remains competitive on video benchmarks. Our results establish reliability as a central consideration in visual token selection.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20505) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20505)

## 122. Pointillist Maize: 3D Reconstruction of Field-Grown Maize Using RGB UAV Imagery

- **作者**：Dewi Endah Kharismawati, Toni Kazic
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：26 pages, 8 figures, submitted to Frontiers in Plant Science

Three-dimensional reconstruction can extend field phenotyping to individual maize organs, but thin, flexible, occluding structures and plant movement complicate reconstruction. We evaluated whether consumer-grade UAV RGB video processed with Structure from Motion and multi-view stereo could recover recognizable field-grown maize structures, and examined the effects of acquisition trajectories and frame sampling. COLMAP reconstructions preserved recognizable leaves, stems, tassel branches, ears, silks, and brace roots. For an equal-frame comparison, we reconstructed one plant from two sets of 295 frames from the same video: a single orbit sampled at 5 frames/second and three orbits at different altitudes (ribbon skirt) sampled at 2 frames/second. After ground removal, the reconstructions contained 520,601 and 375,318 points, respectively. The single orbit yielded greater point concentration within plant structures and fewer visible gaps, while the ribbon skirt yielded fewer scattered points and slightly lower proportions of isolated points. In densely planted nursery rows, visible voids occupied 17.57% of the rendered scene with one orbit at 1 frame/second, decreasing to 5.07% with additional serpentine passes and 2.68% when the original orbit was sampled at 3 frames/second. Additional observations also increased point dispersion associated with plant movement. A curlicue trajectory tested in a different field in 2024 produced distinguishable individual plants without visible duplicated structures, although substantial voids remained, mainly on the soil surface. These results demonstrate the feasibility of recovering recognizable maize organs from consumer UAV imagery and reveal trade-offs between acquisition strategies, reconstruction completeness, and point dispersion. Independent geometric validation is still needed before quantitative phenotyping.

## 123. Beyond Seeing: Fine-Grained Preference Validation and Severity-Aware Alignment for Mitigating Visual Hallucinations in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Abdulaziz Alqahtani, Alwaleed Alharthi, Muzammil Behzad
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：9 pages, 2 figures, 6 tables. Includes a 4-page supplementary appendix with experimental details, equations, prompt templates, and additional comparisons

Large vision-language models can produce fluent responses containing objects, attributes, or relations unsupported by the image. We study an implemented alignment pipeline that normalizes released fine-grained hallucination supervision, validates chosen-rejected preference pairs with vision-language judges, repairs rejected chosen responses, and trains LLaVA adapters with standard or severity-aware direct preference optimization. The study uses 16,143 normalized feedback records and 8,386 released preference pairs, comparing direct training with zero-shot and two-shot judged severity-margin variants. The zero-shot variant reduces Object HalBench CHAIRs from 53.00 to 36.00 and CHAIRi from 15.72 to 10.41. Across aligned models, AMBER-Hal decreases from 35.90 to 25.10-26.90, accompanied by reduced visual coverage. In contrast, the base model retains the highest valid POPE adversarial F1, 84.18 versus 83.87-83.91 after alignment. These results support benchmark-dependent reductions in generative hallucination rather than uniform superiority of severity-aware alignment. We distinguish implemented pipeline components from the preference paths used in the reported experiments and discuss the limits of attributing gains to judging, repair, or the training objective individually.

## 124. Improving X-Ray Baggage Instance Segmentation with Shared Point-Based Mask Refinement

- **作者**：Abdulaziz Alqahtani, Qasim Umer
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：12 pages, 8 figures, 11 tables

Overlapping projections and complex object contours make prohibited items difficult to delineate in X-ray baggage images. This study investigates shared point-based mask refinement to improve instance segmentation within Cascade Mask R-CNN. The framework combines a ResNet-101 backbone with an attention-based feature pyramid and progressively updates uncertain mask locations, increasing mask resolution from $28 \times 28$ to $112 \times 112$. A single refinement module is shared across cascade stages and refinement levels, with transformer and multilayer perceptron (MLP) variants used to examine the role of interactions among selected points. Ten configurations were evaluated on the official PIXray split across three training seeds using the original target-instance annotations for evaluation. The transformer and MLP refiners achieved mean mask average precision (AP) of 71.79 and 72.47, respectively, compared with 70.50 for the unrefined detector, yielding gains of 1.29 and 1.97 AP points. The MLP achieved the highest mean mask AP among the evaluated configurations. Both variants also improved boundary agreement on commonly detected objects, supporting improved mask delineation, while detection diagnostics did not establish a corresponding positive gain. These findings demonstrate that shared point-based refinement can improve segmentation quality in the tested cascade architecture using existing target-mask annotations, without additional occluder supervision. Evaluation on independent X-ray datasets is needed to determine how well these improvements generalize.

## 125. LiveView: Real-Time Camera-Controlled Re-filming of Streaming Videos

- **作者**：Youcan Xu, Xinjie Huang, Jiaxin Shi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Real-time camera-controlled video re-filming aims to synthesize target views from a streaming source video under online user-specified camera trajectories. This task poses two key challenges: maintaining synchronization and content consistency across views, and achieving low-latency rendering with long-horizon stability. Existing approaches are predominantly designed for offline full-sequence processing and rely on bidirectional attention or costly depth-based geometric preprocessing, making them unsuitable for low-latency streaming scenarios. To address these limitations, we develop a teacher-to-student framework that distills a high-fidelity bidirectional re-filming teacher into a few-step causal autoregressive student. We introduce Cross-Frame Injection to interleave each incoming source frame with its synchronized target latent, thereby establishing direct cross-view correspondence and improving content consistency. To ensure stable long-horizon generation, we further propose Shared Relative RoPE, which bounds temporal indices within the pretrained range, and Cycle Streaming Tuning, which exposes the student to extended cyclic trajectories during streaming training. These designs enable low-latency online video re-filming with precise camera control and sustained spatiotemporal consistency over long streaming rollouts.

## 126. VISTA: A Framework for Vision-Informed Semantic Traffic Awareness

- **作者**：Murat Arda Onsu, Poonam Lohan, Burak Kantarci et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.NI
- **备注**：8 pages, 4 figures, Accepted for publication in IEEE Internet of Things Magazine, 2026

The Internet of Things (IoT) is transforming intelligent transportation systems by connecting roadside cameras, edge computing units, vehicles, and traffic infrastructure into a shared sensing and decision-making ecosystem. However, transmitting raw traffic video over vehicle-to-everything (V2X) links is bandwidth-intensive, while deploying large multimodal models directly on resource-constrained vehicles is often impractical. To address these limitations, this article proposes VISTA (Vision-Informed Semantic Traffic Awareness), a multimodal vision-language edge-intelligence framework that combines predictive video representation learning with lightweight language-based reasoning to provide communication-efficient traffic situational awareness. In the proposed architecture, roadside surveillance units employ the Video Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (V-JEPA) and an attentive-pooler module to convert traffic video clips into compact future-scene embeddings. These embeddings are transmitted through V2X links to vehicle-mounted systems, where a TinyLlama small language model, enhanced with a custom visual adapter, interprets the embeddings alongside natural-language (NL) traffic queries. VISTA enables vehicles to obtain interpretable traffic information, reason about upcoming scene dynamics, and identify potential collision risks without receiving raw video streams. Experimental results in a digital-twin urban traffic environment show that VISTA achieves 97.77% accuracy and an 89% F1 score for future collision prediction while reducing the communication payload by several orders of magnitude compared with raw RGB video transmission. These results demonstrate the feasibility of embedding-based multimodal intelligence for low-latency, communication-efficient, and safety-oriented IoT-enabled transportation systems.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20458) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20458)

## 127. LEDGE: Separating Critic and Editor Failures in Text-to-Image Self-Correction

- **作者**：Vyom Agarwal, Mokshda Gangrade, Samet Ayhan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Text-to-image (T2I) self-correction combines a vision-language model (VLM) critic that diagnoses errors and proposes instructions with an image editor that executes them. End-to-end evaluation obscures whether failures arise from incorrect diagnoses or an editor unable to execute valid instructions. We introduce LEDGE (Learning Error Diagnosis in Generation and Editing), a framework that separates these bottlenecks. LEDGE compiles oracle edit instructions from GenEval while holding the generated images and the editor fixed, isolating the editor's correction capability from the critic's diagnostic accuracy. On Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large, oracle instructions raise GenEval from 72.0% to 81.2%, versus 74.1% for the baseline VLM critic. Yet 435 of 644 failed images remain unrepaired, exposing a substantial editor gap. We also use oracle labels from disjoint training prompts to supervise the critic. Oracle supervision raises exact diagnosis from 33.3% (the same VLM with our structured output, untrained) to 70.8%. With the same editor, the resulting critic improves GenEval from 42.2% (uncorrected) to 59.4% on Stable Diffusion 1.5, against 50.9% for the baseline critic, and from 72.0% to 76.0% on Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large, despite training only on Stable Diffusion 1.5. Improvements transfer to unseen object classes, a second editor, and independent T2I-CompBench judges and prompts. A small human evaluation confirms the ranking in estimated net corrections but suggests a smaller gain than detector-based evaluation. LEDGE thus distinguishes two limits of self-correction: knowing what to fix and being able to fix it.

## 128. ReScene: Incremental Scene Chronology from Sparse Revisits

- **作者**：Wei Qin Chuah, Zeji Hui, Amirali Khodadadian Gostar et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages

Scene chronology aims to reconstruct a scene across visits separated by hours or days, during which objects may change in position, presence or shape. Such reconstruction must retain every earlier state, yet each revisit often provides only a few images. Storing a complete model for each visit duplicates the unchanged scene, whereas updating a single shared model alters earlier states. We present ReScene, which stores each visit as an immutable compact update, so that any earlier visit can be recovered exactly by applying the stored updates in order, without retaining past images. To localise each update, ReScene attributes the disagreement between the preceding reconstruction and the new images to individual Gaussians through their compositing weights. This attribution guides the suppression of outdated Gaussians and the refinement of persistent ones, while newly visible surfaces are initialised from learned multi-view depth. We also introduce Multi-State, a benchmark of non-rigid deformation and partial material removal, including synthetic scenes with ground-truth geometry. ReScene achieves quality higher than sequential 3DGS checkpointing with 2.5--4.8$\times$ less uncompressed history storage. At comparable PSNR, it also reduces foreground depth error by 3.2$\times$ relative to sequential ChronoGS, showing that accurate rendering does not necessarily imply accurate geometry. Code and benchmark are available at https://waychin-weiqin.github.io/ReScene/.

## 129. UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge 2026: Site-Calibrated Synthetic Data and Multi-Detector Fusion for Roadside 3D Detection

- **作者**：Arnav Kumar, Brijesh Soni
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.SP

This report summarizes the work submitted by team arnav007 (submission ID 1054222) for the UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge (V2X-Real track) at the IEEE BigData Cup 2026. The primary objective is to train 3D detectors for vehicles, pedestrians, and trucks using only synthetic roadside LiDAR data, and to generate synthetic scans that are realistic enough to be indistinguishable from real ones. A CARLA based pipeline is developed and calibrated to the fixed sensor site, accurately modeling the range image, including no-return beams, based on unlabelled real development scans. A combined inference detector, using multiple 3D detectors (VoxelNeXt, PV-RCNN, and SECOND) trained exclusively on synthetic data, is also proposed. Each scan is aligned to the synthetic ground plane and processed through the detectors at multiple ground heights. The resulting bounding boxes are fused using bird's-eye view (BEV) clustering, followed by class-specific post-processing to refine heading, box size, and filter low-confidence detections. The final submission achieved a combined score of 0.8947. The ablation study shows that per-scan ground leveling more than doubled the real-scan mAP, while fusing boxes across different heights and models further improved performance.

## 130. InterRSGaussian: Interactive Multi-view Instance Segmentation for 3D Remote Sensing Scenes

- **作者**：Zipeng Qi, Zhengxia Zou, Zhenwei Shi
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Remote sensing image segmentation plays a critical role in various applications. Interactive instance segmentation, a specialized task within image segmentation, aims to generate precise segmentation results for target objects based on user input prompts. However, previous studies on interactive instance segmentation primarily focus on single-view images, limiting their ability to comprehensively understand complex 3D remote sensing scenes. In this study, we extend interactive instance segmentation from a single-view to a multi-view framework, which can also be easily converted to a 3D segmentation format, thus achieving more accurate and comprehensive 3D remote sensing scene perception across multiple views. First, we construct a multi-view interactive instance segmentation dataset for remote sensing, comprising five distinct scenes, each captured from about one hundred different views. Second, we propose InterRSGaussian, a novel instance segmentation method based on 3D Gaussians. InterRSGaussian enables users to provide point clicks on target objects from any arbitrary view and generates view-consistent instance segmentation results across all input views without requiring manual annotations. The resulting multi-view segmentation can further be projected onto 3D point clouds for 3D segmentation. To improve training efficiency and segmentation performance, we introduce a prior distillation network for initialization. Overall, this study extends traditional 2D remote sensing scene perception into a 3D multi-view framework, enabling a more comprehensive understanding of target objects in complex environments with user interactivity.

## 131. Training-Free Anonymization of Driving Data with Promptable Segmentation and 3D Human Mesh Recovery

- **作者**：Max A. Buettner, Mark Azer, Fabian B. Flohr
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables

Camera recordings for autonomous driving contain faces and license plates that should be anonymized before labeling, sharing, or training. Dedicated detectors can miss small plates and faces behind vehicle glass. We present FM-Anonymize, a training-free anonymization pipeline that combines promptable segmentation, 3D human mesh recovery, and generative replacement. SAM 3 locates people, faces, and vehicles and searches zoomed vehicle crops for plates, occupants, and windows. SAM 3D Body assesses face visibility from head orientation and projects head regions for blurring. Optional diffusion inpainting replaces eligible blurred heads with synthetic faces, guided by estimated pose and keypoints. On PP4AV, the blur pipeline protects 97.8% of faces and 95.5% of plates, the highest privacy recall of all compared methods at their default settings. On our recordings, where the localization thresholds were set, it protects 97.1% of faces and 93.4% of plates, compared with 93.7%/73.0% for UrbanAnonymizer, 42.7%/40.7% for EgoBlur, and 96.6% of faces for deface. Synthetic replacement raises full-body keypoint average precision over blurring from 54.1% to 79.6% on our recordings and from 39.9% to 68.3% on PP4AV. Code and configurations are available on https://github.com/max-a-ai/FM-Anonymize

## 132. DominoBench: Evaluating Multi-Step Physical Reasoning from Still Images

- **作者**：Emilio Ferrara
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：DominoBench dataset, evaluation harnesses, and benchmarks available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/emilioferrara/DominoBench

Can a vision-language model look at a picture of a chain reaction and tell what will happen? Recent multimodal models describe scenes fluently and solve textbook physics problems, which suggests they should also predict how a scene of ramps, ledges, pendulums, seesaws and dominoes plays out once a ball is pushed. We tested this claim on 30 models from 10 families and reached a clear conclusion: unaided, no model predicts the outcome reliably. We built DominoBench, a benchmark of still images of 2D rigid-body scenes whose outcome a physics simulator computes. It holds 854 items in 427 minimal pairs, in which a change of one to three grid steps in one quantity decides whether one person survives, and every item comes with a four-rung hint ladder ending in the exact calculation that decides the outcome. Unaided, frontier models from Anthropic, Google and OpenAI are right about the person whose fate flips in at most 72% of items and answer both twins of a pair correctly in at most 34%. Pointing at the deciding step helps little, and exact geometry given as text barely helps, yet once the deciding calculation and the quantities it needs, including speeds that a still image cannot show, are written out, the same models reach 82-92% on the deciding person, while small open-weight models stay near chance. Physical prediction from a still image therefore fails at the quantitative step: the strongest models use the deciding quantity when they are given it, but do not produce it on their own. For models that plan or act in the physical world, a fluent account of a scene is thus no evidence of a correct prediction: such models must find and compute the deciding quantity unprompted, and DominoBench tests whether they do.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20385) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20385)

## 133. Signs of Wear: Evaluating Intelligent Speed Assistance Under Synthetic Adverse Conditions

- **作者**：Johannes Fabian Hahn, Mao Shan, Julie Stephany Berrio Pere
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV

Intelligent Speed Assistance (ISA) systems rely on cameras to recognize speed-limit signs, yet existing benchmarks underrepresent the weathered, dirty, and overgrown signs commonly encountered on rural roads. We use generative image editing to introduce different forms of wear into real street-level images of speed-limit signs. The resulting ISA-Wear dataset contains paired original and edited images. Guided by text instructions, a multimodal image-editing model adds snow, rust, vegetation, faded paint, or mud to the speed-limit sign in a street-level frame at three severity levels. Where appropriate, the surrounding scene is adapted as well, and the viewpoint is kept. Every edited frame has an unedited counterpart, and we only evaluate frames whose original is recognized correctly, so any failure can be attributed to the wear. Since the model alters the sign's geometry in 21\% of the edits, all edits are filtered by a geometric check and a manual review. Severity is measured as the LPIPS distance between the original and the edited sign plate. On 716 worn frames, we find that in detect-then-read pipelines, wear breaks detection before it breaks reading. The detector finds only 62\% of the worn signs, even though it finds every one of them in the original frames. Missed detections account for 78--87\% of failed reads, a pattern that a zero-shot detector shares. Given the annotated sign box, as in the crop-based benchmarks, a classifier still reads 78\% of the worn signs, but only 56\% when it has to rely on the detector
