## 134. Trait Collapse in Text-to-Image Generation: Evaluating and Mitigating Personality Inconsistency in Visual Storytelling

- **作者**：Yuanbo Tong, Haoyuan Li, Jibiao Jiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：EMNLP 2026 Main Conference Paper. 26 pages, 5 figures

In visual storytelling, a believable character must not only look consistent across scenes but also \textit{behave} in ways that reflect their personality. Current text-to-image (T2I) models preserve visual appearance effectively, yet consistently fail to maintain personality-relevant behavior---a failure we term \textit{trait collapse}. We show that conventional metrics (CLIP Score, DINO similarity) are blind to this failure, and that abstract personality descriptors and scene-level emotion labels do not produce consistent improvements over neutral prompts across models. To systematically diagnose trait collapse, we introduce \textbf{ViStoryBench-Personality}, a story-level benchmark combining an expert-validated Visual Cues Lexicon (Fleiss' $κ= 0.757$) with a six-dimensional metric suite covering personality alignment, cross-scene stability, and behavioral coherence. Evaluating seven T2I systems, we confirm a persistent semantic gap between abstract trait labels and visual evidence, and find that visual identity consistency does not imply personality consistency. To bridge this gap, we propose \textbf{Trait-Behavior-Visual (TBV)}, a training-free prompting framework that decomposes personality traits into renderable visual constraints. TBV improves Personality Alignment Score by up to $+22.1\%$, with larger gains in models with stronger language-conditioning capacity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20381) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20381)

## 135. VisDrone-ReID: A Unified Multi-Category Object Re-Identification Benchmark

- **作者**：Asmau Lawal Bilbis, Nathanael L. Baisa, Amudhavel Jayavel
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Object re-identification (Re-ID) benchmarks remain predominantly confined to single-category evaluations, limiting the development of unified systems capable of operating across diverse object types. This paper presents VisDrone-ReID, a unified multi-category Re-ID benchmark derived from the VisDrone2019-MOT dataset through a principled conversion pipeline. The pipeline resolves structural incompatibilities between multi-object tracking annotations and Re-ID evaluation requirements, addressing sequence-scoped identity collision, temporal redundancy, and the absence of a principled identity-level train/test split. The resulting benchmark comprises 9,516 identities and 150,016 images across ten object categories - pedestrian, people, bicycle, car, van, truck, tricycle, awning-tricycle, bus, and motor, sourced from 80 unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) sequences captured across urban environments. A leave-one-out (LOO) evaluation protocol is adopted to provide a comprehensive assessment. Baseline experiments across four representative Re-ID models spanning CNN and transformer architectures establish the first systematic performance reference on this benchmark. Results reveal a consistent Rank-1 to mAP gap across all models, suggesting that VisDrone-ReID presents a genuine retrieval challenge beyond nearest-neighbour matching. Per-category analysis identifies pedestrian as the most challenging category and reveals that vision-language pretraining provides a measurable advantage on semantically distinctive categories. VisDrone-ReID provides a consistent platform for studying multi-category object re-identification under the scale variation, viewpoint challenges, and category diversity characteristic of real-world surveillance scenarios.

## 136. Structure-Guided Multi-Granularity Spatiotemporal Transformer for Efficient Monocular 3D Human Pose Estimation

- **作者**：Sheng Xu, Aoduo Li, Rongli He
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Existing multi-granularity methods for monocular 3D human pose estimation emphasize spatial joint features while underusing skeletal structure, including global connectivity and temporal continuity. Moreover, modeling entire frame sequences incurs high computational costs. We propose a Structure-Aware Multi-Granularity Spatiotemporal Transformer that integrates structural priors across spatial and temporal representations for accurate and efficient pose estimation. A three-level feature extractor captures human topology at the joint, bone, and limb levels. Gated Adaptive Spatial Attention (GASA) embeds structural constraints into spatial attention, while Multi-Granularity Feature Enhancement (MGFE) uses hierarchical pooling and fusion to strengthen local details and global semantics. Temporal Segment Fusion (TSF) aligns temporal contexts across granularities to capture both short- and long-range motion patterns. Hierarchical Cross-Attention (HCA) promotes spatiotemporal interaction between granularities, and Keyframe Attention (KFA) selects informative motion segments to reduce redundancy and computational cost. Experiments demonstrate improved accuracy and efficiency over conventional Transformer-based approaches, highlighting the effectiveness of structure-aware multi-granularity modeling for monocular 3D human pose estimation.

## 137. BlockAlign: Preference Optimization for Trajectory-Distilled Video Generation

- **作者**：Jingyuan Zhu, Biaolong Chen, Yangyang Qin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Directly aligning trajectory-distilled video generators slashes rollout costs and optimizes the deployed few-step model without teacher fine-tuning or re-distillation. However, conventional flow-matching preference objectives assume single-head local velocity supervision, mismatching the finite-interval, block-average transition interface of trajectory-distilled generators such as Parallel Decoding Distillation (PDD) and Parametric Trajectory Distillation (PTD). Naively supervising only the primary initial-subinterval velocity sub-head rather than the fused block-average velocity at block starts (DirectAlign) induces a severe training--inference mismatch that degrades quality below the unaligned baseline. We introduce BlockAlign, an interface-consistent preference optimization framework that constructs states at valid block boundaries, queries the fused block-level velocity predictor, and supervises span-normalized transition residuals-seamlessly supporting DPO-, NFT-, and rollout-style objectives while preserving the native few-step sampler. We further build a unified post-training pipeline integrating offline pairs, reward-free semi-online continuations, and online rollouts with hierarchical preference selection and objective-aware block scheduling. Experiments on multi-reference video generation across MiniMax-H3 Ref2VA and an in-house model RefVid-V4 show that BlockAlign consistently outperforms unaligned distilled models and DirectAlign in structural plausibility, reference consistency, and visual quality. Notably, our approach unlocks PDD's 5x few-step rollout speedup for 3x faster end-to-end online training, achieving statistically significant human preference gains over PDD baselines (54.0% vs. 33.3% wins/losses, or 61.8% non-tie win rate on RefVid-V4; 51.3% vs. 34.7%, or 59.7% non-tie win rate on MiniMax-H3) and up to an 82.7% win rate against DirectAlign.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20351) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20351)

## 138. Words Speak Louder? Exploring the Language Shortcut of LLM-Driven Incomplete Multimodal Learning

- **作者**：Wenhao Li, Chong Xiao, Xiaokun Bai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-16 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.MM
- **备注**：19 pages, 7 figures, 14 tables. Project page: https://peacelwh.github.io/CoVeR/

Is a face or a voice still heard once the words are known? Incomplete multimodal learning increasingly uses large language models (LLMs) as fusion engines: modality features enter as token blocks and missing modalities become placeholders. We find that LLM-driven fusion can override non-verbal evidence it can read. Contrasting its full-input prediction with counterfactual views, the same model shown one modality or no content, we identify language shortcuts. We build MR-Bench: MR-Bench-A types every test sample of four sentiment benchmarks; MR-Bench-H contains 900 human-validated clips (620 language-shortcut, LS; 280 non-verbal-shortcut, NS) from seven sources, and every model is scored on all of them. Zero-shot MLLMs solve at most 32.7% of the LS clips; adding the transcript to the muted video lowers vision-language accuracy by 46.1-53.1 points. Yet inverting a text-only model's answers solves 94.4%, so LS accuracy alone cannot establish use of non-verbal evidence. Inside the fusion LLM, fused states align increasingly with the text view late in the decoder. Counterfactual-View Reliability learning (CoVeR) trains views as unimodal judges and withholds fused supervision where they disagree. It achieves state-of-the-art results on five benchmarks without extra inference cost in accuracy mode. On MR-Bench-H, it reaches 61.7% LS and 93.6% NS accuracy; robust mode raises LS accuracy to 63.0% while lowering NS accuracy to 84.8%. Among LS clips solved without text, adding text causes errors on 30.1% with CoVeR versus 32.2% with LLM-Fusion. Project page: https://peacelwh.github.io/CoVeR/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20349) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20349)

## 139. Sidewalk Stewards: A Visual Analytics Approach for Interactive Topology Repair of Sidewalk Networks

- **作者**：Stefan Cobeli, Leonardo Ferreira, Logan Loch et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.HC, cs.CV, cs.CY
- **备注**：Under review

Accurate pedestrian network data is foundational to urban accessibility, routing, and infrastructure planning, yet such data remains scarce and difficult to produce at scale. Automated extraction pipelines have made significant strides by leveraging computer vision to derive sidewalk networks from aerial imagery, and have seen real-world adoption by municipalities seeking cost-effective inventory solutions. Yet, these pipelines consistently produce topological errors (e.g., disconnected components, dangling edges, missed connections, malformed intersections) that undermine the utility of the extracted networks for routing and accessibility analysis. Correcting these errors requires situated human judgment that global automated repair strategies cannot replicate, yet practitioners currently lack dedicated tools to support this work efficiently. We present Sidewalk Stewards, a visual analytics system that bridges the gap between automated extraction and a deployment-ready pedestrian network. Sidewalk Stewards detects candidate topology errors, supports interactive user-guided correction through coordinated spatial views, and provides real-time quality feedback as edits are applied. We evaluate Sidewalk Stewards through a comparative user study, case studies across cities, and a quantitative evaluation. Compared with conventional workflows, the system facilitates error identification and enables faster and more complete correction. The case studies illustrate the workflow across different urban contexts, and the quantitative evaluation shows that a small number of user corrections can enhance multiple sidewalk quality metrics. Sidewalk Stewards is available at urbantk.org/sidewalk.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20330) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20330)

## 140. Unreasonable Confidence from Simulator Misspecification: Directional Robustness on the SPEED+ Sim-to-Real Benchmark

- **作者**：Nikolay Dolgov
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：22 pages, 5 figures, 20 tables. Extended version of a paper at the Sim2Science workshop, NeurIPS 2026. Code, per-image predictions and pre-registration record: https://github.com/lokindoo/spacecraft-pose-estimation

Vision-based navigation systems for orbit must be trained almost entirely on synthetic imagery, because real labelled images of a given spacecraft in flight rarely exist. Benchmarks like SPEED+ are built for that situation: the training set comes from a simulator, and the test sets are real images of a satellite mockup under two kinds of lighting. That simulator approximates how light reflects off mylar and aluminium, and like every scientific simulator it is misspecified. Its error is radiometric, since it misses the specular highlights, hard shadows and sensor saturation common in real images. We probe the resulting synthetic-to-real gap, the central risk for such systems, with a deliberately simple monocular pose estimator. In a 2x2 study of backbone adaptation and photometric augmentation, trained on synthetic images and tested on both real hardware-in-the-loop domains, we find three things. (1) The model does not know it is failing: regions conformally calibrated to 90% on synthetic data cover only 9-15% of rotation errors on sunlamp and 21-38% on lightbox, so a wrong pose carries the confidence of a correct one. (2) Transfer is asymmetric: scaling data and features improved synthetic rotation error from 1.65 to 1.03 rad but moved the harshly lit sunlamp domain by only 0.05 rad, under twice its seed s.d., whereas adapting the backbone's last stage carried 63-83% of its extra synthetic gain to both real domains. (3) Once the backbone adapts, augmentation helps only in the direction it models: our photometric stack improved the diffusely lit lightbox domain from 1.17 to 0.99 rad on every seed but moved sunlamp no more than seed-to-seed variation, while with the backbone frozen it helped sunlamp more. Every post-review experiment was pre-registered, and the code, per-image predictions and pre-registration record are at https://github.com/lokindoo/spacecraft-pose-estimation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20321) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20321)

## 141. PoCoAttn: Position-Content Collaborative Sparse Attention for Fast and High-Fidelity Video Generation

- **作者**：Jianwen Wu, Meiling Wang, Junkang Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：21 pages, 15 figures, 13 tables. Project page: https://ecoai-group.github.io/pocoattn

Video diffusion transformers rely on 3D full attention over tens of thousands of spatiotemporal tokens, and its quadratic cost dominates inference latency. Sparse attention reduces this cost, but existing methods face a dilemma: static methods apply predefined masks and cannot capture input-dependent attention, whereas dynamic methods reconstruct the sparse mask repeatedly, paying online to rediscover positional structure that is largely stable across inputs. To this end, we propose PoCoAttn, a training-free sparse attention framework that separates input-stable from input-dependent attention and resolves the former offline and the latter online. PoCoAttn features: (1) A relative-offset analysis of 3D query-key pairs, which shows that video attention decomposes into an input-stable positional prior that carries most of the attention mass and a small content-dependent residual. (2) A position-content collaborative sparse attention mechanism, which retrieves positional keys from offline-calibrated tables, selects content keys online with a head-adaptive ratio between the two sources, and compensates for the discarded keys to approximate full attention. (3) A step-adaptive budget allocation algorithm, which models how early-step sparse attention errors are amplified along the denoising trajectory and derives the optimal sparsity budget per step. Extensive experiments across four video diffusion models and three tasks show that PoCoAttn consistently achieves a better speed-fidelity Pareto frontier than state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods, improving PSNR by 1.9 to 5.0 dB over baselines and delivering up to 4.1$\times$ attention and 2.0$\times$ denoising speedups over dense FlashAttention-3 attention.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20307) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20307)

## 142. CiGS: Conduction in Gaussian Splats for Interior Temperature Field from Thermal Images

- **作者**：Liu Zhenyuan, Bharath Seshadri, Bailey Miller et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：18 pages

We present a method for inferring the interior temperature distribution of a solid object from registered multi-view visible-spectrum and thermal images, even when the thermal views cover only part of its surface. We formulate steady-state heat conduction as a Laplace boundary value problem and solve it with a direct boundary integral equation discretized over a point cloud: a Gaussian reconstruction of the visible-spectrum images supplies the boundary geometry, and the thermal images supply the boundary data. We do not build surface or volume meshes at any stage in our method. Instead, we sample collocation points from rendered depth without the pixels at depth discontinuities, and use a density subtraction that keeps the interior evaluation accurate up to the surface. The whole chain is differentiable, and we use it to recover the pose and depth of heating pipes, insulation defects, and the shape of cracks from thermal images. We validate our method through experiments on boundary value problems with Dirichlet-only and mixed Dirichlet and Robin boundaries, using synthetic and real captured data on scenes with challenging topology. These experiments show that our method matches analytical solutions and has better accuracy than FEM solvers.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20283) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20283)

## 143. Visual Marking as a Native Reasoning Capability for Multimodal LLMs

- **作者**：Jiazhen Liu, Yanghao Wang, Chaolei Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Visual reasoning often requires maintaining intermediate spatial states that are difficult to express in text. Existing approaches either rely on external drawing tools or image generators, or encode these states in latent tokens without explicit spatial correspondence to the input image. We introduce SelfMark, which enables MLLMs to construct and use visual marks directly in their native visual feature space. By treating the visual token grid as a canvas, SelfMark establishes explicit spatial correspondence between marks and images. This is achieved through a locate-mark-read procedure, where the model identifies relevant locations, constructs task-dependent mark features, and reads the resulting visual features to guide its reasoning. We jointly train a single model on spatial planning, dense counting, high-resolution VQA, and line tracing. Across six benchmarks, SelfMark outperforms state-of-the-art MLLM-based methods specialized for individual tasks. These improvements also persist with a stronger backbone, demonstrating the effectiveness of visual marking across different model capabilities. Overall, SelfMark provides a unified approach to visual reasoning by integrating spatially grounded marks directly into the visual feature space of MLLMs.

## 144. Beyond AUC: Do Mammography Risk Models Predict When Cancer Occurs?

- **作者**：Svetlana Krasnova, Andrey Krylov, Dmitry Sorokin
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：q-bio.QM, cs.CV, eess.IV
- **备注**：7 pages, 2 figures, 1 table. Submitted to ISBI 2027

Longitudinal deep learning models for mammography-based breast cancer risk prediction output either per-year cumulative risks or a discrete distribution over the year of diagnosis, from which cumulative risks F1,...,F5 are derived. These models are evaluated almost exclusively with year-wise AUCs and the concordance index. We show that these metrics are largely insensitive to when cancer occurs: they can be high even for a model that carries no information about the year of diagnosis. We re-evaluate two recent longitudinal models (OA-BreaCR, LMV-Net) on CSAW-CC with a 5-year horizon using timing metrics computed on cancer cases only. Despite C-indices up to 0.75, neither orders cancer cases by time to diagnosis significantly better than chance (c_time 0.51 and 0.55, both 95% CIs covering 0.5). Weighted absolute timing error favours a constant "year 3" prediction over both models, so we use c_time as the primary timing metric. For Mirai's additive-hazard head, also used by LMV-Net, we analyse how per-year increments passed through a ReLU can collapse under severe class imbalance. We also propose a breast-level, two-stage model that separates whether from when. It matches LMV-Net in discrimination (C-index 0.745 vs. 0.746) but orders cancer cases better (c_time 0.58 vs. 0.51, paired 95% CI of the difference 0.01-0.13), on par with Mirai's published weights (0.57).

## 145. Self-supervised learning for zebrafish embryo phenotype evaluation: Systematic comparison of algorithms, label-efficient classification, and transfer learning

- **作者**：Thomas Lautenschlager, Gaëlle Hayot, Quentin Brassart et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Zebrafish embryos are a prominent model organism for image-based phenotyping and especially suited for large-scale imaging studies, for which machine learning (ML) models can automate evaluation. However, training ML models usually requires large labeled datasets, and differences in imaging conditions often impede reuse across laboratories. Self-supervised learning (SSL) addresses both constraints by learning transferable representations from unlabeled images. Yet central aspects like the choice of algorithm remain underexplored for domain-specific applications. To close this gap, we benchmark popular SSL algorithms on the public EmbryoNet dataset, explore label-efficient learning, and test transfer to an unseen setting. Using representations from our strongest model, a ResNet50 network trained with SwAV, a simple linear classifier reaches 86.4% accuracy and a non-linear classifier 87.1%, rivaling the fully supervised EmbryoNet model (87.1%). The SwAV model decisively outperforms supervised training from scratch when labels are scarce (70.2% vs. 45.7% accuracy with 5% of labels). On a three-class problem (normal, dead, falsely cropped) with a small, previously unseen zebrafish toxicity dataset, recorded in a different setting, our model reaches 81.3% accuracy without retraining, while EmbryoNet achieves 75.9%. Overall, self-supervised models for zebrafish embryos seem to mostly facilitate label-efficiency and transfer to new settings rather than improving supervised results.

## 146. EvoMem3D: Adaptive-Depth Temporal Representation Memory for Multi-Camera 3D Detection

- **作者**：Moshe Kimhi, Idan Kligvasser, Yotam Intrator et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Modern multi-camera 3D detectors increasingly rely on large Vision Transformer (ViT) image encoders, making repeated full-depth encoding a dominant inference cost even though adjacent driving frames are highly redundant. We present EvoMem3D, an adaptive-depth temporal framework that caches the most recent full-depth detector-interface appearance representation as a read-only anchor and combines it with policy-selected partial features from the current frame. A lightweight pre-encoding controller chooses among semantic encoder exits before image processing, allocating shallow computation to stable frames and deeper computation when the visual state changes. To reuse appearance without carrying stale geometry, we decouple temporal appearance from geometric alignment: current-frame calibration defines the decoder geometry at every timestamp, while a motion-conditioned positional adapter accounts for anchor-to-current ego motion. On nuScenes, EvoMem3D achieves 61.50 NDS and 52.76 mAP while reducing end-to-end latency from 364.4 to 168.7 ms (x2.16) relative to standard dense DETR3D. With StreamPETR, it reaches 61.36 NDS and 52.55 mAP at a x1.92 speedup. Controlled studies isolate the effects of current evidence, temporal reuse, memory correction, and adaptive routing, while our audit confirms that the policy learns to distinguish semantic driving regimes.

## 147. Streaming across viewing conditions - The effect of viewing distance and display luminance on video quality

- **作者**：Dounia Hammou, Lukáš Krasula, Christos G. Bampis et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV, cs.MM

The visibility of streamed video distortions is affected by the user's display and viewing conditions, such as their display's luminance or the viewing distance. Distortions become less visible at lower luminance levels or farther viewing distances. However, most image and video quality metrics assume fixed optimal display and viewing conditions, making them insensitive to the viewing conditions. In this work, we demonstrate how existing metrics can be adapted to account for both factors and compare such an adaptation with the metrics that natively model viewing conditions. First, we collected a new video quality dataset, HDR-VDC, which contains streaming distortions in 4K HDR videos. The dataset consists of 12 4K and 4 FHD HDR source video content, each distorted with AV1 encoding and Lanczos upscaling from a lower resolution. The quality loss due to the distortions was measured at two viewing distances, corresponding to 60 and 120 pixels per visual degree, and two display mean luminance levels, varying by a factor of 8 in terms of luminance. Next, we developed a pre-processing framework that lets us adapt many quality metrics so that they can evaluate content across the viewing distances and luminance levels. The framework employs a display model and perceptually uniform encoding, PU21, to account for the effect of display luminance, and perspective projection scaling (PPS) to account for the effect of viewing distance. We used the framework to compare the performance of 35 state-of-the-art metrics. This allowed us to identify a selection of existing quality metrics that can be effectively adapted to account for the viewing conditions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20226) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20226)

## 148. Ocular Recognition: Interoperability, Multi-Resolution, and Spectrum Challenges

- **作者**：Fabian Stockhardt, Juan E. Tapia, Christoph Busch
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Paper submitted to IEEE journal in revision process. Abstract reduces to 1920 characteres

Ocular recognition is typically developed under controlled acquisition conditions, limiting its use in semi-controlled and on-the-move scenarios. A key obstacle is the lack of representative data spanning real-world capture conditions. At a border checkpoint, for example, images may range from low-resolution visible-light periocular crops (RGB) to high-resolution near-infrared iris images (NIR). Using this heterogeneous evidence raises three questions: whether resolution-specific processing improves recognition, how to compare templates from different processing routes, and how to allocate training data across resolution specialists. We investigate resolution-specific processing and parameter sharing using three AdaFace-based architecture families: fixed-resolution specialists; Independent Backbones with Shared Heads (IBSH); and models with resolution-specific front-ends for 40x40, 80x80, and 160x160 inputs, followed by a shared identity trunk and embedding projection. We evaluate each architecture with route-specific training subsets and with a full-pool strategy, in which every specialist uses the complete selected training set. Results reveal a trade-off among same-resolution specialization, cross-resolution interoperability, and common-route re-extraction. Route-specific training produces distinct performance profiles across routes, whereas full-pool training makes route performance more uniform and primarily benefits the 160x160 route. Thus, adding training data does not improve all resolution routes equally.

## 149. AstroPIE-53: Towards Understanding Failure Modes of Human Pose Estimation in Space

- **作者**：Stephanie Käs, Marten Flaig, Lukas Woyke et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：4 pages + references, 4 figures. Published in the Proceedings of SPAICE 2026 and i-SAIRAS 2026

Reliable monitoring of astronauts is essential for safety, situational awareness, and autonomous assistance in space missions. While modern computer vision enables accurate human detection and pose estimation on Earth, its robustness in space environments remains largely unquantified due to limited data availability. We present AstroPIE-53, a pilot dataset of 53 annotated astronaut videos collected from publicly available mission footage. To respect copyright restrictions, we release video identifiers, reconstruction scripts, and dense annotations rather than raw videos. The dataset includes bounding boxes, challenge classes, and human-annotated pose failure tags. We benchmark state-of-the-art person detection and pose estimation models under realistic zero-shot deployment conditions and perform the first systematic failure analysis for astronaut monitoring. Our results reveal substantial performance degradation caused by occlusions, unusual body orientations, low contrast, and microgravity motion patterns.

## 150. Radar Panoptic Transformer: Semantic-Guided Mask Queries for Dynamic Scene Understanding

- **作者**：Mayank Mayank, Bharanidhar Duraisamy, Rohit Mohan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：9 pages, 4 figures

Automotive radar provides long-range sensing and direct Doppler velocity measurements, but its sparse and non-uniformly sampled point clouds pose significant challenges for panoptic segmentation. Dynamic objects often yield only a few detections amid a dominant static background, providing limiting the geometric cues for separating object instances. We propose \emph{Radar Panoptic Transformer (RPT)}, an end-to-end point-based network that incorporates radar-adaptive feature extraction with semantic-guided mask prediction. Its backbone aggregates pose-aligned temporal context through grouped vector attention and learns per-point neighborhood radii conditioned on range, azimuth, and radar cross section (RCS). Point-wise semantic predictions initialize queries at likely foreground detections and guide masked cross-attention through query-point class agreement, producing instance masks without geometric clustering. An auxiliary RCS-consistency loss matches instance-level statistics between predicted and annotated instances. RPT achieves state-of-the-art performance on RadarScenes with $71.6\%$ mIoU and $82.2\%$ PQ. We further evaluate RPT on MAN TruckScenes, where it outperforms the evaluated baselines in a long-range, full-surround radar setting with sensing ranges up to $230\,\mathrm{m}$. RPT achieves real-time end-to-end inference at $31$\,Hz on RadarScenes, exceeding the sensor frame rate.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20203) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20203)

## 151. DoF-Query Transformer for Adaptive Fusion of Egocentric Vision and Forearm HD-sEMG in Continuous Hand Kinematics

- **作者**：Jiahang Liu, Wenbo Zhang, Junsong Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.HC, cs.LG

Continuous and accurate estimation of human hand kinematics is important not only for intuitive human-machine interaction, but also for capturing fine-grained human manipulation demonstrations that can provide action supervision for embodied-intelligence datasets and dexterous robot learning. However, individual sensing modalities may be insufficient for reliable estimation under varying operating conditions. We present a finger-unencumbered multimodal framework for continuous regression of 20 hand joint angles from egocentric RGB images and 128-channel forearm high-density surface electromyography (HD-sEMG). The framework combines a Vision Transformer-based visual encoder, a Transformer-based EMG encoder, and a degree of freedom (DoF) query Transformer fusion decoder. To account for the varying informativeness of visual and EMG cues across joint DoFs and input conditions, the decoder assigns a separate learnable query to each DoF, enabling DoF-specific and context-adaptive fusion of the two modalities. The framework was evaluated on synchronized multimodal data from 10 participants. Averaged across participants, the proposed fusion model achieved a mean coefficient of determination ($R^2$) of $0.88$ and a mean absolute error (MAE) of $2.80^\circ$, compared with $0.82$ and $3.21^\circ$ for the vision-only model and $0.68$ and $5.13^\circ$ for the EMG-only model. It also outperformed the evaluated representative unimodal baselines and alternative fusion designs. DoF-wise analyses showed larger fusion gains for visually ambiguous joints and viewing conditions, while synthetic visual occlusion demonstrated greater robustness than vision-only estimation. These results highlight the complementary value of egocentric vision and forearm HD-sEMG for capturing fine-grained human hand kinematics, with potential applications in human demonstration acquisition and dexterous robot learning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20196) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20196)

## 152. GlyphAtlas: Disentangling Stroke Detail from Layout for Scene Text Synthesis

- **作者**：Luozheng Qin, Changhai Zhou, Yiyi Zhou
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Scene text synthesis requires stroke-accurate characters rendered in a specified layout, as well as visual coherence with the surrounding scene. To achieve this, existing methods commonly pre-render the target text at its intended locations to form a glyph image that jointly specifies layout and glyph structure. However, this representation ties the resolution of each glyph to its target region, limiting fine-grained stroke guidance for small, densely packed, or structurally complex text. To tackle this, we propose GlyphAtlas, a scene text synthesis framework that retains the glyph image as a spatial anchor and supplies fine-grained stroke references through a dedicated glyph atlas. The atlas renders and encodes each character or word in a separate cell, decoupling reference resolution from target-region size. We further introduce atlas-anchored attention, which lets each text region retrieve stroke detail only from its corresponding atlas cells to refine the coarse glyph structure provided by the glyph image. This coarse-to-fine conditioning simplifies stroke rendering while preserving explicit layout correspondence. We optimize GlyphAtlas with a mask-weighted flow matching objective and a timestep-weighted text perceptual loss for writing accuracy and background consistency. Random resolution augmentation of atlas cells supports adjustable atlas budgets at inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GlyphAtlas achieves stroke-accurate synthesis of small, dense, and structurally complex text, zero-shot transfer to languages unseen during task-specific training, and leading performance in scenes with many text regions. project page: https://fr0zencrane.github.io/project-glyphatlas/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20192) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20192)

## 153. GeoReVis: Learning Selective Revision and Evidence Consolidation for Remote Sensing Segmentation

- **作者**：Ke An, Liang Chen, Yupei Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：31 pages

Recent methods try to inspect and revise masks to mitigate referential ambiguity and localization errors caused by complex spatial relations in language-guided remote sensing segmentation. However, these solutions often lead to Revision Degradation: revising missing regions may introduce errors into previously correct ones. Even a globally better mask can omit correct regions found in inferior masks. We term this Evidence Fragmentation: correct target evidence is scattered across imperfect masks. All masks generated by the model at this time are suboptimal. We propose GeoReVis, which unifies revision guided by execution feedback, Candidate Selection, and Cross-Mask Evidence Consolidation. It learns fine-grained revision from real actions that improve segmentation when executed and learns to select a better mask through quality comparisons between actual candidates, reducing the risk of degradation during revision. The selected whole mask can still discard useful regions. GeoReVis therefore retains all candidates across rounds, partitions their spatial disagreements into composable regions, and jointly reconstructs regions conditioned on the full instruction and context. This makes complementary and fragmentary evidence reusable regardless of its source candidate's overall quality, yielding improved segmentation. We further build a data pipeline to generate fine-grained multi-round segmentation trajectories and derive supervision for revision actions, candidate selection, and region composition from the model's own outputs, extending training beyond whole-mask IoU supervision. We also propose ReVis-Eval, a benchmark protocol evaluating multi-round revision and cross-round evidence consolidation accuracy. Experiments on ReVis-Eval and 5 benchmarks for remote sensing reasoning and referring segmentation demonstrate the effectiveness of GeoReVis.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20183) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20183)

## 154. Aerial Person Detection under Edge Constraints: Held-Out Evaluation and Edge-Ground Verification

- **作者**：Raghu Venkat, Tricha Anjali
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：14 pages, 1 figure, 8 tables. Code, truth registers and result files: https://github.com/numberz-ai/aerial-person-detector

A search drone sees a missing person as a few pixels, carries a small computer, and shares a narrow radio link. We study person detection under those limits; the primary evaluations were declared before they ran, and later analyses are marked exploratory. In an exploratory comparison, holding out recording blocks inferred from thermal frame identifiers cut recall at a fixed false-alarm target from 32.7% to 12.3%. With nothing tuned on test blocks, a 46,000-parameter detector gated by a contrast cue finds 10.4% of people against 5.7% for the same network applied everywhere. A standard detector, YOLO11n, trained under the same folds finds 54.5% at a similar false-alarm rate for about eleven times the arithmetic, and on our 4K field footage at 117 m has a walking person among its top 16 detections in up to 92% of frames against 74% for our board. Run alone, YOLO11n's thresholds set at one location overshot their false-alarm target 1.7 to 3.4 times at new locations, and its best configuration at altitude cost 4 to 24 standard passes per frame. Re-scoring its 16 best crops (154 KB per frame) with an 842,000-parameter ground model, thresholds frozen on a validation location, raised recall from 44.9% to 53.7% against the same 16 crops with YOLO's own scores; in a post hoc paired analysis over 15 test locations the gain was +8.8 points [+2.4, +12.5]. Pooled false detections fell from 0.86 to 0.64 per frame, but that paired change was not distinguishable from zero. Coverage of a still person in earth tones was very low for both detectors in the clips we flew.

## 155. Sequential Semantic-Non-Semantic Watermarking: Robustness Against UnMarker Attacks

- **作者**：Md Ifraham Iqbal
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CR, cs.CV, cs.MM, eess.IV
- **备注**：13 pages, 16 figures. Preprint based on M.Sc. thesis research

Advancements in artificial intelligence (AI) have accelerated digital content generation and intensified concerns around fraud, misuse, counterfeiting, and content provenance. Defensive image watermarking provides a practical mechanism for identifying and tracing digital content, but recent watermark-removal attacks challenge its robustness. This study investigates Sequential Semantic Non-Semantic Watermarking (SSNSW), which combines HiDDeN and StegaStamp as complementary non-semantic and semantic watermarking schemes. Using 50 images from MS-COCO and CelebA-HQ, both embedding orders are evaluated, followed by UnMarker's non-semantic and semantic attacks applied individually and sequentially. Results show that both watermarks remain highly decodable after dual embedding, with only limited embedding-order effects. Under individual attacks, one watermark generally remains strongly decodable: StegaStamp under the non-semantic attack and HiDDeN under the semantic attack. Sequential attacks are therefore required to substantially reduce both watermark signals, while attack order has a limited and configuration-dependent effect. The semantic attack also shows a broader effect across the two watermark types than the non-semantic attack. LPIPS remains relatively low in several settings, although qualitative inspection reveals noticeable distortions, particularly in selected facial images, indicating that LPIPS should not be used alone to assess visual quality. Overall, the findings show that combining complementary watermark types can increase robustness against single-stage removal attacks and motivate broader multi-layer watermarking, stronger perceptual evaluation, and further study of sequential watermark-removal strategies.

## 156. Trading Parameters for Accuracy in MambaVision for Chest Radiograph Classification

- **作者**：Qaiser Farooq, Tehseen Zia
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 4 figures, 9 tables. Submitted to an IEEE journal

MambaVision, a hybrid state-space and attention backbone, places most of its parameters in its widest, fourth stage. We quantified what that stage contributes to multi-label chest radiograph classification. The fourth stage was replaced with lighter single-scan state-space blocks adapted from Spatial-Mamba, with multi-scale dilated depthwise convolutions applied to the scan output; the first three stages were retained. Tiny, Small, and Base models were evaluated across nine dataset-scale combinations drawn from two source cohorts: the National Institutes of Health ChestX-ray14 archive (full ChestMNIST and a high-resolution subset) and Indiana University Open-i. At the Tiny scale, replacement reduced parameter count by 35.7% and increased graphics processing unit throughput by 19.3%; the savings came from the stage's dense projections, not from removing quadratic attention. Without distillation, the mean area under the receiver operating characteristic curve on ChestMNIST was 0.004 lower than the baseline (95% bootstrap confidence interval, 0.002 to 0.006), and 0.003 to 0.006 lower across all nine combinations. Knowledge distillation from an unreplaced teacher narrowed the gap to the undistilled baseline to 0.001-0.002 and met the equivalence criterion on ChestMNIST, but distilling the baseline with the same recipe improved it by 0.001-0.003, so a gap of 0.002-0.005 persisted under symmetric training. At the Base scale, the unreplaced Small model was both smaller and more accurate. Fourth-stage replacement is a useful trade-off only for Tiny and Small models. These measurements let developers of chest radiograph screening tools weigh model size and speed against a quantified loss of diagnostic discrimination.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20103) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20103)

## 157. Geo2Gcode: From 3D Geometry to CNC Programs through Structured Machining Primitives

- **作者**：Sangmin Hong, Dohee Cho, Mohsen Yavartanoo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Turning a 3D shape into a Computer Numerical Control (CNC) program is still largely manual. For every part, a machinist chooses setups, tools, step-downs, and toolpath patterns. We present Geo2Gcode, which converts a target mesh directly into standard G-code through structured machining primitives. Each primitive couples an indexed machining direction, a closed per-view reconstruction of the target, and explicit tool-radius, layer-height, and clearing-pattern parameters, and compiles deterministically into layerwise toolpaths via signed-distance queries. Most of the fidelity comes from this compiler. A lightweight selector trained with a voxel stock simulator fills in primitive parameters without per-part manual settings, approaching the finest fixed setting at about a quarter of its toolpath length. In simulation on ABC parts, Geo2Gcode reaches roughing fidelity comparable to an expert-tuned commercial CAM setup with a moderately longer toolpath, and clearly improves on a default CAM configuration. Generated programs run in a commercial G-code simulator, and one part is machined on a physical CNC mill. These results demonstrate that roughing programs can be generated directly from geometry without per-part CAM tuning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20092) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20092)

## 158. Latent World Modeling for Point-Cloud Video Pretraining

- **作者**：Zhuoyue Zhang, Jian Liu, Jihua Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Masked modeling is the dominant self-supervised paradigm for point-cloud videos. However, it leaks the information it is meant to predict, whether it reconstructs masked points as in MAE-style methods or predicts their latent features. Because point-cloud tokens are anchored at points sampled from each frame, querying a target at its own position reveals where its geometry has moved, in reconstruction and latent prediction alike. For latent predictors, random spatio-temporal masks introduce a further shortcut, as visible frames on both sides of each target allow interpolation rather than forecasting. The pretext task thus reduces to completion. Once the target positions are withheld, a masked latent predictor performs only slightly better than copying the last frame. To remove this leakage, we propose PointWM, a latent world model that turns completion into forecasting by predicting the features and geometry of future frames from strictly earlier context. Each target is queried only with its last-seen position and timestamp, while a latent action learned without annotations is meant to capture the part of the future that the past leaves undetermined. On HOI4D action segmentation with a P4Transformer backbone, PointWM improves F1@50 over training from scratch by 24.9 offline and 29.1 under strictly causal inference. These results show the promise of latent world models as a leakage-free pretraining paradigm for point-cloud videos.Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/InitalZ/PointWM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20090) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20090)

## 159. See the Lives of Stars in a Handful of Light

- **作者**：Shuhong Liu, Gengjia Chang, Quanfeng Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Galaxies evolve over hundreds of millions of years, and no telescope has ever watched one move. Cosmological simulations make this evolution visible as a sequence of multi-view images, inviting the question of whether a galaxy can be recovered in four dimensions from its images. This setting defeats existing dynamic scene methods, since millions of stars span only a few pixels each, faint populations blend into diffuse light, and free deformations reproduce the images without obeying gravity. We present Cosmos4D, which rests on two components. Gravitational 4D Gaussian Splatting represents each star as a self-luminous Gaussian whose motion is integrated through a gravitational potential and a low-dimensional force field shared by all stars. Luminosity-stratified dynamics give bright stars individual orbits supervised across image scales and carry faint populations with a velocity field estimated from optical flow. We further build a 4D dataset of eight galaxies from four cosmological simulations, on which Cosmos4D outperforms dynamic Gaussian baselines in both photometric quality and physical accuracy. With these advancements, we glimpse eight hundred million years of a galaxy's life in ninety-six frames.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20089) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20089)

## 160. SGIP: Learning Scene-Grounded Interaction Priors for Scene-Aware Human Motion Prediction

- **作者**：Donghyeon Soon, Jiil Yoon, Hyeondong Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://sgip2026.github.io

Human motion prediction (HMP) aims to forecast future movement from an observed motion sequence, and recent methods have incorporated 3D scene geometry and object semantics to improve long-term plausibility. However, the destination of future motion remains ambiguous in cluttered scenes: the same observed motion can lead to different interaction targets or navigation modes. Existing methods either handle this ambiguity implicitly or rely on auxiliary signals like gaze that are hard to obtain in practice. To address this, we propose SGIP, which explicitly predicts where in the scene future motion will be grounded across both objects and open navigation regions, and uses it to condition future trajectory and full-body pose prediction. This scene-grounded interaction prior is learned via intent distillation: at training time only, a teacher model scores candidate scene regions by combining an LLM prompted with a textual description of the person's intent with geometric motion-scene features. A student model learns to reproduce these scores from only the observed motion and 3D scene, making intent and LLM unnecessary at test time. Across multiple benchmark datasets, SGIP outperforms prior scene-aware baselines on both trajectory and pose accuracy in a realistic deployment setting where neither gaze nor intent is available at inference, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework. The project page is available at https://sgip2026.github.io.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20076) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20076)

## 161. Prompt Re-Specification and Target-Mask Availability in Mask-Conditioned Visual Reinforcement Learning for Moving-Platform Landing

- **作者**：Ho Tim Tsui, Jongyun Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：11 pages, 2 figures, 12 tables

Visual reinforcement learning (RL) policies that land a drone on a moving platform can fail when the landing pad looks different from the one used in training. Using binary target masks as the visual input removes most pixel-level appearance information while preserving the spatial representation of the target, and lets a text prompt specify the target. In simulation, we test whether a frozen (fixed-weight) mask-based DrQ-v2 policy can handle a new pad when only the prompt of its fine-tuned SAM 3 detector changes. A banana-themed pad lowered landing success from 75.6% to 12.0%, and SAM 3 rarely detected it under the original prompt. A revised prompt, chosen after testing several candidates, restored success to 68.4% with no weight updates. In a separate experiment with a policy trained on ground-truth masks, filling in masks that were missing while the pad was visible helped landing far more than the tested mask-centroid and area corrections. Tracking the pad over time supplied masks more often and also kept producing them after the pad was confirmed absent from the rendered image, but it did not establish a reliable improvement in landing success. Whether a usable mask reaches the policy at all thus appears to be an important limiting factor for this approach.

## 162. MARLIN: Multiscale And Directional Sparse Representation Learning for Imbalanced SAR Ship Classification

- **作者**：Benyamin Hosseiny, Kamirul Kamirul, Odysseas Pappas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Despite recent progress in SAR maritime monitoring, ship classification remains challenging due to limited labeled data, class imbalance, large intra-class variability, fine-grained inter-class similarity, and variations in target scale, orientation, and backscattering structure. To address these challenges, this paper proposes MARLIN, a class-frequency-aware framework with directional sparse and hierarchical multi-scale feature extraction for ship type classification in SAR images. The proposed architecture first constructs two complementary representations of each input image, where each views are processed by parallel encoder branches. Each branch begins with a directional sparse convolutional stem designed to capture elongated and orientation-dependent ship scattering patterns, followed by customized hierarchical multiscale feature extraction blocks. To improve learning under imbalanced class distributions, we introduce a class-frequency aware objective. The proposed objective combines margin-based classification with class-frequency-weighted prototype compactness and class-pair frequency weighted margin separation. This formulation shapes the embedding space by encouraging samples from the same class to form compact clusters while enforcing stronger separation for class pairs involving underrepresented ship categories. Extensive experiments on three complementary datasets demonstrate that in addition to being lightweight and parameter efficient, MARLIN improves both overall accuracy and class-balanced metrics, confirming its effectiveness for robust SAR ship-type classification under limited and imbalanced training conditions. The codes are available at github.com/benyaminhosseiny/marlin.

## 163. Machine Learning for Near Real-Time Identification of Wildfire Burned Areas

- **作者**：Khoi Nguyen, Adam W. Bargteil, Milton Halem
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV, eess.IV

In this article, we present a near real-time method that estimates burned probability anywhere in the world using satellite images. Our approach takes advantage of the state-of-the-art pix2pix model to translate a preprocessed index calculated from Landsat 8/9 images into an approximate of USGS Burn Probability indicating per pixel burn probability. Since this is a machine learning-based approach, it can be automated with a minimum amount of human intervention with significantly less time delays and geographic restrictions compared to other contemporary methods. We include results from our experiments and compare our approximation with the USGS Burn Probability using RMSE score.

## 164. acuSimNet: Multi-View Self-Occlusion-Awared Visibility Learning for Cranio-Cervical Acupuncture Points

- **作者**：Jiatao Ma, Qilei Sun
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：19 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables

The localization of acupuncture points (acupoints) in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) presents unique challenges since they are defined by abstract principles rather than distinct anatomical landmarks. Existing approaches are typically constrained to single view or rely on indirect calculations of relative positions with respect to other landmarks, thereby overlooking human anatomical variations. Furthermore, acupoint visibility assessment, which determins whether points are occluded by human itself, hair, clothing, or other objects, has received limited attention in practical applications. Acupoint localization itself does not require 3D reconstruction, but inferring their occlusion relationships with anatomical surfaces does, which adds computational cost and limits real-time inference. In this work, we introduce acuSimNet, an efficient hierarchical multi-task learning architecture for multi-view, self-occlusion-aware visibility prediction of acupoints, achieving 99.97% accuracy on the validation set. Our approach also addresses the challenges of high-dimensional classification (174 acupoints in cervicocranial region), negative convergence issues for visible acupoints, and inter-task scheduling optimization, resulting in substantially accelerated convergence. We improved the training efficiency from the exisiting methods of 3000 epochs to achieve 99% validation accuracy, to our optimized framework achieving 90% accuracy in 39 epochs and 99% accuracy in 86 epochs. This architecture overcomes the limitations of existing methods, could enable practical applications in acupoints detection and visualization, advancing the automation of TCM.

## 165. FastGRN: Few-Step Generative Refinement Networks

- **作者**：Haoyang Hu, Masataka Seo, Liang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Generative Refinement Networks (GRN) generate an image by repeatedly re-predicting and refining every token of a discrete latent. They reach high fidelity, but need dozens of Markov steps. We therefore train few-step GRNs on the distribution of their own samples. We use its own uniform-state corruption to perform distribution matching on discrete tokens, which lets a discrete generator learn by backpropagation, and we pair noise with samples in the manner of rectified flow. We find that the steps of a few-step GRN do not carry the same amount of information or the same repair load. We therefore exploit its discrete refinement during training and build few-step GRNs in three parts. Oversampling samples the student with more steps than it was trained for and refines its judge on these samples. Non-uniform sampling trains only the last step and hands it most of the information at sampling time. Refinement confidence keeping holds on to the tokens the student is already sure of. We train a fast class-conditional generator on ImageNet 256 and, with the same method, a 2B text-to-image model. On ImageNet 256, the 3-step FastGRN beats the FID of GRN. On a 2B binary-token text-to-image GRN, a 4-step student also nearly matches the GenEval of GRN-T2I 2B.

## 166. ReactBench-AD: Do Driving VLAs and World-Action Models React to Imminent Hazards in Their Path?

- **作者**：Nikos Theodoridis, Reenu Mohandas, Sushil Sharma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO

Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) and World-Action Models (WAMs) have emerged in autonomous driving with the promise of strong generalization to long-tail scenarios. Although these models achieve state-of-the-art results on autonomous driving benchmarks, most existing benchmarks are dominated by nominal scenarios and therefore cannot test this promise. To test one such long-tail capability that is critical for safety, we introduce ReactBench-AD, a benchmark that measures how driving models react to objects that appear in the ego vehicle's path. Built on real NAVSIM and PhysicalAI-Autonomous-Vehicles scenes, ReactBench-AD composites a crossing pedestrian, a standing pedestrian, or a stationary car consistently across all camera views and history frames. Each edited scene thus forms an exact, pixel-level counterfactual pair with its original, which allows us to isolate the causal effect of the inserted object on the predicted trajectory. Evaluating four VLAs and two WAMs, we find that they react weakly, often barely at all. Finally, we test how easily one of the evaluated VLAs, ReCogDrive, can be taught to brake hard for imminent hazards: a short fine-tuning stage on edited scenes substantially improves its reactions, with the learned behavior generalizing to unseen objects and fully real scenes, at minimal cost to nominal driving. These results call into question the out-of-the-box long-tail capabilities of these models, but suggest that they can be a strong foundation for acquiring them with little additional training, highlighting the value of real or synthetic data that demonstrates the long-tail behaviors nominal datasets rarely contain. Code and data will be made publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20011) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20011)

## 167. PlanDiv: Test-Time Scaling with Latent Plans for Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Hanbo Zhang, Lianlei Shan, Liuhaichen Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：10 pages, 2 figures

Test-time scaling improves vision-language-action (VLA) policies by drawing several action candidates and executing the best one. We find that its returns diminish quickly when the candidates are resampled from one decoder: they differ by noise rather than by plan, and for each of three resampling sources, going from four to eight candidates adds only 0.06-0.07 to the average number of completed tasks on CALVIN. We introduce PlanDiv, an action head that makes the candidates differ by plan: a lightweight planner proposes K latent plan hypotheses, a shared masked-diffusion executor decodes one action chunk per plan from a single backbone pass, and the policy executes the chunk that its own likelihood ranks highest. The plans are learned from demonstrations alone, without plan labels or rewards. With the backbone, data and training recipe held fixed, eight plan-conditioned candidates raise five-task chain success on CALVIN ABC$\rightarrow$D from 56.9% to 69.9%, while eight candidates from autoregressive, plan-free or same-plan resampling reach at most 64.9%. The same-plan baseline is PlanDiv's own checkpoint with the plan held fixed, so this gap comes from the plans, not from drawing more samples. On LIBERO-Long, four plans remove 40% of single-candidate failures, against at most 21% for matched resampling. On a real Franka arm the gain appears where a task admits more than one solution: PlanDiv is on par with a single-output OpenVLA-OFT+ baseline on a one-solution task and succeeds in 51 of 54 trials against 40 of 54 on two tasks with two solutions.
