## 168. Glance, then Select: Evidence-Tempered Visual Token Selection for Extremely Compressed Multimodal LLMs

- **作者**：Yuxuan Wang, Guangxiang Zhao, Yuxuan Tian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) encode a high-resolution image into thousands of visual tokens, which dominate the cost of the prefill stage. Visual token reduction removes most of these tokens, but two problems remain at extreme budgets. The vision encoder still processes every patch of the image, and the few surviving tokens often miss the small regions that hold the answer. In this paper, we propose Glance-ETD, a two-stage token reduction framework guided by a lightweight question-conditioned scorer. In the first stage, a glance encoder views the image at low resolution and re-encodes only the question-relevant regions at native resolution. In the second stage, Evidence-Tempered Diversity (ETD) selects the final tokens by combining diversity with the evidence predicted by the scorer, so that the kept tokens cover the scene and retain the answer. A single-rollout self-distillation recipe trains the MLLM with the glance encoder. With only 5% of the visual tokens, Glance-ETD retains 91.6% of the full-token performance of Qwen3.5-4B across fourteen benchmarks and outperforms the strongest prior method, LT-OPD, by 17.3 points and the same training recipe with DivPrune by 6.0. It also speeds up the time to first token on 4K images by 3.1x and generalizes to three other MLLMs and to high-resolution document benchmarks, with the largest gains on text-rich tasks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20006) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20006)

## 169. What Matters for Cross-Modal Alignment

- **作者**：Yipeng Sun, Jiajun Wang, Siyuan Mei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

The Platonic Representation Hypothesis posits that models trained on different modalities converge toward a shared representation. Related work aligns frozen image and text encoders on natural images without paired data. We test zero-pair alignment on five datasets across four modalities: natural images, environmental audio, chest radiographs, and 3D computed tomography (CT). It succeeds only on COCO and fails on the other four. We analyze the failures and find that alignment must first locate the subspace where the modalities co-vary and then orient the mapping within it. Consistent with previous studies, medical imaging is the hardest case: its shared subspace carries the smallest fraction of the variance. When this fraction is small, alignment needs image-text pairs, and random-matrix theory predicts how many pairs are needed. These findings motivate LightAlign: a few pairs let a ridge regression between two frozen encoders locate and orient the mapping simultaneously, and a classifier fitted only on report text then predicts findings from the mapped images. On 3D CT, LightAlign surpasses models trained end to end on image-report pairs without finding-level labels: with only 200 pairs, LightAlign outperforms CT-CLIP on CT-RATE (0.754 vs. 0.733 AUROC), and with only 100 CT-RATE pairs, it does so on RAD-ChestCT, an out-of-distribution dataset from a different hospital. On CheXpert, it reaches 0.862 mean AUROC with only about 5% as many pairs as models trained on MIMIC-CXR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20000) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.20000)

## 170. KneeFS-Net: A Lightweight Deep Learning Model for Knee Osteoarthritis Severity Assessment with Cross-Domain External Validation

- **作者**：Shafqat Alam, Galib Anjum Talukder Mahi, Yeasir Rabbani Tanvir et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：23 pages, 6 figures, 4 tables

Knee osteoarthritis (OA) grading from radiographs using the Kellgren-Lawrence (KL) scale requires expertise that may be scarce. We present KneeFS-Net, a lightweight ordinal grader (0.8 million parameters; 0.33 GFLOPs) combining spatial, Fourier and state-space features with landmark-guided attention. On the Osteoarthritis Initiative (OAI) test set, KneeFS-Net achieved a quadratic weighted kappa (QWK) of 0.863, mean absolute error (MAE) of 0.325 and 97.2% within-one-grade accuracy, comparable to models with 9-35-fold more parameters. We evaluated cross-population generalization on two Bangladeshi cohorts, a single-center hospital cohort and a multi-center teleradiology cohort. Without further training or adaptation, the OAI-trained model achieved QWK 0.803, MAE 0.339 and 98.2% within-one-grade accuracy on its hospital cohort, declining to QWK 0.656 and MAE 0.522 on multi-center teleradiology images. Self-supervised pretraining on 6,092 unlabeled radiographs followed by joint fine-tuning achieved QWK 0.823, MAE 0.329 and 99.7% within-one-grade accuracy in cross-validation. These findings demonstrate accurate, efficient KL grading and potential adaptation to new populations with limited annotation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19988) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19988)

## 171. Morphology-Decoupled Segmentation for Multiple Tunnel Defects Under Complex Environments

- **作者**：Mohan Ren, Hang Li, Yifei Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：16 pages, 7 figures

Automated tunnel-lining inspection must segment different defect geometries: cracks are thin, elongated, and connectivity-sensitive, whereas leakage, lining falling off, and segment damage are spatially extensive and context-dependent. Existing approaches generally apply a common decoder across defect classes or introduce boundary, topology, and directional mechanisms as isolated enhancements. This paper introduces Morphology-Decoupled Segmentation (MDS), in which defect morphology becomes the organising principle for feature routing, decoding, and interaction. A supervised routing module partitions multiscale encoder features into complementary linear and areal streams. The linear stream employs Deformable Strip Attention with image-conditioned direction hypotheses to preserve crack continuity without assuming fixed horizontal or vertical orientations, while the areal stream combines Pyramid Pooling with feature-pyramid fusion to capture diffuse boundaries and regional context. The representations are coupled through gated cross-morphology attention, allowing complementary information exchange while retaining morphology-specific features. A resolution-adaptive tiling and probability-blending pipeline preserves fine defects across variable-resolution imagery. MDS achieves 81.17% mIoU on the internal validation set and improves foreground image-level recognition over a backbone-matched SegDINO model, primarily for areal defects. Controlled ablations demonstrate the complementary contributions of routing supervision and cross-stream interaction while revealing a trade-off between areal-class performance and crack specialisation. The submitted model achieved 67.55% mIoU on an organiser-controlled hidden test, and the team placed second. Selective dynamic INT8 quantisation reduced model storage by 2.83 times with negligible accuracy loss.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19981) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19981)

## 172. CreaTrace: Video Creative Plagiarism Detection and Evidence Tracing

- **作者**：Chang Liu, Sheng Li, Zhenxing Qian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Video creative plagiarism refers to the unauthorized reproduction of creative ideas across videos despite substantial differences in visual appearance, posing a new challenge to video copyright protection. However, existing video copy detection methods primarily focus on visual content similarity, struggling to capture abstract creative that are entangled with specific visual realizations. To address this issue, we introduce a novel task, named Video Creative Plagiarism Detection (VCPD), and propose an iterative creative evidence tracing framework, termed CreaTrace, to verify creative similarity and accurately localize the corresponding evidence segments. Specifically, we design a Multimodal Semantic Event Segmentation strategy to divide similar long videos into coherent narrative events, facilitating event-level dataset construction and retrieving similar segments. Based on these events, CreaTrace employs Coarse Creative Matching to identify candidate event pairs through narrative semantics and camera language. Then CreaTrace presents Creative Decoupling to disentangle abstract creative patterns from specific visual realizations. Meanwhile, Creative Swap Contrastive Learning is devised to enhance creative consistency through cross-realization feature swapping. Benefiting from the above designs, our CreaTrace enables accurate creative similarity detection and temporal localization, while providing interpretable evidence report. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of CreaTrace across diverse creative plagiarism scenarios.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19976) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19976)

## 173. Multimodal AI in Healthcare: Review of Vision-Language Foundation Models for Real-World Medical Applications

- **作者**：Taha Razzaq, Murtaza Taj, Asim Iqbal
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 Pages, 3 figures, 3 Tables

The emergence of foundation models has marked a transformative shift in AI, enabling robust generalization across diverse downstream tasks through putative zero-shot learning. Large Language Models and Vision Language Models have demonstrated strong capabilities in tasks such as image interpretation, report generation, and question answering by effectively learning from multimodal data-images paired with associated text often with minimal supervision. In the healthcare domain, this ability to align visual and textual information reduces the reliance on extensive manual annotations, as models can leverage existing clinical reports and imaging data to learn meaningful representations. This integration holds promise for improving diagnostic support, treatment planning, and overall patient care, even in data constrained settings. In this review, we provide a definitive taxonomy of the medical VLM landscape, tracing the evolution from early Contrastive Alignment and Generative MLLMs to the cutting edge frontiers of Dense Pixel Grounding, Sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE), and Reasoning Incentivized (RL) architectures. We critically examine the "medical bottleneck" identifying the persistent challenges of data scarcity, the "hallucination" risks in generative diagnostics, the computational strain of 3D volumetric processing, and the lack of standardized, clinically grounded evaluation metrics.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19958) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19958)

## 174. FinVision: Multimodal AI for Visualizing Financial Narratives

- **作者**：Yixuan Tang, Hui Wang, Yi Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Financial narratives contain rich information about firms' performance and business conditions. Conventional text analysis often summarizes this information into numerical representations, which may not fully preserve the dynamic structure described in the narrative. In this study, we develop FinVision, a domain-specific multimodal generative model that translates financial narratives into visual representations of contemporaneous market dynamics. Using 87,940 paired observations of Management Discussion and Analysis (MD&A) disclosures and stock price charts, FinVision learns the average relationship between a firm's narrative account of a fiscal year and its stock-price dynamics over the same period. Given an MD&A alone, the trained model generates a text-implied stock chart, extending financial text analysis beyond numerical representations toward text-to-visual generation. We validate FinVision in two ways. First, held-out tests show that the model generates stock-chart representations that closely resemble observed charts and outperform direct LLM prompting, general-purpose text-to-image models, and the same architecture without financial-domain adaptation. Second, we examine the economic validity of the generated representations by comparing text-implied and observed stock charts. The resulting discrepancy is systematically associated with weaker investor responses around subsequent 10-K filings and with worse subsequent market and operating outcomes. Overall, our findings show how domain-adapted multimodal generative AI can create a new visual representation of financial text and provide an additional modality for studying business communication.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19956) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19956)

## 175. Two-Stage Prompt Learning: A Unified Framework for Person ReID without Clothing Hypothesis

- **作者**：Yuxuan Xiao, Shanshan Zhang, Shengcai Liao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Person re-identification (ReID) in real-world scenarios involves three clothes conditions: the same clothes (SC), changed clothes (CC), and the unknown (GEN) scenario where clothes changes are not specified. Existing methods typically train separate SC- or CC-specific models, which limits generalization across all conditions. Meanwhile, pre-trained visual-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP have shown strong generalization ability in various vision tasks, motivating recent ReID methods to incorporate textual prompts. However, to distinguish SC and CC, prior approaches rely on fixed predefined instructions, which lack learnable context embeddings in prompts and cannot effectively handle the GEN scenario. To overcome these limitations, we propose Two-Stage Prompt Learning (TSPL), a unified framework that integrates clothes-identity decoupling and scenario-aware task prompts within VLMs. In Stage 1, appearance-decomposed prompt learning disentangles clothes and identity information under frozen encoders, producing stable embeddings where clothes cues can be selectively preserved or suppressed. In Stage 2, task-aware prompt learning trains the image encoder with learnable context embeddings guided by Stage-1 representations, enabling robust adaptation across SC, CC, and GEN conditions. Extensive experiments on five benchmarks show that TSPL achieves state-of-the-art performance under all scenarios, improving mAP by up to 9.2% on LTCC (SC) and by 6.4% on VC-Clothes (GEN), respectively. The code will be released at https://github.com/YuSuen/TSPL.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19939) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19939)

## 176. SAGE-VQA: A Training-Free, Self-Evolving Agentic Framework with Grounded Evidence for Knowledge-Based Visual Question Answering

- **作者**：Ruirui Shen, Jianqi Gao, Xiangfeng Luo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Knowledge-based visual question answering (KB-VQA) aims to combine visual information with external knowledge to answer user questions. Existing methods face two main bottlenecks: visual perception errors can propagate into hallucinations, and generalization across scenarios remains limited. To address these issues, we propose SAGE-VQA (Self-evolving Agent framework with Grounded Evidence), a training-free agentic framework that combines evidence verification with experience evolution. Our key insight is to explore an Evidence-Driven Adaptive Reasoning (EDAR) strategy. It accurately acquires and verifies question-relevant fine-grained visual information. It also extracts and refines reusable experience to support robust reasoning across tasks. Specifically, we design a Co-verified Visual Grounding module that verifies question-relevant visual information and provides grounded evidence for subsequent reasoning. We further introduce a Self-evolving Hierarchical Memory (SHM) mechanism. SHM organizes experiences into declarative and procedural memories based on their nature. These memories can be reused in subsequent reasoning and continually refined through feedback, without updating model parameters. Experimental results show that our method outperforms competing methods based on lightweight open-source vision-language models (VLMs) as well as those based on closed-source VLMs. Moreover, our method can serve as a plug-and-play reasoning framework for existing VLMs without modifying their architectures.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19933) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19933)

## 177. Does synthetic pre-training pay off on a microcontroller? Accuracy, latency and energy across two MCUs

- **作者**：Achintya Yadav
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AR, cs.CV
- **备注**：43 pages, 5 figures, 14 tables; supplementary tables, figures and notes as ancillary files. Analysis plan registered on OSF (doi:10.17605/OSF.IO/BPQGA). Under review

Rendered images are cheap to label, but a microcontroller classifier must survive 8-bit integer (INT8) quantisation and compute correctly on the chip. Five models, from MobileNetV2 at 224 px to a 61K-parameter convolutional neural network (CNN), were fine-tuned on a six-class scene dataset with and without a synthetic stage, against thresholds fixed in advance, and deployed on an ESP32-S3 and an RP2040. With full real data the stage gave no detectable gain. For the main deployable model the INT8 difference was +0.312 percentage points (pp), its interval below the +1.6 pp gain recomputed from the source study; quantisation did not detectably change it, nor did model size. With 10% of the real data the stage paid off for the CNN, by +3.438 pp on one seed and +2.715 pp over three; its cause is open. On the ESP32-S3 the main model took 152.7 ms and 16.3 mJ above idle per inference, and adding the stage changed neither beyond preset bounds. The model's INT8 outputs matched TensorFlow Lite's reference kernels exactly, but only after a memory overrun in a vendor kernel was patched. On the RP2040 the same model took 1.80 s and 27.3 mJ above idle, about twelve times slower, and the largest 96 px model exceeded the board's flash-capacity limit. Neither the RP2040 nor the host interpreter used for scoring reproduced the reference arithmetic. A synthetic stage is worth adding only when real data are scarce, and on-device INT8 outputs should be checked against reference kernels, not inferred from host scores.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19931) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19931)

## 178. Lao Siji: Adapting openpilot to Closed-Loop End-to-End Driving in Neural Reconstruction

- **作者**：Chengzhi Gao, Yongzhi Liu, Jinchang Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages

We present Lao Siji, an end-to-end driving policy that ranks third in the 2026 NVIDIA AlpaSim E2E Driving Challenge, ahead of most entries built on foundation models. Instead of training from scratch on a general-purpose vision backbone, we adapt openpilot, an open-source L2 production model. We freeze its vision encoder and fine-tune the policy layers with an ego-state adapter and a route memory. On closed-loop rollouts in neural-reconstructed scenes, we supervise the policy with a privileged map teacher for lateral self-correction and a body-extent loss for road and obstacle clearance, while distilling the base model's remaining outputs to retain car following. Ablations show that self-correction samples carry the gain, while the clearance loss substitutes for them where no teacher exists. Longitudinal safety instead rests on the base model's lead-vehicle estimate behind a rule-based speed layer. Longer training, imitating human logs, and human-log speed targets yield either no measurable gain or adverse effects. Without further training, Lao Siji also places among the leaders on HUGSIM and Odyssey. The final training takes only one GPU-hour and shifts the weights by 3.9% from the shipped model. Code will be released after review.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19922) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19922)

## 179. HEmIF-Bench: Benchmarking H&E-to-mIF Models BeyondVirtual-StainFidelity

- **作者**：Fuxian Zhao, Hyokeun Park
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 3 figures, 2 tables

Hematoxylin and eosin (H&E)-to-multiplex immunofluorescence (mIF) generators predict spatial protein expression from routine histology, yet pixel-level marker reconstruction does not establish downstream utility. We present HEmIF-Bench, evaluating five frozen generators across eight datasets spanning molecular, cellular, tissue, and patient scales. HEmIF-Bench decouples internal encoder features from generated outputs, benchmarking both against the pathology foundation reference UNI2. Mirroring the clinical workflow where pathologists rely on mIF to define cell phenotypes, delineate tertiary lymphoid structures (TLSs) and germinal centers (GCs), and stratify patient risk, we evaluate external CD3 recovery, single-cell classification, dense nuclear segmentation, TLS/GC segmentation, and patient survival prediction. Across the benchmark, foundation-model-based MIPHEI-ViT leads among the generators, while non-foundation models show task-specific strengths. Comparing the two routes reveals that internal encoder features generally outperform generated outputs, and larger output marker panels do not guarantee stronger downstream utility across datasets. Moreover, neither internal encoder features nor generated outputs consistently surpass frozen UNI2. These findings show that pixel-level stain fidelity does not equate to downstream utility, and that generators trained at current paired-data scales have yet to surpass large-scale pure-H&E foundation models. With the expansion of paired translation cohorts and the advancement of foundation-based generative architectures, HEmIF-Bench establishes a definitive benchmark for tracking when H&E-to-mIF generation crosses the threshold to deliver genuine value beyond routine histology for tumor microenvironment analysis and precision oncology.

## 180. Remember, Replay, Adapt: Learning from Past Experience in Online Vision-and-Language Navigation

- **作者**：Chenhao Ding, Jizhou Han, Lixin Xiu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Existing feedback-based methods for online vision-and-language navigation (VLN) primarily learn from current episode outcomes, leaving previously acquired feedback underutilized. Inspired by human episodic memory, we propose R2A, an online VLN adaptation framework that continually reuses successful and failed navigation experiences for policy learning. Specifically, Success-Failure Memory (SFM) retains successful and failed navigation episodes with acquired feedback and retrieves task-relevant experiences based on incoming instructions, enabling historical feedback to guide subsequent navigation. Prior-Anchored Adaptation (PAA) learns from these experiences by reinforcing successful trajectories and suppressing failed ones relative to a frozen pretrained policy, while regularizing behavioral drift. Together, these modules enable continual feedback reuse and stable policy adaptation without source-domain data or expert supervision. Extensive experiments across three benchmarks, REVERIE, R2R, and R2R-CE, and five navigation backbones demonstrate consistent performance gains. On the REVERIE validation unseen split, R2A improves DUET's SR from 46.98% to 69.84% and SPL from 33.73% to 50.98%.

## 181. HIVE-ECIA: Identity-Aware Historical Evidence Acquisition for Bandwidth-Efficient Multi-Object Tracking

- **作者**：Hai Nguyen, Tarik Taleb, Qize Guo
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Panoramic multi-object tracking (MOT) requires reliable identity association across a 360° field of view, yet bandwidth-constrained operation forces a trade-off between low-resolution (LR) identity ambiguity and costly high-resolution (HR) transmission. We propose HIVE-ECIA, an identity-aware framework that selectively retrieves historical HR evidence to recover fragmented identities without modifying detection boxes or the underlying tracker. An ECIA Base-Guided query policy determines when additional HR evidence is warranted; HIVE ranks historical LR observations to select an informative retrieval timestamp; and ECIA Full integrates the retrieved HR crop through learned gated fusion and conservative identity safeguards. We further introduce DualPano-MOT, a real-world dual-fisheye panoramic evaluation dataset featuring controlled pedestrian interactions, panorama-seam challenges, and occlusions, with model-assisted, manually verified tracking annotations. On held-out QuadTrack sequences and zero-shot DualPano-MOT, HIVE-ECIA improves identity association across four MOT trackers without tracker-specific retraining. Across these trackers, identity switches decrease by up to 21.8% and 23.4%, while the largest IDF1 gains reach 3.81 and 6.74 percentage points, respectively. Under an offline JPEG-based acquisition model, our framework reduces encoded data volume by 89.2% and 79.3% relative to continuous Full-HR acquisition.

## 182. Illusion of Explainability: A Sanity Check for Spectral Band Attribution in Satellite Time-Series Crop Classification

- **作者**：Sumit Tahiliani, Avi Mathur, Sandarbh Agarwal et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted at the 4th Computer Vision for Earth Observation (CV4EO) Workshop at WACV 2027

Attribution methods are commonly validated through convergence across methods and consistency with domain knowledge, yet neither criterion establishes whether identified features are necessary for a task. This limitation is particularly relevant in multispectral remote sensing, where overlapping spectral information makes individual bands potentially substitutable. We investigate this gap in crop-type classification using GradientSHAP, Shapley sampling, and Occlusion on a U-TAE segmentation model trained on Sentinel-2 time series from the PASTIS benchmark. All three methods identify vegetation-sensitive spectral regions consistent with domain knowledge, while Shapley and Occlusion exhibit strong ordering sensitivity in deletion and insertion tests. However, these properties do not reliably predict retraining performance. Aggregate attribution consensus masks substantial per-class disagreement, with pairwise mean Spearman correlations ranging from 0.09 to 0.79. Models retrained from scratch using the five lowest-ranked bands perform comparably to or better than those using the five highest-ranked bands. Furthermore, attribution rankings from an independently trained model with identical configuration and seed identify a different top-five subset that yields better retraining performance. Analysis of all 252 five-band subsets reveals consistently low effective dimensionality (1.35 to 2.62), suggesting substantial spectral redundancy. These findings demonstrate that plausible and apparently faithful attribution rankings do not necessarily identify task-relevant bands for improved retrained performance, highlighting the limitations of band-level feature attribution in spectrally redundant input spaces.

## 183. PanDWM: Panoramic 4D Driving World Models

- **作者**：Jiajun Sun, Zeyu Zhang, Zeyu Ren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/PanDWM, Website: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/PanDWM

Driving world models usually generate videos from six pinhole cameras with separate optical centres and small overlaps, and must learn to keep these views consistent. We present PanDWM, which stitches them into one $360^\circ$ panoramic band. Camera boundaries then fall inside one image, so keeping the views consistent means keeping the image continuous, and each pixel still records its camera ray. Conditioned on layouts, a causal student generates the band window by window in four steps; it is distilled from a causal teacher and trained on its own rollouts. A feed-forward decoder lifts each frame along these rays into 3D Gaussians, and static Gaussians from neighbouring frames complete them into 4D scenes. On nuScenes, scored in one stitched $360^\circ$ view, PanDWM has lower FID and FVD than the multi-camera baselines at $8$-$46\times$ their speed, and keeps its 50-step teacher's FID while lowering FVD by 22-25%.

## 184. Passable for Whom? Few-Shot Personal Calibration of Sidewalk Accessibility Perception

- **作者**：Wesley Maia, Angel Martinez-Sanchez, Maitrayee Keskar et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Whether a sidewalk is passable depends on who is trying to pass: people who use the same mobility aid disagree about the same image, so one label per scene, or per aid, is wrong for many of them. We introduce few-shot personal accessibility perception: from K answers of a person never seen before, predict their judgment of sidewalk scenes never seen before. PassCal separates a shared description of the scene from the person's interpretation of it. The description comes from the internal state of a frozen vision-language model (VLM) and from barrier type, severity and context properties predicted by a detector trained on 14.9k public audits; the interpretation, a calibration of the person's response style and a weighting of the properties, is fit from their answers. On 8,883 judgments in which each of 135 verified mobility-aid users rated the same 50 sidewalk scenes, with people and scenes both held out, eight answers lower the Brier score from 0.550 to 0.479. The same VLM prompted with those answers reaches 0.562, and the same calibration without the scene 0.521. Conformal risk control turns predictions into passable, blocked or "depends on you" statements with a bounded expected rate of wrong statements over people; at 5%, eight answers raise the share of decisive scenes from 27% to 42%. Audit-supervised properties improve all sixteen vision and VLM encoders we test and outperform attributes from a sidewalk navigation VLM.

## 185. AGPF: Dynamic Source Selection for Feature Pyramid Fusion

- **作者**：Xu Yang, Gady Agam
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Feature pyramids provide dense predictors with features at several spatial resolutions. However, it remains largely unexplored which pyramid levels each output location should aggregate information from. We investigate this question by allowing each output location to attend to features from every pyramid level and measuring how attention is distributed across scales. Surprisingly, locations on the finest output grid allocate approximately 75\% of their attention to features two levels coarser, rather than to the current or adjacent levels. Even on the same output grid, the balance shifts toward coarser features as object size increases. These observations motivate Asymmetric Global Pyramid Fusion (AGPF), a neck that preserves the original output grids while allowing each location to dynamically select its contextual sources across scales. Each location interacts with a multiscale memory whose features are aligned in image coordinates, enabling flexible cross-scale aggregation without altering prediction resolutions. Compared with FPN, AGPF improves all 19 object detection and all seven instance segmentation configurations evaluated on COCO. A single fusion block improves Cascade Mask R-CNN by 1.7 box AP and 2.3 mask AP, and the gains consistently transfer to semantic and panoptic segmentation.

## 186. Beyond Vehicle-Centric Data: Scaling Autonomous Driving through Roadside-to-Vehicle Generation

- **作者**：Yichen Zhang, Zihan Wang, Hao Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Training autonomous driving models requires large and diverse datasets, collected primarily by vehicles. Scaling them entails investment in fleet operation, data processing and annotation. Road infrastructure records traffic and multi-vehicle interactions, yet the viewpoint gap between roadside and onboard sensors limits its direct use for training. Here we introduce AnyR2V, a framework that transforms multimodal roadside observations into seven-camera surround-view videos for different vehicle perspectives. It aggregates sequential observations into a shared scene representation of geometry and road-user motion, then renders appearance, depth and layout conditions along target-vehicle trajectories. Supervised by spatiotemporally aligned roadside-vehicle pairs, AnyR2V generates surround-view videos guided by scene structure and road-user motion. Existing roadside 3D bounding boxes are transformed into target-vehicle coordinates and paired with generated images, allowing one traffic recording and its annotations to yield training data from multiple vehicle perspectives. We construct R2V360, a dataset of 89 real traffic sequences at an urban intersection, with aligned multimodal roadside data and onboard surround-view videos. AnyR2V outperforms six scene reconstruction methods in image fidelity and in depth and semantic agreement between generated and real onboard images. We further evaluate generated videos along other vehicles' trajectories by adding them to real training data. Adding generated frames equal to 50% of the original real training frame count improves mean average precision (mAP) by 3.3-3.4 AP-points across three 3D object detectors on the real onboard test set, relative to real-data-only baselines. These results demonstrate that roadside traffic records can provide training data from multiple vehicle perspectives, offering a new data source for autonomous driving.

## 187. PRISM: Primitive-Integrated Separable Modulation with Priority Visual Memory Queue for Compositional Zero-Shot Learning

- **作者**：Jianhua Wang, Zhihang Zheng, Yang Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Compositional Zero-Shot Learning (CZSL) recognizes unseen attribute--object pairs by recombining primitives from seen compositions. Existing methods stop sharing evidence at deployment: fixed prototypes cannot exploit unlabeled test streams, while composition-wise refinement cannot propagate corrections across pairs sharing a primitive. We introduce PRISM, an online CZSL framework that extends primitive sharing to test-time adaptation. Its Hierarchical Knowledge Accumulation Module (HiKAM) factorizes prototype migration into composition-, attribute-, and object-level residual banks. A Priority Visual Memory Queue (PVMQ), initialized by Prototype Queue Initialization (PQI), grounds text updates with bounded visual evidence. The same adaptive core supports exhaustive Full PRISM and recall-calibrated Adaptive PRISM-Lite, which selects candidate budgets from held-out ranks. Comprehensive closed- and open-world experiments on UT-Zappos, MIT-States, and C-GQA demonstrate the state of the art, with Full PRISM achieving the best open-world AUC and harmonic mean on all three benchmarks and the best closed-world results on UT-Zappos and C-GQA. Fine-grained long-tail and non-stationary primitive-block evaluations reveal gains on under-supported concepts and stable adaptation under prolonged skew. Adaptive PRISM-Lite further delivers substantial efficiency gains in the full open-world space with only a small accuracy cost. PRISM thus combines primitive-shared online adaptation, multimodal grounding, and candidate-aware inference.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19828) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19828)

## 188. When Does Reusing Layers Help Multimodal Embeddings?

- **作者**：Zhizhang Hu, Yue Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We ask whether a compact universal multimodal embedding models can produce better search embeddings by running the same layers again. Looped transformers add computation with nearly unchanged model size, making them an appealing alternative to a larger encoder. Their success in language-model reasoning and recent use for retrieval motivate the idea, but higher retrieval scores alone do not reveal what an extra pass contributes. We study eleven loop designs and training variants on three checkpoints of one vision-language architecture. Our protocol distinguishes whether a model uses its extra pass, whether its image states respond to the query, and whether those updates improve retrieval. We prove that an image placed before the query remains query-independent through repeated layers when information only travels forward. We then open a query-to-image path in three ways, including order-alternating recurrence, which places the query first on the next pass. Each path produces query-sensitive image states. For bidirectional attention and order alternation, freezing later visual updates does not lower accuracy. On all 78 datasets of the Massive Multimodal Embedding Benchmark version 2 (MMEB-V2), a standard added loop loses 0.46 points against its trained control; an intermediate generative stage changes the effect to +0.36. A model pretrained with recurrence gains +4.23 points over its own loop-removed control and benefits from query-conditioned image updates when answering visual questions or locating objects from text.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19827) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19827)

## 189. The Flip Test: Video World Models Fail to Learn the World State

- **作者**：Mi Luo, Lijun Yu, Adams Wei Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **备注**：Project Page: https://flip-test.github.io/

Video world models promise to predict the future of a scene from its present. Do current models keep this promise? We probe this question counterfactually. When a small change to the present flips the future, such as a box resting a few millimeters before or past a table edge, a model that tracks the state of the world must flip its prediction too. We build FlipTest, a three-level hierarchy of increasingly strict tests over 1,680 initial frames from 10 simulated and 15 real-world tasks. At the base, visual minimal pairs, two nearly identical frames whose true futures differ, test whether predictions change across the boundary; matched controls go further and test whether they change only where the future does; and dense sweeps, the strictest level, locate exactly where they change. All 11 video world models we evaluate fall short. The best averages 18.3% pair accuracy over real and simulated scenes, below the 25% of random guessing. Models flip as often on steps that leave the future unchanged as on steps that change it, and their response stays flat across the sweeps. On the same frames, a vision-language model is selective and traces a near S-shaped curve near the boundary, and a coding agent that measures the image comes close to the true boundary. This implies that the deciding information is in the frame, and video models just miss it. We therefore urge the community to rethink world modeling as pure video prediction. Next-frame prediction must do more than draw plausible pixels; it must also identify the causal states that control future dynamics.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19822) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19822)

## 190. Pros4D: Modeling Evolving 4D Worlds in Latent Space for Dynamic Spatial Reasoning

- **作者**：Rongxin Gao, Dongxuan Liu, Chu Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://pros4d.github.io/

Dynamic spatial reasoning from monocular video enables vision-language models (VLMs) to understand how 3D environments evolve over time, supporting physical world understanding and embodied intelligence. Existing efforts primarily address observed-dynamics understanding, whereas agents in dynamic environments also require future-dynamics prediction beyond the observation boundary. Moreover, external geometric processing and textual descriptions can increase inference complexity or lose information when representing continuous dynamics. These limitations motivate a shared internal state that preserves observed dynamics and remains predictive beyond the input video. Accordingly, we present Pros4D, a framework for modeling evolving 4D worlds in latent space that unifies retrospective understanding of observed-dynamics with prospective reasoning for future-dynamics prediction. Specifically, Pros4D-Engine derives geometry-grounded supervision and constructs paired examples for both tasks, while Pros4D-Bench evaluates them across eight spatial task categories spanning dynamic cameras, objects, and scenes. At the model level, Pros4D compresses observed dynamics into latent observed states and autoregressively rolls them forward into latent future states. 4D Latent Alignment (4DLA) grounds these states through complementary visual-semantic and geometric supervision, while joint language supervision connects them to task reasoning. Experiments across benchmarks show consistent improvements over strong baselines in both capabilities.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19803) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19803)

## 191. Fine-Grained RLAIF for Visual Safety: Learning to Zoom into Hazards for Multimodal Safety Alignment

- **作者**：Chunxue Xu, Yiwei Wang, Yujun Cai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent multimodal safety alignment heavily relies on a text-centric paradigm, assuming risks are explicit in language or globally prominent in images. However, real-world hazards are often visually subtle or peripheral, causing existing models to fail due to their reliance on coarse, global visual representations. In this work, we study fine-grained visual safety alignment and introduce a challenging 1,200-sample benchmark across four domains, where hazards necessitate localized inspection. To address this, we propose Fine-Grained RLAIF (FGR), a reinforcement learning framework that casts image zoom-in as a learnable perception policy. FGR employs a decomposed reward design to jointly align three core safety behaviors: (i) where to look via a visual evidence reward, (ii) when to act via a tool-formatting constraint, and (iii) how to reason via visually grounded safety explanations. FGR achieves accuracy ranging from 86.71% to 93.00%, consistently outperforming strong multimodal and agent-based baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19786) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19786)

## 192. A Threshold-Free Visual-Semantic Matching Metric for Evaluating Consistency in Subject-Driven Image Generation

- **作者**：Bowei Shan, Heng Yin
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 5 figures

In subject-driven text-to-image generation, quantitatively evaluating localized visual consistency across varying poses and environments remains a major challenge. Existing metrics either rely on global image embeddings (CLIP, DINO) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs) that pool spatial representations and miss fine-grained textural artifacts, or depend on scalar threshold-gated dense metrics such as Visual-Semantic Matching (VSM) that suffer from point-alignment dropouts, severe threshold sensitivity, and score instability. To overcome these limitations, we propose TF-VSM, a fully continuous, threshold-free visual-consistency metric paired with an extended feature-disentanglement retraining schedule for dual-branch ResNet feature-aggregation networks. Rather than enforcing rigid binary cutoffs $(T_s,T_v)$, TF-VSM weights every object point by a non-linear semantic match confidence $w_j=ReLU(\hat{s}_j)^2$ and continuously integrates visual similarity over all consistency levels $r\in[0,1]$, yielding a closed-form confidence-weighted score without any hand-tuned hyperparameters. On a controlled 100-pair benchmark with ground-truth inpainting oracles, TF-VSM attains Pearson $0.638$ and Spearman $0.674$ oracle correlation, improving over the pretrained VSM baseline ($0.454/0.589$) by $+0.171/+0.116$ after retraining, and drastically outperforming CLIP ($-0.052/-0.004$), DINO ($0.087/0.121$), and VLMs ($0.096/0.003$). Six diagnostic ablations confirm that smooth confidence weighting is the right mechanism, that hard semantic gating is brittle (dropping to $0.533$ Pearson at $T_s{=}0.8$), and that the threshold-free metric is essentially invariant to the training-time loss balance $α$ that the thresholded metric still depends on.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19780) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19780)

## 193. When Backdoors Can Be Switched Off: Suppression-Aware Backdoor Attacks on Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Nur Afsa Syeda, Mohamed Elmahallawy
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CR

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models map visual observations, robot states, and natural-language instructions to robotic actions, but their multimodal interfaces introduce new security risks. Existing VLA backdoor attacks primarily study trigger-based activation and stealthy action manipulation, leaving conditional suppression of malicious behavior underexplored. We investigate suppression-aware backdoors, which combine conjunctive trigger activation with instruction-conditioned suppression. We instantiate this design through three variants: Cross-Modal Trigger, combining robot-state and spatially separated visual triggers; Self-Suppressing Trigger, requiring a state trigger and a [DISRUPT] instruction token for activation, with [RESTORE] token suppressing the attack; and Multi-Trigger, additionally requiring a visual trigger while retaining suppression even when physical triggers persist. We evaluate these attacks using SmolVLA on two SO-100 manipulation datasets against four baselines: State Backdoor, BadVLA, TabVLA, and SilentDrift. Self-Suppressing and Multi-Trigger reduce Isolation Forest detection from 96--98% to 1--18% and action-space l divergence by 10--14X, achieving 63--71% attack success rates with perfect clean-task performance. Experiments with OpenVLA on LIBERO further show that the attacks transfer to a 7B-parameter policy, evading STRIP and action-distribution-shift detection. Our findings establish conditional suppression as an important dimension for evaluating backdoor threats in VLA policies.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19774) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19774)

## 194. SemLite-GS: Semantic-Selective Parallel Gaussian Splatting for Lightweight Building Reconstruction from UAV Oblique Imagery

- **作者**：Tengfei Wang, Xin Wang, Zihan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Lightweight 3D building models support urban spatial analysis, but their production often requires dense scene reconstruction, building extraction, and mesh simplification. Although 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is more efficient than conventional multi-view stereo, existing large-scale methods lack semantic selection and parallel instance-level training. We present SemLite-GS, a building-centric framework that reconstructs lightweight building meshes from large-scale UAV oblique imagery. First, an orthographic reference map establishes globally consistent building identities, which are propagated to oblique views to form camera-instance pairs and regions of interest. Second, semantic-selective parallel PGSR training jointly processes multiple pairs within relevant image regions while updating only target Gaussians; contextual Gaussians remain fixed to preserve visibility. Third, architectural planes extracted from rendered depth, opacity, and normal maps guide signed distance field optimization, producing meshes with adaptive polygon allocation and well-defined structural boundaries. Experiments on multiple large-scale UAV datasets demonstrate that our SemLite-GS substantially reduces building reconstruction time compared with representative full-scene 3DGS methods. With comparable polygon budgets, SemLite-GS achieves superior preservation of dominant planar structures and sharp architectural transitions compared with conventional dense reconstruction followed by mesh decimation. The source code is available at https://github.com/IRIS-LAB-whu/SemLite-GS.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19773) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19773)

## 195. JEPA4R: Embodied JEPA World Models with 4D Point Planning

- **作者**：Jiarui Dong, Zhiheng Liang, Yanjun Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/JEPA4R, Website: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/JEPA4R

Joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA) world models predict future embeddings instead of pixels and plan in latent space. Two challenges remain: 2D images leave the metric geometry that manipulation acts on implicit and entangled with appearance, and what these models imagine stays hidden, as they are mostly judged in simulation by short-horizon latent planning. We present JEPA4R, a JEPA world model that observes single-depth-camera point clouds, predicts and plans in their latent space, and acts through a policy head on its frozen latents; we call this 4D point planning. A decoder on the frozen world model shows what it imagines as 4D point clouds, with a mean point error within 1.1 cm over 200 steps. Under the policy-learning protocols of OGBench and CortexBench, JEPA4R leads pixel JEPA models on OGBench Cube-Single, Cube-Double, Cube-Triple and Scene, and ranks first on MetaWorld and Adroit Relocate with 98.4% and 88.0%, above the runner-up, R3M, at 96.0% and 65.3%. Trained only in a digital twin, JEPA4R and its policy heads run zero-shot on the real arm with both a gripper and a dexterous hand.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19771) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19771)

## 196. When Does an AUROC Comparison Survive a Change in Population Mix?

- **作者**：Mahtab Movahhedrad, Hamidreza Hasani Balyani, Seyed Pouyan Mousavi Davoudi
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 5 figures. This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible

Does an AUROC comparison survive a change in the proportions of tasks, populations, or image corruptions? A pooled-AUROC ranking of failure detectors or uncertainty scores describes one particular mix of groups. We study two fixed scores for a common binary target under mixtures of fixed group-conditional laws. Using the cross-group AUC decomposition, we prove that every rational symmetric quadratic comparison geometry is realizable by finite scalar-score distributions. Universal dominance is thus coNP-hard, and even exhaustive checks of every mixture on fewer than $K$ groups can miss a $K$-group reversal. A grid bound gives a dimension-uniform approximation certificate with sharp $1/m$ order. Common score marginals across groups make the comparison numerator linear, with an error envelope for imperfect alignment. These results yield exact finite-bank audits, explicit reversal mixtures and distances, and simultaneous population statements. For failure detection with a frozen ResNet-50 on ImageNet-C, 15 of 18 confidence-score comparisons win in every corruption family on a discovery split, and all 15 are finite-bank certified over all family mixtures on a held-out split; over all 15 corruption types, of 14 discovery winners the corrected grid certifies 10, leaves 3 unresolved, and finds one confirmation reversal of at least 0.05 AUROC. An audit of all 11,019 released Stable LM 2 12B outputs and 35 uncertainty methods finds 66 confirmation-set comparisons that win within all four tasks yet reverse under mixing. Discovery-fitted marginal rank alignment removes all 66; an alignment-error envelope certifies 83 comparisons. Label checks also change several selected reversals. The findings distinguish robustness of frozen scores from robustness after calibration; they do not establish human-error detection, robustness to unseen corruptions, or generalization beyond the evaluated banks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19762) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19762)

## 197. Spatiotemporal Reconstruction of Auroral Precipitations with Neural Fields

- **作者**：Nazar Misyats, Mizuki Fukizawa, Yoshimasa Tanaka et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, physics.space-ph
- **备注**：Accepted to the IEEE/CVF Winter Conference on Applications of Computer Vision (WACV 2027). Camera-ready version with supplementary material. 17 pages, 15 figures

Auroras provide remote optical observations of energetic electrons precipitating into the upper atmosphere, and whose energy distribution encodes key information about large scale magnetospheric processes. Recovering the underlying electron precipitation field from ground-based camera images of auroras remains a challenging inverse problem. Existing auroral tomography techniques are often limited to simplified static settings, discrete spatial and energy grids, or strong assumptions on the electron energy spectrum. We propose to model the dynamic auroral electron precipitation field via a temporally conditioned neural network. The state of the precipitation field at each frame is represented by a per-frame latent code driven by learned discrete dynamics. A shared decoder maps horizontal positions and latent states to electron energy spectra. The predicted flux is converted to multi-wavelength volume emission rates and rendered into camera images accounting for delayed emissions. We evaluate the method on synthetic dynamic auroral sequences and real multi-view auroral videos, showing that the proposed representation produces temporally coherent precipitation reconstructions and image projections consistent with the observations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19757) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19757)

## 198. Drift-Conditioned Structural Replay for Continual Cardiac Ultrasound Segmentation

- **作者**：Zahid Ullah, Jaeyoon Seo, Jihie Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Conventional experience replay typically applies fixed preservation constraints to historical exemplars, overlooking variations in how learned representations, predictions, and anatomical structures change during sequential training. To address this limitation, we propose Drift-Conditioned Structural Replay Loss (DCSRL), an exemplar-adaptive supervision mechanism that regulates anatomical preservation according to source-referenced knowledge drift. DCSRL operates within a reliability-guided replay framework that selects informative historical exemplars using anatomical content and boundary characteristics while adaptively regulating replay during training. For each retained exemplar, DCSRL constructs a frozen source-task reference signature and subsequently measures changes in feature representations, class probabilities, boundary responses, and predicted anatomical structures. These complementary drift measurements are aggregated and normalized to generate bounded, exemplar-specific coefficients that dynamically adjust boundary and structural replay losses without modifying the underlying memory-selection policy. We evaluate DCSRL under sequential learning across CAMUS, CardiacNet, and EchoNet-Dynamic, comparing its performance with regularization-, distillation-, and replay-based continual-learning approaches. Experimental findings indicate that DCSRL improves overall final segmentation performance and anatomical boundary preservation, particularly under heterogeneous imaging conditions, although trade-offs remain in knowledge transfer across tasks. These findings suggest that source-referenced multidimensional drift conditioning offers a promising direction for adaptive anatomical knowledge preservation in continual cardiac ultrasound segmentation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19756) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19756)

## 199. Input-Space versus Feature-Level Conditioning for Robust Facial Expression Recognition

- **作者**：Weihang Yuan
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables

Facial expression recognition (FER) degrades sharply when illumination, exposure or resolution departs from the training distribution. Two remedies dominate practice: enriching the input signal, or conditioning intermediate features on an estimated image quality. The two are rarely compared under matched backbones, training budgets and condition annotations, and their cost is seldom reported. We present a controlled comparison on RAF-DB. Input-space conditioning uses four deterministic, label-free views (logarithmic stretching, inverse-logarithmic stretching, down-up resampling and identity), each processed by a shared backbone with a branch-specific BatchNorm and linear head, with the branch logits averaged. Feature-space conditioning is implemented as FiLM and Conditional BatchNorm under an identical protocol. Across six backbone architectures the input-space variant improves top-1 accuracy on every one (mean +0.94 percentage points; bootstrap interval [0.51, 1.63]; sign test p = 0.016), with a seed-controlled estimate of +0.42 percentage points on ResNet-18, whereas feature-level conditioning yields +0.10 and +0.03 percentage points. We attribute the gap to the information content of the heuristic condition label rather than to the conditioning mechanism. The gain costs 4.00x FLOPs, 4.18x latency and 3.79x training time, i.e. 0.26 percentage points per additional GFLOP, and we state an explicit rule for when the trade is worth making.

## 200. SEAT: Sign-Type Evidence-Audited Transport for Multi-Label Tongue Diagnosis

- **作者**：Liuyi Xu, Qing Wang, Zikai Liang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables

Automated tongue-sign recognition can improve the consistency of non-invasive examinations that otherwise rely on subjective visual judgment. A single tongue image often exhibits multiple coexisting signs characterized by heterogeneous evidence, including localized morphology, global chromaticity, and inter-label dependence. However, existing methods typically rely on shared representations and uniform multi-label heads, making it difficult to verify whether predictions stem from clinically relevant evidence rather than spurious visual correlations. We propose SEAT, an evidence-audited framework that matches distinct evidence types with dedicated prediction and evaluation mechanisms. Built on frozen DINOv2 patch features, SEAT couples mask-guided sign-specific attention for localized morphology, a semi-relaxed optimal transport pathway over LAB/HSV color prototypes to enhance pale-tongue recognition, and a co-occurrence-aware transport regularizer to shape the batch-level predicted label marginal. These components are evaluated through audits matched to their evidence types. On the TongueDx benchmark, SEAT achieves a mean macro-F1 of 72.59 with a standard deviation of 1.04 across six random seeds, with a six-model ensemble reaching 74.15. The ensemble outperforms a backbone-matched baseline and a larger, domain-specialized vision-language model by 8.06 and 2.99 percentage points, respectively. Our results demonstrate that aligning dedicated prediction pathways and audits with sign-specific characteristics improves multi-label accuracy while offering more targeted evidential grounding than uniform post-hoc spatial explanations. Code is available at https://github.com/NEU-TCM-Lab/Sign-type-Evidence-Audited-Transport.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19748) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19748)

## 201. How Far Is Too Far? Object Recognition Declines Monotonically with Semantic Distance

- **作者**：Domenic Bersch, Hai Van Tran, Gemma Roig
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 (Spotlight)

Vision models are sensitive to object-background context, yet it remains unclear how recognition changes as the semantic distance between objects and backgrounds increases. We introduce ImageNet-OOC1k, a benchmark enabling graded control of object-background relationships along a continuous consensus visual-semantic dissimilarity axis. This formulation reveals a highly consistent monotonic decline in recognition accuracy across 35 models spanning convolutional neural networks, Vision Transformers, and vision-language models. Internal analyses of Vision Transformers reveal patterns consistent with aggregation failure, where object evidence is present at the patch level but not consolidated into the final prediction. Using this characterization as a diagnostic probe, a simple spatial re-ranking strategy recovers a subset of these errors and improves in-sample accuracy by up to 5.7 percentage points. These results establish context sensitivity as a continuous and predictable property of modern vision systems, with Vision Transformer analyses suggesting that aggregation limitations may contribute to some failure types even when object evidence remains available.

## 202. Event-based object detection under adverse lighting and motion: lessons for construction sites

- **作者**：Zhuoran Feng, Xiaoshan Zhou
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 12 figures, 9 tables

Vision-based monitoring on construction sites fails at night, at dawn and dusk, in tunnels and under floodlights, when conventional RGB cameras under- or over-expose. Event cameras record per-pixel brightness changes with microsecond latency and over 120 dB of dynamic range, but their benefit for site-relevant object detection has not been quantified on real data. We compare three recent event-based detectors (SMamba, SATE and EvRT-DETR) with an RGB detector trained on the same data, using the pixel-aligned PEOD dataset. On low-light frames (70% of the test set) the best event detector reaches 27.2 AP against 21.3 AP for RGB, and a training-free late fusion of both sensors is the best option in every lighting condition (33.3 AP). An object-level analysis of 53,091 objects shows that brightness has little effect on event detectors, whereas fast motion matters: above 20 pixels per frame RGB finds 11-19% of medium-size objects and EvRT-DETR 39-60%. Activation patching locates the damage caused by losing events at the 1/16-resolution features, with small objects depending on the finest scale, and shows that event detectors fail when few signal events meet added noise. A model-agnostic pruning study keeps about 95% of accuracy with 60% of the input but halves small-object recall. On rendered construction sites, used zero-shot, dimming the floodlights drops RGB to near zero while the event detector keeps most of its AP50. We close with camera-placement guidance for construction sites.

## 203. TIVS: Mapping the Sensitivity Landscape of Visual Token Transformations in MLLMs

- **作者**：Yikang Qiao, Jianye Zeng, Duan Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, 12 figures, 3 tables

Recent efforts to improve the efficiency of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have explored visual token pruning, quantization, and feature compression. However, the properties that determine whether transformed visual tokens remain usable by a frozen decoder are not well characterized. We introduce \textbf{TIVS (Token Identity Versus Statistics)}, a training-free intervention framework that separately studies sequence permutation, token retention, numerical precision, and statistical replacement under controlled conditions. On 192 POPE questions, three token permutations change TinyLLaVA accuracy by only $-0.17$ percentage points on average, while changing 4.69\% of individual predictions. Diagonal and rank-64 Gaussian replacements decrease accuracy by 25.0 and 28.1 points on the same subset. Further experiments with exact moment constraints, quantization error, token retention, and additional visual tasks reveal how strongly the effects depend on the original feature values and the evaluation setting. These findings provide an empirical basis for assessing visual token transformations in frozen MLLMs and designing more reliable multimodal interfaces.

## 204. From Cloud to Edge: A Fully Local Pipeline for Pothole Risk Assessment and Driver Warning Generation

- **作者**：Abdalmalek Aburaddaha, Samir A. Rawashdeh
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, 8 figures, 7 tables. And submitted to MDPI Sensors

Vision-language models can turn road images into clear, geometry-aware pothole warnings, but current systems depend on commercial cloud services. Our earlier five-stage pipeline needed two cloud calls per scene, taking about 20 s and USD 0.105, and could not run offline. We rebuild every stage to run on a single local GPU and evaluate it with the same data, prompts, and protocol. Segmentation keeps the original Segment Anything weights but runs them in half precision with graph compilation, which makes it 2.7x faster without changing any downstream risk class; five lightweight variants could not match its masks on small road defects. Severity is graded by six ConvNeXt students distilled from 3092 cloud labels. They reach Cohen's kappa = 0.268 against human consensus at 28 ms per pothole, close to the cloud model's 0.321, within the camera setup they were trained for. A local 8-billion-parameter vision-language model writes the warnings under a grammar that admits only in-lane maneuvers. Three blind, independent raters scored these warnings safer than the cloud warnings (4.18 vs. 3.65 of 5; p = 0.001; 50 scenes) and equally clear, whereas an automated LLM judge ranked them the other way. The complete pipeline runs at 1.27 s per scene, 16x faster than the cloud system, offline and at no per-scene cost.

## 205. Cache or Denoise Less? Residual Reuse in a Flow-Matching World Action Model

- **作者**：Abhinav Gupta, Nissan Pow
- **投稿**：2026-11-15 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Honorable Mention at the CoRL 2026 Workshop on Efficient Foundation Models for Real-Time Embodied AI (EEAI). Code and data available at https://github.com/gabhinav001/cache-or-denoise-less

Flow-matching robot policies generate each action chunk through repeated network evaluations, one per denoising step. Cross-cycle caches such as C$^3$ache reuse computation from earlier replanning calls, but whether they beat simply taking fewer steps is unclear. We compare the two in Fast-WAM, a 6B-parameter world action model, on LIBERO-Goal. Two uncached steps keep the success of ten: on all 500 initial states they are non-inferior within two percentage points, for one checkpoint and noise seed. Under PyTorch's default compile mode, they also cut a replanning call on an RTX A6000 from 202 to 92 ms. This leaves little room for caching. A cache that keeps two fresh steps cannot be faster than two uncached steps: every call still encodes the images, runs the video expert and computes those two steps. Caching the first of two steps, refreshed every fourth call, saves at most 11--14% of a call by our cost model. Cached policies are also fragile. Their losses tracked the share of the flow path the cached steps cover: under Fast-WAM's current time shift, C$^3$ache's eight cached steps cover 80% of the path instead of 44%, and the cached policy succeeds on 63% of states against 95% uncached. Resampling the noise on every call cost caches covering 60% or more of the path 14--40 points. These results argue for fewer-step baselines and for testing reuse under the deployed sampler and noise settings. We release our evaluation harness, logs and a checklist.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19709) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19709)

## 206. Beyond the Training Cameras: Coverage-Controlled Probes of Viewing Direction in Frozen Vision Models

- **作者**：Peize Ding
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision models are trained on 2D images of a 3D world. Do their hidden features encode the direction from which the camera views a scene, and if so, in what form? We study the relative viewing direction $α$ of two objects in rendered scenes where both object centres stay at fixed pixels while the camera moves, so that perspective, shading and shadows change but the projected layout does not. In eight pretrained models, from diffusion models to self-supervised and image-text encoders, $α$ is encoded in a strikingly simple form: a single two-dimensional plane in the hidden space, shared across scenes, in which the difference between the two objects' features traces $(\cosα, \sinα)$. The plane keeps its orientation when lighting, colours, floors and object shapes change. This encoding is more than a summary of a few visible measurements: readouts fitted on one set of camera paths stay accurate at camera positions outside their range in seven of the eight models, whereas decoders of the measured object and shadow geometry, equally accurate within that range, fail there. In the diffusion transformer PixArt-$α$, the plane forms abruptly at a single block and carries direction information to later blocks, yet editing it changes the objects rather than the generated viewpoint. We also examine how this encoding carries over to scanned real objects.

## 207. Floorplan-Context-Aware Cross-Environment Human Activity Recognition from mmWave Radar Point Clouds

- **作者**：Zixiong Han, Xilai Wang, Chenzhe Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.SP
- **备注**：Submitted to ICASSP 2027

Human activity recognition (HAR) models based on mmWave radar point clouds and trained in controlled open spaces often experience performance degradation when deployed in furnished rooms. We propose a Floorplan-Context-Aware (FCA) HAR enhancement method that augments the input with subject-furniture relative position information to improve cross-environment performance. Because existing mmWave point-cloud datasets lack registered floorplans, we introduce a pseudo-landmark augmentation strategy for training the FCA model. In cross-environment evaluation on the mm-ADL-Home dataset, FCA achieves 77.70% accuracy and 65.49% macro-F1, improving upon the point-cloud-only direct-transfer baseline by 10.73 and 10.05 percentage points, respectively, and exceeding the strongest evaluated adaptation baseline by 7.37 and 6.27 percentage points. At deployment, FCA requires only a one-time registration of furniture landmarks, with no target-domain activity annotations or model weight updates.

## 208. Where slide-level foundation-model classifiers fail on atypical breast lesions

- **作者**：Nerea Hernandez, Luis J. Herrera, Cesar Ramirez et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Whether a ductal breast lesion is called atypical hyperplasia or low-grade carcinoma in situ increasingly determines surgery. However, slide-level classifiers built on pathology foundation models (PFMs) perform worst on these lesions. Using four PFMs, cross-validation on BRACS and preregistered hypotheses, we studied where the classification of atypia fails. Because slides are labelled with their most severe lesion, only 39% of the slides with annotated atypical ductal hyperplasia (ADH) were labelled ADH. Attention ranked the lesion high but rarely first, and showing the model only the lesion helped little, whereas classifiers trained per decision with lesion-level labels improved all four atypical boundaries. ADH regions inserted into benign slides raised the probability of malignancy more than that of atypia. In a hospital with pathologist-labelled single-subtype slides and in HISTAI, benign slides were scored closer to lesions and triage specificity fell from 0.48-0.53 to 0.00-0.07 and 0.22.

## 209. Toward Planning Representations That Separate Task Structure from Visual Content

- **作者**：Aarav Srivastava, Vishwas Sathish, Rajesh P. N. Rao
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Effective planning requires representations that preserve task-relevant geometry and object identity while ignoring visual variation that does not alter the task. Existing evaluations of visual generalization often change several factors simultaneously, making the source of a failure difficult to identify. We introduce a controlled evaluation of object-goal navigation (ObjectNav), the task of reaching an instance of a named object category, in procedurally generated ProcTHOR houses. Training and evaluation houses share floor plans, object locations, goals, and start states, while visual factors are changed individually or in combination. Across model-free reinforcement learning (RL), model-based RL, and agents using frozen pretrained visual representations, our results show that appearance changes impair navigation, but their effects differ sharply by factor. Surface material changes account for most of the average change in success, whereas clutter and lighting have little effect. The magnitude of a global image statistics shift does not reliably predict performance: changing only the goal object's appearance can substantially impair an end-to-end agent despite a small image-level change. We found that a model based agent that plans over frozen features from the pretrained DINOv2 image encoder achieves the highest success (0.83) under the full shift, but remains sensitive to surface appearance. Our experiments reveal an important requirement for planning representations: they must separate invariant spatial task structure from appearance.

## 210. Learning Spatially Aware Audio-Visual Features from Videos with Mono Audio

- **作者**：Mengyu Yang, You Zhang, Vishnu Raj et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：WACV 2027

Comprehensive audio-visual perception requires both semantic and spatial reasoning. Existing spatial models suffer from poor generalization due to data scarcity, while semantic-only models fail to capture spatial characteristics completely. We introduce a model capable of jointly reasoning over binaural audio and vision on semantic and spatial tasks with improved generalization. To overcome the shortage in real-world data, we develop an automatic pipeline that localizes the sound source in a video, estimates its relative 3D trajectory, and renders binaural audio. We then use this to spatialize VGGSound, allowing us to train at a much larger scale. We demonstrate improved performance and generalization on spatial reasoning tasks while still remaining competitive on semantic tasks compared to semantic-only models.

## 211. EndoComparer: Comparative Surgical Video Question Answering with Pseudo-Siamese Visual Adaptation and Perceive-then-Compare Reasoning

- **作者**：Shi Li, Lalith Sharan, Joël L. Lavanchy et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：33 pages, 14 figures, 8 tables

Purpose: Surgical training benefits from explicit comparison between cases: in patient condition, in technique and in skill proficiency. Surgical vision-language models (VLMs) offer a promising and flexible interface for this purpose. Yet current VLMs typically see one video at a time, and they often fall short when asked to compare videos. Methods: We propose EndoComparer, a VLM for comparative surgical video question answering with two components. (a) A Pseudo-Siamese Visual LoRA (PSVL) gives each video slot its own low-rank adapter over a shared frozen vision encoder, separating the two videos in the visual pathway at the cost of a single encoder pass. (b) A Perceive-then-Compare chain of thought (PtC-CoT) trains the model to first state what each video shows, then derive the answer from these statements. We further curate EndoDiffVQA, a benchmark of nearly 11K multiple-choice questions on video-pair over cholecystectomy and gastric bypass procedures, covering anatomy, tool, action and skill. Results: EndoComparer outperforms strong generalist and surgical VLMs on detecting and specifying difference between videos. Ablations show that two proposed components contribute complimentarily, and the model remains more robust under a video-swap control that exposes confusion between the two videos. Conclusion: Providing a dedicated visual reasoning path through PSVL and PtC-CoT makes cross-case comparison of surgical videos more robust for VLMs. EndoDiffVQA and EndoComparer provide a benchmark and a strong baseline for this task. Project page: https://camma-public.github.io/endocomparer

## 212. Consistency-Aware Map Generation at Multiple Zoom Levels Using Aerial Image

- **作者**：Linwei Chen, Zheng Fang, Ying Fu
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 9 figures

The multi-level tiled map service is widely used and serves as a kind of digital infrastructure. These map tiles are usually rendered from vector data, whose update needs to walk or drive with professional equipment to check every points of interest. This leads to inconvenience and expensive cost in timely updating maps. Compared with vector data, aerial images are much easier and cheaper to obtain. In this paper, we propose a novel multi-level map generation framework that can automatically generate accurate and consistent maps with multiple zoom levels from aerial images. It consists of a level-aware map generator and a consistency-aware map generator. The level-aware map generator is able to generate accurate initial maps with realistic details for each zoom level. The consistency-aware map generator regards initial maps at each zoom level as a sequence and builds the connection between them, so as to guarantee content consistency between maps at different zoom levels. Furthermore, we collect a large-scale high-quality dataset called Multi-Level Map (MLM) for map generation at multiple zoom levels. Experiments on our MLM dataset show that our method outperforms previous state-of-the-art map generation methods on both comprehensive quantitative metrics and perceptual quality.

## 213. Spectral Steering: Local Inverse Geometry for Frozen Diffusion Generators

- **作者**：Angelo Moroncelli, Alessandro Leanza, Loris Roveda
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：Under review

A frozen diffusion generator may need to change several output properties while preserving others. We study when guidance faithfully realizes such a structured request and whether local inverse correction improves its direction. Our account links this benefit to the interaction between the request and the objective-specific remaining-sampler Jacobian: conditioning alone is insufficient. Spectral steering combines a request-specific cosine-gain predictor with damped inverse correction and fixed-time relinearization for finite edits. Scalar nulls and matched-movement controls test the predicted mechanism. Across 8,923 instances in a retrospectively identified local regime, predicted and realized gains correlate at Spearman 0.79, versus -0.11 for condition number. Experiments span images, audio, robotics, and control and planning, including complex character motion generation. On 50 paired inpainting cases, four corrections improve unobserved-pixel PSNR over Adam by 3.25 dB at equal total derivative traversals. Together, the analysis and controls connect request-dependent geometry to directional gains and establish where these gains translate into downstream performance.
