## 214. Sharp First-Hit Identification from Finite Depth Samples

- **作者**：Hamidreza Hasani Balyani, Mahtab Movahhedrad, Seyed Pouyan Mousavi Davoudi
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：36 pages, 9 figures. This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible

Which depths in a new camera view are determined by finitely many source-depth measurements? Pointwise uncertainty bounds do not answer this question: unobserved geometry between samples can change which surface is encountered first. We study a bounded Lipschitz class of continuous source inverse-depth fields with interval observations and known camera pose. Building on classical Lipschitz envelopes, we characterize the exact set of attainable first-hit depths and construct a globally admissible scene for every value in that set. The set can be disconnected. We also give a necessary and sufficient test for several proposed depths to be first hits of one shared scene. An epipolar reduction uses scalar cone inequalities without enumerating surfaces. In a rectified model with source spacing $h$, interval half-width $\varepsilon$, baseline $b$, and slope bound $L$, we prove a sharp worst-case inverse-depth diameter of $\max\{Lh+2\varepsilon,2\varepsilon/(1-bL)\}$ when $bL<1$, subject to sufficient depth and domain range for attainment. Exact samples still give the sharp bound $Lh$ at $bL=1$, while above this threshold ambiguity need not vanish as sampling becomes arbitrarily dense. Any positive measurement uncertainty can destroy uniform stability at the critical threshold. We provide constructive examples, an exact rational validation oracle, and a frozen ten-scene evaluation using reference, stereo, and learned-depth observations. The experiments separate solver correctness under the scene assumptions from failure of those assumptions: fixed learned-depth bands often yield narrow but incorrect sets. The results identify what a specified scene class permits; they do not infer a continuum regularity or physical-scene guarantee from sampled predictions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19563) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19563)

## 215. Read, Then Shade? How Visual Evidence Changes Incentive-Driven Misreporting in Vision-Language Advisors

- **作者**：Hamidreza Hasani Balyani
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：45 pages, 5 figures. This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible

Vision-language models (VLMs) increasingly read a scene and report it to someone who acts on the report, sometimes under objectives that differ from that person's. We ask whether a stated incentive moves what a VLM reports, and whether this changes when the value must be read from pixels rather than given as text. In seeded synthetic scenes, a model reads the position of a target on a labelled axis and reports one integer to a fixed receiver; it is paid, in simulation, for accuracy or for a report a few units to one side, and every scene is also presented with the true value as text. Across a pilot and three follow-up studies with hash-frozen protocols, four small open VLMs from three families, and 15,170 held-out trials, a stated payoff moved reports in both modalities, and exact compliance at three units was significantly lower with the image than with text for three of four models; Qwen2.5-VL 7B showed no reliable difference. Adding the true value as text to the image prompt removed the gap in every model. For InternVL3 the gap vanished on scenes it read correctly when it wrote its reading first; for Gemma 3 it persisted. For Qwen2.5-VL 3B, which read every scene correctly, the gap held in four of five payoff paraphrases, nearly vanished when the model wrote its reading before its report or when the prompt stated that the client wants the truth, vanished with an unpaid goal, and persisted when the model reported before stating its reading; how often it shaded at all, even with text, depended strongly on the wording. When told that the client wants the true value, three of four models still shaded every text report. Visual input did not make these advisors reliably more truthful; it made compliance with a stated incentive depend on whether the read value is made explicit and on output order, a dependence that text-only evaluations and mean-shift metrics do not reveal.

## 216. Lesion-Aware Explainable AI for Diabetic Retinopathy Grading from Fundus Images

- **作者**：Truong Thinh Nguyen, Hai Phong Nguyen, Truong Thanh Hung Nguyen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：12 pages, 6 figures, 9 tables. Truong Thinh Nguyen and Hai Phong Nguyen contributed equally. Corresponding author: Truong Thinh Nguyen

Post-hoc explanations of deep-learning models for diabetic retinopathy (DR) grading are difficult to trust because different attribution methods highlight different retinal regions for the same image, and a visually plausible heatmap does not show that the highlighted pixels drive the prediction. We propose the Consensus-Calibrated Explanation Map (CCEM), which fuses Grad-CAM++, Ada-SISE, and SmoothGrad Integrated Gradients (SG-IG) using image-specific weights derived from annotation-free reliability cues: deletion sensitivity, inter-method agreement, compactness, retinal containment, and peak structure. CCEM explains a frozen ordinal grader, an EfficientNet-B7 with CBAM and a scalar regression head, pre-trained on EyePACS 2015 in Stage 1 and fine-tuned on APTOS 2019 in Stage 2 with quadratic weighted kappa (QWK)-optimized thresholds; it reaches a QWK of 0.920 on the hidden Kaggle test set and 0.559 on the external MAPLES-DR cohort. Explanations are evaluated on MAPLES-DR, 198 MESSIDOR images with expert pixel-level lesion annotations that are never used for training, threshold fitting, or fusion. On the 193 images with annotated lesions, CCEM obtains the highest Soft Pointing Game score (69.61%; 95% CI 63.7-75.3), defined as the ratio of the strongest lesion-supported saliency to the global map maximum rather than a lesion detection rate, together with the lowest deletion AUC (0.570) and the best deletion-insertion balance (0.115); each gain is significant against the strongest single explainer on paired tests. Annotation-free, per-image reliability weighting therefore turns three disagreeing explainers into one map whose peak evidence is more lesion-supported and more faithful to the model than that of any single explainer.

## 217. PathLoop: Self-Evolving Multi-Agent Reasoning for Evidence-Grounded Whole-Slide Image Analysis

- **作者**：Jincheng Li, Zhengyi Liu, Zelin Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Analyzing whole-slide images (WSIs) requires combining evidence across tissue regions and spatial scales. Multi-agent systems offer a promising approach to this task. However, existing systems make limited use of molecular evidence, often select visually prominent but less representative regions while overlooking global context, and struggle to reuse experience from previous cases. To address these challenges, we present PathLoop, a self-evolving multi-agent framework that integrates omics data for evidence-grounded WSI analysis. Specifically, PathLoop coordinates visual and omics interpreters across slide, patch, and pixel levels, using molecular signals to guide region selection and combining local tissue details with global slide features. A judgment agent then checks the consistency of the collected evidence against Pathology-Wiki, a knowledge base built from 48 entries across 24 organ systems, and triggers re-examination when the evidence is inconsistent. To reuse past experience, a four-level memory stores reasoning trajectories, practical tips, skills, and tools to guide future cases, forming a loop of evidence collection, review, and memory update. We evaluate PathLoop on four TCGA cohorts with biomarker prediction, survival prediction, and subtype classification. The results demonstrate the effectiveness of PathLoop across these tasks and support its use for evidence-grounded WSI analysis.

## 218. DBHiCo: Dual-Branch Hierarchical Contrastive Learning for MRI Image Classification

- **作者**：Chengsheng Zhanga, Wei Li, Chenghao Sun et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages

Automated Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) image classification provides a crucial non-invasive approach for neurological diagnosis, yet it is fundamentally challenged by inherent acquisition noise and high inter-subject structural similarity, which obscure subtle pathological variations, thereby limiting the performance of deep neural networks. While contrastive learning (CL) offers a promising mitigation strategy, existing paradigms rely on rigid binary partitions that ignore negative sample heterogeneity, and they fail to capture the cross-view structural consistency vital for fine-grained anatomical alignment. To address these barriers, we propose Dual-Branch Hierarchical Contrastive Learning (DBHiCo), a novel framework explicitly designed to collaboratively enforce structural consistency and semantic discrimination. Specifically, DBHiCo leverages a natural anatomical multi-view pair (original and skull-stripped MRI scans), processing them through a dual-branch architecture to seamlessly integrate holistic structural robustness with localized intracranial details. Crucially, we introduce a hierarchical contrastive loss that discards conventional binary constraints in favor of a margin-ranked similarity hierarchy. This mechanism explicitly orders positive and negative pairs based on their structural and semantic difficulty, thereby amplifying the fine-grained semantic discriminability of the learned representations. Extensive experiments across three diverse neurological datasets demonstrate that DBHiCo achieves state-of-the-art performance, consistently outperforming existing MRI multi-view representation learning and advanced contrastive fine-tuning methods.

## 219. Vegetation Health Classification for Low-Data and Edge Hardware Deployment

- **作者**：Yifei Cheng, Haoyu Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) equipped with cameras are popular, viable tools for vegetation and environmental health monitoring. However, existing image classification methods are incompatible with UAV implementation for independent practitioners in remote, resource-constrained environments due to large dataset requirements, GPU hardware, inflexible retraining, and low interpretability. This paper presents a lightweight, interpretable vegetation classification algorithm operating with as few as five reference images per class, without GPUs or heavy machine learning frameworks. It uses a 2D hue-saturation histogram feature representation and Earth Mover's Distance or Quadratic Form Distance, paired with a log-likelihood probability score, to classify images into three vegetation health categories. We evaluated six systems: three custom pipelines and three established models across low-data scenarios ($n = 5, 10, 25$) and a full-data regime of 2,172 drone images from 28 South American locations. Our custom pipelines achieve competitive Top-1 accuracy (81--85\%) under low-data constraints, maintaining sub-1ms inference times that enable real-time classification and subsequently tasks such as seeding and watering. The algorithms also support incremental retraining without full model reconstruction. We validate edge feasibility by deploying the QFD variant on an ESP32 microcontroller, achieving 82.8\% accuracy with 1.01\% heap memory utilization (45kb), demonstrating that effective vegetation classification is achievable on low-cost, low-power embedded hardware without cellular connectivity.

## 220. Beyond Paired Inputs: Learning to Leverage RGB-T Cross-Modal Relations in Multimodal Large Language Models

- **作者**：Tianyi Zhao, Qihao Xu, Jiawen Xi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.MM
- **备注**：9 pages, 4 figures

RGB and thermal infrared (TIR) sensors provide complementary observations, yet their distinct sensing characteristics make effective cross-modal coordination challenging. Although existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can process paired RGB-T inputs, they often fail to translate cross-modal relations into effective task-specific decisions, leaving the potential of these relations to improve task performance and robustness largely untapped. Motivated by this observation, we introduce RelMLLM, an RGB-T MLLM that explicitly learns to exploit cross-modal relations in task-specific decision making. It represents modality reliability, the cross-modal relation, and the dominant modality as a verifiable decision state. We derive task-grounded supervision for this state by comparing RGB-only, TIR-only, and paired RGB-T task outcomes. A hierarchical relation-guided reward supervises modality-specific evidence, relation-state prediction, and the final task output, while encouraging consistency across evidence, relation, and decision. We evaluate RelMLLM on natural RGB-T grounding, RGB-T visual question answering, and held-out sensor degradation. RelMLLM improves grounding accuracy and degradation robustness over its Qwen3-VL baselines while preserving performance on general RGB-T reasoning benchmarks. These results demonstrate that effectively exploiting cross-modal relations leads to more accurate and robust RGB-T decisions. Code and model weights are publicly available.

## 221. PALIN: Pixel-Aligned LiDAR-NIR Multimodal 3D Object Detection with Benchmark

- **作者**：Yanan Zhang, Amit Vishnia, Chengbo Ai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages, including references and appendices

3D object detection is critical for intelligent transportation systems. LiDAR has been widely adopted for accurate geometric measurements; however, purely geometric representations remain ambiguous under sparse observations and occlusions, while cross-sensor multimodal fusion suffers from calibration and synchronization errors. To overcome these challenges, we leverage an Ouster LiDAR to construct PALIN (Pixel-Aligned LiDAR-NIR), a large-scale intrinsically pixel-aligned multimodal dataset jointly capturing geometry and NIR intensity, comprising approximately 8,000 annotated frames from static urban intersections and diverse mobile suburban scenes. Building upon this dataset, we propose CMambaFusion, a novel fusion framework consisting of a four-directional Cross-Mamba module for bidirectional cross-modal interaction and a Gated Concat-Mamba module for joint feature refinement. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PALIN enables effective multimodal learning, and CMambaFusion achieves superior accuracy-efficiency trade-offs over both LiDAR-only and representative fusion baselines.

## 222. StyleJudge: A Human-Validated, In-Context-Learning Vision-Language Evaluator for Artistic Style Transfer

- **作者**：Lokesh, Pramukto Mandal, Somanshu Juneja et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted in ICVGIP 2026

Automatic evaluation of artistic style transfer remains an open problem: existing metrics score an image pair in isolation, without seeing an example of the intended style transformation, and correlate poorly with human judgment. We introduce StyleJudge, a compact vision-language evaluator conditioned on a small set of reference source-to-stylized pairs before scoring a query pair, mirroring how a human rater calibrates to a style before judging a candidate. On a human-annotated benchmark of 878 rated pairs across 12 artistic styles (728 from 10 training/validation styles, 150 from 2 unseen styles), our efficient 0.8B-parameter StyleJudge reaches a Pearson correlation of 0.291 (Spearman 0.325) with human ratings on seen styles and 0.381 on unseen ones. On seen styles, it achieves the highest positive correlation among tested metrics: Gram-matrix style loss and CLIP similarity correlate weakly ($r=0.041$, $r=0.168$), while LPIPS (scored higher when closer to the source) correlates negatively ($r=-0.404$). We achieve this by distilling evaluation knowledge from a 72B-parameter vision-language teacher used only during training, which reaches $r=0.327$, confirming that most of its human alignment survives compact distillation. For training, we build a dataset of source-to-stylized pairs across the 12 styles via LoRA-adapted diffusion, densified with graded intermediate examples from a high-resolution image-morphing pipeline so the evaluator recognizes partial and complete stylization. On two held-out validation styles, StyleJudge reaches 28.2% exact-match agreement with its teacher's structured scores versus 1.1% for the untuned base model, with 67.8% shorter reasoning traces, and scores a pair in 39 s (median) using 2.5 GB of GPU memory on one NVIDIA T4. We release the human-rating benchmark, annotation guideline, SDXL-adapted FreeMorph code, and model weights.

## 223. InvarOrd: Self-Supervised Ordinal Knowledge Distillation with Rank-Invariant Alignment for Label-Efficient Cross-Species Hyperspectral Fish Freshness Assessment

- **作者**：Kazi Nabiul Alam, Omenuko Joseph Nwebonyi, Pooneh Bagheri Zadeh et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted at IEEE TAFE

Hyperspectral models of fish freshness rarely transfer between species, and labelling a new species is slow and destructive. Freshness is also ordinal, and standard domain alignment can erase that order. We present InvarOrd, which adapts to a new species from 30 labelled cubes (5 per rank) in three stages: self-supervised spectral pretraining on unlabelled source cubes, cross-species alignment by a shrinkage covariance discrepancy computed only within matched ranks, and ordinal distillation into a compact student. The alignment term is provably invariant to the rank means, so it gives no incentive to collapse the ordinal axis. On paired salmon and cod data (800 cubes each, 16 days), a source-trained teacher reaches 1.6 to 1.9 days mean absolute error (MAE) on the other species, with quadratic weighted kappa (QWK) below zero. The 423K-parameter student, 2.54 times smaller than its teacher, attains the lowest MAE (1.476 days) and highest within 1-day accuracy (56.1%) among 14 configurations, matching the best of six ImageNet-pretrained backbones (ConvNeXt-T, 1.503 days) at 66 times fewer parameters, 1.44ms per cube on a GPU and 5s re-adaptation. InvarOrd beats target-only training by 0.29 days (p = 0.031). Among alignment objectives, rank-invariant alignment retains the most ordinal structure (embedding rank correlation 0.183, QWK 0.112), whereas global discrepancy lowers the rank correlation to 0.049 and QWK to zero at only 0.014 days higher MAE.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19472) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19472)

## 224. Semantics First, Pixels Later: Mission-Driven Event Streams for Drone Video over Bandwidth-Starved Links

- **作者**：Cong Xu
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 3 figures; includes the supplementary material as an appendix; a demonstration video is attached as an ancillary file

A surveillance drone on a bandwidth-starved link cannot send the video its operator would need, yet the operator's questions are often not about pixels: did three people converge, when, and where? We study what should cross a link of 1 Mbps down to 1 kbps instead, and in what order. BPS, a behavior-primitive stream, sends semantics first and pixels later: it runs a detector and a tracker onboard and sends short records (25 bytes on average) of multi-frame events selected by a mission card of 16-125 bytes, ahead of positions and pictures. We measure it by what a ground-side language model answers from it, under a preregistered protocol with a held-out set, against H.265 read by the vision fine-tune of the same model, H.265 on which the ground runs our own perception, a stream of quantized tracks, and onboard captions. On the development set (84% of questions about convergence, mostly one location) the event stream answers 74.3% of questions at 0.13 kbps. A reader that receives nothing answers 57.4%, and the video reader 59.6% at 100 kbps and 64.7% on the source frames. When the ground instead runs our perception on the video, video matches the stream at 100 kbps, about 800 times the stream's rate, and falls behind at 64 kbps and below. A track stream matches it at 96 times the bytes and loses 0.03 at 17 times. On held-out data the result is weaker: with events taken from annotated tracks, an oracle, the stream beats H.265 at 100 kbps (+0.13), but with onboard perception it does not (+0.01 [-0.04, +0.06]) and is no better than receiving nothing. Sent alone through emulated links the event records answer 0.739 at 1 Mbps and still 0.724 at 0.3 kbps; an every-frame track stream holds down to 1 kbps and video with ground-side perception down to about 100 kbps. On a Jetson Orin Nano the unchanged pipeline runs at 4.5 fps and misses most events.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19463) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19463)

## 225. Few-Shot Visual Concept Extraction for Steering Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Nabyl Quignon, Antitza Dantcheva
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026 Main Track

Image steering aims to provide fine-grained control over concepts in generated images, enabling targeted and continuous manipulation without broadly altering the generation process. Recent steering methods for diffusion models primarily focus on the text-conditioning space or rely on costly auxiliary training. Deviating from that, we here propose a novel steering approach that extracts visual concept directions directly from the image-side activations of Diffusion Transformers. Specifically, we showcase few-shot concept extraction in the activation space using a simple difference-of-means estimator over intermediate residuals. We observe (a) scene-level concepts such as lighting, weather, and style are distributed across image tokens, as well as (b) local concepts such as object colors, textures, materials, and facial attributes are concentrated in specific regions. Towards controlling both concepts, we introduce a new unified training-free framework that uses global difference-of-means for (a) distributed concepts and masked difference-of-means for (b) local concepts, preventing local signals from being diluted by global averaging. Notably, using only a handful of positive and negative prompt pairs, our method identifies and extracts reusable concept directions that can be injected at inference for continuous steering. Quantitatively, our method outperforms text-encoder steering and training-free activation-editing baselines, as well as is competitive with specialized editing models. Our results suggest that as concept directions can be applied at patch level, our method enables precise targeted and compositional steering, manipulating multiple concepts in the same image.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19440) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19440)

## 226. Prepare Lighter and Faster for Open-Vocabulary Queries: A Query-Wise 3D Segmenter for Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Nhat-Quynh Le-Pham, Khoi Nguyen, Binh-Son Hua
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.GR
- **备注**：Accepted at WACV 2027

Open-vocabulary 3D understanding aims to localize and segment arbitrary objects beyond predefined categories. However, existing approaches based on Gaussian Splatting often rely on expensive preprocessing and lengthy training, limiting their applicability when fast and lightweight solutions are desired. We propose a lightweight, training-free framework for query-driven 3D instance segmentation on Gaussian Splatting. Instead of conventional mask-wise CLIP embeddings, we use dense pixel-level CLIP features to preserve fine-grained semantic information and enable flexible text-driven localization without relying on predefined instance proposals. Given a text query, we identify a high-quality target region in a carefully selected pivot view and propagate it across views using a video segmentor, providing consistent and comprehensive coverage of the target instance. We then lift the resulting 2D tracklets to 3D Gaussians through a novel visibility voting strategy, yielding accurate 3D segmentation masks with clean object boundaries. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on LERF-OVS, ScanNet, and ScanNet++, competitive results on 3D-OVS, and significant speedups over existing open-vocabulary 3D segmentation methods. These results highlight the effectiveness of our framework for efficient open-vocabulary 3D understanding without per-scene training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19429) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19429)

## 227. Memorization Lives in a Few Attention Heads: Mitigating Memorization in Diffusion Models via Projection-Based Suppression

- **作者**：Puwei Lian, Yujun Cai, Songze Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CR

Diffusion models can memorize and reproduce training samples during inference, raising serious privacy and copyright concerns. Existing inference-time mitigation methods often suppress conditional information globally, which may reduce memorization but can also harm semantic alignment and image quality. In this paper, we revisit memorization from the perspective of cross-attention heads. Through systematic analysis and targeted ablation, we find that memorization is mainly driven by a small subset of shallow-layer attention heads with abnormally strong semantic control, rather than by the entire cross-attention mechanism. Motivated by this finding, we propose Projection-based Top-k Attention Head Suppression, a targeted inference-time mitigation method. Our method first identifies the top-k attention heads most related to memorization using a semantic-control score, and then removes their condition-specific components through directional projection between conditional and unconditional representations. This design selectively weakens memorization-related features while preserving useful semantic information. Extensive experiments on different diffusion models and datasets show that our method effectively reduces memorization and achieves a better trade-off between memorization mitigation and image quality than existing approaches.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19427) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19427)

## 228. SplatFlow3D: Learning Scene Flow via 3D Motion Feature Splatting

- **作者**：Bo Wang, Dekang Zhu, Jian Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Considering the motion continuity and stability of 3D objects over short inter-frame intervals, introducing multi-frame settings in scene flow estimation can potentially address occlusion. In this paper, we propose a two-frame backbone, SimpleFlow3D, which integrates dense information from the image plane and sparse information from the depth dimension in a decoupling-followed-by-coupling manner by hierarchically constructing the 3D motion feature. SimpleFlow3D achieves SOTA results with minimal parameters. We also propose a weight-sharing multi-frame framework to splat the previous 3D motion feature to the current estimation process. The framework unifies the first and non-first frame inference by constructing pseudo inputs. After combining the proposed framework and backbone, the newly born multi-frame method SplatFlow3D further advances the state of the art. It achieves lower errors than the two-frame backbone, with fewer parameters than current methods. On both KITTI and Spring, SplatFlow3D ranked 1st over all published and submitted methods. Experiments show that SplatFlow3D improves occlusion handling, non-rigid motion estimation, and generalization to real-world scenes. Code is available at https://github.com/wwsource/SplatFlow3D.

## 229. Detecting Small Space Objects for Space Situational Awareness with Collaborative Edge AI

- **作者**：Xingyu Qu, Peng Hu
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.SY

With the rapid growth of space activities, near-Earth space is becoming increasingly congested, leading to a higher risk of spacecraft collisions and space debris generation. Recent close-proximity encounters and collision incidents necessitate efficient and cost-effective onboard systems for space object detection (SOD) and collision avoidance. While deep learning (DL) enabled Edge AI using low-cost vision sensors on single or multiple satellites has shown promises for SOD, significant challenges remain, including limited datasets, small SOD, and the lack of comprehensive performance assessments. This paper presents a collaborative Edge AI framework for multiview SOD and systematically benchmarks DL models and fusion strategies. Results show that higher image resolution significantly improves small SOD. For multiple views, image-level fusion reduces the combined communication and inference latency by 30.13% and 32.80% on average compared with feature-level and attention-based fusion across the evaluated inter-satellite link (ISL) bandwidth. For larger view sets, feature-level and attention-based fusion achieve better detection accuracy at the cost of analysis in the sensing-communication pipeline, revealing that multiview collaboration introduces minimal latency overhead under ISL constraints when reception capacity is not exceeded. Our work provides valuable design insights for future collaborative satellite network design, supporting the emerging space situational awareness and space traffic management requirements.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19394) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19394)

## 230. Pixel-KWS: Rendered-Glyph Representations for Orthographically Robust Open-Vocabulary Keyword Spotting

- **作者**：Adarsh Arigala, Ramesh Gundluru, Srinivasan Umesh et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：eess.AS, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.SD
- **备注**：5 pages, 4 figures, 3 tables

Open-vocabulary keyword spotting (KWS) allows users to enroll arbitrary keywords at inference time. However, character and phoneme-based text encoders rely on fixed symbol vocabularies learned during training, limiting their ability to handle unseen or visually similar symbols such as homoglyphs, l33tspeak substitutions, and accented characters. We propose PIXEL-KWS, an open-vocabulary KWS framework that represents characters through rendered glyphs rather than learned embedding lookups. We render each character as a grayscale image and encode it with a lightweight CNN, enabling the text encoder to generalize from visual form rather than a fixed vocabulary. On LibriPhrase, PIXEL-KWS and its matched character-based counterpart achieve 9.57% and 9.55% EER on LibriPhrase-hard, 0.43% and 0.41% EER on LibriPhrase-easy, outperforming all published baselines without pre-training or synthetic data. PIXEL-KWS provides substantially greater robustness to both homoglyph and l33tspeak symbol-level perturbations. These results highlight glyph-based representations as a simple and effective approach for robust open-vocabulary KWS in practical settings.

## 231. AcousticBEV: Acoustic BEV Object Localization for Intelligent Vehicles

- **作者**：Ziang Liu, Shiming Wang, Joseph Adeola et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：Ziang Liu and Shiming Wang have equal contributions. Shiming Wang is the project lead

Modern autonomous driving and driver assistance systems rely predominantly on cameras, radars and LiDARs for environmental perception. However, these modalities face different environmental limitations, including poor illumination for cameras and adverse weather for LiDAR. Acoustic sensing, particularly using microphone arrays, could provide complementary perception capabilities for autonomous driving, but has remained largely underexplored. In this work, we design a horizontally arranged microphone array that functions as the "ears" of a vehicle, enabling localization of nearby moving vehicles in a bird's-eye view (BEV) representation that captures both their relative direction and distance. Using this array, we collect a real-world driving dataset, AcousticScenes, which contains 2,117 static and 2,863 dynamic samples with synchronized spatial acoustic signals, LiDAR point clouds and 3D bounding box annotations. Building on this dataset, we propose AcousticBEV, a learning-based method for acoustic BEV object localization, which estimates the center locations of moving traffic participants in BEV from the microphone-array signals, conditioned on the ego vehicle's own speed. Experimental results demonstrate that AcousticBEV outperforms both classical signal-processing baselines and other learning-based methods, achieving F1 scores of $47.7 \pm 1.2$% on "Easy" scenarios and $45.1 \pm 1.4$% on "Difficult" cases. Our findings demonstrate that acoustic signals from a low-cost, vehicle-mounted microphone array can localize nearby moving vehicles in both azimuth and distance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19381) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19381)

## 232. XLSAR-Bench: Towards Extremely Large SAR Imagery Understanding of MLLMs

- **作者**：La Jiang, Lingfeng Chen, Jiaqi Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV

The rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) has highlighted the need for comprehensive benchmarks to assess their capabilities and limitations in remote sensing (RS). However, evaluating MLLMs in large-size synthetic aperture radar (SAR) scenarios remains challenging, as understanding complete scenes requires both global semantic interpretation and fine-grained target perception. Existing SAR benchmarks predominantly rely on small image patches, limiting their ability to assess these capabilities jointly. To address these limitations, we present XLSAR-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating the perception and reasoning capabilities of MLLMs in large-scene SAR imagery. XLSAR-Bench comprises 405 images with an average size of 7153$\times$7153 pixels, ten tasks spanning visual question answering, visual grounding, and detailed image captioning, with all evaluation samples meticulously annotated manually, assisted by a semi-automatic captioner. Experimental results from general-purpose and domain-specific MLLMs reveal persistent challenges in understanding large SAR scenes and accurately perceiving small targets. We have open-sourced XLSAR-Bench to support further research in developing more powerful MLLMs for complete large SAR scenes.

## 233. RETR: A Structure-Preserving RGB-Event Transformer for Robust 3D Lane Detection

- **作者**：Jingtao Dong, Hao Zhuang, Hao Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted by NIPS2026

Robust 3D lane detection requires accurate metric road geometry recovery from visual inputs, yet conventional RGB-based approaches suffer from fragile lane evidence under adverse illumination, motion blur, and long-range perspective compression. Event cameras offer complementary high-temporal-resolution, high dynamic-range structural cues that are robust to these challenges, but their sparse, motion-dependent responses cannot be directly fused into consistent 3D geometry. In this paper, we present the first attempt to introduce event cameras into 3D lane detection. To enable systematic research, we first build two multimodal benchmarks with metric 3D lane annotations: DSEC-3DLD (real-world sequences) and Ev-OpenLane (large-scale simulated sequences). We further propose RETR, a structure preserving transformer that converts complementary RGB and event observations into coherent 3D lane geometry. RETR first aligns modality consistent evidence through Reciprocal Context Flow Fusion, then preserves thin and uncertain lane structures with Uncertainty Aware Structural Consolidation, and finally decodes ordered lane hypotheses using a Geometry State Decoder with proposal conditioned initialization, reference conditioned query evolution, and diversified geometric inquiry. Extensive experiments show that RETR achieves state-of-the-art performance on both benchmarks, especially showing strong improvements in challenging lighting and complex road geometry scenarios. Code and dataset will be available at https://github.com/duskswell/RETR.

## 234. HongguoTrending: A Longitudinal, Multimodal Archive of Trending Videos and Topics on a Leading Micro-Drama Platform

- **作者**：Rongxin Ouyang
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CY, cs.CV

Micro-dramas (duanju) are reshaping digital production and consumption, yet a lack of longitudinal data connecting series to their platform visibility persists, limiting our understanding of the emerging medium. We introduce HongguoTrending, a longitudinal, multimodal dataset of hourly snapshots from seven trending lists of Hongguo, China's largest micro-drama platform. Collected through a two-stage asynchronous pipeline, the archive links 63,200 ranking entries across 632 web list-hours and 157 client hours (889 distinct series) to metadata, cover images, sampled episode videos, and audience engagement metrics. Exploratory analyses show distinct turnover rates between web and mobile trending lists, production-driven mainstream values alongside kinship-centered synopses, genre-specific cover palettes, and strong associations between user sharing and next-day rank gains. Accessible via a RESTful API, this dataset supports empirical research on algorithmic gatekeeping, popularity dynamics, and multimodal digital culture.

## 235. Attention U-Net Cellular Automata for Multi-Class Land Use and Land Cover Change Simulation in Lucknow, India: Evaluation Against Null and Classical Baselines

- **作者**：Shubham Phapale, Poonam Sharma
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：10 pages, 4 figures, 7 tables

Convolutional networks are often used to learn the transition rules of land-use cellular automata (CA), yet their predictions are seldom compared with a null model that predicts no change. We built AttentionUNet-CA, which reads a 32 x 32 neighbourhood of land-cover states with four drivers (elevation, distance to major roads, distance to the central business district (CBD) and population density) and predicts the state of each cell ten years later. We tested it on Lucknow, India, with GLC_FCS30D land cover for 1990, 2000, 2010 and 2020, a 30 m series mapped consistently over time, and compared its built-up class with GHSL and ESA WorldCover. AttentionUNet-CA was trained on 1990-2010, selected on held-out spatial blocks and evaluated once on 2010-2020, with three seeds. Baselines were the null model, a random-forest CA (RF-CA) and a U-Net CA without attention; each network was trained with and without class weights. Only 8.1% of the study area changed in the test decade, so no model matched the overall agreement of the null model (91.9%). On change, measured by the figure of merit, the class-weighted networks scored highest (U-Net CA 22.0 +/- 1.2%, AttentionUNet-CA 21.0 +/- 1.4%), ahead of RF-CA (20.3%), but by margins comparable to seed-to-seed variation; the networks estimated the amount of change better than RF-CA. The attention gates did not improve accuracy. Population density and distance to the CBD were the most useful drivers. Projected to 2030, built-up land grows by 22-35%, mostly at the city edge.

## 236. A Think Block Is Conditioning, Not a Plan

- **作者**：Jian Xu, Delu Zeng, Qibin Zhao
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Generative models increasingly write a chain of thought before drawing, and report large gains from doing so. We ask what that text contributes, by substitution rather than ablation: holding the generator, sampler and seeds fixed, we replace the think block with controlled alternatives and measure the image. Across a unified model with a think mode, a reinforcement-learned reasoning generator, and a planner driving a frozen diffusion model, the same picture emerges. Content-free prose of matched length is worth nothing or less ($-0.05$ to $-0.15$). One sentence from an unrelated 8B model, naming the subject and a few identifying details, matches the generator's own plan in three to six times fewer conditioning tokens. The value sits where the referent is in doubt: writing down a name is worth $+0.33$ when it is right and $+0.05$ when it is wrong, and where the generator already succeeds the whole effect is $-0.07 \pm 0.07$. Cutting a plan at the text encoder's window leaves 272 of 286 images byte-identical, and a plan rewritten to fit that window ties a bare identification ($+0.003 \pm 0.044$), so a plan contains nothing the identification does not already supply. Field-level interventions find only colour executed reliably, and a control writing the same sentences into the user prompt shows that limit belongs to the generator rather than to the planning channel. Two judges and two annotators replicate the central contrasts. A think block earns its keep as a specification of what to draw, not as a plan that is carried out.

## 237. Unlocking Fine-Grained Cell Representations via Soft-Interior Rim Dense-Grid Pooling in Pathology Foundation Models

- **作者**：Wenwen Lou, Mingyi He, Xinyi Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Fine-grained cell representations are essential for characterizing cellular heterogeneity and tissue organization in computational pathology. Pathology foundation models (PFMs) provide powerful pretrained features for transferable tissue analysis. HoVer-Net uses task-trained segmentation and typing, whereas CellViT++ averages native-grid tokens assigned to detected nuclei. However, the former requires cell-specific training; the latter ignores fractional nuclear coverage and can mix nuclear content with neighbouring tissue. Therefore, we propose SRDP, short for Soft-interior Rim Dense-grid Pooling, a training-free geometry-aware readout. We apply the frozen patch projection at half stride, interleave four offset lattices, map the cell mask to fractional coverage weights, and concatenate whole-cell pooling with two inner boundary pools. Across four datasets and six frozen encoders, SRDP improves mean balanced accuracy by 5.68 percentage points over native token averaging across 24 settings, including 11.22 points on average for CoNIC and Lizard. SRDP converts a coarse frozen PFM lattice into a mask-aligned, fine-grained cell representation without updating the encoder.

## 238. SAR-TopoGuard: Training-Free, Speckle-Aware Topological Verification for Structure-Preserving SAR-to-Optical Translation

- **作者**：Satabarto Sarkar, Debojyoti Ghosh, Subhranil Biswas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：10 pages, 4 figures, 7 tables. Accepted manuscript of the article published in IEEE Geoscience and Remote Sensing Letters (2026). \c{opyright} 2026 IEEE. Code: https://github.com/researchworksofsomeone/SAR-TopoGuard_Repo

SAR-to-optical translators often synthesize detail that the radar measurement does not support, and pixel metrics alone cannot reveal it. We propose SAR-TopoGuard, a training-free inference-time layer for frozen generators: a stochastic sampler draws K candidates (Monte Carlo dropout for GANs, seed variance for diffusion), a speckle-aware verifier ranks them by gradient-magnitude correlation with the SAR input and a hallucination-frequency index, and a SAR-guided frequency gate admits high-frequency detail only where SAR edges support it. With all five hyperparameters frozen and three training seeds, we evaluate on 500 scene-disjoint SEN1-2 test patches, 500 patches of a held-out summer scene, and 200 cross-dataset SEN12MS winter pairs. The layer improves Pix2Pix by +0.060, +0.012, and +0.077 dB PSNR and by +0.0034, +0.0057, and +0.0135 SSIM, respectively (Wilcoxon p < 1e-6), and it improves CycleGAN and CUT on all three sets (+0.013 to +0.076 dB). It raises edge precision at the cost of edge recall (edge F1 -1.3% on the test set). On the latent diffusion model COP-GEN, it adds +0.33 dB (base) and +0.16 dB (Beta) on the winter set, the only set with dual-polarization SAR. An ablation shows that the gate produces the gain for MC-dropout GANs, whose candidates are nearly identical, whereas verifier selection contributes up to +0.26 dB for diffusion, whose samples differ. The layer has zero trainable parameters and adds 0.4 ms per patch beyond the K=5 generator passes.

## 239. VLN-Flash: Parallel Action Decoding with Adaptive Execution for Vision-and-Language Navigation

- **作者**：Ruizhe Xie, Honghua Chen, Binglin Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

When a vision-and-language navigation policy generates an action sequence autoregressively, the robot waits for a chain of dependent predictions. A small discrete action vocabulary makes it possible to score all short-horizon plans in parallel. We introduce VLN-Flash, a training-free decoder that evaluates the complete action-prefix tree in a single forward pass. Branch-specific attention and matched token positions preserve the original policy's conditional action distribution for each prefix in exact arithmetic. We use the score margin between the stepwise-greedy prefix and its strongest alternative at each depth to decide how many actions to execute. The robot then executes the plan with the highest joint score at the chosen depth before observing again. On matched inputs, VLN-Flash achieves a nearly $3\times$ speedup in action decoding over native autoregression on Uni-NaVid and StreamVLN. Compared with the native autoregressive decoders, it improves success rate and success weighted by path length (SPL) on R2R-CE and RxR-CE, with success gains up to 2.7 percentage points. On a Unitree GO2, VLN-Flash improves navigation success and cuts the mean wait between actions by about 160 ms after subtracting network transmission time.

## 240. Generate, Align, and Restore: MLLM-Empowered Diffusion for Generalizable Low-light Image Enhancement

- **作者**：Kaisheng Pang, Feng Li, Chen Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 13 figures

Low-light image enhancement (LLIE) is essential to numerous vision applications operating under adverse illumination. Despite substantial advances, real-world nighttime enhancement remains challenging due to the scarcity of strictly aligned day-night pairs and complex degradations involving extreme darkness and heterogeneous artificial light sources, leading to fragile generalization. To overcome these challenges, we propose MrLIE (MLLM-empowered reference Low-light Image Enhancement), a novel reference-based unsupervised LLIE framework that synergizes the generative and reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with conditional diffusion. MrLIE employs the MLLM as a high-fidelity reference synthesizer and diagnostic critic, iteratively generating, diagnosing, and correcting normal-light references directly from degraded nighttime observations. Based on this, our method governs the enhancement process through two complementary mechanisms, in which a confidence offset module rectifies spatial-geometric inconsistencies for reference reliability and a structure-anchored injection scheme harmonizes aligned reference cues, low-light structural constraints, and ControlNet additive anchoring during the denoising process. Extensive experiments on diverse benchmarks demonstrate that MrLIE achieves superior perceptual quality, natural appearance, and robust generalization.

## 241. LC-ISP Benchmark: Learning Advanced ISP with Live and Capture Streams

- **作者**：Kai Chen, Junyu Lou, Jinhua Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/CVL-UESTC/LC-ISP

High-quality paired RAW-to-sRGB datasets are essential for data-driven ISP, yet existing datasets often rely on cross-device collection, causing misalignment and failing to faithfully reflect real capture conditions. We therefore introduce LC-ISP, a large-scale benchmark built from the intrinsic gap between a camera's live preview and offline capture streams. LC-ISP contains 53.9k spatially aligned groups, each comprising live RAW/live sRGB and multi-frame fused RGB/capture sRGB, and covers day, night, indoor, outdoor, and HDR scenes. It also provides capture-stream intermediates for modular supervision. We further develop a compact two-stage baseline that uses these intermediates as auxiliary targets. Under a 2.5-GFLOP budget for 448$\times$448 RAW inputs, it outperforms the strongest single-frame baseline by over 2 dB PSNR while improving texture, denoising, and color reproduction. LC-ISP supports realistic benchmarking, the design of supervision strategies, and the development of lightweight ISP models.

## 242. dit.xpu : Taming DiT Sparse Inference on Mobile SoCs

- **作者**：Jiayu Chen, Jinye Hu, Maoliang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) enable on-device image generation and editing but incur high attention costs at high resolutions. Existing sparse attention reduces computation, yet dynamic sparsity introduces preprocessing and irregular data accesses that poorly fit mobile NPUs. We present DiT.XPU, a sparse DiT inference framework for heterogeneous mobile SoCs. DiT.XPU combines tile-aligned sparse attention with anchor-based error compensation by exploiting slowly varying dense-sparse output residuals across denoising steps. Heterogeneous XPU collaboration overlaps NPU dense attention with GPU sparse calibration, while mask-guided dataflow reduces data transfers and layout conversion by overlapping data movement with computation. Across five DiT models on two Snapdragon smartphones, DiT.XPU achieves up to 1.56x end-to-end denoising speedup over the Hexagon-NPU backend and 11.5x attention-kernel speedup over the fastest alternative, while preserving generation quality close to dense attention. Code is available at https://github.com/IF-LAB-PKU/dit.xpu.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19261) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19261)

## 243. Size-Matched Nulls Reveal What Block Ablation Measures in MambaVision

- **作者**：Tahmina Kousar, Tehseen Zia
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 5 figures

Hybrid vision backbones interleave Mamba state-space mixers with self-attention, and a block's role is usually probed by removing it and measuring the damage. We test what that measurement means in MambaVision. On all 50,000 ImageNet validation images and three model sizes, every block ablation is paired with a size-matched null, the same perturbation with its channels shuffled or their signs flipped. In top-1 accuracy and in true-class rank, real and null damage are nearly indistinguishable in absolute terms: pooled over the blocks of MambaVision-T, the content effect lies within [-0.044,+0.016] percentage points (90% interval). Two attention-only controls show that the test detects content, with real ablations worse than their nulls by up to 4.2 points in DeiT-Small and 1.0 point in Swin-T. Part of MambaVision's near-equivalence reflects how little damage its blocks do (0.11--0.34 points on average, against 1.7 and 2.1). Scaled by damage, its content share is comparable to Swin-T's in accuracy, lower in rank, and far below DeiT-Small's: single-block damage in MambaVision is small and mostly positional. Ablation also hides what blocks carry together: removing all blocks of one type compounds damage to 3.7--8.4 times the sum of single effects, far more than size-matched nulls, including nulls that keep the alignment between the blocks' perturbations; for adjacent pairs, aligned nulls reproduce most of it. Freezing input-dependent selection in all Mamba mixers shows that it accounts for 20--32% of the Mamba contribution, mainly through the B and C projections. We turn these results into a protocol for causal attribution in hybrid networks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19248) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19248)

## 244. GameWM: Toward Next-Generation Video Games with Interaction-Centric World Models

- **作者**：Bing He, Runze Wang, Fei Zha et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：35 pages, technical report

For more than half a century, video games have pursued better graphics, more natural interaction, and more engaging gameplay. Generative video models (VMs) now bear on all three. Neural networks already draw most of the pixels in shipped games, and recent world models let a lightweight game engine keep the world state while a VM turns the engine's coarse proxy video into final frames. These systems, however, treat the VM as a renderer that only restyles motion the engine has already authored, which leaves most of its generative capacity unused and limits the diversity a game can offer. We present GameWM, a design for next-generation games built on a clear division of labor. The GameWM-engine keeps what must be exact: rules, state, camera, and the layout of the world, which also spares the video model from having to remember it. The GameWM-VM generates what is expensive to author by hand: the look of the world and the detail of the player's interactions. One game mechanic can then be realized in many visually and physically distinct ways, while its outcome stays exactly as the engine computed it. To realize this division of labor, we build GameWM as a complete system: a data engine centered on interactions rather than exploration, an interface through which authored character sheets, scene art, and skill descriptions enter the model, and a model family from 5B to 33B parameters with a real-time causal variant for play. Together they give every player a fresh version of the same game: each player's own character, world, and skills turn the same engine mechanics into a new experience, while every event keeps the timing and outcome the GameWM-engine computed.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19247) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19247)

## 245. Beyond Alignment: Supervising the Generator for EEG-to-Image Reconstruction

- **作者**：Sangmin Jo, Wootaek Jeong, Heung-Il Suk
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Visual brain decoding translates neural activity into human-interpretable images. While recent EEG-to-image reconstruction methods rely on aligning EEG signals with pretrained visual embeddings to condition an image generator, we empirically demonstrate that stronger cross-modal alignment do not translate into comparable gains in reconstruction quality. This indicates that improving the input condition alone is insufficient; the generative process itself requires explicit supervision. Although Representation Alignment (REPA) offers a solution by guiding diffusion transformers with pretrained vision encoder features, we find that EEG-conditioned generators struggle to follow these fixed targets. To address this mismatch, we introduce generation-derived supervision (GDS): an image-conditioned teacher that translates static image-encoder features into the internal representation space of a diffusion transformer. Our EEG-conditioned student generator closely tracks these dynamic targets during denoising, discarding the teacher at inference. Evaluated on THINGS-EEG and THINGS-MEG, our approach consistently outperforms conditioning-centric baselines, achieving state-of-the-art reconstruction fidelity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19232) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19232)

## 246. Incremental Learning of Vision-Language Models via Task Subspace Projection and Dynamic LoRA

- **作者**：Hao Fu, Qian Feng, Hanbin Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Pre-peer-review version of an article accepted for publication in the International Journal of Computer Vision (IJCV)

Recent pre-trained vision-language models often encounter a Multi-Domain Task-Incremental Learning (MTIL) scenario in practice, where a set of classes of multi-modal tasks arrive incrementally. Due to privacy concerns and memory constraints, MTIL with pre-trained models encounters forgetting of knowledge from old tasks, degradation of zero-shot transfer capability, and underfitting of new-task knowledge. To overcome these challenges, previous MTIL methods attempt to learn a discriminative cross-task identification (CTI) module and an effective new-task adaptation (NTA) module. However, current CTI modules suffer from severe task confusion between seen and unseen tasks, and NTA modules cannot adaptively balance the performance and parameter cost while incorporating task-specific knowledge. To alleviate the above dilemmas, we propose an effective and efficient TSP-DLoRA method for MTIL, which consists of Task Subspace Projection (TSP) and Dynamic Low Rank Adapter (DLoRA) modules. Specifically, our TSP module includes a task identifier classifier based on task-specific subspaces and a feature projection strategy that can determine the identifier associated with samples from both seen and unseen tasks. Our DLoRA improves the knowledge adaptation from new tasks by dynamically assigning Low Rank Adapter (LoRA) across transformer layers based on the task distributions. Experimental evaluations across 11 datasets, using three performance metrics, demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed method. Code is available at https://github.com/FerdinandZJU/TSP-DLoRA.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19213) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19213)

## 247. LogT: Logically Think with Images for Visual Search

- **作者**：Yanjun Fu, Quanzeng You, Jiadong Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026

Thinking with images empowers vision-language models (VLMs) to use an external Zoom-in tool for fine-grained visual search. Recent works employ supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL) with tool-specific designs to enable such capabilities. However, achieving logical reasoning alongside accurate and efficient tool use remains a significant challenge. To address this, we shift the focus away from increasing RL complexity and instead identify SFT data quality as a key bottleneck. We then propose LogT (short for Logically Think with Images), a fully automated SFT data engine that explicitly enforces logic-chain-guided reasoning. LogT generates an SFT dataset featuring challenging, visually grounded questions and structured tool-use traces with human-like logic. We validate our approach by fine-tuning two base models, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and InternVL3.5-8B, on the dataset and evaluating them across seven benchmark test sets. We show that LogT-SFT based on Qwen2.5-VL-7B outperforms the state-of-the-art trained with both SFT and RL, despite using 3.1$\times$ less data and $>120\times$ fewer GPU hours. Furthermore, applying a simple RL recipe without tool-specific design on LogT-SFT yields a model that surpasses prior methods with complex RL setups by a substantial 9.59-point margin. In addition to strong performance gains, our model exhibits higher tool-use accuracy and efficiency, as well as more structured and logically consistent reasoning traces, directly validating the effectiveness of our approach.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19200) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19200)

## 248. Ref-MIL: Plug-and-Play Posterior Refinement for Multiple Instance Learning in QSM-Based Brain Disease Classification

- **作者**：Ang Gaoa, Rui Jina, Yin Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV
- **备注**：5figure, 6tables;

Quantitative susceptibility mapping (QSM) is a novel quantitative MRI contrast for studying tissue susceptibility changes in brain disorders, including Parkinson's disease (PD) and multiple sclerosis (MS). However, small cohorts and the lack of local annotations make developing deep learning models for QSM-based diagnosis challenging. In this work, we propose PoRef-MIL, a weakly supervised multiple instance learning (MIL) framework combining iterative slice relabeling and network retraining with a plug-and-play posterior refinement module (PRM). During training, a 2D dual-branch diagnosis network and a subject-level classifier were alternately trained for five rounds. The classifier was used to update slice pseudo-labels for retraining the diagnosis network. The PRM was applied during inference to refine slice probabilities through bidirectional posterior inference and calculate entropy-derived confidence from the refined probabilities, which was then used in sliding-window scoring to obtain subject-level predictions. PoRef-MIL was evaluated on an in-house PD cohort of 287 participants and a public MS cohort of 150 participants. Compared with eight classification methods, PoRef-MIL achieved the highest mean areas under the receiver operating characteristic curve of 0.942 and 0.872, and accuracies of 0.926 and 0.833, for PD and MS classification, respectively. Ablation experiments were conducted to validate the effectiveness of iterative relabeling and PRM, and the plug-and-play feature of the proposed PRM was also evaluated using four state-of-the-art MIL models. Overall, PoRef-MIL offers a promising approach to QSM-based disease classification in small cohorts without manual slice annotations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19193) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19193)

## 249. Calibrating the Uncertainty of Self-Supervised Monocular Depth without Labels

- **作者**：Jun Seok Kim, Rui Huang
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables

Self-supervised monocular depth networks can predict a per-pixel uncertainty, which is mainly evaluated by how well it ranks pixels according to their error. Such metrics are insensitive to the magnitude of the uncertainty, so an uncertainty half as large as the actual error obtains the same score. In this paper, we investigate whether the predicted uncertainty is as large as the depth error, both on average (scale) and across confident and uncertain, near and far pixels (shape). On KITTI, the scale is miscalibrated for every uncertainty estimate we evaluate, and the error of MonoProb is nearly twice its predicted uncertainty. We propose MonoCal, which corrects both the scale and the shape without ground-truth depth. Scale calibration multiplies the uncertainty by a single constant measured from stereo pairs, requiring no retraining, and this constant closely matches the one fitted with ground truth. Since a constant cannot change the shape, we further retrain the uncertainty to predict where the depth of the network disagrees with that of a second self-supervised network. Experiments on KITTI show that MonoCal achieves better calibration across uncertainty levels and depth ranges than rescaling with ground truth, while still using a single image at inference. Code will be made publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19188) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19188)

## 250. Do VLMs Really Look at the Road? A Diagnostic Evaluation for Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Shankar Gangisetty, Muskan Singh, C V Jawahar
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly being evaluated for autonomous driving through question-answering benchmarks, yet high benchmark accuracy does not necessarily reflect visually grounded reasoning. We present a diagnostic evaluation framework that systematically probes whether VLMs base safety-critical driving decisions on visual evidence or instead rely on language priors, answer biases, and dataset regularities. Our benchmark comprises two task categories, Adverse Weather and Junctions \& Intersections, with a nuScenes spatial reasoning extension, and evaluates open-source (general, driving, and spatial-reasoning) VLMs along four diagnostic protocols: image versus no-image evaluation, counterfactual weather sensitivity, linguistic robustness under paraphrasing and negation, and spatial reasoning. All ground-truth labels are derived programmatically from BDD100K and DriveLM annotations, enabling reproducible evaluation without manual labeling. Across all models, we find that competitive overall accuracy conceals systematic failure modes, including strong affirmation bias, limited sensitivity to counterfactual scene changes, near-chance performance on negated questions despite high paraphrase consistency, and confident driving decisions even without visual input. These results demonstrate that current open-source VLMs remain insufficiently grounded for safety-critical autonomous driving and underscore the need for diagnostic evaluation beyond aggregate accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19184) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19184)

## 251. WA-JEPA: Goal-Aware Predictive Feedback Action Refinement for a World-Action Model

- **作者**：Tao An, Weihang Liang, Tianqi Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 5 figures, including supplementary material. Code available at https://github.com/Dovisya/WA-JEPA

JEPA-based world models predict action consequences in latent space, but representative planning approaches rely on candidate-action search, requiring repeated evaluation of action sequences and incurring substantial online computational cost. Direct action generation avoids this search, yet how to leverage latent predictions to guide action generation given task goals remains a key challenge in translating learned physical dynamics into effective decisions. We introduce WA-JEPA, a world-action model that couples action generation, action-conditioned latent prediction, and goal-aware feedback. The model first generates an action proposal and predicts the resulting future scenes and proprioceptive states, then refines the proposal through two complementary feedback paths, using the task goal as a reference. Goal-discrepancy residual feedback (GDRF) uses differences between the latent representations of predicted scenes and the task goal to guide chunk-wise action refinement. Future-scene context feedback (FSCF) preserves fine-grained predicted scene representations to guide subsequent action refinement. Jointly learned from offline demonstrations, this process iteratively updates the same plan before execution, making future predictions directly participate in action generation without candidate-action search. We evaluate short-horizon goal reaching, long-horizon tasks, and full-task execution. The results show that goal-aware predictive feedback supports competitive task performance while maintaining a compact model and low online computational cost. Code is available at https://github.com/Dovisya/WA-JEPA.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19183) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19183)

## 252. Isolating Perception for Multi-View Planning under Occlusion-Induced Partial Observability

- **作者**：Kevin Nguyen, Ceyhun Efe Kayan, Feng Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：EACL 2027

Vision-language models (VLMs) are beginning to saturate visual planning benchmarks, yet they fail to resolve depth ambiguity and degrade sharply with problem size under occlusion-induced partial observability (PO). We introduce BW-PO based on the Blocksworld domain, a controlled benchmark that reduces existing VLM planners and formalizers to near-zero performance, and propose VLM-as-perceiver, a paradigm that narrows the VLM's role to local perception while delegating cross-view evidence reconciliation and plan generation to external solvers. Across model-specific challenging subsets of BW-PO, this raises plan success from 1% to 97% for Gemma-4-31B, 0% to 95% for Qwen3.5-122B-A10B, and 3% to 84% for Gemini-3.1-Pro. Isolating perception in this way separates two bottlenecks that are otherwise entangled: reconciliation under depth ambiguity, and completeness of visual extraction under scene density.

## 253. EviRef: Evidence-Specific Memories for Logical Anomaly Detection across Capture Conditions

- **作者**：Yaoting Huang, Wenqi Cai, Yijin Wei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Logical anomalies arise when every component looks normal but their combination violates the scene rules. In practical inspection, these rules must hold under varying backgrounds, illumination, and imaging quality, with only a few dozen normal images per scene, whereas existing methods couple normality with appearance and merge different constraints into one reference. We model logical normality as decomposable constraints and propose EviRef, a training-free method that builds a separate reference memory for each family of evidence. Component prototypes built once per scene parse images under any capture condition into one label space, on which occupancy and relation constraints are verified. Semantic constraints are verified with whole-image embeddings independent of parsing, and additional evidence such as text is admitted through a task-level gate without modifying existing memories. On VID-AD, EviRef attains a mean image-level area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of 92.1% (88.9% with visual evidence alone), the highest among the compared methods under each of five capture conditions, with a standard deviation of 0.5. Even with memories built from only 20 normal images per task, it surpasses all compared methods that use 50 normal images per task.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19172) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19172)

## 254. From zero-target transfer to target-site personalization: multi-source training across fall-detection models

- **作者**：Jaeseung Ryu, Jae-Hun Jung
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video-based fall detection could support timely assistance when a person cannot call for help. Unfamiliar cameras and scarce local labels challenge deployment. We examine whether joint training improves cross-dataset detection and subsequent local training. We analyze 2,697 clips from eight OmniFall datasets and one AI-Hub dataset. Positive clips contain a fall or collapse transition, while negative clips do not. We hold out each dataset in turn, training on the other eight. We evaluate three vision-language models (VLMs), Qwen, LLaVA, and InternVL, and three visual baselines, CLIP-temporal, VideoMAE, and Pose-TCN. We evaluate the stored source model without target data for training or selection. For each label budget, we update a separate copy using labeled clips from the held-out target dataset. We call this target-site personalization. We compare the Source-initialized model with Target-only, which skips source training but uses identical target clips and updates. Across nine targets, source training raises mean area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) from frozen-model values of 0.821 to 0.912 for Qwen, 0.883 to 0.947 for LLaVA, and 0.939 to 0.962 for InternVL. With five target examples per class and 26 updates per run, 95% confidence intervals for the AUROC benefit over Target-only lie above zero for Qwen, CLIP-temporal, VideoMAE, and Pose-TCN. With full-target training, they lie above zero only for Qwen and LLaVA. Separate VLM analyses reveal threshold sensitivity and post-fall false detections. The benefit varies by model and label budget.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19156) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19156)

## 255. Puzzle: One Pass Is Not Enough for Open-Vocabulary Remote Sensing Segmentation

- **作者**：Wensong Xu, Siqi Lu, Jibao Yuan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Open-vocabulary remote sensing image segmentation (OVRSIS) enables the segmentation of unseen classes without additional annotation or retraining. By analyzing the predictions of existing OVRSIS methods, we find that many misclassified patches coexist with visually similar, correctly classified patches of the same class within the same image. Unfortunately, most of these methods stop after a single inference rather than using their predictions to guide further inference. Motivated by this observation, we propose \textbf{Puzzle}, a plug-and-play iterative re-inference framework that combines prediction history with a visual-similarity prior to progressively refine patch-level attention. Puzzle employs a lightweight history-conditioned controller to regulate each patch's contribution as an information source and its reliance on visually similar patches, while an adaptive stopping rule determines when to stop. It keeps the segmentation model frozen and reuses the same controller across different models and datasets without retraining. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent performance improvements across diverse OVRSIS models and datasets, highlighting the strong transferability of Puzzle. Moreover, Puzzle can further improve the performance of SOTA methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19146) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19146)

## 256. DecoEdit: Beyond Flat Control for 3D-Aware Unified Image Editing

- **作者**：Xiaotang Gai, Jinbo Xing, Zijie Meng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

While recent advances in multimodal image editing have achieved impressive results, current frameworks remain largely confined to ``flat" 2D controls (e.g., text prompts, bounding boxes, or masks). Precise 3D-aware geometric manipulation -- such as coordinating an object's location, physical scale, and 3D orientation while strictly preserving its fine-grained identity -- remains an elusive challenge, typically suffering from severe detail degradation or requiring expensive per-instance fine-tuning. In this paper, we present DecoEdit, an all-in-one image editing framework that goes beyond flat control by seamlessly integrating 1D textual, 2D spatial, and 3D volumetric inputs under a unified architecture. First, to power this unified model, we establish a data curation pipeline and curate a large-scale dataset, namely DecoDataset, containing 1.6 million samples, encompassing diverse multi-dimensional (1D/2D/3D) tasks and their complex compositions. Furthermore, we establish a comprehensive image editing benchmark named DecoBench to systematically evaluate multi-task editing performance. Finally, to address the fundamental trade-off between complex spatial transformations and identity preservation, we propose a novel decoupled pipeline: DecoEdit factorizes the editing process by utilizing structural guidance from SAM-3D to anchor the target geometry, scale, and spatial orientation, while concurrently leveraging a detail-preservation path to lock in high-fidelity textures. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DecoEdit achieves state-of-the-art results. Notably, on the challenging 3D spatial pose and orientation control tasks, DecoEdit significantly outperforms leading closed-source models such as Seedream 5.0 in both geometric accuracy and identity consistency.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19121) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19121)

## 257. DEFDVO: Deformable Deep Visual Odometry for Equirectangular Images

- **作者**：Burak Sevsay, Egor Bondarev, Erkut Akdag
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to WACV 2027

Omnidirectional ($360^\circ$) cameras capture the full surrounding scene in every frame, providing a strong advantage for visual odometry (VO) over pinhole sensors with a limited field of view. However, their equirectangular projection (ERP) introduces latitude-dependent distortion, an artificial seam at the image border, and a sampling bias toward the poles. Existing $360^\circ$ VO methods address these challenges only partially. DEFDVO closes this gap with a patch-based deep VO method that operates directly on ERP imagery, combining spherically initialized deformable feature extraction, end-to-end seam-aware estimation, and spherical-gradient patch selection. The deformable feature extractor combines spherical geometric priors with learned sampling offsets, while seam-aware processing preserves ERP continuity, and spherical-gradient selection reduces polar sampling bias. Trained exclusively on synthetic TartanAirV2 ERP imagery, DEFDVO improves the best prior average absolute trajectory error (ATE) by $4.8\times$ (0.201 m to 0.042 m) on the TartanAirV2 evaluation set defined by 360DVO and achieves the lowest average ATE on both splits of the real-world 360DVO benchmark. Code is available at https://github.com/BurakSevsay/DEFDVO

## 258. FDAM: A Frequency-Direction-Aware Multi-Task Framework for Pavement Crack Grid Localization

- **作者**：Haiyang Xu, Bin Pan, Mengze Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Pavement crack grid localization remains challenging for thin and tiny cracks due to weak visual cues and background interference. Box detection often misses these cracks, while supervised semantic segmentation faces limited pixel-wise annotations and high labeling costs. In this paper, we propose a Frequency-Direction-Aware Multi-Task framework (FDAM) with box detection and grid classification branches. The core grid classification branch comprises Frequency-Domain Enhancement (FDE) and Directional Feature Refinement (DFR). FDE adaptively weights spectral bands and fuses reconstructed features with spatial features. DFR captures thin crack patterns through multi-scale strip convolutions, direction-sensitive modulation, and feature recalibration. Sparse positive cells and ambiguous local predictions motivate adaptive grid supervision. Adaptive Importance Reweighting emphasizes uncertain predictions and local label transitions, with focal supervision for all cells. Our contribution is to couple the box detection and grid classification branches through shared feature learning for pavement crack grid localization. We validate FDAM on our RoadDistress2026 dataset of thin and tiny cracks and further evaluate it on public datasets Crack500 and DeepCrack. The code is available at https://github.com/Lab-PANbin/FDAM
