## 259. SearchChord: Prompt Preselection and Field Recomposition for One-Step Image Editing

- **作者**：Boyang Deng, Yuzhen Lu
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 3 figures, conference

Finding effective prompts and seeds for one-step image editors requires balancing edit fidelity, source preservation, and computational cost. To address this challenge, we explore a large collection of candidate edits, where each candidate is defined by a unique prompt-seed pair, and generate corresponding images on demand. We present SearchChord, a training-free framework that leverages a large pool of such candidates, combining text-based preselection, image verification, and latent-field recomposition. Our ablation studies across pool sizes up to 200 candidates show that a pool of 100 offers a cost-effective operating point. Compared with feeding all 100 candidates into recomposition, TextSim preselects a subset of 10 candidates. Rather than uniform averaging, we incorporate image-verifier scores to weight each candidate during recomposition. Experiments on PIE-Bench and the proposed CropWeed-EditBench reveal a trade-off: candidate selection improves target-text alignment, while recomposition better preserves unmodified source content. The oracle verifier achieves the best performance in both preservation and target-text alignment. Practical verification partially recovers semantic alignment, but recomposition still compromises edit fidelity, including in agricultural image edits. These results motivate text-first preselection and stronger verification to balance preservation, fidelity, and cost. Code and data will be made publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19076) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19076)

## 260. Hard or Mislabeled? Failure Screens Enrich Vision Benchmarks for Label Errors

- **作者**：Haolin Guo
- **投稿**：2026-11-14 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：28 pages, 5 figures, 16 tables

Hard vision benchmarks often keep images that strong classifiers get wrong. Since a classifier is also marked wrong when the label is wrong, such failure screens may collect label errors. We measure this on ImageNet's twice-relabeled validation set with 103 classifiers. Among the 1,892 images that all 66 supervised classifiers get wrong, 61% have a wrong label, versus 11.1% overall. The fewer images a screen lets through, the more of them are mislabeled, along approximately one curve. Fit on one model family, this curve predicts the screens of two held-out model families with a mean error of 2.4 percentage points, and replays of ImageNet-D's and ImageNet-Hard's screens within 0.1 and 6.3 points. On new pools, ImageNet-V2 and CIFAR-10, enrichment at the strictest screens is stronger than the curve predicts: on ImageNet-V2, by human relabeling, 67% of the images that all 66 classifiers get wrong are mislabeled, against 46% predicted. Screens that let models choose among only some of the classes, as ImageNet-A's does (200 of 1,000), fall below the curve. By ReImageNet, 32% of ImageNet-Hard's ImageNet images have no correct label. ScreenAudit, which comprises an audit tool, a script that rebuilds corrected answer sets for the 5,464 decided ImageNet-Hard images (of 5,646) from ReImageNet's gated release, and per-image audits of 1,198 benchmark images, will be released later.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19044) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19044)

## 261. Role-Separated Diffusion Transformers: Global Rule Isolation and Deterministic Sampling Manifold Compression

- **作者**：Zhiguang Liu, Yi Shang
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Github Repo: https://github.com/lz7fd/RoSeDiT

We present Role-Separated Diffusion Transformers (RoSeDiT), a new diffusion transformer architecture that isolates the global rule from the image tokens. RoSeViT showed in visual reasoning that a vanilla ViT spreads a shared rule over all $L$ tokens at an entropy of order $Ld$, whereas an enforced controller route reduces it to order $d$. In class-conditional DiT and SiT, the class and the noise level are explicitly conditioned by adaLN, so a RoSeViT-style controller channel on the ViT side is insufficient to establish the full role separation. By introducing the adaLN listening mechanism, which makes adaLN compute under the controller's command, RoSeDiT brings the entropy theorem and role separation into the latent diffusion domain. Under the same SiT training and evaluation protocol at the same level of parameter count, RoSeDiT surpasses SiT at all four model scales at 400K steps, and RoSeDiT-L/2 even surpasses SiT-XL/2's 17.2 FID with 15.34 and 32% fewer parameters. RoSeDiT-XL/2 surpasses SiT-XL/2's 7M-step FID of 8.3 at 1.4M steps with 7.98, using one-fifth as many training steps. We further observe that models with the same denoising loss can have a large FID gap, which indicates a misalignment between the denoising objective and the image manifold. We formalize this phenomenon into Deterministic Sampling Manifold Compression (DSMC), a two-stage training schedule with which RoSeDiT-XL/2 surpasses SiT + REPA's 4M-step FID of 5.9 at 2.4M steps with 5.84.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19009) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.19009)

## 262. Probing Where Surprise Happens: Localized Violation-of-Expectation Evaluation of Video World Models

- **作者**：Yanbin Xu, Ming bo Cai
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, eess.IV
- **备注**：8 pages, 4 figures

Violation-of-expectation (VOE) benchmarks evaluate intuitive physics in video world models by testing whether physically impossible events produce greater prediction errors than matched possible events. However, conventional VOE evaluations aggregate prediction errors across space and time, making it unclear whether successful discrimination reflects model's understanding of physical violations at the locations and time where they occur. To address this limitation, we introduce localized VOE evaluation, a framework that measures prediction error differences between possible and impossible events within spatial regions and temporal intervals associated with physical violations. We compare global, temporally localized, and spatiotemporally localized evaluations of V-JEPA models pretrained on developmental egocentric videos or large-scale natural video datasets. Our results reveal that global and localized judgments can diverge in both directions: global aggregation can either obscure correct localized discrimination or produce apparent discrimination without corresponding localized evidence. During continued pretraining on developmental egocentric videos, agreement between global and localized judgments changes non-monotonically, while confidence in localized judgments increases despite modest improvements in accuracy. Together, these findings establish localized VOE evaluation as a complementary diagnostic for assessing intuitive physics in video world models and demonstrate that global prediction errors do not necessarily reflect the spatiotemporal evidence of physical violations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18979) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18979)

## 263. Effects of Conventional, Adaptive, and AI-Powered Upscaling on the Legibility of License Plates

- **作者**：Hany Farid, Joshua D. Bucci, Emily A. Cooper
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Small details in digital images -- a face, a street sign, a license plate -- are routinely upscaled in hopes of making them interpretable. Upscaling options now range from conventional interpolation, whose operations are spatially invariant and fully explainable, to AI-powered methods that synthesize detail potentially absent from the source. Here, we ask which of these techniques most reliably helps human readers. In two online experiments, we measured legibility of 255 US license plates ranging in resolution from 128 to 8 pixels tall, as well as their legibility after upscaling by eight different methods. Legibility of the un-upscaled plates collapsed from ceiling to floor across a narrow band of plate heights (32 to 10 pixels). Conventional upscaling techniques (bilinear, bicubic, and Lanczos interpolation) gave modest gains of about 5-9 percentage points within this range. Nearest-neighbor interpolation, however, was harmful, rendering unreadable many plates that were readable without any upscaling. Advanced methods diverged: SwinIR, an open-source AI enhancer, raised performance by up to 38 points relative to no upscaling, while an adaptive denoiser-based image prior and commercial AI upscaler tended to impair legibility. We found no strong evidence of consistent character hallucinations across methods. Decomposing the task, conventional interpolators mainly helped readers count characters, whereas SwinIR alone made the characters themselves more identifiable.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18949) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18949)

## 264. Learned Regularization for Sparse-View CT: Accuracy, Lesion Detectability and Distribution Shift of a Plug-and-Play Prior against Tuned Total Variation

- **作者**：Mohammed Abo Oraig
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV, eess.SP
- **备注**：62 pages, 22 figures, and 30 tables. Preprint (not peer-reviewed); also available on Zenodo: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24107061. Code and stored results are available at: https://github.com/AboOraig/learned-regularization

Learned image priors are often proposed to replace hand-crafted regularizers in sparse-view computed tomography (CT), yet they are usually judged by PSNR on data resembling their training set. We evaluate one noise-conditioned DnCNN denoiser used as a Plug-and-Play prior against filtered back-projection (FBP), Tikhonov and total-variation (TV) regularization, with every method tuned on validation data and all differences paired. On synthetic 28x28 digit images, the prior beats box-constrained TV by about 3.4 dB at moderate noise (sigma = 0.05, 20 angles), but the advantage shrinks at low noise and reverses under distribution shift: an MNIST-trained prior is 3.1 dB (Fashion-MNIST) and 4.3-4.6 dB (Shepp-Logan phantom) below TV, whereas a Fashion-MNIST-trained prior beats TV by 2.2 dB on Fashion-MNIST, consistent with prior mismatch rather than a failure of PnP itself. In a lesion-insertion test the prior detects lesions worse than TV in distribution despite its higher PSNR, and under mismatch it produces more lesion-like false blobs than box-constrained TV for seven of eight lesion types. On real LoDoPaB-CT slices (patient-disjoint split; 30, 60 and 120 angles; three dose levels; 40 test patients), the prior does not consistently outperform a well-tuned TV in PSNR (-0.93 to +0.33 dB) and has lower SSIM in eight of nine conditions. Its lesion-detection AUC is higher than this TV's in all nine conditions (significant in six) but never significantly higher than that of Hann-filtered FBP, which the PSNR-tuned TV falls below in detection in all nine conditions (seven significant). On non-anatomical phantoms its PSNR falls below TV in all six conditions. PSNR and lesion detectability thus rank the methods differently. Our conclusions concern one small denoiser, and the CT analysis was developed after seeing results, without pre-specified primary comparisons.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18936) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18936)

## 265. Lasso: Towards Sustainable AI-Generated Media Detection with Intel QAT On-Chip Compression

- **作者**：Darsh Asher, Joshua Kalyanapu, Kaushal Mhapsekar et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.AR, cs.AI, cs.CR, cs.CV

Synthetic text, images, and video are rising. Software-only detectors are power-hungry, too slow, and costly, and do not scale with demand. When software cannot scale, historically hardware often comes to the rescue. We perform the first reverse engineering of a compression accelerator--Intel QuickAssist Technology (QAT) microarchitecture and--find different amount of time on each, a novel data structure-dependent property. Using this information, we then build Lasso, a low-cost, high-performance detector for AI-generated content that turns the accelerator into a measuring instrument. For text detection, Lasso achieves 100% accuracy on original DetectRL text and retains 92% average accuracy after paraphrasing, compared with 78% for Binoculars and 55% for DetectGPT. Across original, paraphrased, and perturbed text, it achieves 82% average accuracy, outperforming all five baselines. With topic, length, and formatting matched, accuracy reaches 95% for instruction-tuned generators, including when the tested generator is unseen. On RAID, accuracy averages 82% when trained on one generator and tested on another, and 74% across eight held-out domains. Detection takes 0.74 microseconds, a speedup of at least 83,784x across five baselines. At the same time, Lasso consumes over 99% less energy per document than both Binoculars and DetectGPT. At the same time, Lasso detects AI-generated videos, software code, and images with 67.5%, 70% and 78% accuracy, respectively, with low latency and power opening avenues for practical real time and edge device detection of multimodality AI artifacts for the first time. No other compared technique supports detection across all four modalities: text, video, images, and code.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18895) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18895)

## 266. CISyn: Human Mesh Recovery in Close Interactions with Physics-Simulated Synthetic Data

- **作者**：Yufan Deng, Lorenza Prospero, Christian Rupprecht
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

3D human mesh recovery (HMR) in close-interaction scenarios is hindered by the lack of large-scale, accurately annotated training data: real capture is expensive, and existing synthetic datasets rarely include physical contact. We introduce CISyn, a large-scale synthetic dataset purpose-built for close human-human interactions, generated via physics simulation and Blender-based rendering. By simulating collision dynamics between SMPL-X-rigged humanoids, CISyn naturally produces the contact, mutual occlusion, and pose entanglement that characterize close-interaction scenes, with no manual annotation effort. The released set provides 25,000 sequences with accurate 3D annotations. We further propose an uncertainty-aware loss that allows models to learn more effectively from the heavily occluded examples in our data. Augmenting state-of-the-art HMR training with CISyn and replacing the regression loss with our uncertainty-aware variant yields consistent improvements on close-interaction benchmarks, validating CISyn as a scalable complement to real-world data. The dataset, generation pipeline, and code are available at https://github.com/yfde/CISyn.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18894) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18894)

## 267. Bring Back Language to Actions: Restoring VLM capabilities in VLA Models via Parameter Merging

- **作者**：Mohamed Salim Aissi, Olivier Sigaud, Laure Soulier et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Accept at EACL 2027 (Main)

Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) are obtained by fine-tuning pre-trained Vision-Language Models (VLMs) on robot demonstration data, enabling action prediction from visual observations and language instructions. However, this fine-tuning induces catastrophic forgetting, substantially degrading the semantic and visual grounding capabilities of the original VLM. We address this by merging the parameters of a VLM and its VLA derivative without retraining either model. Concretely, we extract the task vector (the difference between VLA and VLM weights) and decompose it via Singular Value Decomposition. For each component, we learn a scalar mixing coefficient controlling how much of the VLA adaptation is retained, optimised to jointly preserve action prediction and vision-language capabilities. Experiments show that our merged model performs well on both axes simultaneously, consistently outperforming established merging baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18891) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18891)

## 268. RAVEN-Bench: A Paired EO-IR Video QA Benchmark for Aerial Multimodal Understanding

- **作者**：Yu Hu, Jianyang Gu, Yue Cao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 (Datasets and Benchmarks Track)

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have advanced rapidly in image and video understanding, yet their ability to reason over paired aerial electro-optical and infrared (EO-IR) videos remains poorly understood. Such video poses a harder test than standard RGB video QA: targets are often small, evidence may appear only briefly, viewpoints change with the platform, and useful cues may appear in only one stream. We introduce RAVEN-Bench (Reasoning over Aerial Videos with EO-IR aligNment), a paired EO-IR video QA benchmark for low-altitude aerial multimodal understanding. RAVEN-Bench contains 100 temporally aligned EO-IR video pairs and 1,200 human-authored four-way multiple-choice questions, each independently audited for answer keys, modality requirements, evidence intervals, and group validity. It supports EO-only, IR-only, and paired EO+IR evaluation, and uses hierarchical capability levels and structured question groups to diagnose paired-input gain beyond the stronger unimodal condition, unimodal reliance, target consistency, and evidence-chain coherence. We evaluate frontier, open-weight, and remote-sensing MLLMs, showing that aggregate accuracy can overstate evidence-supported EO-IR reasoning. RAVEN-Bench provides controlled modality conditions and group-level diagnostics for auditing whether progress on RGB video benchmarks transfers to aerial cross-spectral understanding. Code and data are available at https://github.com/yuhu990424/RAVEN-Bench and https://huggingface.co/datasets/TerraSenseAnalytics/RAVEN-Bench.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18880) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18880)

## 269. UnSPAD: Uncertainty-aware Single Photon Burst Photography

- **作者**：Sachin Shah, Kalyan Bhetwal, Levi Burner et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at The Winter Conference on Applications of Computer Vision (WACV) 2027

We introduce UnSPAD, an uncertainty-aware burst photography framework capable of fusing and denoising arbitrarily long sequences of binary single photon avalanche diode (SPAD) measurements. Unlike prior methods that require fixed burst lengths or rely on powerful generative priors, UnSPAD combines learned motion estimation with uncertainty-informed measurement fusion and denoising to produce temporally consistent, hallucination-free reconstructions. UnSPAD first forms an initial scene estimate by averaging flow-aligned binary frames. UnSPAD then uses per-pixel uncertainty maps to perform uncertainty-aware denoising; the denoiser is informed about which regions accumulated the fewest observations (and thus contain the most relative shot noise) due to motion, occlusion, or alignment errors. This two-step reconstruction pipeline helps mitigate hallucinations---outside of the structure implicitly introduced by the flow estimates, the initial scenes estimates are entirely prior-free. Experiments on real and simulated SPAD and quantum image sensor (QIS) benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art reconstruction quality, improved temporal consistency, and superior color fidelity relative to the state of the art.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18869) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18869)

## 270. FiReIQ: Field-Level Confidence Estimation with Reasoning and Image Quality in Document Extraction

- **作者**：Xiao Liu, Yage Wang, Qi Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Work in Progress

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have demonstrated strong performance in structured document extraction, yet their deployment in high-stakes applications requires reliable confidence estimates for individual fields. Existing uncertainty estimators often aggregate signals across an entire generation, obscuring field-specific reasoning uncertainty while overlooking visual degradations that may lead to confident extraction errors. To address these limitations, we introduce FiReIQ, a field-level confidence estimation framework that combines field-aligned reasoning signals from a single VLM generation with local and global image-quality features through quality-conditioned visual fusion. To facilitate systematic evaluation, we further introduce FiReIQ-Bench, a benchmark spanning financial receipts and legal contracts. Experiments with two reasoning VLMs show that FiReIQ consistently improves confidence discrimination, selective prediction, and probabilistic accuracy over existing estimators. These gains extend to private financial service data, where FiReIQ improves AUROC by 6.8% and coverage at 95% precision by 10.6% over an existing baseline. Together, these results highlight the potential of FiReIQ to reduce manual review while enabling more reliable document automation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18865) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18865)

## 271. Guided Group Featurizers (GGF): Improving Block-Sparse Featurizers with Object-Guided Learning

- **作者**：Waldemar Chang, Andrii Krutsylo
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Block-sparse featurizers (BSFs) use groups of learned directions to capture the multidimensional structure of visual concepts. However, grouping alone does not ensure that information about a concept is consolidated within a single group. We introduce Guided Group Featurizers (GGF), a novel object-guided extension that uses object annotations for initialization and training while preserving the BSF architecture. We assess whether each group's strongest response regions share an object type, part or material across images. Blinded reviews give GGF groups an average semantic coherence score of $94.1\pm0.9$%, versus $83.0\pm1.9$% for SLiCS and $65.3\pm2.2$% for supervised Vanilla BSF. GGF's consolidation advantage extends to unguided categories: one selected group per category covers $65.6\pm2.4$% of target-containing crops, versus $58.2\pm2.2$% for supervised Vanilla BSF, at approximately 5% false-positive rates for both. Across COCO and VOC, GGF also improves single-group object Capture and localization over Vanilla BSFs, SLiCS and Concept-SAE. Its groups produce more focused and reproducible object responses while supporting recognition and retaining associations under rotation, scale and brightness changes. GGF also uses fewer learned parameters than Concept-SAE and shorter recorded VOC training loops than both supervised alternatives. These findings support object guidance for organizing more interpretable BSF groups and show that response concentration extends to categories excluded from guidance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18861) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18861)

## 272. Technical Report of Auriga's 1:10 Scale Autonomous Vehicle Robot

- **作者**：Marya Sabouri Dodaran, Ali Taheri Garakani, Mandana Shabanzadeh Nasrollah-Abadi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, eess.SY
- **备注**：62 pages, 32 figures, 11 tables, 1 algorithm. Technical report of Team Auriga's 2025 1:10 scale autonomous vehicle platform (Robotics and Intelligent Automation Laboratory, Shahid Beheshti University)

This report presents the design and implementation of Auriga Robotics' 1:10 scale autonomous vehicle, developed at Shahid Beheshti University's Robotics and Intelligent Automation Laboratory. The platform integrates a custom mechanical chassis, a distributed embedded system based on ESP32 microcontrollers, and a Jetson Orin NX module for high-level perception and control. The embedded layer manages sensor interfacing, real-time data acquisition, and actuator control, while the Jetson handles environment perception through deep-learning--based lane, sign, and traffic light detection, as well as decision-making and trajectory control. System communication is achieved via structured UART links, ensuring synchronized operation between perception and control subsystems. The vehicle's software stack incorporates ROS, modular firmware design, and multiple control algorithms---Stanley, PID, and Pure Pursuit---to achieve stable autonomous navigation in structured driving scenarios. The overall system demonstrates robust performance and reliability in urban-like environments, serving as a modular research platform for future autonomous driving studies.

## 273. Scaling Channel-Adaptive Self-Supervised Learning

- **作者**：Alice V. De Lorenci, Seungeun Yi, Theo Moutakanni et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent advances in self-supervised pre-training of foundation models for natural images have made them a popular choice for various visual systems and applications. Self-supervised strategies are also promising in non-RGB scientific imaging domains such as in biology, medical and satellite imagery, but their broader application is hampered by heterogeneity in channel composition and semantics between relevant datasets: two datasets may contain different numbers of channels, and these may reveal distinct aspects of an object or scene. Recent works on channel adaptive strategies report substantial advantages for those that account for variable channel compositions without sacrificing the ability to jointly encode channels; yet, how these strategies behave at scale remains unclear. We here show that, surprisingly, trained across large-scale datasets, independent-encoding of channels outperforms joint-encoding methods by a substantial margin. We validate this result along an extensive set of experiments on various datasets from cell microscopy to geospatial imagery. Our DINO BoC approach sets a new state-of-the-art across challenging benchmarks, including generalization to out-of-distribution tasks and unseen channel combinations. We open-source code and model weights for a new general-purpose feature extractor for fluorescent microscopy.

## 274. LongCat-Video-Avatar 1.6 Technical Report

- **作者**：Meituan LongCat Team, Xunliang Cai, Yiqi Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent advances in audio-driven avatar video generation have enabled increasingly realistic synthesis, yet achieving precise lip-audio synchronization, natural, human-like motion, and visual consistency over long sequences remains challenging. We present LongCat-Video-Avatar 1.6, which builds on LongCat-Video-Avatar 1.5 to improve expressive naturalness and reduce error accumulation during extended generation, e.g., exaggerated lip movements and overly red, temporally unstable lip coloration, enhance the naturalness of facial expressions and coordinated head and body movements. These improvements are driven by a refined post-training pipeline that integrates reinforcement learning, improved classifier-free guidance strategies for DMD-based few-step distillation, long-video rollouts, and on-policy distillation. Together, these refinements improve expressive realism and enable the generation of videos exceeding 10 minutes while maintaining visual quality and temporal consistency. Arena evaluations show that LongCat-Video-Avatar 1.6 outperforms its predecessor and competing commercial audio-driven avatar models, demonstrating the effectiveness of the improved post-training pipeline.

## 275. Foundation Model Fine-Tuning with Attention-based Multi-View Fusion for Multi-Task Cardiac Function Assessment

- **作者**：João Freitas, Warre Snaet, Jan D'hooge et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, 1 figure. Original pre-peer-review manuscript. Accepted for publication in the MICCAI 2026 Satellite Events (Workshops and Challenges) proceedings. The accepted version includes methodological revisions and is available at https://papers.miccai.org/miccai-2026-sat/paper/EchoRisk2026_018.pdf

In cardio-oncology, left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) is the standard biomarker for monitoring cardiac function during cancer therapy. However, its estimation from echocardiography is highly operator dependent, and clinically meaningful declines are often detected only after myocardial injury has progressed, motivating automated methods for standardized assessment and earlier detection of LV dysfunction. To address this need, we propose a multi-task framework integrating cardiac cycle-based temporal standardization, multi-view spatiotemporal feature extraction, and joint optimization for LVEF estimation and LV dysfunction classification. Apical four- and two-chamber echocardiographic sequences are temporally standardized by sampling a clip over an estimated cardiac cycle before being processed by the EchoPrime foundation model, which is fine-tuned end-to-end within the proposed architecture. View-specific features are fused through an attention-based aggregation module to generate a patient-level embedding, from which two task-specific prediction heads estimate continuous LVEF and binary LV dysfunction. To broaden the LVEF distribution encountered during training, samples from the MIMIC-IV-ECHO dataset are incorporated using a balanced sampling strategy. Evaluated on the EchoRisk-MICCAI Challenge, our method achieves an MAE of 4.003 for LVEF estimation (Task 1) and an AUC of 0.764 for LV dysfunction detection (Task 2) on the validation set, demonstrating the effectiveness of foundation model fine-tuning with attention-based multi-view fusion for automated cardiac function assessment in cardio-oncology.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18816) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18816)

## 276. RescueVLM: Model-Agnostic Vision-Language Framework for Rescue Drone Imagery

- **作者**：Niklas Voigt, Tim Sibum, Julien Meine et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：7 pages, 6 figures

A single unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) mission over an incident site produces hundreds of images, far more than a rescue commander can review under time pressure, and human screening of destruction imagery carries a documented psychological cost. The two obvious remedies both fail in search and rescue (SAR) practice: cloud vision-language model (VLM) services are excluded because the imagery must not leave the incident network under GDPR and German civil-protection (BOS) rules, and fine-tuned specialist detectors age quickly while retraining and re-certifying them exceeds most SAR organizations resources. We present RescueVLM, an open-source, training-free and model-agnostic triage tool built on SketchVLM: a general-purpose open-weight VLM runs fully on-premise and is swapped for a newer model through a simple dropdown selection, so triage quality grows with the underlying models because all task knowledge lives in prompts and an output contract rather than in trained weights. One natural-language query over a mission folder returns a ranked shortlist with per-image justifications, editable SVG overlay annotations, and a GPS map of the hits. In a qualitative evaluation on German fire-brigade field data from three real deployments and a live fire exercise, a single query condenses an entire survey mission into a handful of annotated frames that a commander can review at a glance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18813) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18813)

## 277. CARET: Clinical Assertion-Aware Residual Calibration for Medical Image-Text Alignment

- **作者**：Yifan Jin, Tao Liu
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Medical vision--language models align radiographs with free-text reports, but radiology language distinguishes explicit findings, negation, uncertainty, and omission. Treating these report states as interchangeable during feature adaptation can change image predictions without supporting visual evidence. We introduce CARET, a parameter-efficient image-side adaptation method that retains pretrained image and text encoders and uses paired positive and negative clinical statements to define a low-dimensional semantic correction space. A bounded residual adjusts image features along these directions, while assertion-aware supervision distinguishes explicitly present and absent findings from uncertain or unmentioned ones. At inference, the adapter requires only an image; textual references remain fixed. In zero-shot classification, CARET achieves 67.80\% accuracy on CheXpert 5$\times$200 and 65.20\% on RSNA. These scores exceed the published CLEFT-LoRA results by 1.06 and 0.27 percentage points, respectively. Comparisons to literature baselines are informative but not controlled for differences in pretraining and evaluation protocols. Ablations across eight components identify the four-state distinction and assertion objective as major contributors, with task-dependent effects for nonmention preservation and pair alignment.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18811) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18811)

## 278. Accelerating Variational Cone-Beam CT Reconstruction with Learned Warm Starts

- **作者**：Héloïse Dudoignon, Clément Delmas, Laurent Facq et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted to IEEE ISBI 2027

Variational reconstruction provides a rigorous framework for cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) by combining an explicit acquisition model with image priors. However, iterative optimization remains computationally demanding, particularly under severe noise and sparse-view conditions. Although learning-based methods achieve high reconstruction quality, they often replace the original variational formulation with learned optimization schemes or nonlinear equilibrium models. We propose a neural network-guided warm-start framework that accelerates variational reconstruction while preserving its original formulation. Projection data are first reconstructed using the Feldkamp-Davis-Kress (FDK) algorithm, and the resulting image is refined by an image-domain neural network. The network output is used solely to initialize the subsequent model-based optimization, leaving the forward model, data-fidelity term, regularization, and optimization scheme unchanged. A pre-regularization step further improves compatibility with the variational priors. Experiments on pelvic CT images from the SynthRAD2025 challenge show a more than threefold reduction in reconstruction time while maintaining comparable image quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18801) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18801)

## 279. From Retouched Images to Paired Videos: A Synthetic Benchmark for Face Video Retouching

- **作者**：Peiyuan Zeng, Fengchuang Xing, Shiming Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Paired unretouched/retouched face videos are costly to produce because professional editing must remain consistent over time, while releasing identifiable face footage raises privacy, consent, and governance concerns. We present DeepVFR, an initial controlled synthetic benchmark-not a new retouching architecture-for paired face video retouching. Starting from paired synthetic DeepFRR images, Wan2.2 generates mild-motion source clips, and backend-specific face transfer constructs synchronized proxy retouching targets, followed by automatic validity screening and manual visual inspection. DeepVFR contains 1,024 pairs of 150-frame clips at 30 fps, evaluated under an identity-disjoint protocol with explicit frame correspondence; release metadata will record the construction route. Within-benchmark baselines, external checkpoints applied to DeepVFR, qualitative analysis, and a 17-participant ranking study establish reference behavior in the controlled domain. The constructed targets exhibit a substantial low-preference tail in the human study. Because both streams inherit synthetic content and the targets additionally inherit commercial-API or face-transfer artifacts, the benchmark neither replaces artist-retouched captured video nor establishes synthetic-to-real generalization. DeepVFR provides a starting point for paired supervision and controlled evaluation. Data, individual study rankings, and training logs will be made publicly available upon acceptance. Broader motion coverage and real-video validation remain future work.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18791) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18791)

## 280. Same Evidence, Different Verdicts: Delivery of CT Images and DICOM Metadata Changes VLM Quality-Control Decisions

- **作者**：Nicolas Avelar
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：4 pages, 2 figures, 1 table. Submitted to IEEE ISBI 2027

Vision-language models (VLMs) are being proposed as automated checks on clinical imaging inputs, where a scan and its DICOM metadata can disagree; a truncated CT series wrongly admitted to volumetric follow-up, such as lung-nodule growth assessment in screening, biases the measurement. A natural safeguard is to let the model judge the image before it sees the metadata. We find this backfires. On 60 NLST chest CT pairs with coverage defects whose ground truth is computed from scan geometry, a false header that Claude Haiku 5.5 resisted when shown with the image (2% of its detections overruled) overturned 91% of them when it arrived after the model's answer (Nemotron-3.5-VL 43% to 100%). With the order reversed, the image overturned 98-100% of the header's errors (Claude models): whichever source came second won. This is not generic sycophancy: a neutral "please confirm" reversed 0 of 1,072 correct refusals, and the effect persisted when the header arrived as a tool result, as in agentic pipelines. One warning sentence cut sequential reversal to at most 9%, and the pattern held on 24 real mismatched pairs. Contributions: (i) evidence that VLM input-check decisions on identical evidence depend on delivery; (ii) a testbed with exact geometric ground truth; (iii) a validation protocol for clinical VLM input checks. Code, prompts and all call logs: https://github.com/nav92061/ct-vlm-delivery-order

## 281. Personalized and Adaptive Federated Learning for Canine Cardiomegaly Keypoint Prediction

- **作者**：Jialu Li, Youshan Zhang, Honggang Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-13 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CR, cs.CV

In the field of medical imaging, limited data and cross-institutional heterogeneity remain major challenges for training robust deep learning models. Federated learning (FL) has emerged as a promising approach that enables collaborative training across institutions while preserving data privacy by keeping datasets local. In this work, we propose a structure-aware personalized and adaptive federated learning framework, PAFL, for automatic vertebral heart score (VHS) estimation and cardiomegaly classification from canine thoracic X-rays. Our method treats the task as anatomical keypoint prediction followed by geometry-based VHS computation, introducing implicit structural consistency and improving interpretability. To address cross-institutional heterogeneity, we adopt a parameter-decomposed architecture that separates shared feature representations from client-specific adaptation modules. Furthermore, we introduce an adaptive, quality-aware aggregation strategy that dynamically weights client contributions based on both dataset size and model quality, thereby enhancing robustness to non-IID data distributions. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed PAFL framework improves models' predictive performance compared to isolated training and to conventional and existing personalized federated learning (PFL) methods. Our results demonstrate the potential of integrating structure-aware modeling with PFL for reliable and interpretable medical image analysis.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18789) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18789)

## 282. RouteDiff: Low-Dose CT Denoising via Fidelity-Refinement Routing and Hypergraph State Regulation

- **作者**：Xinyuan Wang, Ruili Li, Tianyang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-12 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 10 figures, 7 tables. Submitted to Knowledge-Based Systems

Diffusion models have become a strong prior for low-dose computed tomography (LDCT) restoration. However, existing samplers apply one structure-detail trade-off to the whole image, set the restoration emphasis of each step by the timestep, and derive each next state recursively from the current one, carrying its errors forward. We propose RouteDiff, a state-aware dual-route diffusion framework that routes detail to where it can be trusted, regulates each reverse step by the state it has reached, and re-enters the trajectory from an anchored estimate. Fidelity-Refinement Routing (FRR) equips one shared U-Net with a conservative fidelity route and a detail-oriented refinement route, fuses them pixel-wise with a confidence map, and exposes their disagreement as route evidence. Hypergraph State Regulation (HSR) organizes the current features and this evidence into a state hypergraph, whose soft hyperedges group scattered regions in the same state. Its readout yields an ordered stage judgment that modulates the bottleneck, and its incidence guides feature interaction in the skip connections. Estimate-Anchored Re-entry (EAR) re-projects a route-aware anchor onto the trajectory at a fixed step and hands the new state back to HSR to be judged again. With the ten network evaluations of its baseline, RouteDiff achieves the best peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) and structural similarity index (SSIM) on four datasets with inserted, real and simulated noise (40.95 dB and 95.13% on Mayo 2020; 45.18 dB and 97.23% on Mayo 2016). It also generalizes to unseen doses of real scans and yields the highest Dice (80.92%) in downstream segmentation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18786) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18786)

## 283. Third-Place Solution for the UCF UrbanTwin V2X-Real Track at ECCV 2026: Synthetic Point Cloud Generation and Sim-to-Real 3D Object Detection

- **作者**：Antong Deng, Yuxin Zhang, Zhongxiang Xing et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-12 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 1 figure, 4 tables. 3rd place, Track 7 (V2X-Real), UCF UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge, 6th DriveX Workshop at ECCV 2026

This report presents a complete technical solution for the UrbanTwin V2X-Real Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge, covering both synthetic point cloud generation and 3D object detection. For data generation, we adopt per-axis quantile calibration. Public V2X-Real training data are used to construct the real reference distribution, and independent one-dimensional quantile mappings are estimated for the x, y, and z axes of the base synthetic point clouds. The calibrated point clouds are then resampled using a fixed random seed and clipped to the valid spatial range, producing 50 frames with 2,000 float32 XYZ points per frame. For detection, we use a two-stage Voxel R-CNN (VoxelRCNN) detector trained with RangeLDM-enhanced synthetic point-cloud data, point clouds downsampled to 50k points per frame, and reconstructed pedestrian data. Training is performed on four NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 GPUs with a global batch size of 16 for 40 epochs. Final testing uses direct single-model inference with the original VoxelRCNN model. On the official evaluation server, the normalized Realism score is 0.7905 and the normalized Detection score is 0.5583, with mAP@0.5 of 0.3908, BEV mAP@0.5 of 0.4219, and mAP@0.7 of 0.1047. According to the official weighting rule, the final combined score is 0.6 x 0.5583 + 0.4 x 0.7905 = 0.6512. This report documents the complete implementation details of data construction, model training, inference, submission formatting, and official evaluation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18778) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18778)

## 284. Quantum-inspired orthogonal adapters for certifiably robust medical image classification

- **作者**：Olga Tsurkan, Laia Domingo, Victoria Orlova et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-12 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, quant-ph

Medical image classifiers require not only high accuracy but also reliable predictions under small input perturbations. Certified robustness provides mathematical guarantees of prediction stability, but preserving these guarantees when adapting pretrained models to new tasks remains challenging. We propose a fine-tuning approach based on quantum-inspired orthogonal butterfly layers that preserves the Lipschitz bounds of pretrained certified networks. Inspired by quantum circuits of reconfigurable beam splitters, these layers preserve orthogonality by construction using only $\mathcal{O}(n\log n)$ trainable parameters. Their structured design enables efficient classical implementation and offers a path toward future quantum implementations with logarithmic circuit depth under suitable connectivity assumptions. Controlled experiments on MNIST and MedMNIST demonstrate improved certified robustness compared with conventional alternatives and, in the DermaMNIST transfer, greater resistance to the tested attacks than low-rank adaptation (LoRA). We then apply it to trichoscopic image classification for alopecia diagnosis and alopecia areata activity assessment. By adapting a certified ImageNet-pretrained network, butterfly fine-tuning achieves approximately twice the certified accuracy of conventional fine-tuning and LoRA, with comparable classification accuracy on the same backbone and data split and similar empirical resistance to the tested adversarial attacks. Larger robustness guarantees, including certificates covering certain image-processing transformations, can be obtained at the cost of reduced accuracy. These results highlight the potential of quantum-inspired butterfly layers for parameter-efficient adaptation of medical image classifiers while preserving the mathematical guarantees underlying certified robustness.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18775) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18775)

## 285. Agri-MRAG: Parameter-Enhanced Multimodal Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Agricultural Visual Question Answering

- **作者**：Fuyong Liu, Xiantao Lei, Xining Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-12 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Multimodal Retrieval-Augmented Generation (MRAG) has shown promise for Agricultural Visual Question Answering (Agri-VQA), but existing methods still face challenges in retrieving relevant knowledge while avoiding redundancy and in integrating retrieved evidence beyond simple prompt concatenation. To address these issues, we propose Agri-MRAG, a multimodal retrieval-augmented framework for agricultural VQA. Specifically, we first introduce a semantic-consistency-guided clustering mechanism that combines multimodal clustering and weighted relevance scoring to select high-confidence knowledge subsets and adaptively determine the retrieval size $k$. We then design a parameterized knowledge injection strategy, where retrieved agricultural knowledge is encoded into LoRA adapters and merged into the feed-forward network (FFN) layers of the MLLM, allowing external knowledge to influence generation through parameter-level adaptation. In addition, a perplexity-guided (PPL-guided) routing mechanism is employed to switch between implicit parametric reasoning and explicit retrieval-assisted generation according to model uncertainty, balancing inference efficiency and answer reliability. For evaluation, we use PlantVillageVQA as a broad agricultural benchmark and construct PlantVQA, a region-specific forestry and fruit crop VQA dataset containing 7,514 images and 15,028 expert-verified question-answer pairs. Experiments show that Agri-MRAG achieves ROUGE-L scores of 41.21 and 46.58 on the two datasets, outperforming mRAG by 11.70 and 4.51 points, and VisRAG by 8.80 and 3.39 points, respectively.

## 286. AerialOcc: Unified 3D Aerial Perception without Task-Specific Head

- **作者**：Fan Liu, Xiang Gu, Baoyu Lv et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-12 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Aerial perception systems are increasingly expected to support diverse tasks, yet existing solutions remain fundamentally tied to task-specific outputs. Detection, segmentation, and other perception objectives are usually learned either by separate models or by sharing a backbone with multiple task heads, leaving the model itself unaware of the common structure underlying these tasks. We are interested in asking: can a single perception representation support different levels of understanding without being designed for any specific task? We notice that occupancy is a promising task-agnostic representation for drone perception. By modeling the spatial existence and semantics of the environment, it naturally bridges different perception granularities, from coarse scene understanding to fine-grained object localization. Therefore, we introduce AerialOcc, a unified occupancy-based perception model that enables multiple drone perception tasks from a single inference process, without maintaining task-specific prediction heads. To facilitate this direction, we construct AerialOcc-20K, a large-scale multi-class occupancy dataset for aerial environments. Extensive experiments show that AerialOcc achieves competitive performance across diverse perception tasks while preserving a unified architecture. Our findings demonstrate that occupancy can serve as a universal perception interface, offering a new perspective toward flexible and scalable visual intelligence for autonomous drones from a common 3D scene representation. Models and data are publicly available at https://github.com/guxiang2002/AerialOcc

## 287. Frame First, Decode Once: Object-Centric Coordinates and Single-Pass Decoding for System-1 World Models

- **作者**：Abu Anas Ibn Samad
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：12 pages, 6 figures, 7 tables. Code, frozen configurations and per-seed results will be released

Single-pass primitive decoders such as LocateAnything and VFlash predict all quantized coordinate tokens of a geometric primitive in one forward pass, enabling fast, System-1 visual planning. We study what matters when such a decoder serves as a System-1 world model: a fast action-conditioned predictor that ranks candidate pushes by their predicted outcome. On ManiSkill3 PushT-v1 (512 held-out test families, 8,192 rollouts) and on human demonstrations from lerobot/pusht (2,384 test windows), we compare decoders, training losses, rigid-body projections and coordinate frames, using matched checkpoints, hierarchical bootstrap confidence intervals and Holm correction. Two factors of similar size dominate. First, expressing inputs and outputs in the SE(2) frame of the object's initial pose lowers decision regret by 1.9-2.2x for each of five decoders, and the object-frame model trained on 128 families matches the world-frame model trained on 512. Second, single-pass decoding outperforms iterative decoding: in the object frame, single-pass heads reach 0.020-0.021 regret at 1.6-1.8 ms per decision, against 0.046 for 10-step DDIM diffusion and 0.059 for KV-cached autoregression at 10.3 and 12.9 ms. The choice among single-pass heads has no measurable effect on regret, and our registered hypothesis that the frame effect exceeds the decoder spread is not supported. Soft-token expectation with an end-to-end 2D Kabsch projection gives the lowest trajectory error (11.35 px on ManiSkill3, 3.78 px on lerobot/pusht), exact rigid-body consistency, and lower regret than diffusion at every budget from 4 to 50 DDIM steps, at 3-29x lower latency.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18761) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18761)

## 288. Conv-Reluplex: A Verification Framework For Convolution Neural Networks

- **作者**：Jin Xu, Zishan Li, Miaomiao Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 8 figures, 5 tables. Published in SEKE 2021

In recent years, machine learning has demonstrated impressive performance in many real-world tasks, especially in computer vision and natural language processing. However, to apply them in safety-critical systems one needs formal guarantees on the neural network outputs. The Reluplex tool is proposed to verify the safety of deep neural networks (DNNs), and in case the DNN fails to give a correct output, can generate adversarial examples. Since the tool can only handle DNNs, it is necessary to extend the tool to process image data. Therefore, in this paper, we propose the Conv-Reluplex framework, which is designed to verify the convolutional layer and pooling layer in convolutional neural networks (CNNs), and generate adversarial examples when classification is misguided. We conduct several experiments on MNIST to evaluate our approaches. The results show that the original CNN is improved using the adversarial examples generated by our tool, and the precision of classification can be increased significantly.

## 289. From Hosted Service to Field-Owned Infrastructure: Open, Self-Hosted Real-Time Camera-Trap Alerting with Regional Open-Weight Models

- **作者**：Paul Fergus
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：31 Pages, 8 figures

Cellular camera traps can alert field teams within minutes, supporting anti-poaching, threatened-species protection and human-wildlife conflict mitigation. Most systems use central servers, sending images of people and sensitive species locations outside the collecting organisation and often across national borders. We ask whether this capability can run on infrastructure owned by field organisations. Trap Tracker RT is an open-source, self-hosted application that receives images by email or locally via FTP/SFTP, classifies them with a project-specific model and sends alerts through a mail server chosen by the organisation. We evaluated DeepFaune-SSA v1.2, pairing MegaDetector v6 with a DINOv3-based classifier for 33 sub-Saharan taxa, against three open alternatives on 31,131 out-of-distribution images from Kenya, Namibia and Malawi, independent of its training data. DeepFaune-SSA achieved image-level species accuracy of 0.901 (95% CI 0.894-0.908), versus 0.530 for the global SpeciesNet classifier and 0.556 and 0.547 for YOLOv10x and YOLO26x detectors trained on the same regional data. DeepFaune-SSA had the highest recall for all 26 species evaluated. The single-stage detectors missed animals in about a quarter of images; SpeciesNet assigned species labels in only 57%, usually falling back to coarser labels otherwise. In bench tests of the fully local path, alerts were issued about 6 s after upload completion, including with slowed and interrupted uploads. The two-stage pipeline processed 19 images per minute on eight CPU threads without a graphics processor. Trap Tracker RT is used by five organisations in the United Kingdom and Kenya, including for human-elephant conflict mitigation. We discuss how data paths, rather than inference location, determine data sovereignty, the responsibilities of self-hosting and the ethics of real-time monitoring.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18751) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18751)

## 290. Feature-Space Latent Forecasting for Endoscopic Surgical Video Understanding

- **作者**：Songtao Hu, Hang Su
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Surgical workflow assistance is inherently anticipatory: systems must reason about how procedures evolve, not just recognize the current frame. However, many prior approaches perform prediction in pixel space by synthesizing future frames, which primarily focuses on appearance fidelity rather than the semantic representations required for downstream reasoning. To address this limitation, we propose a feature-space semantic forecasting framework that models temporal dynamics directly within the representations of a frozen Vision Foundation Model (VFM). Specifically, we build a hierarchical target representation from multi-layer VFM features, replace future-frame feature tokens with learnable \texttt{[MASK]} tokens, and train a masked transformer to forecast the corresponding future latent features from observed context, thereby formulating procedural evolution as masked future feature prediction. Experiments on public laparoscopic benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements in phase anticipation, multi-label tool presence prediction, and remaining-time estimation over RGB-based forecasting baselines, while enabling a plug-and-play representation interface for lightweight downstream heads.

## 291. Video-as-Experience: Learning Computer Use via Audio-Visual Understanding

- **作者**：Guangzhi Sun, Zhonghao Zhai, Yixuan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Multimodal large language models (LLMs) have enabled capable computer-use agents that perceive graphical user interfaces, reason over instructions, and execute actions. However, existing approaches to learning from video demonstrations typically convert videos into screenshot-based action trajectories, rather than directly leveraging their rich audio-visual information. We introduce VEX (Video as EXperience), a framework that uses audio-visual understanding to unify learning from external demonstrations and reflecting on an agent's own behavior. Following a watch-act-rewatch paradigm, the agent extracts procedural guidance from tutorial videos containing visual demonstrations and spoken explanations, executes tasks, and verifies its progress by rewatching its execution as video. We instantiate VEX with video-SALMONN-VEX, a 9B-scale audio-visual computer-use agent built on the video-SALMONN 2 architecture with a Qwen3.5-9B backbone. We further introduce Action-Grounded Guide Optimization (AGGO), a reinforcement learning method that optimizes video-derived guidance for downstream action prediction, and augment AgentNet, OSWorld, and CUA-Gym with over 24K real-world tutorial videos. Experiments on OSWorld and a complementary subset of CUA-Gym demonstrate consistent improvements, with video-SALMONN-VEX achieving a 22% relative increase in task success over its Qwen3.5 backbone on OSWorld. Moreover, its generated guidance improves Gemini-3.5-flash by 10% relatively on OSWorld. These results highlight the potential of audio-visual understanding as a unified interface for procedural learning and self-verification in computer-use agents.

## 292. VTG-RAG: Long-Video Reasoning via Query-Adaptive Coarse-to-Fine Sampling and Temporal Graph Retrieval

- **作者**：Yao Lu, Yunhao Wang, Yaxin Gao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Long-video reasoning requires a system to localize sparse evidence over extended temporal sequences, preserve temporal structure, and generate answers despite substantial multimodal distractors. Existing video retrieval-augmented generation methods typically build indexes from uniformly sampled video segments. In long-video settings, this design readily introduces irrelevant context and is unreliable for questions with explicit temporal constraints. We propose VTG-RAG, a framework for long-video question answering that combines query-adaptive coarse-to-fine sampling with temporal graph retrieval. First, a two-stage sampling strategy selects core segments with high mean query relevance or high intra-segment relevance variance. The framework then integrates subtitles, speech transcripts, visual descriptions, entities, and relations from these segments into a temporally ordered multimodal graph. Finally, it performs temporally filtered, two-pass retrieval and reasoning across frame, text, and graph evidence spaces. A lot of experiments have proven the effectiveness of the VTG-RAG. Specifically, we evaluate the framework on Video-MME and LongVideoBench. On LongVideoBench, the reported Qwen2.5-VL-32B and 72B results improve from 48.16% to 50.56% and from 52.13% to 53.32%, respectively, with 24 answer-input frames. These correspond to gains of 2.40 and 1.19 percentage points. The ablation experiments of VTG-RAG further support that the contributions of each component are very important.

## 293. Neural Latent Gaussian Process for 3D reconstruction of Mouse Brain Lipids

- **作者**：Daniel Trejo Banos, Artiom Casapu, Luca Fusar Bassini et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：24 pages, 14 figures, 5 tables

We address the problem of reconstructing continuous three-dimensional lipid profiles of the mouse brain from lipid measurements acquired only on a sparse set of two-dimensional tissue sections. Using MALDI-MSI measurements from coronal sections of two densely sampled and six sparsely sampled mouse brains, registered to the Allen Mouse Brain Common Coordinate Framework (CCF), we seek to interpolate the measured hundreds of lipid profiles across the unobserved voxels of the reference brain. We introduce a Neural Latent-Space Gaussian Process (LGP) architecture for this task and critically evaluate its ability to reconstruct spatial lipid distributions, using held-out mouse brain sections to assess generalization. We propose a Gaussian Process prior for the distribution of low-dimensional lipid profiles across the mouse brain (with Matérn or a Riemann manifold kernel). This allows us to encode 3D coordinates in a small number of latent 3D volumes that are then mapped to particular lipids using a multilayer perceptron (MLP). We performed an exhaustive analysis over a range of algorithms from simple linear regression, XGBoost, MLP to state of the art models such as Neural Transcriptomic Field, graph-based SPA3D for spatial omics reconstruction and DEEPSPATIAL. We additionally evaluate GPLFR as an alternative Gaussian-process-based approach. Across 173 measured lipids, LGP outperforms all evaluated baselines, including recent spatial-omics models and GPLFR, achieving a mean correlation of 0.613 and demonstrating the effectiveness of our approach for sparse 3D lipid interpolation.

## 294. Recognizing Events Is Not Enough: Why Streaming VLMs Struggle in Compositional Instruction Triggering

- **作者**：Yehua Huang, Penglei Sun, Xiang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recognizing individual events does not guarantee correct execution of compositional instructions in streaming video. For example, given the request "Notify me when the pot boils after someone turns on the stove and leaves the kitchen", existing models may recognize the individual events yet notify too early or miss the intended moment, failing to track how their temporal conditions are satisfied over time. To evaluate this gap, we introduce CoTriggerBench, which composes annotated video events into temporally constrained instructions and verifies notification timing and silence through executable event plans and source-event replay. To address this challenge, we propose a real-time VLM agent, TEMPI. TEMPI decomposes instructions into atomic event queries and temporal constraints, and uses Scenario Memory to continuously maintain task progress as event evidence arrives, allowing earlier observations to support subsequent decisions beyond the detector's visual window. Even when no notification is required, relevant events continue to update the scenario state. On CoTriggerBench, TEMPI achieves 44.4% instruction-level success, outperforming the strongest closed-source and fine-tuned open-source VLM baselines by 15.6 (Qwen3.8-Omni-Flash-Realtime) and 18.1 (MOSS-VL-Realtime) percentage points, respectively. To support efficient on-device streaming inference, we further optimize the streaming inference pipeline by overlapping partial visual encoding and LLM prefix prefill with frame arrival. On an 8 GB RTX 5060, our optimized detector implementation achieves a 4.90$\times$ speedup.

## 295. Target-Free Autocalibration of Stereo PIV Camera Systems via Persistent-State Motion Representations

- **作者**：Nabil Madali
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Stereo Particle Image Velocimetry (PIV) recovers three-component velocity fields by triangulating tracer particles observed by synchronized cameras. It requires an accurate extrinsic calibration, which is still obtained by inserting a physical target into the flow and must be repeated whenever the rig is moved. We present a target-free method that calibrates a stereo PIV rig directly from particle image sequences. Because tracer particles are visually indistinguishable, we match motion rather than appearance: building on CUT3R, each camera stream is processed by a recurrent transformer whose persistent state accumulates particle motion over time. The state is decoded into per-camera optical flow and queried by a cross-view decoder that regresses bidirectional point correspondences, from which the relative pose is recovered with a five-point RANSAC solver. We train on a new synthetic stereo PIV dataset with ground-truth poses and evaluate on standard PIV optical flow benchmarks and on 200 real rig placements. Our model achieves lower flow error than existing PIV networks, more accurate cross-view correspondences than COTR, and higher pose accuracy than the strongest detector-matcher pipelines on both synthetic and real data, without any calibration target.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18741) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18741)

## 296. TyphoonWorld: A Residual Token World Model for Probabilistic Forecasting of Typhoon Imagery

- **作者**：Xiaoyu Chen, Tianyi Li, Xuanyi Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-11 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：15 pages, 3 figures, 7 tables

A satellite forecast of a typhoon should give a low-error picture of the next two days, together with an ensemble whose members are coherent fields and whose spread responds to the storm at hand. A small direct regression network already gives the lowest squared error on held-out storms, so a generative model has to earn its cost through the part of the future that the regression smooths away. We present TyphoonWorld, a discrete-token world model that generates only that part. Conditioned on tokens of the regression forecast, the latest frames and the best-track state at forecast time, it samples residual fields lead by lead, and members add these residuals to the regression center. We compare it under one protocol with per-pixel Gaussian networks, a diffusion model of the same residuals, residuals from other storms and an unconditional token world model, on 276 forecast windows from 46 held-out storms of Digital Typhoon. TyphoonWorld matches the squared error of the regression, lowers the CRPS of the area colder than 220 K, a direct measure of deep convection, by 14 to 39% against every other ensemble, and ties its spread to forecast error as closely as the Gaussian network, with a correlation of 0.78 across windows. It uses 56% of the parameters of the unconditional world model.

## 297. AITHER: Authority-Informed Evidence Routing for Mixed-Authority Vision-Language Reasoning

- **作者**：Xinjin Li, Haoyue Zhang, Xinyi Gou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-10 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.MM

Reliable vision-language reasoning requires a control decision that conventional grounding methods leave implicit: which input is entitled to determine the answer? Auxiliary text may be a defeasible claim about an image, a binding task instruction, or compatible context. Correcting every image-text conflict toward the image therefore repairs false claims but overwrites valid task rules. We formalize this setting as mixed-authority vision-language reasoning and introduce AITHER, an Authority-Informed Text-Image Hierarchy for Evidence Routing. Its final policy, the Semantic-Frame Router, is a training-free black-box control layer that (i) compiles explicit task directives, (ii) recovers standardized spatial frames from metadata-scrubbed pixels, (iii) resolves the semantic polarity of remaining notes, and (iv) routes among frozen observable candidates with fail-closed semantics. This ordered decomposition converts authority arbitration from an unconstrained prompt preference into a reproducible inference program. Across 1,620 MATRIX predictions, AITHER reaches 95.1% accuracy, exceeding the strongest task-and-frame baseline by 9.1 points with a 95% record-cluster interval of 6.8-11.5 points and no harmed records. It further reaches 100.0% on 1,800 controlled FRAME predictions and 93.3% on GQA-MATRIX. The results show that explicit authority routing can simultaneously improve false-claim correction, preserve task-defined answers, and eliminate clean-context overcorrection.

## 298. Is It Still There? Place-Registered Verification Makes VLMs Reliable Scene-Change Detectors

- **作者**：Jinjie Xie, Baihua Li, Qinggang Meng
- **投稿**：2026-11-10 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：23 pages, 13 figures, 11 tables. Code: https://github.com/Oran9er/STILL

A robot that returns to a space must tell which objects were added, removed or moved since its last visit, while its trajectory, viewpoints and lighting differ between the two visits. We find that multi-image vision-language models (VLMs) fail at this task mainly because they cannot find the place where an object was. A VLM recognises what each frame shows but cannot check whether an object is still at its place in the other capture. It therefore reports objects that only one capture shows and misses most removed and moved objects. We propose STILL, which reformulates change detection as object verification at a registered place. 3D geometry registers the place of each change hypothesis in the other capture, and one VLM answers, from a scene view and a local place view, whether the object is still at that place, trained with supervision of the same form. Under the official SceneDiff protocol, STILL raises the main metric obj/sc AP from 24.0 to 37.8, and from 11.6 to 20.6 on the egocentric kitchen subset without any kitchen training data. It improves F1 by more than 21 points over a VLM fine-tuned on the same annotations and adds only about 11% to the runtime.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18734) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.18734)

## 299. LiNO: Lightweight Network for Occupancy Estimation

- **作者**：Ruan Bispo, Tim Brophy, Reenu Mohandas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-10 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages / 6 figures / 10 tables

Occupancy estimation for automated vehicles is a crucial task that builds a 3D map using vehicle sensors. This map allows the vehicle to identify drivable areas and various obstacle classes. The task involves complex subtasks such as depth estimation, object classification, and segmentation to align semantic information with the 3D grid. Moreover, producing these estimations from multiple sensors demands significant computational power and memory, as high-dimensional data fusion requires complex feature extraction and processing. Consequently, most existing methods rely on large and computationally intensive architectures that are not suitable for real-time or real-world applications. We present a real-time, lightweight architecture that leverages camera and radar sensors through a residual multi-stage head. Unlike prior methods that rely on heavy camera-only blocks, our architecture employs camera-radar fusion and lightweight components. Additionally, we introduce a task-specific data augmentation strategy that enhances data diversity and improves generalisation. Our method achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance, attaining 45.7 mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) on Occ3D and 41.9 Ray-level Intersection over Union (RayIoU) (41.9) on OpenOcc datasets, running at 17.2 frames per second (FPS) and using only 5.3 GB of memory, compared with 4.1 FPS and 7.7 GB for STCOcc.

## 300. ShiftGuard: Evaluating Confidence and Feature Distance for Risk-Controlled Image Classification

- **作者**：Mitra Varun
- **投稿**：2026-11-10 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A classifier that is usually right still makes confident mistakes, and a deployed system needs some way to decide when to answer and when to abstain. Cheap post-hoc scores such as maximum softmax probability (MSP) and predictive entropy are the standard answer. Distances in feature space are a popular alternative, and fusing several signals into one score is a natural next step. We ask whether adding a feature distance signal to MSP and entropy improves error detection, and whether it increases the share of predictions that can be accepted at a certified error rate when only a small labelled calibration set is available. We train a CIFAR-adapted ResNet-18 from scratch on CIFAR-10 (92.92% test accuracy, one seed) and compare five reliability scores: MSP, entropy, distance to the predicted-class centroid, a logistic fusion of MSP and entropy, and a logistic fusion of all three signals. Acceptance thresholds are chosen on a held out calibration set with Clopper Pearson upper bounds, Bonferroni corrected over a prespecified family of 315 rules at 95% simultaneous confidence. Confidence based scores were best. Error AUROC was 0.9230 for MSP, 0.9233 for entropy and 0.9235 for MSP plus entropy, against 0.8814 for the three signal fusion and 0.8224 for distance alone. At a 5% risk target with 1,000 calibration images, entropy accepted 84.82% of test images with 2.05% observed error, while three signal fusion accepted 80.61% with 2.21%. With 250 calibration images, neither distance nor the three-signal fusion could certify the 5% target at all. The results come from one seed, one dataset and one architecture, with no corruption experiments, so they say nothing about distribution shift detection. Within these limits, the distance signal did not earn its added complexity.

## 301. SCALE: Towards whole-slide counterfactual explanations in pathology

- **作者**：Weiyi Qin, Yingci Liu-Swetz, Shiwei Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-10 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Cancer diagnosis relies on histopathological evaluation, where tumor morphology guides disease classification and informs treatment decisions. Deep learning (DL) models excel at predicting diagnostic properties from histopathology images. Unlike typical medical images featuring small critical areas, histopathological images are textural, with relevant texture spanning the entire image; this makes it challenging to explain DL predictions by locating relevant image regions. Counterfactual explanations offer a promising alternative by asking "what changes in the image could have altered the model prediction." To achieve this, we propose SCALE, a framework for generating such counterfactual explanations for histopathological DL models. Across nine counterfactual directions spanning multiple cancer types, SCALE generates whole-slide counterfactual explanations that consistently shift predictions toward the intended target while maintaining biological feasibility. Blinded assessment by six board-certified pathologists showed higher morphological plausibility for SCALE (with a win rate of 91.7% over baselines) and promising counterfactual effectiveness (with a target consistency of 96.7%).
